Once a criterion's follow-up is filed, `workpad.py update --mark-deferred-filed '<criterion line verbatim>'` writes a durable `<!-- prflow:deferred-filed … -->` marker as its own isolated `## Progress` bullet, so a second Phase 4 entry files no duplicate; a value matching no `kind=deferred` record only breadcrumbs (it discharges nothing, and is never a mutation failure). Both readers scan **only** isolated `## Progress` bullets whose whole note text is the marker, so a marker-shaped literal pasted into an AC row, a reflection, or free-text note prose is invisible to them.
- **Rule:** never create a second workpad; always verify a status update actually landed. The caller signal for every `update` mutation is the **machine-readable terminal stderr line** `workpad.py update: outcome=<token> remedy=<token>` (issue #1562), written as the last line by any call that reaches its own exit path. `outcome=` is one of `landed`, `replay`, `landed-partial-ticks`, `landed-status-unverified`, `landed-partial-ticks-status-unverified`, `not-persisted`, `precondition-mismatch`; each is paired by a fixed table in `scripts/workpad.py` with a `remedy=` token from `none`, `retick-named-rows`, `reset-status`, `retick-and-reset-status`, `reissue-call`, `re-resolve-state`, so the caller acts on the named remedy rather than on the shape of the prose above it. The landed-`Status` read-back an exit code cannot discharge is folded into that token: the `-status-unverified` outcomes are exactly the calls whose PATCH response carried no readable Status or a Status disagreeing with the one requested, and their remedy is a follow-up `--status` call rather than a re-send. **`reissue-call` is paired only with `not-persisted`**, so no remedy ever re-sends a call whose PATCH already landed and double-writes the append-only notes; an outcome the emitting path does not itself select is resolved from an observed "the PATCH returned" flag rather than from the exit code, so a crash after a landed write is never reported as `not-persisted`. **An absent line means the write is unverified, never landed** — a crash or a harness refusal emits nothing. Exit codes are unchanged (see the failure-isolation rule below), and every pre-existing prose line — the success breadcrumb naming the PATCHed comment id, the checkpoint-replay line, the Status-mismatch `WARNING`, the volatile-miss report — is unchanged and still written before the outcome line. `update` still writes nothing to stdout by default (issue #814); `--print-body` restores the old whole-body echo for a caller that genuinely consumes it.
- **Failure isolation (volatile vs. structural).** A `update` call PATCHes once. A **structural** failure (a missing target section, a missing `Status`/`Last updated` line, an unreadable `--*-file`, a failed `gh` PATCH, or a terminal `--status Complete` write with any non-post-merge `## Acceptance Criteria` row still `- [ ]` — the self-record gate that keeps a run from recording itself Complete over an unmet AC; unticked `## Plan` rows and an un-mirrored AC placeholder only warn) aborts the whole call before any change is persisted — all-or-nothing, as before. The one exception is a **volatile per-row tick miss** (a `--tick-*` substring matching zero or multiple rows, or a `--tick-*-n` index out of range or landing on an already-ticked row, *inside a present section*): it is **isolated, not aborted** — every other mutation (`--status`/`--note`/`--reflection` and any tick that did resolve) still applies and PATCHes, and the call then exits non-zero with a stderr report naming each missed tick. A miss is therefore never silently dropped, but because the body still PATCHes with the target row left `- [ ]`, that path's outcome line reads `landed-partial-ticks` (or `landed-partial-ticks-status-unverified` when the `--status` read-back was also unreadable), whose remedy directs the caller to re-tick the named row(s) rather than re-send the call. The volatile-miss path is the one arm that still echoes the patched body to stdout under the default (issue #814) — it is the row inventory that positional re-resolution reads — and it writes **no** success breadcrumb, so a success-shaped stderr line never accompanies a failing exit code.
- In **cloud runs**, a lightweight `gate` job creates a lean workpad *before* the heavy Claude job boots, so the user gets immediate acknowledgment.
- **Run-link refresh on resume, and stopped-run note mirroring (issue #2060).** When a cloud `/prflow:implement` run stalls mid-PR and the stall-backstop auto-resumes it (or a human re-triggers), the resumed run lands on a **new** GitHub Actions run with a new run URL. The issue workpad's `Run:` link is rewritten to the current run at the workflow (`gate`) level (the early-workpad step calls `workpad.py update --run-link`), so an operator reading the **issue** clicks through to the active job's logs. The draft **PR** description's `[View run](...)` line is refreshed to the resumed run **at that same gate-job seam** — the gate is the PR link's single owner (issue #2060 moved this off the former §1.4 Resume pre-check seam, closing the stale-link window that seam left open). At resume time, *before the agent starts*, the gate's adopt arm runs `scripts/refresh-pr-on-resume.sh` (resolve the open PR closing the issue, strip any stopped-run note block, and refresh the `[View run]` line — best-effort REST `gh api` PATCH `pulls/{n}`), so a reviewer arriving via the **PR** reaches the live run even if the resumed `claude` job never starts. A **stopped-run note** — the same reason text recorded on the workpad — is mirrored into the top of the open PR body inside an HTML-comment-marked block (`<!-- prflow:stopped-run-note-start -->` … `<!-- prflow:stopped-run-note-end -->`) when a run stops before completion: the three stop terminals — a `Blocked` reflection the agent records, and the `Failed`/`Cancelled` statuses the stall backstop stamps — mirror through `scripts/workpad.py`'s terminal write paths. Three strippers remove it — the next resume's gate strip, the agent-side resume pre-check (`agents/branch-setup.md`, for local resumes), and the completion-time PR-description regeneration — so a **completed** PR shows no note while a stopped one shows why it halted.

- **Startup-lifecycle observability (issue #537).** A cloud run's workpad timestamps **up to four** startup boundaries (a normal fresh run writes three — the `gate-adopted` row only fires on the adopted/resume path) so an operator can attribute startup latency from the workpad alone, and it uses **truthful lifecycle wording** — a normal first run no longer falsely reads "run resumed". The mechanism has these parts:
  - **Handoff record.** The `gate` job decides this run's provenance — exactly one of `created-current-run` (gate saw no workpad and created one), `adopted-existing` (gate found an existing workpad), or `unknown` — and publishes it through a job output. The `claude` job normalizes it into a five-field JSON record (`schema_version`, `issue`, `run_id`, `run_attempt`, `origin`) written to `.prflow/tmp/implement-handoff-<issue>-<run_id>-<run_attempt>.json` before the action. It is workflow-owned, gitignored, non-secret, advisory, and is **not** passed through Claude action settings.
  - **`workpad.py handoff-state`** validates that record **offline** (no network) and prints the origin token, degrading every malformed/mismatched/missing shape to `unknown` with a targeted breadcrumb (so a lost record degrades visibly, never guesses).
  - **Lifecycle wording.** Phase 1.3 selects the hydration event from provenance × live status: `created-current-run` → `agent initialized; Phase 1 workpad hydrated` (never "resumed"); `adopted-existing` + an **interim** workpad → `/prflow:implement run resumed; …`; `adopted-existing` + a **terminal** workpad → `/prflow:implement new run initialized from terminal workpad; …`; `unknown` → `agent initialized; workpad provenance unavailable; …`. `run resumed` is reserved for adoption of an interim workpad from an earlier execution. Local runs read no cloud record and select from live status alone.
  - **Idempotent checkpoints.** `workpad.py update --checkpoint KEY TEXT` writes one timestamped `## Progress` row carrying a hidden `<!-- prflow:checkpoint KEY -->` marker. Keys are `gha:<run_id>:<run_attempt>:<stage>` with the closed stage vocabulary `gate-adopted` / `claude-invoke` / `phase1-entered` / `phase1-hydrated`; including the run **attempt** means a GitHub re-run gets fresh rows while a replay inside one attempt does not. A checkpoint-only replay whose keys already exist is a pure no-op — no `Last updated` refresh, no PATCH. The same keyed-checkpoint mechanism carries the **Phase 4.3 pre-ready base-update evidence record** under the fixed key `base-update-checkpoint-4` (issue #1050) — deliberately **outside** the `gha:` prefix, because the review / review-and-fix tier discriminator reads any `gha:`-prefixed checkpoint as a *cloud* marker and checkpoint 4 runs on **both** tiers, so a `gha:` key would misclassify every local run. That row's marker is what `lib/fetch-pr-context.sh` derives into the bundle's `base_update_checkpoint4_present` field, converting the checkpoint-4 evidence obligation from prose-only into a machine-consumed downstream field. Since issue #1348 the terminal `--status Complete` write is **gated** on this keyed row being present, so the phase prose's earlier degrade-to-`--note` fallback for a non-canonical workpad body is **removed outright** — it wrote an unkeyed row the gate cannot read — and a `--checkpoint` that structurally no-PATCHes now fails Phase 4.3 closed rather than degrading. An **absent** `## Progress` is not one of those failure cases: it is *repaired* by `--checkpoint` itself since issue #1347, created at the head of the section list during its own planning, ahead of the section-shape validation (on that one shape the `--note` fallback never worked either — `--note` located the same section and raised). A duplicated `## Progress` and an empty body still fail closed, and no skeleton is ever synthesized for an empty body (that is `new-body`'s job). The same issue added a second declared key, `base-update-checkpoint-4-tier-refused`, carrying the Phase 4.3 arm where the *tier* refused the checkpoint invocation — a distinct fact from a clean reconciliation, and non-`gha:` for the same discriminator reason — and `workpad.py update --strip-inherited-checkpoints`, which the Phase 1.3 resume arm issues on **both** tiers to clear those declared rows so the record describes *this* attempt rather than any attempt; the strip is scoped to the declared set, so `gha:`-prefixed rows are unreachable by it, and combining it with a `--checkpoint` for one of the declared keys is rejected before any PATCH (inserts are planned against the pre-strip body, so that combination would silently drop the row). Bounded-write discipline (AC19): the gate-adopted checkpoint is issued as a **separate** best-effort PATCH following the run-link refresh (a combined call would abort structurally on a non-canonical workpad and silently drop the run-link refresh too — #1347 narrowed *which* shapes those are, since a merely **absent** `## Progress` is now repaired rather than aborted, but a duplicate section or an empty body still aborts, so the split stands), but it fires only on the **adopted/resume** path — never on a normal fresh run, which creates rather than adopts — while phase1-hydrated rides the Phase 1 hydration update, so a normal fresh cloud run adds at most two standalone PATCHes (`claude-invoke`, `phase1-entered`).
  - **Read-failure & race safety.** Phase 1 preserves `workpad.py id`'s three-way exit contract (0 found / 2 the sole create authorization / 1 unreadable-stop, never a duplicate create) and `status`'s 0/1/2/3 contract, reads the live body before resetting Status, and passes the observed comment ID + Status word as `--expect-comment-id`/`--expect-status` so a concurrent terminal-backstop flip or delete/recreate aborts the stale hydration before any PATCH (exit 4). Checkpoint failures are best-effort (warn and continue); the incomplete-vendor guard fails the job before the action when the vendored `workpad.py` is missing.
  - **Consumer version-skew.** An **old workflow + new plugin** lacks the handoff record, so Phase 1 degrades to `unknown` provenance and still runs. A **new workflow + old plugin** has a helper lacking `--checkpoint`/`handoff-state`, so the checkpoint steps warn and continue and Phase 1 keeps legacy `run resumed` wording. Duplicate-read protection is complete only once **both** the shipped workflow and the pinned plugin carry this fix.

### Phase 1: Setup
- **1.1** Fetch the issue (`gh issue view`), then **classify it bug-report / non-bug from its content** (title + body), with a `bug` label as one input signal; content overrides the label on a positive read, and ambiguity defaults to the label when present (else non-bug). Phase 2.1.5 keys on this recorded classification, not the label.
- **1.2** Parse Acceptance Criteria via `scripts/parse-acs.py`; tag post-merge items.
- **1.3** Initialize or resume the workpad; if `Status: Blocked`, surface the reflection and pause. Record the 1.1 classification as a superseding `classification: ` note and reconcile the reproduction row to it, and repair the prompt-extension rows (`--record-classification` / `--reconcile-reproduction` / `--reconcile-extension-rows`) — a fresh run, a resume with no note, or a terminal re-trigger classifies afresh; an in-flight resume reads the recorded note. The same step also records a durable `resume-kind: ` note (`in-flight` / `terminal-re-trigger`; a fresh run records none, which the gate reads as not in-flight) — the compaction-surviving signal the **Phase 2 §2.0 resume-idempotency gate** reads back (the live `Status` cannot serve, since 1.3 overwrites it to `Setup` on every arm), written as a bare literal and compared by exact value, never containment. **The marker classifies the *workpad*, not the repository, and decides no branch (issue #1134):** it is derived from workpad content alone, its only reader is that gate, and an absent marker says *this workpad carries no record of a prior attempt* — never *no prior attempt exists*, which is exactly what a run whose workpad writes were dropped reports. Branch adoption is decided by §1.4's resume pre-check on observable pull-request state, which runs on every §1.4 entry whatever this marker says; the marker is deliberately not fed from that state, because a terminal re-trigger routinely still has an open PR closing the issue and relabeling it in-flight would fire the §2.0 gate over the prior run's stale Plan.
- **1.4** Create or detect the branch (`scripts/branch-for-issue.py`, off the config-driven `base_branch`). Since issue #1582 the branch resume pre-check, the reuse-vs-create Signals, feature-branch creation, and the §1.4.0.5 Verdict-B classification run in a **dispatched `prflow:branch-setup` subagent** (`agents/branch-setup.md`) that **shares the orchestrator's checkout — never a worktree** — so the orchestrator resumes on exactly the branch the agent leaves it on; the decision (and the §1.4.1 checkpoint contract, its invocation, and §1.5) stays orchestrator-inline. The orchestrator commits any uncommitted tree state before dispatching (issue #1254) and, after the agent returns, re-reads disk state itself (`git branch --show-current`) rather than trusting the returned record. The **resume pre-check runs first on every entry** — fresh run, resume, and terminal re-trigger alike — and is the sole authority on branch adoption: it queries the issue's open pull requests by head branch and by body reference, adopts the head branch of one that closes the issue, and records its outcome durably as a `resume-precheck: ` note in the workpad's `## Progress` section on all three arms (adopted / queried-cleanly-none-found / unresolvable), so a reader can tell an adoption from a first attempt without opening the run log (issue #1134). Its adoption operand is an open pull request, never the bare existence of a branch named for the issue, and branch creation is reachable only through a recorded pre-check outcome. Branch creation is otherwise skipped when the run is already in a linked git worktree (naming-independent harness signal) or when the current branch matches a recognized feature-branch pattern — the existing branch is reused as-is. On the adopted-branch arm and (since issue #780) the landed-resume arm, §1.4.0.5 then runs the ahead-of-base branch-state preflight (Verdict B — `scripts/preflight.py branch-state`) inside the agent, before the update-branch checkpoint and the push (see the *Ahead-of-base branch-state preflight* note below). The agent returns a structured record naming which arm it took (PR-adopted, landed-resume, harness-worktree-switch, or fresh-create) and which of the pre-check/Signals/Verdict-B steps it evaluated (issue #1580); on an in-scope terminal STOP it sets the workpad `Blocked` itself with no history mutation, and the orchestrator performs the terminal ritual. The **update-branch checkpoint is then the last step of §1.4 on every arm** — new branch, adopted branch, and landed resume alike (issue #779), invoked orchestrator-inline after the agent returns `proceed` — so Phase 1 reconciles with the base on every path, while the freshness record stays adopted-arm-only.
- **1.5** Push the branch.

### Phase 1.3.5: Early declared-dependency preflight
Between workpad hydration (**1.3**) and branch work (**1.4**), `/prflow:implement` runs an extracted declared-dependency preflight (`scripts/preflight.py`). It runs before branch adoption, creation, base reconciliation, and push, so open or unresolvable dependencies end the run Blocked **without a new branch side effect**. The cloud implementation requires both the vendored `scripts/preflight.py` helper and its explicit `devflow-implement.yml` allowlist grant.

### Phase 2: Discover, Plan & Implement
- **2.0 Resume-idempotency gate** (fires on an auto-resumed run before §2.1). The Phase 2 `code-explorer`/`code-architect` subagents produce **ephemeral, read-only, in-context** output — neither declares a `Write`/`Edit`/`Bash` tool, so a stall-backstop auto-resume cannot recover their analysis and the entry-gate `Read` restores only the §2.1/§2.2 *procedure*, which unconditionally re-dispatches them. The gate is the Phase 2 analogue of §1.4's Resume pre-check, keyed on the conjunction of a durable Phase 1.3 `resume-kind: in-flight` marker and a committed non-placeholder `## Plan`. On a fire it **skips** the discovery/architecture dispatches and re-plan and builds on the committed Plan and the §1.4-adopted branch — still running §2.2.5/§2.2.6 idempotently and re-verifying against a fresh tree (the #429 rules stay in force), routing to Phase 3 when the Plan is fully ticked and nothing remains — and, for an un-ticked step whose work the fresh-tree re-verification finds already present, ticking it (with a re-verification note) rather than re-implementing it, so a resumed run that lost a tick to a crash-before-delivery does not re-ship committed work; only genuinely-absent steps are re-implemented. A fresh run, a run that died mid-§2.1 before any Plan, and a terminal re-trigger each fail a conjunct, so full discovery runs unchanged. Conjunct (a)'s marker is workpad-derived and takes no input from §1.4's observable branch/PR state (issue #1134), so a run whose pre-check **adopted** a prior attempt's branch with no marker recorded does not fire this gate — it runs full discovery over the adopted branch, the safe direction, and its workpad recorded no Plan either. The gate's fire conditions and skip scope are stated authoritatively at §2.0 of `skills/implement/phases/phase-2-implement.md`.
- **2.1** Discovery via the first-party `prflow:code-explorer` subagent.
- **2.1.5 Reproduce-First Gate** (fires when the recorded content classification is bug-report, not on the `bug` label): capture a reproduction signal (failing test / error log) *before* planning; if it can't reproduce → `Blocked`.
- **2.2** Assess complexity. **Simple** (≤5 files, clear, no architecture) → implement directly. **Complex** → the first-party `prflow:code-architect` produces a blueprint (held in context, never committed). Sub-gates: **2.2.4 Reuse & Altitude** (reuse existing helpers by `file:line`), **2.2.5 Scope-Adjustment** (multi-PR issues), **2.2.6 AC-Plan reconciliation**.
- **2.3 Implement** with mandatory post-write **sweeps** (the discipline that prevents half-finished changes — each heading states its own trigger; the six always-on sweeps — convention, boundary-assumption, self-authored-claim, coverage-claim enumeration, simplification, and error-handling — are the ones no diff shape deselects; each sweep's own heading stays authoritative, and 2.3.4b's states its trigger as a diff that adds prose):
  - **2.3.0** Changed-contract sweep (a modified signature/renamed-or-moved symbol/tightened validator/rerouted predicate — **or a relocated prose literal, heading, section, or file path** whose old-location citations, a `run.sh` pin / docs cross-reference / config-key list, would otherwise dangle → reconcile every dependent site; re-run after any merge/rebase of main)
  - **2.3.0a** Peer-checkpoint completeness sweep (a change that **adds** a rule which must hold at two or more co-equal peer sites, or at every path through one unit of code → classify the rule first, then enumerate: a shared-marker search for textually co-locatable peers, and a **call-edge trace** of the swept unit when the rule is a control-flow property, whose evidence note records the unit traced and the reach of the technique so a traced note cannot read as a closed set)
  - **2.3.0b** Enum-enumeration reconciliation sweep (added value to an enumerated set → reconcile every enumerating site, incl. doc/comment enumerations and fall-through consumers)
  - **2.3.0c** Operand-trace sweep (a diff that adds a guard/predicate/validator/coverage invariant in code, or ships agent-executed policy-stating prose → trace every operand back to its producer; the load-bearing "what OTHER inputs produce the same value?" check owns exactly the in-diff code 2.3.4 carves out)
  - **2.3.0d** Describing-prose reconciliation sweep (a **removal** from an enumerated set, or a **weakened** universal — softened, scoped, or removed → reconcile prose the change falsified without editing it: describing prose naming no member literal, and surviving full-strength copies of the weakened claim; repo-wide enumeration reusing §2.3.0's normalized search, with an explicit unrunnable arm naming the covering backstop instead of a clean pass)
  - **2.3.1** Orphaned-setup sweep
  - **2.3.2** Stranded-dependents sweep
  - **2.3.3** Convention-compliance sweep
  - **2.3.4** Boundary-assumption verification sweep (claims the diff *depends on* about boundaries it doesn't own)
  - **2.3.4a** Self-authored-claim reconciliation sweep (every behavioral claim the diff *authors* in internal/external docs, code comments, and test prose, reconciled against the shipped code path before commit; the code is the fact)
  - **2.3.4b** Coverage-claim enumeration sweep (every **coverage universal** the diff's *added prose* asserts — a sentence claiming a universal about the change's own coverage, "every call site is updated" — grounded before commit one of three ways: pinned by an executed enumeration of the set the quantifier ranges over, scoped to what the change actually covers, or removed. Reading the sentence back does not discharge it. Seeded by `scripts/stale-prose-lint.py`'s recognition-only coverage-universal tier run in its `--worktree` mode over the uncommitted delta; the detector's rows are a floor, not the population. Its twin 2.3.4a owns claims about what the shipped *code* does — a coverage universal describes the *change*, so that trace never selects it)
  - **2.3.5** Simplification & efficiency sweep
  - **2.3.6** Error-handling & silent-failure sweep
  - **2.3.7** Collection-cardinality sweep (trigger-gated: an added collection output with ordering/dedup/aggregation logic → a multi-element test case; a single-element test does not discharge it)
- **2.4** Run tests + lint in parallel; fix failures.
- **2.5** Commit (`feat:`) and push — the run's **final durability checkpoint** (below).
- **Durability checkpoints (issue #1139).** Historically §2.5 held Phase 2's only commit and push, so a run that terminated anywhere earlier — an arbitrary external interruption, or an execution-ceiling kill — lost **every** change it had produced (observed at full cost: a 2h 24m run left zero commits on its branch). Phase 2 now takes a durability checkpoint at **each sub-step boundary it crosses before §2.5** — after §2.1, §2.1.5, §2.2 and §2.4 — and, because §2.3 routinely outlasts the window on its own, **additionally at each §2.3.x sweep boundary**, bounding the worst-case loss to a **roughly ten-minute** window (a design target that sizes where checkpoints go, not a wall-clock assertion). Every checkpoint — and §2.5's own commit — goes through one executable helper, `scripts/phase2-durability-checkpoint.sh`, which owns the durability contract so no boundary re-implements it: it stages **only explicitly-named paths** (`git add -- …`, never `git add -A`/`.`/intent-to-add, so §2.2's sweep-scoping and the fix loop's explicit-path scoping hold and unrelated untracked files stay out of history); it owns the **cloud-tier workflow-edit guard's detect-and-do-not-stage half** (on a cloud run whose `DEVFLOW_APP_ID` is empty it will not stage a repo-own `.github/workflows/` path the `GITHUB_TOKEN` fallback cannot push, matching the **relative `.github/workflows/…` spelling only** — an absolute path, a `../`-reaching form and the bare directory are disclosed non-matches, so the Phase 2 revert stays the primary control — and the coupled-file enumeration and 2.2.5 routing stay Phase 2 prose); it keeps §2.1.5 temporary proof edits out of pushed history **by ordering** (no checkpoint runs with an unreverted proof edit present, and no history is ever rewritten); and it treats a push as landed **only** when `git rev-parse HEAD` equals `git rev-parse @{u}` after pushing — a rejected non-fast-forward leaves them unequal and is a failure to land, and the comparison is against the **local** remote-tracking ref, which nothing here fetches, so a remote branch deleted or rewound after a successful push is a disclosed blind spot — mirroring the remote-anchored re-check in `skills/implement/references/doc-deliverable-self-heal.md`. A checkpoint with nothing new makes no commit, so a resumed run adopting the branch sees the prior content exactly once. **Phase 2's accruable workpad writes ride these same boundaries (issue #2039).** Rather than one `workpad.py update` per completed Plan step and per mid-Phase-2 status flip, those timing-insensitive mutations — the per-step `--tick-plan` ticks, the §2.2 `--status Planning`/§2.3 `--status Implementing` flips, and post-hoc evidence notes such as sweep-result notes — accrue in run context and are delivered as **one combined `workpad.py update` per taken boundary, after that boundary's checkpoint push**, with the `--tick-plan` set re-derived from durable state (re-read the `## Plan`, tick the steps the just-checkpointed commits contain) so a compaction loses no tick. Records whose timing a consumer reads — every reflection, `--record-*`/`--checkpoint` producer record, terminal `--status` flip, `--expect-*`-guarded call, and the criterion-route/test-first/sweep-selection/§2.2.7-map/`§2.3 operand ledger:` notes — keep their immediate call sites; a boundary the run skips as an empty no-op carries its accruals forward and §2.5 always delivers. Authoritative procedure: §2.0.5 of `skills/implement/phases/phase-2-implement.md`; the git-state contract is covered by `lib/test/modules/phase2-durability-checkpoint.sh`. **Durability is not the only reason Phase 2 commits (issue #1254).** A dispatched Agent-tool subagent works in the orchestrator's own checkout unless the runner gives it one, so a committed tree is also the precondition for every mid-Phase-2 dispatch — including the prompt-surface edit helpers — and the one window a checkpoint cannot supply it is a live §2.1.5 proof edit, which the ordering above deliberately keeps uncommitted. The condition, its park-and-restore arm and its `Blocked` terminal are stated once, in `CLAUDE.md`'s commit-before-dispatch convention.

### Phase 3: Review & Fix
- **3.1** Create the **draft** PR against the configured `base_branch` (`Resolves #{issue}`) — behind an **existing-PR guard**, because a resumed run (a §2.0 gate fire, or any run whose §1.4 pre-check adopted a branch/PR) reaches 3.1 with the prior attempt's PR already open and a bare create would abort. The resolution is branch-selecting logic, so it lives in `scripts/resolve-existing-pr.sh` — invoked as a single leading-token vendored-literal command taking only the issue number, with the head branch and base re-derived inside the helper — and `lib/test/run.sh` drives every arm and the arm order over the full input matrix, per the repo's workflow inline-shell extraction convention. The helper queries `gh pr list --head … --state open` (open-scoped and branch-explicit — never `gh pr view`, which resolves across OPEN/CLOSED/MERGED), picks the newest by `createdAt`, and prints exactly one token with a matching exit code: `ADOPT <n> OK` (0), `ADOPT <n> WARN:<checks>` (0), `CREATE` (2), `REFUSED` (3). It has no silent path, so a fence that prints nothing is a harness refusal routed as `REFUSED`. **Adopt** takes the found PR (never re-writing its body, and reading its URL by explicit PR number so link and number cannot disagree) — and adoption is *validated*, not head-branch-match alone: the helper checks that the PR lists the run's issue in `closingIssuesReferences` and targets the run's base, naming each failed check in the `WARN:<checks>` token (`closes-issue`, `base-ref`). A `WARN` adoption still proceeds — a visibility obligation, not a stop — with the failed checks recorded durably on the workpad as a `note` reflection. **Create** runs when the query cleanly found none; **REFUSED** (never collapsed onto create) is on a *resume* a terminal `Blocked` stop with a durable reflection rather than a duplicate PR, and on a *fresh* run falls through to the create (no prior attempt to duplicate). A create that itself fails takes the same terminal stop; the create and PR-link fences print their own outcome tokens so success is read, not inferred.
- **3.1.1** On the **CREATE** arm only (never ADOPT), best-effort **assign the new draft PR to the triggering user** (issue #1165) through `scripts/apply-pr-triggerer.sh <pr-number>`, so reviewers read ownership from the standard assignee field. It resolves the triggerer by tier — a cloud run reads the authorized comment sender the workflow propagates through `DEVFLOW_TRIGGERING_USER` (`github.event.sender.login`; **fail-closed** — an empty value on the cloud tier is a deployment-skew signal that skips assignment rather than substituting the token owner / App identity / `GITHUB_ACTOR`), a local run resolves `gh api user --jq .login`. It POSTs to `repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/{n}/assignees` and confirms the login is present in the response before reporting `assignment: applied <login>` (GitHub can silently ignore an unassignable login → `assignment: skipped unconfirmed`); reapplying is idempotent and removes no existing assignees. The helper always exits 0 and prints one outcome token, so a skip or a no-output harness refusal routes to a `dropped-failed` workpad reflection and never gates the run. An adopted PR's assignees are left untouched. See [`docs/internal/implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md) and [`docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`](cloud-setup.md).
- **3.2** Self-review with the built-in `/simplify` skill; commit fixes (`refactor:`).
- **3.3** Run `/prflow:review-and-fix <pr-number> --push-each-iteration --issue <issue-number>` — the draft PR number Phase 3.1 printed is passed as a **bare leading numeric token** so the fix loop runs in **PR mode** against the run's own PR (when Phase 3.1 printed no number the token is omitted and the loop falls back to current-branch mode), the `--push-each-iteration` flag propagates each iteration to remote so CI validates, and `--issue <issue-number>` supplies the acceptance-criteria source. Handles the loop's verdicts, including the special `APPROVE WITH UNRESOLVED SHADOW FINDINGS` (a bounded single re-review). A non-clean exit (AWUSF or `REJECT`) routes through a **severity-aware exit**: only a genuine unresolved Critical (or an unparseable verdict) takes the Blocked path; a non-Critical residual soft-proceeds (surfaced, PR left review-ready). A loop that never ran the engine is routed apart from that: where the engine root's delivery completeness could not be established the loop halts at `engine-root: incomplete` and reaches no Loop Exit, so it returns no verdict and no marker, and this phase takes the Blocked path **directly** — the severity-aware exit would otherwise grade an empty residual set as non-Critical and soft-proceed on a PR no reviewer read. The degraded engine-read arm — taken when the `Skill` invocation is refused twice and the engine is read from the tree — additionally `Read`s Step 1's completeness predicate from `references/loop-control.md` (that skill did not load on this arm, so its text is absent) and records the sibling terminal `engine-predicate: unread` when it is that predicate read, not the engine root, that failed, so the durable Blocked reflection names the file that actually failed.
- **3.4 Acceptance Criteria Gate:** every **non-post-merge** AC checkbox must be ticked before the phase passes — but **not from the orchestrator's own inline check** (issue #1575). Because the orchestrator drove Phase 2's implementation and Phase 3.3's fix loop, a gate it resolves in its own context inherits its own assumptions about what it built (the #1450 failure mode: it "ticks criteria satisfied without re-verifying the shipped claim"), and a lone dispatched verifier that is wrong is silently wrong. So the gate **dispatches two fresh-context verifiers in the same turn** — after committing any uncommitted tree per issue #1254's shared-checkout convention, and resolving the extension-governed facts (test command, single-flight helper paths, plugin root) and passing them **by value** into each dispatch prompt (the `[[PLUGIN_ROOT]]` pattern; neither verifier reads or reloads the consumer prompt extension) — and routes the gate from their **reconciled** per-criterion record:
  - **`prflow:ac-evidence-verifier`** (`agents/ac-evidence-verifier.md`) establishes each in-scope criterion's verification evidence and is the **only** one that runs an in-env verification command or touches the single-flight coordination, so the two never race the same command run.
  - **`prflow:ac-claim-verifier`** (`agents/ac-claim-verifier.md`) checks the shipped code against each criterion's literal claim from the diff and current tree and **executes nothing** — its declared `tools:` list (`Read, Grep, Glob`, no `Bash`) is what enforces that inability. For a verification-command criterion it reads the command's *source* and checks each clause of the criterion has a corresponding assertion; for a **measurement criterion** (one whose verification names a measuring instrument whose output is a value — a `wc -c` byte count, a `git merge-base`-driven list comparison) it grades whether that instrument measures the criterion's literal claim (byte counter for a byte ceiling → `satisfied`; byte counter for a word ceiling → `unmet`), leaving the measured value to the evidence verifier, so it no longer reports `unestablished` merely because producing the value needs execution.

  Each verifier reports per criterion one status — `satisfied`, `unmet`, or `unestablished`, never collapsing an `unestablished` onto either — **and a `dispositions` object declaring what procedure it ran** (issue #1580): one entry per named step of its own charter (evidence verifier: `type-decided`, `command-run`, `single-flight`, `evidence-recorded`; claim verifier: `claim-traced`, `command-source-read`, `evidence-recorded`), each written `yes` or `no` with a one-clause reason. A stated `no` is a permitted, fully discharging value — the gate asks for a *stated* disposition, never a particular one — while a slot left unstated, or stated without a parseable verdict-plus-reason, is **undischarged**. Neither verifier dispatches a further subagent or writes to the workpad; the orchestrator performs every mutation, and records the whole gate's dispositions on the workpad once, before any criterion is ticked. The orchestrator reconciles the two reports through `scripts/reconcile-ac-verifiers.py`, where the **slot gate resolves first, per side, before the two statuses are paired**: a side that left any named charter step undispositioned is forced to `unestablished` up front, so a criterion **both** verifiers called `satisfied` still blocks whenever either failed to attest (an absent record, and a duplicate-poisoned one, name no undischarged slots — each is a missing vote, not an attestation failure). The paired rules then apply as before: **both agreeing records that status; any disagreement records `unestablished`** (never resolved by preferring one verifier), and a **`satisfied` never lands without an evidence pointer** from at least one verifier (a `satisfied` with no evidence reconciles `unestablished`). An `unreadable`/malformed report (exit 3) is likewise an unestablished measurement. The reconciled record carries `evidence_dispositions`, `claim_dispositions` and side-qualified `undischarged_slots` (e.g. `evidence:command-run`), plus `evidence_status_reported` / `claim_status_reported` — each side's own conclusion retained even where the slot gate overrode it, so a criterion blocking on a real `unmet` stays distinguishable from one blocking only on an attestation gap. A reconciled `unestablished` **blocks exactly as an unmet criterion blocks** — so a verification command that passes while its assertions exercise a *different* claim than the criterion states no longer yields `satisfied`. The orchestrator then applies the existing routing (post-merge exemption, documentation-AC deferral to Phase 4.1, retroactive retagging, the in-env-only verification rule, the Blocked escalation) **unchanged in substance, from the reconciled record** rather than from its own check. The gate ticks confirmed ACs **by 1-based section index** (`--tick-ac-n`, repeatable in one call) rather than hand-picked prose substrings, and gates on the tick call's **exit code**: a volatile index miss still PATCHes the body while leaving the AC `- [ ]`, so the gate passes only when the targeted row reads `- [x]` **and** the tick exited 0 (an unresolved index re-resolves or routes to Blocked). A `(post-merge)` tag is allowed **only** for a criterion that genuinely needs a runtime environment absent during the run (a live deploy target, a real third-party endpoint). A criterion that is runnable on the orchestrator host — or blocked only by a local tooling/environment gap — or any criterion whose purpose is to confirm a behavioral claim the PR already asserts, or any **self-reconfiguration** criterion whose only unmet precondition is the orchestrator's own session/harness/account being in the configuration the diff just shipped (a hook the diff registered now active, a flag/setting the diff added now enabled — issue #338), is **never** retagged post-merge; it is run-and-evidenced (an automated test driving the now-active path, or a separate session observing the change live) or takes the existing **`Blocked`** escalation path instead. Every mid-run `(post-merge)` retag via `--rewrite-ac` is auditable: `workpad.py` structurally rejects a `--rewrite-ac` call that appends the tag (a single pair or a crafted multi-pair sequence) without a non-empty `--note` rationale (issue #338). This makes the gate enforce "verified before merge" rather than trust the run's narrative. As a special case, an AC whose verification is *running a test/lint/build command* is satisfied **only by an in-environment observed pass** (issue #405) — the run executes the command in its own environment (the evidence verifier's context) and never waits on, polls, or cites CI for its own progress: an in-env pass ticks the criterion, an in-env failure is a real failure (fix it or take **Blocked**, never `(post-merge)`), and a command **not granted** in the run's allowlist takes the **Blocked** path naming `prflow_implement.allowed_tools` as the remedy — never a CI deferral. CI (`lib + python tests`) remains the required post-PR check that gates the human merge, not an in-run verification channel. A grep of a few SKILL-contract pins is not a substitute for the suite.

### Phase 4: Documentation
- **4.0** File follow-up issues for deferred ACs. `skills/implement/phases/phase-4-documentation.md` §4.0 is a compact predicate stub: it asks `workpad.py deferred-presence` whether a Phase 2.2.5-deferred acceptance criterion is still outstanding (or unestablished) and, only on that arm, `Read`s the gated reference `skills/implement/references/deferred-ac-followups.md`. **Composition and the GitHub writes are split (issue #1604):** the reference **dispatches the first-party `deferral-drafter` subagent** (`agents/deferral-drafter.md`, tools `Read, Grep, Glob, Write` — no `Bash`, no `Agent`), which **composes** each follow-up issue body under `.prflow/tmp/` (gitignored via `/.prflow/*`) and returns a **filing plan naming each draft by path** — never inline body text — plus the normalized `marker_value` the orchestrator discharges each deferral marker against. The subagent makes **no** GitHub write and dispatches nothing; the reference then performs every **GitHub write** itself from that plan — the `gh issue create` fence, `deferred.labels` application, the `Blocked by #N` blocked-by dependency registration, and the `--mark-deferred-filed` discharge. Before those writes the reference **reads the plan's degradation signals** — `writing_standard_loaded`, `parent_slots_source` and `notes:` — and records every non-clean one as a durable `dropped-failed` workpad reflection, and it leaves a criterion the plan reports as **unplaced** undischarged so a later Phase 4 entry re-files it; without that read those signals reach no decision and no durable record, and thin follow-ups are filed and permanently marked done. The dispatch is authorized by `skills/implement/SKILL.md`'s self-maintaining injection-condition clause (issue #1602) — the reference is an implement-bundle member — with **no** SKILL.md edit, and the orchestrator commits its tree before dispatching per the shared-checkout convention (issue #1254). The `deferral-drafter` prompt is agent-executed prose with no automated regression coverage by design; `lib/test/run.sh` asserts only the machine-consumed boundary — that `agents/deferral-drafter.md` matches none of `gh issue create`, `gh issue edit`, `apply-labels.sh`, `ensure-label.sh` and carries no Agent-tool dispatch, so the GitHub writes and the one-subagent-layer constraint both rest on the orchestrator.
- **4.0.5** Ask `scripts/discover-deferral-manifests.py --presence-for-pr <N>` whether any deferred review finding is present — over the run-scoped manifests **and** the slug-level aggregate, because a first entry has no aggregate and a re-entry after filing has no unconsumed manifests. It reports present / absent / unestablished as exit `0` / `1` / `2` (a malformed invocation reporting `2`, and discovery mode's `3`/`4` split deliberately collapsing into it), and every outcome **except** exit `1` carrying the literal `absent: 0` line loads `skills/implement/references/deferred-review-findings.md`, where the filing procedure below lives — the skip is the narrow arm and the load is the residual, because `1` is also a crashing interpreter's status and a harness refusal prints nothing at all (issue #1374). Then, from that reference: discover the run-scoped deferral manifests (via the same helper in its root-argument discovery mode), merge them into one slug-level aggregate, then file **one follow-up issue per source file** (via `scripts/file-deferrals.py`) for review findings deliberately deferred during the fix loop. Discovery is **status-bearing** (issue #555): the helper searches each candidate root *independently*, classifies each `ok`/`absent`/`failed`, and carries the outcome in its exit code (`0` clean, `3` partial, `4` all-failed) with fixed stderr markers on the degraded outcomes — so a search that *failed* is no longer indistinguishable from a genuine no-match. The phase records `discovery=` on its unconditional sentinel and routes fail-closed: only `ok`/`partial` may file, `failed` (or a refused discovery command, which prints nothing and lands as `discovery=[]`) records a `dropped-failed` reflection and files nothing, and only `discovery=[ok]` with an empty manifest is read as the clean no-op. The predecessor — one multi-root `find … | sort` whose exit status the pipe masked and the capture discarded — read a degraded search as that clean no-op and silently stranded acknowledged deferrals (observed live on issue #533).
- Both deferral channels (4.0 and 4.0.5) label the follow-up issues they file with the configured **`deferred.labels`** (comma-separated, default `PRFlow,Deferred`) by a single leading-token `apply-labels.sh <issue> --config-key .deferred.labels --config-fallback PRFlow,Deferred` call per filed issue — the helper resolves the value through `config-get.sh` itself, normalizes it (an empty/whitespace-only value applies none), creates each label, and applies them best-effort after creation so a label hiccup never blocks filing, printing one stdout outcome token the channel routes on. Config-resolution lives in the helper's config mode, not `file-deferrals.py` (config stays resolver territory — read through `config-get.sh`, not from Python); the docs channel (Phase 4.1) uses the same helper mechanism with `--config-key .docs.labels`. It is independent of the hardcoded `PRFlow` provenance label (and its superseded `DevFlow` spelling) the retrospective scan matches.
- **4.1** Update internal + external docs + release notes (via the `/prflow:docs` subagent); commit (`docs:`); apply configured labels (default `Documented`).
- **4.2** Generate the PR description (`/prflow:pr-description`). In *this* repo the skill's own prompt extension (`.prflow/prompt-extensions/pr-description.md`) additionally has it run `scripts/prompt-surface-growth.py` and insert that stdout verbatim: a table of the byte delta this branch introduces in each changed tracked `*.md` under `skills/`, `agents/` and `.prflow/prompt-extensions/`, plus the running total at `HEAD` and a whole-surface aggregate row (issue #1350). It is **measurement only** — no threshold, ceiling or budget, always exit 0, and a stated one-line breadcrumb instead of a table when `HEAD` is the merge-base, when no covered path changed, or when the merge-base cannot be resolved. The figure is generated rather than composed because an agent asked to report its own prose growth will estimate. Consumer-inert: the helper ships with `scripts/`, but nothing invokes it without this repo-local extension.
- **4.3** Run the pre-ready base-branch update checkpoint, then publish the PR (`gh pr ready`) — unless `prflow_implement.implement_pr_state` is `draft`, which leaves it the Phase 3.1 draft for a human to publish; set status `Complete`; emit the 🎉 reaction (both cases). Since issue #779 both the publish and the `Complete` flip are **gated on the checkpoint reporting a clean token** — a non-clean one records `Blocked` naming the observed line instead (see [`docs/internal/implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md)).

### Single-flight verification (issue #528, Wave 2)

A PRFlow lifecycle can launch the same multi-minute verification profile many times — Implement's inline review pass and each Review-and-Fix iteration relaunch the full suite, and a timeout, lost tool result, or compacted context can force yet another launch. `scripts/verification-flight.py` coordinates each **same-checkout** full-verification flight so an unchanged suite is not re-run, **without ever executing the command itself** (it launches no subprocess, accepts no executable argv, and runs no `git` — the existing caller keeps command ownership and allowlist enforcement).

- **Handle lifecycle.** The owner `claim`s a flight (atomic `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` publish, one owner only; a one-time owner token is returned and only its SHA-256 digest is stored), records `mark-running` immediately before launching its already-authorized command, then `finish`es with terminal evidence (suite summary, exit status, `skipped_checks`). A suite result is established from the runner's **terminal summary line** — wherever the runner writes it, stderr included — never from a bare process or wrapper exit status when the runner printed a tally; a command silent on success is established from its own exit status, and a command that never ran is established as nothing. A later same-checkout caller attaches and `status`/`wait`s to **consume** a `passed` result instead of relaunching — but a consume is a pass only when the read *verified the working tree*: `status`/`wait` enforce the state-pass **AND** checkout-verified condition themselves (issue #1243), so a bare read given no fresh `--current-checkout-file` — or one that drifts — reports non-pass and exits non-zero rather than reusing a pass over a tree it never confirmed; a caller that genuinely wants the state dimension alone asks for it with `--allow-unverified-checkout`. Every **owner** transition (`mark-running`, `finish`) is a token-checked compare-and-swap; attacher, stale-token, replay, and post-terminal transitions are rejected. The two **read-time invalidations** are deliberately tokenless and non-owner — a `claimed` handle whose lease expired before `mark-running` becomes `incomplete`, and an active handle whose stored checkout no longer matches a supplied current checkout becomes `stale` — and each can only move an active handle to a **non-pass** terminal state, never to `passed`.
- **The flight key.** The reusable identity binds a **command descriptor** — sorted-key compact UTF-8 JSON of `{profile_version, argv, cwd, environment, toolchain, dependencies}`, SHA-256'd by the helper — plus a five-field `checkout` fingerprint (`checkout_id`, `head`, `index_digest`, `tracked_digest`, `untracked_digest`). That fingerprint has a single producer, `scripts/checkout-fingerprint.py` (issue #1243), whose header defines each field's git derivation once and whose four content fields are git object ids; `_validate_checkout` requires exactly that object-id shape, so an invented placeholder (`"v"`, `"clean"`, …) is rejected rather than silently keying a flight over a fingerprint of nothing. Commits, edits, merges, rebases, profile changes, and relevant dependency changes all change the key and so **invalidate reuse by construction**; generated suite outputs under declared `output_roots` never churn the key — **an exclusion the caller-supplied `checkout` fingerprint carries, by the caller's discipline; `_derive` folds only `profile` and `checkout` into the key and enforces no output-root rule of its own.** Separately (issue #668), `scripts/verification-flight.py` accepts an optional top-level `candidate_identity` declaration field — the content-based identity `scripts/reception_identity.py` derives (see below), recorded verbatim on the handle as an **independent observability field that does not participate in the key**. It is a sibling of `checkout`, never a member of it, so `descriptor_digest` and `flight_key` stay byte-unchanged and every stored handle stays valid.
- **Unknown is never a pass.** States are exactly `claimed`, `running`, `passed`, `failed`, `timed_out`, `cancelled`, `stale`, `incomplete`. Only `passed` with complete matching bindings satisfies verification. Missing/partial/timed-out/unreadable/stale state is an attributable non-pass and never authorizes an automatic relaunch; a lease that expires before `mark-running` becomes `incomplete`; checkout drift becomes `stale`; missing terminal evidence becomes `incomplete`. A `wait` bound elapsing returns a non-mutating `wait_expired` observation — only the owner records a terminal `timed_out`.
- **A truthfully-declared non-hermetic profile is recordable but never reusable (issue #2080).** A `claim` declaration's `external_services` field names either the canonical hermetic value `none` or, truthfully, a live external service the verification depends on; only a malformed shape (non-string, blank after stripping, or a string that strips to `none` without being exactly `none`) is refused before any state write. A hermetic declaration stores the flight under the original `SCHEMA_VERSION` exactly as before. A non-hermetic declaration is accepted and stores the flight under a **distinct** per-record schema marker, `SCHEMA_VERSION_NON_HERMETIC`, so a pre-#2080 reader fails closed on it (`unknown_schema_version`) rather than treating it as a reusable pass. A `passed` non-hermetic flight reports `satisfies_verification: true` — it backs completion evidence exactly like a hermetic pass — but a shared `_reusable` predicate makes `status`, `wait`, and the claim-attach view all report `reuse_ready: false` for it, so the reuse path never serves it as a clean prior result; the caller re-runs verification and re-claims on every later attach instead of attaching to the stale non-hermetic handle.
- **Consumers.** Implement persists the handle + terminal evidence in its durable state and re-anchors after nested work/compaction; Review-and-Fix persists them in its `iter-<N>.json`. On `wait_expired`, Implement and implement-driven Review-and-Fix take the existing **Blocked** path without relaunch; standalone Review-and-Fix records `verification_evidence.result: skipped` (reason `flight_wait_expired`) and cannot report a clean pass. **CI-grounded standalone `/prflow:review` creates no flight and runs no verification command.** Config lives under the versioned `verification_flight` namespace; the vendored helper is granted in `devflow.yml` and `devflow-implement.yml` only — `devflow-runner.yml` gains no grant.

### Phase-boundary event instrumentation (issue #1853)

Phase 2 and Phase 3 dominate a long implement run's wall clock, but historically neither wrote a clock-authored timing record, so an expensive run's interior — where the time and money went between phase boundaries — could not be reconstructed after the fact from anything but untimestamped `- [x]` ticks and agent-volunteered aggregates (an unestablished measurement under issue #1489). The `event <name>` subcommand on `scripts/verification-flight.py` closes that gap by **generalizing the same clock-authored event shape** the single-flight coordinator already uses — rather than adding a second recording idiom.

- **The subcommand.** `verification-flight.py event <name> [--payload <json>] [--log-dir <dir>]` appends one record `{"event": <name>, "recorded_at": <iso>}` to an append-only JSONL log at `.prflow/logs/phase-events/phase-events.jsonl` (`O_APPEND`; the directory is created best-effort). The timestamp originates in the helper's **own clock** (`_now()`/`_iso()`), never the caller — so the record cannot be model-forged. An optional `--payload` object merges extra keys in, but `event` and `recorded_at` are the record's clock-authored identity and a payload key can never shadow them. A malformed or non-object `--payload` — a JSON `null` included — is dropped with a stderr breadcrumb and the **base event is still recorded**, while an empty `--payload` is treated as absent, like the flag's own default.
- **Never blocking.** The append **always exits 0**; a failed write (e.g. an unwritable log directory) emits a stderr breadcrumb and the run continues. Instrumentation that can fail a run would be worse than the observability gap it closes, so every instrumented boundary invokes it best-effort, with the vendored literal as the command's leading token (no `VAR=` prefix, expansion, or redirect, which the cloud matcher would silently deny).
- **The instrumented boundaries.** The implement Phase 2 durability-checkpoint boundaries emit `phase2-checkpoint` (`skills/implement/phases/phase-2-implement.md`, `phase-2-sweeps-quality.md`, at each sub-step boundary where `scripts/phase2-durability-checkpoint.sh` already runs); Phase 3 emits `phase3-simplify-start` / `phase3-simplify-end` around `/simplify` (`skills/implement/phases/phase-3-review.md`), `phase3-reviewers-dispatch` / `phase3-reviewers-return` around the Phase 3.3 `review-and-fix` dispatch and its return (`skills/implement/phases/phase-3-fix-loop.md`), and `phase3-shadow-entry` at shadow-review entry (`skills/review-and-fix/references/shadow-review.md`). The result is a run's interior timeline reconstructible from disk without trusting the agent's recollection.
- **Scope.** No new agent-volunteered duration field is added and no existing telemetry field changes meaning. The helper is already granted with a wildcard over its arguments (`Bash(.prflow/vendor/prflow/scripts/verification-flight.py:*)` in `lib/capability-profiles.json`), so the new subcommand adds **no** grant and avoids the trigger-time grant bootstrap. The phase-prose additions are agent-executed prompt prose with no wording pin; the helper's own subcommand carries the automated boundary (record shape, clock-authored timestamp, exit-0 contract, failed-write breadcrumb), owned by the `harness-python-guards` module. Out of scope, each owned elsewhere: the whole-suite command's own elapsed time (#1808) and in-flight wait/stall detection (#1027).

### Receiving-review session artifacts (issue #668)

The `receiving-code-review` skill's **Reception Preflight** now produces a machine-checkable session identity instead of only rendering in-chat facts. Two bundled helpers ship (by the `prflow_version` vendor fetch), and their cloud grants ship by `install.sh`'s workflow copy loop — two independently-upgraded artifacts, so the two-halves upgrade coupling applies.

- **`scripts/reception_identity.py`** — an importable, python3 standard-library-only routine exposing `derive_candidate_identity()`. **The candidate identity is the git tree object ID of the working-tree *content*** — tracked files at their working-tree content plus untracked non-ignored files, with gitignored content and HEAD excluded. It is derived through git's own index-cached plumbing: a temporary index **seeded from the current index** (load-bearing — it preserves the skip-worktree entries a cone-mode sparse checkout leaves off disk), a `git add -A` staging all such content including deletions and renames, and a `git write-tree`. **The current index is an input, not an exclusion:** for an ordinary path `git add -A` resolves the entry to worktree content, but for an entry git deliberately does not re-stat — a skip-worktree (sparse) entry, or an `assume-unchanged` (CE_VALID) entry — the index content decides the value, so an `assume-unchanged` path's worktree edit does not change the derived identity. That is the documented consequence of seeding from the real index, which sparse-checkout correctness requires. The repository's own index is never modified and no history is read. **Contract:** for a working state whose content is then committed unchanged (a commit form that stages untracked content), the derived value is byte-equal to `git rev-parse HEAD^{tree}` after that commit — commit-invariant across the recorded content — while any tracked-content change after the verified state compares unequal. `git commit -am`, which stages tracked modifications only, correctly compares unequal for a derivation that included a new untracked file. Every failure mode raises a named breadcrumb and yields no identity.
- **`scripts/reception-record.py`** — the CLI. One `record` invocation derives the identity (via the library — exactly one implementation ships), mints a per-session cryptographic **claim-context nonce**, and writes an **identity artifact** and a **findings ledger** under the gitignored session directory keyed by the nonce, plus a fixed-name **session pointer** naming them. It confirms the directory is ignored — through `git check-ignore`, not a path-shape assumption — before writing, so a repository lacking the ignore rule is reported rather than given a self-invalidating identity. `append-disposition` appends one per-finding disposition with a **helper-assigned finding id**; a deferral disposition must name one of the skill's four durable channels or is rejected.
- **The two new preflight facts.** The block grows from nine to **eleven facts** — candidate identity (fact 10) and claim-context token (fact 11) — each carrying a status from the skill's existing six-member closed set. Both render `established` only when the invocation exits 0 **and** its stdout parses as a JSON object carrying both values; any non-zero exit, absent or non-parsing stdout, or a stderr `{"ok": false}` record renders both `missing` and the run continues (causes: absent, denied, unavailable interpreter, an unignored/unwritable session directory, or a failed identity derivation). The predicate is the exit status plus the stdout parse, not "produced no output" — every failure path writes a record to **stderr**, so a no-output test would never reach the `missing` arm. The editing gate is untouched (it bars only on a `mismatch` head-match verdict or an `ambiguous` subject).

### The completion-evidence check (issue #550)

A receiving-review completion claim is now gated on a **producer-owned completion-evidence check** so a claim's own evidence is validated before a maintainer merges on it. `scripts/check-completion-evidence.py` is a thin, deterministic, semantic-free validator (python3 stdlib-only, repo-agnostic): it re-derives the claim-time candidate identity by **calling** `scripts/reception_identity.py` (one identity implementation, never a second) and validates a claim against current, producer-owned artifacts — the durable verification record (the #528 handle), the candidate-identity and findings anchors, the disposition ledger, and any deferral traces — printing exactly one verdict line, `completion-check: <token> — <detail>`.

- **Eight tokens, first failing class wins** (presence/parseability/binding evaluated before any value comparison): `pass` (exit 0) → `missing-evidence` → `stale-candidate` (the recorded candidate identity differs from claim-time content) → `verification-not-pass` → `skipped-checks-present` (a blocking-gate or unclassified skip, fail-closed; host-capability skips are surfaced in the detail but never flip the token) → `undischarged-findings` → `non-durable-deferral` → `unverifiable-trace`. Each non-pass exits 1; an internal failure exits 2 and prints no verdict line. Unknown never collapses onto `pass`.
- **What stays out (semantic exclusion).** The validator re-grades no severity, re-judges no fix, re-runs no test, and evaluates no finding content beyond the presence and binding of its disposition trace — it validates references (existence, parseability, identity-binding, set-emptiness, trace presence). The loop's own routing pre-scopes the effective fix set (findings marked `in_fix_set`), so the validator performs no severity comparison. A run.sh negative asserts the validator source spawns no subprocess whose head is anything other than the resolved `gh` (its only spawn, on the remote-trace arm); git is confined to the imported identity-derivation routine.
- **Two invocation contexts.** The `receiving-code-review` Verification Gate gains a fifth evidence item — run the bundled check via the portable anchor, quote its verdict line verbatim, and phrase the claim "complete" only on a quoted `pass` (a named non-pass token states what is missing; no verdict line at all is the `degraded: unvalidated (<reason>)` arm, never read as `pass`). The `review-and-fix` loop discharges the same item at **Loop Exit** over its run-scoped records, refreshing the verification record on an identity-keyed mismatch (the Step 3.5 inner-re-fix case) and carrying any non-pass token into its final verdict line (`<verdict> — completion evidence: <token>`); where an issue workpad exists a non-pass verdict is additionally recorded as a `## Devflow Reflection` bullet (the status-word vocabulary is untouched).
- **Deferral traces.** A deferral is durable when its trace resolves in one of the four channels: the loop-record manifest, an in-tree code-comment marker, or a remote PR-thread / follow-up-issue reference. A remote reference that 404s **in the validator's own repository** (read access established) is a provable absence → `non-durable-deferral`; a 404 **outside the credential's provable scope** is `unverifiable-trace` (GitHub serves 404 for unauthorized reads, so absence there is unknown). The remote arm reads `gh` via `DEVFLOW_GH` with no probe and degrades to `unverifiable-trace` when GitHub is unreachable — no network in the test suite (the suite's `gh` stubs drive every remote arm).
- **A third context: `implement` completion (issue #1087).** The implement engine's terminal `workpad.py --status Complete` write is gated on a canonical verification-flight record for the run's final in-env verification command, validated through this same module's `validate_implement_completion` importable entry point (reusing the closed eight-token vocabulary — no ninth token). Its policy is **stricter** than the loop/direct contexts: it requires the flight to be in the terminal `passed` **state** (not merely a `passed` result), a nonempty `suite_summary.command`, an integer-`0` `suite_summary.exit_status`, an **empty** `skipped_checks` list (no skip population at all — not even a surfaced host-capability skip), and a `candidate_identity` equal to the current tree. Defects map onto the shared tokens in the documented order (missing → stale → not-pass → skipped). Phase 4.3 produces the record for the final tree (via `reception_identity.py` + `verification-flight.py`) and records the validated key with `workpad.py --record-completion-evidence`, which writes one hidden `completion-verification:<flight-key>` marker on the existing keyed-checkpoint family; `workpad.py`'s `_terminal_complete_gate` re-validates it immediately before PATCH so post-Phase-3 edits are caught. The off-switch (`.verification_flight.enabled: false`) suppresses only flight *reuse* for an implement run — the record is still produced — and a standalone `workpad.py` copy lacking the validator sibling fails a Complete write closed with `missing-evidence`. The validator and its transitive import closure (`reception_identity.py`, `section_parse.py`) are byte-pinned in `scripts/devflow-cloud-writer-contract.json`.
- **The completion validator applies no schema/hermeticity gate (issue #2080).** It checks `state`/`result`/`suite_summary`/`skipped_checks`/`candidate_identity` regardless of which `schema_version` the record carries, so a `passed` non-hermetic flight — recordable but never reusable, per the single-flight section above — validates here exactly like a hermetic one and backs a `Complete` write; the non-hermetic marking only ever suppresses reuse, never completion-evidence validity.
- **The implement-completion member accepts a second evidence family: a CI-derived reading (issue #1611).** Alongside the verification-flight family above, `_terminal_complete_gate`'s completion-evidence member also accepts a CI-derived record recorded with `workpad.py --record-completion-evidence-ci <head-sha> <tier> <run-url> --completion-ci-check <name> <conclusion> …` (issue #1898 added the `<tier>` operand and the repeatable `--completion-ci-check` set), validated **offline** (no network, no `gh`) by this same module's `validate_implement_completion_ci` importable entry point — it passes only when the `tier` is `local` (a `cloud` tier is refused), the `head_sha` is 40 lowercase hex equal to `git rev-parse HEAD` over a clean tree, the recorded checks cover the required-check set declared by the `# prflow:required-check` markers in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, and every recorded `conclusion` is a success, mapping defects onto the same closed eight-token vocabulary (no ninth token). It rides the keyed-checkpoint family under a distinct `completion-ci:` key namespace, and exactly one completion-evidence marker is required across both families together. This exists so a local/interactive implement run following issue #1607's tier ladder — whose completion gate is a CI reading, not an in-env suite pass — can write `Complete`; which family a run records is a tier-scoped policy in `CLAUDE.md`, and a consumer repository's run never produces the CI marker. Canonical detail: [`docs/internal/implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md).
- **`_terminal_complete_gate` has a fourth member: the review-coverage gate (issue #1453).** After the completion-evidence and required-artifact members, and before the AC reconciliation, `workpad.py`'s `_review_coverage_verdict` requires the post-mutation `## Progress` section to carry exactly one resolvable **review-coverage record** — a four-axis payload (coverage, dispatch, roster, checklist) riding the existing keyed-checkpoint marker family under a `review-coverage:` key, stamped by Phase 3.3 with `--record-review-coverage` on every Phase 3 exit that can reach a Complete write. The axes, their closed vocabularies, the clean values and each axis's gap token are declared once in `workpad.py`'s `_REVIEW_COVERAGE_AXIS_SPECS` and validated at import, so no derived view or prose copy is the source; `unestablished` is a member of every axis and clean on none, which is what keeps an unresolved fact from being collapsed onto a pass. **The record is self-anchoring (issue #1510):** it carries an as-of anchor — the reviewed head SHA it was derived from (the loop-verdict/`git rev-parse HEAD` at Phase 3 exit, passed via `--record-review-coverage-head`; the literal `unestablished` when that head could not be derived) and the UTC write time (`_utc_now_compact()`) — as two optional trailing colon-joined fields appended to the `<coverage>:<dispatch>:<roster>:<checklist>` payload. `_parse_review_coverage_payload` accepts either the four legacy axis fields or the six anchored fields, and `_parse_review_coverage_anchor` reads the anchor (both absent-not-error for a pre-#1510 record, so those still parse and are never refused). A declared coverage gap's reflection wording is scoped to this run's own review pass at that anchor rather than claiming the PR's final review state, so a later standalone review that closes the gap no longer leaves the workpad record reading stale — the standalone `/prflow:review` path stays workpad-free by design. A record showing a gap completes only when each gap carries a `--review-coverage-disposition <gap> <cause-class> "<reason>"` row **over a record reading `dispatch=attempted`** — the honest-degradation channel of issue #1230, gated on the recorded dispatch fact rather than on the reason's wording, so a run that never dispatched the shadow cannot complete at all. **Issue #1984 closed two self-excuse holes:** a `dispatch=attempted` record whose `roster` is a measured value (`complete`/`short`) must be corroborated by `--record-roster-member` rows covering all four always-on reviewers with at least one `dispatched` (else `[review-coverage-dispatch-uncorroborated]`; the lost-write `roster=unestablished` shape is exempt), and the disposition now carries a **cause class** from a closed set with no elective member — `environment-denial` (a capability the runner did not expose; corroborated by a recorded `missing` roster row) or `dispatched-but-lost` (a dispatched reviewer whose result was lost) — riding the marker key as a third colon-joined segment (`review-coverage-disposition:<gap>:<cause-class>`); a cause outside that set, an uncorroborated `environment-denial`, or a legacy two-operand marker with no cause segment discharges nothing (`[review-coverage-cause-inadmissible]` / `[review-coverage-gap]`), so a budget belief or a "partial pass judged adequate" has no admissible disposition and stops at `Blocked`. The six refusals (`[review-coverage-unestablished]`, `[review-coverage-gap]`, `[review-coverage-dispatch-uncorroborated]`, `[review-coverage-cause-inadmissible]`, `[review-coverage-undispatched]`, `[review-coverage-boilerplate]`) are structural aborts before PATCH by a pure read that takes nothing from `args`; each accepted disposition also files a `dropped-failed` reflection, and Phase 4.3 mirrors the same verdict as a publish precondition ahead of `gh pr ready`. See [`docs/internal/implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md).
- **The attach-path residual is closed here.** `candidate_identity` is outside the flight key, so two declarations sharing a checkout fingerprint but declaring different content identities map to one handle. As the first consumer to read the handle's `candidate_identity` as authoritative, the check closes the PR #681 residual: on attach, a declared `candidate_identity` that differs from the handle's is no longer a reusable pass (`reuse_ready`/`candidate_identity_match` are False) — while `descriptor_digest`/`flight_key` stay byte-unchanged, so every stored handle stays valid.

The plugin tree (the validator + skill bodies + tests) ships via the `prflow_version` vendor fetch; the two workflow allowlist grants ship via `install.sh` file-copy — the two-halves upgrade coupling applies. A consumer that upgrades one half gets a silently-denied validator on cloud runs, which the no-output-is-degraded rule converts into a visible `degraded: unvalidated` outcome, never a silent pass.

### Runtime main-thread context (issue #1209)

Separate from the phase files' *static shipped size*, a long `/prflow:implement` run accumulates a large **runtime main-thread context** across its many turns — and, because each phase file is loaded one at a time at phase entry and mandatorily re-read on every re-entry and after every nested-skill return, the **re-read count** is the multiplier worth measuring, not the once-off byte size. The behavioral instrument `scripts/implement-context-eval.py` (maintainer-run over a transcript corpus; never on the skill's runtime path, so the instrument itself needs no tool grant — only its focused test is granted, as for #767) measures the peak main-thread context per run, the per-phase-file read counts, the main-thread tool calls bucketed by category, and the distribution of wall-clock gaps between consecutive main-thread tool calls — each per run and aggregated across the corpus. It is the implement-side counterpart of the create-issue instrument (§11); the findings, the two corrections, the tier-split non-goal, and any recorded snapshot live in [`docs/internal/implement-context.md`](implement-context.md), the single source of truth for this axis. It is not paraphrased here.

---

## 8. Deep dive: the review engine

`/prflow:review` and `/prflow:review-and-fix` share the **same engine**: Phases 0–4.3 are executed verbatim by both. `/prflow:review` reports a verdict; `/prflow:review-and-fix` wraps the engine in a fix loop and skips the GitHub-posting phase.

### The engine is a bundle, not one file (issue #529)

The engine used to be a single `skills/review/SKILL.md` that callers read end to end, whatever the run actually needed. It is now a **bundle**: a thin state-owning root at `skills/review/SKILL.md` plus per-phase reference files under `skills/review/phases/`. The root keeps only what the whole run shares — the live-progress-comment machinery, the per-subagent overrides block, the cross-phase invariants, the prompt-extension load, the phase routing table, and the fail-closed entry. **Each phase's authoritative procedure lives in its own reference**; the routing table's phase descriptions are orientation only, and the reference wins wherever the two could be read against each other. Some references are gated out of a given run by predicates the earlier phases establish — standalone-only mode, a config toggle, an adjudication that has something to adjudicate — so an ordinary run loads only the references it needs, which is what makes the default path cheaper than the old read-everything monolith. Keeping the root and the per-phase references lean stays an ongoing goal — less prompt prose loaded per pass is less budget spent — and *how lean* remains a matter of authoring discipline. A mechanically enforced ceiling now sits above that discipline, answering a different question: `lib/test/lint-reference-size.py` (issue #1595) fails the suite when a boundary-gated reference or a skill root exceeds 61,750 bytes and holds no live exemption, because above that size a single read returns the file's `start` marker and no `end` marker. For the *reference* half of that population the boundary gate recovers such a read by paging the file whole, so the ceiling forces a trim rather than a hard `truncated` failure; a skill root reached through the `Skill` tool arrives under no such gate, so the recovery does not cover it (the review engine root reached instead by a file read is gated on delivery completeness — see the entry split under *Root identity* below). The one file still over it — `skills/retrospective-weekly/SKILL.md` — is carried as an expiring exemption in `lib/test/reference-size-exemptions.json`; the review engine root sits under the ceiling and holds none. It is a reader-capability limit, not a budget: it says nothing about how long the prose ought to be.

**The entry gate (mandatory on every phase entry).** Before acting in a phase, the engine re-derives **root identity**, `Read`s that phase's reference, and clears the reference's **boundary contract** — in that order, never from an earlier read or a remembered summary. This applies to a `/prflow:review-and-fix` Step 2.6 **shadow** entry too: a shadow entry is a phase entry.

- **Root identity.** At engine entry the root resolves its skill directory and hashes itself and its references with `git hash-object`, authoring a bundle manifest — canonical root path, root hash, and each reference's path and hash — into the run-scoped scratch dir. **How the directory is resolved depends on entry.** Reached via the `Skill` tool (manual `/prflow:review`), the root resolves its own directory from the portable single-statement runner anchor (see §6). Reached by a caller that read the engine as a file (`/prflow:review-and-fix`'s fix loop and shadow pass, and the implement tier's degraded engine-read arm), the caller locates the engine directory by an ordered, repo-root-anchored candidate list — the repo-root `skills/review`, then `.prflow/vendor/prflow/skills/review`, then the superseded `.devflow/vendor/devflow/skills/review` — resolved once per engine entry, and the bundle binds its references and root-identity hashing to that **located** directory (never to the caller's own runner anchor, which has no `phases/`). The candidate list depends on neither the runner's file-search-tool semantics nor the process working directory, and when every candidate misses the run stops with a message naming `/prflow:init`. On that file-read path the located root is additionally gated on **delivery completeness** before it is executed.
