The canonical statement of that gate is `skills/review-and-fix/references/loop-control.md` Step 1; the restatement that follows here is non-authoritative and the canonical statement wins wherever the two could be read against each other. Selection turns on **path resolution**, not on whether content came back: a path the reader reports as non-existent is absent and simply advances the list (the ordinary consumer shape), while any other message that **affirms the path exists** — a permission denial the reader raised *on that path* among them — positively resolves it, making that the selected candidate whatever the read returned; a refusal returned before the path was ever consulted (a harness or sandbox policy denial of the read itself) affirms nothing about existence and is unclassifiable rather than a resolution. A reader message the caller cannot classify **as to whether the path resolved** is the third arm and not a selection: resolution is unestablished, the candidate is not selected, and the scan advances with that skipped candidate recorded — the fail-closed default belongs on delivery, not on a question whose answer at the earlier candidates is normally "absent" anyway, where it would stop a consumer run that has a perfectly good vendored engine one candidate later. Completeness is then established by a **positive end-of-file confirmation** read from the reader itself — after a read that appears whole, one further bounded read starting at the line immediately after the last one held (a later start would step over undelivered lines and confirm an end beyond a hole). End of file is established **only** by the reader reporting that the file is shorter than the position asked for, or returning literally nothing at all; that report is itself attributable to the position requested, and is the ordinary outcome on a whole body. Any content returned there — **a blank line included**, since that position exists — means the earlier read was truncated, so the caller appends it and re-confirms, each confirming read starting at the line immediately after the one before it and a finite file reporting its end, so the re-confirmation terminates. Reading a returned blank line as an end-of-file signal was itself a fail-open: roughly a third of the engine root's lines are blank, so a truncated body whose next undelivered line happened to be blank would have read as complete. Attribution is what makes the confirmation load-bearing: a reader that ignores or clamps the start position and re-returns the head satisfies "nothing further came back" while proving nothing about the end of the file, and neither does one answering a position later than asked. The engine root carries no boundary-marker pair, so an absent truncation notice is no evidence of a whole body — which is exactly the measured over-cap shape, and why the notice is not an operand. A selected candidate left unestablished — a reader or permission error, an oversize error returning no content, a gap in the page sequence, an empty or whitespace-only body, a confirming read refused or erroring otherwise than by that end-of-file report, one whose reply could not be attributed to the position requested, or one that did not start at the line immediately after the last one held, or a runner whose file-read tool takes no start position and so cannot issue the confirmation at all — stops at `engine-root: incomplete` naming that file, without executing it, without falling through to a later candidate (a different engine copy), and without repairing it. An intact engine on a non-paging reader therefore stops too: that reader's inability to page is the reason to name, since nothing else on this path separates it from a truncated body. The disposition is scoped by engine entry: on the fix loop's **Step 1** entry the loop halts before any mutation rather than looping back, so it never reaches Loop Exit and renders no verdict marker and no verdict headline — that non-convergence is not eligible for the implement tier's severity-aware soft-proceed (the engine never ran, so its "every residual gradeable as non-Critical" test would pass vacuously on an unreviewed PR) and reaches the workpad only through the caller's own blocked path, while on the **Step 2.6 shadow** entry the same stop is a structural shadow failure taking that pass's outcome 3, which records `coverage: "not_verified"` and does not block. This covers the file-read path only; a root reached through the `Skill` tool is loaded by the runner under no such gate. Re-deriving means re-reading the manifest and re-hashing the root and the reference about to be read against that same located directory. A hash that cannot be derived, a manifest that is absent or unparseable, a root that has moved, or a hash that no longer matches each stops the phase under its own named label. Every path resolves relative to the **located root**, never to the working directory — which finds nothing under a vendored install and would strand the engine.
- **Boundary contract.** Each reference carries a `prflow:review-ref` start marker as its literal first line, and the matching end marker as its literal last line. Each marker names the reference's own phase id and bundle-relative path, and the match is decided against that marker-carried path — the path the run resolved and read the file from is not compared — so a mis-routed read (a marker naming a different phase or file) matches nothing and fails closed. After the `Read`, the engine quotes the body's actual first and last lines and decides the outcome from those quoted lines rather than from an impression that the markers look right. A refused read, an empty body, absent markers, a half-present pair, duplicates, a reversed pair, and a pair that is present but not first-and-last each carry a distinct stop label, tested in a fixed order so the first to fire is the attributed shape.

On any identity or boundary stop the engine reports the label with the phase id and reference path and does **not** act on the body, improvise the phase from its orientation text, or repair the file. This is the point of the contract: a body can read as complete and correct and still fail these checks, and that case *is* the reason they exist — a defective boundary or identity means what the engine holds is not the bundle it was built against, so the body's plausibility is worth nothing.

**The bundle's failure mode when the root itself never loads (measured 2026-08-06 → 2026-08-09).** Every protection above begins at the root, so a root the `Skill` tool fails to load protects nothing: the agent holds no routing table and no reason to open a phase file, improvises from its surrounding prompt, and posts a merge-gating verdict having executed none of the engine — exiting `success`. A render-time `` !`cmd` `` placeholder in `skills/review/SKILL.md` did exactly that for ≈67 hours; 72 of 72 in-window `Skill{prflow:review}` loads returned `is_error=true` with no body. The closed incident record — population, blast radius, the discriminating field a detector must key on, and what the measurement does *not* establish — is [`docs/internal/review-skill-load-outage-2026-08.md`](review-skill-load-outage-2026-08.md). The surviving observability residual is issue #1446.

**The adjacent failure mode — a root that loads *partially* — was probed and none was found (2026-08-11).** Abort and truncation are different failures of the same load, and only the first is measured above: a root delivered with its tail missing would leave the agent holding a plausible-looking body that silently lacks its later rules, and no `SKILL.md` root carries the boundary markers that catch this on a `phases/*.md` `Read`. A control-based probe on the local tier found every measurable body delivered whole, up to 83,427 file bytes — a floor on any ceiling, not a ceiling, and testimony rather than a measurement. The dated record, its limits, and what it means for the proposed size guard in issue #1595 are [`docs/internal/skill-body-load-delivery.md`](skill-body-load-delivery.md). Note that the `Read` tool's observed 25,000-token per-read cap governs the `phases/*.md` references and **not** the Skill-tool-reached root; the two loaders are separate, and neither figure is evidence about the other.

Because the bundle is the engine, the **reviewed surface is every source in it, not just the root**: the command-head and command-shape scanners take the whole bundle in one call, and the suite asserts engine-content pins against the concatenated bundle, so a sentence may move between references without breaking its pin.

> **The thesis (good for a technical-deck slide):** don't trust a single review pass. Build an *independent, evidence-based* checklist of every claim the diff makes, verify each claim against the actual source, run a panel of specialized reviewers, and use cross-reviewer corroboration to *calibrate confidence* in each finding, a single-source finding is flagged for extra human scrutiny, never silently dropped.

### Phase 0: Setup & diff classification
Caches the diff to a run-scoped path. **Phase 0.5** classifies the diff with five flags that decide the engine profile:
- `small_diff` (<100 changed lines AND ≤3 files)
- `config_only` (all changed files are config/docs extensions)
- `has_new_types` (added code defines classes/interfaces/types/enums/structs/traits)
- `engine_self_modifying` (a three-arm engine-surface path set: PRFlow's own source `skills/**`/`agents/**`/`lib/**` — repository-relative to this repository — **plus**, at any depth and matching on a consumer too, a prompt extension under the `.prflow/`/`.devflow/` state directory whose filename ends in `.md`, **or** a file whose basename is `CLAUDE.md`). A prompt-extension diff and a `CLAUDE.md` diff therefore reach the full checklist rather than the lean config-only path; how a prompt-extension diff is classified is stated authoritatively in `skills/review/phases/phase-0-setup.md` §0.5.
- `detect_all_audit` (the diff adds/changes a "detect-all" scanner/audit/coverage-invariant — code that *enumerates a population* AND *asserts a completeness property* over it)

**Phase 0.4 — issue discovery and acceptance-criteria resolution (runs before 0.5).** Two independent resolutions happen here: *which* issue this PR is about, and *which* set of acceptance criteria the engine judges against.

*Issue-number precedence.* A caller-supplied `--issue N` — the flag both `/prflow:review` and `/prflow:review-and-fix` parse out of `$ARGUMENTS` and bind as `$ISSUE_OVERRIDE` — **suppresses** both derivations: it is not run alongside them and never compared against them. With no override, the engine falls back to the PR body's `Resolves`/`Fixes`/`Closes` reference, then to the `issue-{N}` branch-name pattern `/prflow:implement` sets. (The cloud comment tier is unaffected: `scripts/resolve-command-trigger.sh` synthesizes a two-token command, so a `--issue` typed into a trigger comment is discarded before the skill runs and that path keeps the derivations.)

*Criteria precedence.* The engine does **not** read acceptance criteria off the GitHub issue body. `/prflow:implement`'s authoritative set lives in the **workpad comment** — Phase 2.2.5 narrows it, 2.2.6 rewrites its text, 3.4 retags it — so a review judging the issue body would be judging the PR against a specification the implementing run had already, legitimately, moved. Phase 0.4 therefore calls `scripts/workpad.py acs-resolve`, which does the whole resolution deterministically in one process: it fetches the issue body itself, resolves **both** surfaces (never a short-circuit chain, because the non-selected set is the divergence comparand), parses the workpad section once and renders that one item list twice — unfiltered as the comparand, post-merge-filtered as the reviewer-facing value — runs a **PR-identity guard** confirming the workpad section belongs to *this* PR rather than to a sibling PR's run on the same issue, and selects the value. The precedence is: the workpad set when it is confirmed as this PR's, otherwise the issue body. The selected criteria are handed to later phases with their tick column **neutralized** — a tick is Phase 3.4's assertion *by the author of the code under review* that a criterion is met, and the merge-gating judge must not be handed a specification pre-annotated by the party it is judging.

*The source token.* Every resolution names its own source, and `acs-resolve` exits 0 on every resolvable state — an absent or unreadable workpad is a *routed outcome carrying a token*, not a run-ending error. The token set (`workpad`, `issue-body`, `workpad-unmirrored`, `workpad-read-failed`, `pr-identity-mismatch`, `resolver-unavailable`, `none`) is defined authoritatively in `skills/review/phases/phase-0-setup.md` §0.4, and Phase 4's `## Issue Compliance` section renders **each token in its own distinct wording** (`skills/review/phases/phase-4-verdict.md`) — collapsing any two destroys the signal. The distinctions that carry the most weight: `resolver-unavailable` means the helper never ran, so *neither* surface was examined and nothing is known about whether criteria exist — an unestablished measurement that is never collapsed onto the real value `none`; `workpad-unmirrored` means a PRFlow run's mirroring silently failed, the opposite claim from a PR that simply has no workpad; and `workpad-read-failed` is a transport failure that must never present as a normal issue-body resolution. The engine also reports **normalized** divergence between the two surfaces — post-merge tag stripped, tick state ignored, whitespace collapsed — so a criterion the issue body carries and the workpad dropped with no recorded scope decision surfaces as a finding, while decisions the run did record read as audited narrowing.

*Criteria are never truncated.* Phase 0.4's 200-line cap on the issue body bounds the narrative `issue_context` **only**. It never bounds the criteria, which `acs-resolve` locates structurally and carries in full however far into the body their section begins.

A `small_diff AND config_only` change skips the checklist phases (intentional). An `engine_self_modifying` change forces the **full** checklist — it is a checklist-only override and forces no Phase 3 agent on; the Phase 3 roster is decided by Phase 3.1's applicability gates on every profile. `detect_all_audit` is additive (it never suppresses the other flags' profile): it forces a **Phase 3.1.5 completeness-critic pass** that independently re-enumerates the audit's target population by a signal *other than the audit's own pattern* and records any uncovered member as a finding — the engine's guard against a vacuous or self-certified "detect-all" audit.

*The `checklist_skipped` field.* The outcome the flags above drive is recorded in the review-coverage record's `checklist_skipped` field, whose three legal values are `"intentional"` (the profile row authorized the skip on a `small_diff AND config_only` diff), `"failure"` (checklist generation failed), and `null` (the checklist ran). The coverage-record `checklist`-axis spellings `skipped-intentional` / `skipped-failure` and the field values `"intentional"` / `"failure"` are the same axis in two spellings. Because a clean `skipped-intentional` claim asserts an entitlement to that profile row, `scripts/workpad.py` recomputes the diff (changed lines, file count, extensions, and — only in this repository — the engine-source path set) from git before accepting it: a resolved recomputation that disproves the row refuses the write with the failed condition named, an unresolvable one records the axis `unestablished`, and an override channel downgrades the claim to bare `skipped` (non-clean, disposition-required) — issue #1509.

**Phase 0.6 — deterministic stale counted-prose lint (runs immediately after 0.5).** A deterministic pre-pass, `scripts/stale-prose-lint.py` (python3-stdlib), that flags **diff-added prose whose counted claims a later commit outgrows or falsifies** — the authoring-speed front line in front of the LLM self-contradicting-diff carve-out. It reads the unified diff the engine already cached in 0.2 on stdin and resolves each claim's referent against the current head via an explicit `--rev HEAD` (never `git merge-base`, so it is shallow-clone safe), emitting TSV rows (`verdict<TAB>rule<TAB>file<TAB>line<TAB>detail`) and exiting `0` (no STALE), `1` (at least one STALE), or `2` (internal error). Four rule classes, defined authoritatively in the helper's own header (the engine does **not** paraphrase them): **R1** a range header its block outgrew ("Cases A–B"), **R2** a legend sum contradicting an `Expected total = N`, **R3/R3b** an exact `count-locked` header (exact/two-item counts), and **R4** a deny-absolute about a shell operator token the same file also asserts permitted. Each `STALE` row enters **Phase 4.2 verdict computation** as an engine finding at the config-gated severity, carrying its TSV row verbatim as evidence — no new verdict rule and no new accounting rule. `UNRESOLVABLE` rows are informational and **never gate**; `VERIFIED` rows need no action. The lint is **move-aware** (issue #629): a diff-added prose line that is a byte-identical relocation — an extraction refactor moving prose it did not author — is never graded `STALE` but is *demoted* to an `UNRESOLVABLE` row naming the relocation, under multiplicity and referent rules the helper's header defines authoritatively (again, not paraphrased here) along with the design record bounding the exemption. The lint also honors an **illustrative-example opt-out** (issue #635): a prose/comment line carrying the plain, language-agnostic marker `stale-prose-lint: example` is skipped for **all** rule recognition (R1–R4 and both non-gating recognition tiers) — so a design-record example, a fixture-comment idiom, or engine docs *describing* a claim shape are no longer graded as a real claim of that shape — and instead of falling silent the helper emits one non-gating `UNRESOLVABLE` "EX" audit row, exactly as with the move-aware demotion; the marker match, its trailing negative-lookahead (so `examples`/`example-driven` do not match), and the disclosed non-goals are defined authoritatively in the helper's own module-header design record. Because the engine is shared, standalone `/prflow:review`, `/prflow:review-and-fix`, and the shadow pass all inherit the phase identically. A second **recognition-only** tier (issue #818) sits alongside the `count-locked` one and the `EX` audit row: it recognizes a **coverage universal** — a *coverage-scope* token from a closed set (deliberately wider than the universal quantifiers: `only`/`complete`/`entire`/`whole` are scope claims and are recognized too) adjacent to a coverage-referent noun from a closed set ("every call site", "all four arms") — and emits a non-gating `CU` row, plus an `RT` row for the declared `stale-prose-lint: rule-text` opt-out, whose visibility property mirrors `EX`'s; both closed sets are specified authoritatively in the helper's header, not here. Like the `count-locked` tier it resolves no referent, never emits `STALE`, and leaves the four gating classes' own verdicts untouched — the one control-flow change is that R4's short-circuit now fires only when R4 actually emitted a row, so a deny-absolute carrying no backticked operator token no longer silently swallows either non-gating tier (it can add only non-gating rows, never change the exit code). The two non-gating tiers overlap by design and neither terminates the other, so a line matching both emits both rows. Phase 0.6 emits these rows like any other, but does not **act** on them — their consumer is the implement engine's Phase 2 §2.3.4b sweep, which runs the helper in its `--worktree` post-image mode — the on-disk file rather than `git show <rev>:<path>` — so an **uncommitted** tree, including a brand-new `.changeset/*.md`, resolves to its post-change content and the claim is grounded before commit rather than at review time. Detection ships in the engine; the pin-or-don't-write policy for a `count-locked` row lives in this repo's `.prflow/prompt-extensions/review-and-fix.md`.

*Cross-run adjudication carry-forward (issue #466).* A STALE row is re-derived from scratch every run, so a false positive the lint keeps raising (a `count-locked` claim that is actually accurate against HEAD — the lint miscounted) would re-gate on every run with no channel to make a prior triage stick. The engine carries a false-positive adjudication forward across runs. **Producer (Phase 4.1.7, PR mode):** when a run's Phase 4 triage resolves a STALE row's claim against HEAD and finds it accurate, it renders the finding Informational with the **concrete referent evidence** (the true count and what the lint miscounted) and stamps one hidden payload line — `<!-- prflow:lint-fp-adjudicated <base64 of the row's TSV> -->` — inside a sentinel-delimited adjudications section of the run's `prflow:review-progress` comment (written only by the Phase 4 finalize write). base64 keeps a `--` in the detail excerpt from terminating the HTML comment; a row with no recorded referent evidence is not stamped, and **an `R4` (modality-conflict) row is never stamped** — see the rule-scope carve-out below. **Consumer (Phase 0.6, PR mode):** before STALE rows are finalized as findings, the deterministic, network-free helper `scripts/match-lint-adjudications.py` (the sibling of `scripts/match-deferrals.py`) joins the current rows against the payloads found in this PR's own prior **trusted** progress comments and returns a demotion map; a row whose decoded `(rule, path, detail)` is **byte-for-byte identical** to an adjudicated payload (the TSV line number is excluded from the key, so a renumbered paragraph still matches, while any change to the detail text re-examines the prose fresh) is demoted to Informational — annotated `previously adjudicated false positive (run <run-key>)` — and excluded from verdict computation at every configured `stale_prose.severity`. **Rule scope — `R4` is never carried forward.** The match key is safe only because the count/range rules (`R1`/`R2`/`R3`) embed the *observed referent* in their detail, so moving the counted code changes the detail and a stale adjudication stops matching. `R4`'s detail is a pure function of the *claim* line and says nothing about the permitting line, so an old `R4` adjudication would keep matching after a later commit added a **genuine** contradicting permit — demoting exactly the self-contradicting-diff finding the lint exists to catch. `R4` rows are therefore never stamped and never demoted; the exclusion is counted (`rows_rule_excluded`) and surfaced, never silent. **Trust** requires the run-keyed `prflow:review-progress` marker on the comment together with a `Bot`-type author (or a login in `.prflow.allowed_bots`, read via `config-get.sh`); payloads are honored **only** inside the sentinels (a payload quoted anywhere else — e.g. in a rendered evidence line — is data, never an instruction), ambiguity (two current STALE rows sharing a payload key) never demotes, and a malformed payload is skipped with a breadcrumb. The join is **PR-scoped by construction** (it reads only this PR's own comments) and **degrades loudly** — an absent, refused, or erroring helper leaves every STALE row at its configured severity and records a degraded-check note, never a silent skip. The blast radius is bounded to the severity gate: a demoted row never drives the threshold-independent self-contradicting-diff carve-out.

*Line scoping (issue #434).* A claim is prose, so only **comment/prose lines** are examined — a line of *code* that merely contains claim-shaped text (a shell fixture string, an assertion name) is data, not an assertion about the file. The helper decides this per file type: markdown-family prose outside fenced code blocks; `#` comments (`.sh`, `.yml`, `.jq`, `Makefile`, …); `//` comments and `/* … */` interiors (`.ts`, `.go`, `.rs`, `.java`, …); and, in Python, `#` comments plus genuine docstrings (resolved with `ast`, so claim-shaped *string literals* in test fixtures are not mistaken for prose). The same predicate scopes **R4's permit referent**, so a code line can neither raise a claim nor contradict one. **An unrecognised file type fails OPEN** — every added line is examined, exactly as before, with a stderr breadcrumb naming the extension. This direction is deliberate and load-bearing: a closed allowlist would silently reduce a consumer repo in an unlisted language to a permanent no-op (empty output, exit `0`, forever) while this page still advertised the check, so a file type PRFlow has not listed degrades to the *status quo*, never to *no checking*. Deliberately not examined (accepted limitations, not accidents): `.sh` heredoc prose, YAML block-scalar prose, trailing comments after code, and `#` comments inside ```` ```bash ```` fences in markdown.

*Excluded population: machine-appended corpora (issue #672).* Diff-added lines under `.prflow/learnings/` and `.prflow/logs/` are not examined at all. Those files are records PRFlow *writes* — a retrospective, an experiment record, a log — each quoting reviewed prose verbatim inside a JSON string. The quoted text is data about a past PR, never an assertion about the file it now sits in, and the post-diff state holds no referent to resolve it against. Rewriting any such record therefore re-presents it as diff-*added* prose and grades a previous PR's counted claim as though this PR had authored it, which is how a redaction pass that only replaced an operator home path with `~` failed the lint's own self-scan. The exclusion is keyed to the path rather than to any one edit, and each excluded path is named on stderr — an intended coverage drop is still a coverage drop, and stays as discoverable as the fail-open arm above. `CHANGELOG.md` and `.changeset/` are deliberately **in** scope: both are human-authored prose about the current change, exactly the surface the lint exists to grade.

*Config gate.* `prflow_review.stale_prose.enabled` defaults `true` (only an explicit `false` disables the phase — the fail-safe, feature-on direction) and `prflow_review.stale_prose.severity` defaults `important` (enum-validated inline to `critical|important|suggestion`, falling back to `important` on a bad/absent value with a breadcrumb), both read through `config-get.sh`.

*Degradation arms (fail-safe, never fail-silent — the run proceeds in every arm and the note is visible in the review report):* **(a) harness-refused invocation** — the command never executes (the consumer-skew state: a consumer's installed workflow `TOOLS` grants lag the vendored plugin) → record a note **naming the missing grant and the tier-appropriate remedy** (for the cloud review runner: re-sync the installed workflow's `TOOLS` line — `prflow_runner.allowed_tools` is appended post-floor only inside `devflow-runner.yml`'s `provision_env` gate, which defaults off, so it bridges the grant only on a reviewer already running `provision_env: true`; `prflow_implement.allowed_tools` / `prflow.allowed_tools` for the implement / command tiers); **(b) helper absent** (`No such file`) → note that the vendored helper was not found; **(c) helper internal error** (exit 2, no verdict rows on stdout) → note the internal error and carry its stderr (the empty stdout is **not** read as a clean pass); **(d) config-disabled** → the recorded config-disabled note. No arm stalls the run and no arm silently skips the phase.

**Phase 0.3.6 — blocker-recheck fast path (standalone `/prflow:review` only).** Evaluated before issue discovery (0.4) and diff classification (0.5). When a PR's most recent recorded verdict is a REJECT driven **solely** by enumerated self-contradicting-diff carve-out blockers (zero checklist FAIL/INCONCLUSIVE, and every REJECT-driving finding — at any severity — carries the producer-emitted ` [self-contradicting-diff carve-out: {file}]` marker), **and** every commit since the rejected head touches only the enumerated blocker sites, the engine re-verifies each named blocker at the PR's pushed head (`$PR_HEAD_SHA`) with a single blinded `general-purpose` verifier, then finalizes its own progress comment per the Phase 4 update protocol (flip Status, write the report, stamp `Reviewed HEAD = $PR_HEAD_SHA`, and record the `checklist not run (blocker-recheck fast path)` sentinel tally) **before** posting a refreshed verdict through the existing Phase 4.4 machinery — an APPROVE reuses `dismiss-stale-rejections.sh` unchanged — instead of a full four-phase re-review. This makes clearing a carve-out REJECT (a stale doc/release-note line, comment, or test the diff itself introduced) proportionate to the one-line fix it demands, rather than mortality-prone (a full re-run that dies mid-flight leaves the REJECT outstanding at merge). Two coupled producer keys in the Review bundle feed it: a machine-detectable `Reviewed HEAD:` line stamped on the finalized review-progress comment at Phase 4 (the deterministic join between the REJECT's reviewed head — `$REJECTED_HEAD`, read from the verdict marker's `head=` for a shape-(a) review, its reviews-API `commit_id` for a markerless shape-(b)/(c) review (issue #1247) — and its report-carrying comment — the root owns that comment's shape, so its producer rule sits with the rest of the live-comment machinery in `skills/review/SKILL.md`), and the unconditional ` [self-contradicting-diff carve-out: {file}]` marker Phase 4.1 stamps on every carve-out finding (a Phase 4 procedure, so it lives in the Phase 4 reference under `skills/review/phases/`) (its `{file}` field is the only place a blocker's path survives into the rendered report, so it is what precondition 4 enumerates blockers from). Every unmet, unparseable, or errored precondition falls **closed** to the full pipeline; any still-unfixed blocker re-REJECTs; and the gate is **off** for `/prflow:review-and-fix` (which passes `head_override = local`), so the fix loop never takes it mid-loop and the shared engine stays single-sourced. It is a review with a formal verdict, **not** a bypass — it narrows, never closes: the scoped verification confirms each blocker's claim is now true but does not re-review the blocker file for new problems (precondition-5 bounds the blast radius to the enumerated sites).

### Engine ground truth: the injected block (cloud runs only)

A cloud review used to have no ground truth about which commands it may run, nor about the CI that already ran on the commit under review — it discovered them by trial and denial, and a run could burn its whole budget probing the boundary and terminate with **no verdict**, leaving a red required `Devflow Review` check with no findings and no stated cause. Cloud runs now **prepend a `> [!IMPORTANT]` engine-ground-truth block** to the prompt, on **every** cloud path: the automated `devflow-review.yml` path (through `devflow-runner.yml`'s `Compose review prompt` step) and every command the light listener dispatches (through `devflow.yml`'s `Compose engine grounding block` step, which is unconditional). On that tier only `/prflow:review` renders in the default `review` mode; `/prflow:review-and-fix` and `/prflow:pr-description` render in `MODE=generic`, which omits the three sections gated on a reviewed commit and adds none of the implement tier's Phase 3 scope clause — the fix loop because its own prompt extension makes the in-environment whole-suite pass its verification channel and forbids it to cite CI for its own progress, and the PR-description command because it reviews no commit at all. The block is rendered by `scripts/render-grounding-block.sh` — the single deterministic renderer of a load-bearing prompt surface, a convention `scripts/render-audit-prompt.py` (the create-issue Step 3.6 audit prompt, issue #600) is the **second** application of, with one disclosed transport divergence: the grounding block is *workflow-injected* into the review prompt, whereas the audit prompt is *fetched by the auditor* running the renderer in its own context (the transport that removes the orchestrator's per-dispatch emission). The grounding block states these ground-truth things, listed in the order the renderer numbers them in `review` mode:

1. **The CI conclusions already observed for the reviewed HEAD**, produced by `scripts/summarize-ci-checks.sh`: one line per signal, drawn from the paginated `actions/runs?head_sha=` runs and their per-run `/jobs`, plus `commits/{sha}/check-runs`. It excludes PRFlow's own workflow, the duplicate `github-actions` app check-runs (already counted once via the jobs API), and the reviewer's own `Devflow Review` check. It **always exits 0**; on any query failure it prints the literal `CI status unavailable` with a query-specific stderr breadcrumb, and a head with genuinely zero signals prints `No CI signals reported for this commit` — neither ever renders as passing, so an unknown CI state is never laundered into a green one. Check **names** are attacker-controlled free text (anyone opening a PR can name a job), so they are sanitized to printable ASCII, stripped of fence/markup characters, truncated, capped at 50 lines, and rendered inside a plain ` ```text ` fence beneath prose declaring the names untrusted data; the **conclusions** beside them (`success`, `failure`, `in_progress`) are API facts, not attacker text.
2. **The exact `--allowed-tools` string the run resolved**, so the engine plans only commands the harness actually grants rather than rediscovering the list by probing.
3. **The command shapes this run's harness accepts (section 3)** — a granted command *head* is not sufficient, because the harness also denies whole command *shapes* silently, so the block lists the permitted shapes and the denied ones rather than leaving the engine to discover them by refusal.
4. **The headless-run discipline (section 4)** — that ending the turn ends the process, so every dispatched subagent's completed result is in hand before the run proceeds past its dispatch point, and a launch acknowledgment is never treated as the return. `implement` mode appends the one implement-only clause naming Phase 3's inline `review-and-fix` dispatches as its own.
5. **That mutually independent tool calls are issued in one message (section 5, issue #1723)** — tier-agnostic in the renderer's sense, meaning all three *modes* carry it (`review`, `implement`, `generic`), which is **mode** reach and not tier reach: the whole block is cloud-only, so a local/interactive run never receives this section and the site-local mandates below remain its only statement of the rule there. A request's billed cost tracks the context it re-sends rather than the work it asks for, so calls made one per message pay a full request each for work that could have shared one. The section states independence as the whole test and fails closed on it: calls whose independence cannot be established are treated as dependent and stay in separate messages. It also makes writing the same target a dependency in its own right — two edits to one file, and two dispatches that can both write one checkout — so the result-independence test cannot license a concurrent write race. That write-target dependency turns on where the writes actually land rather than on the capability to write: dispatches whose writes provably cannot reach the same path (each writing only where its own identity determines, as a batch of verifiers writing per-item result files does), or which the runner gives separate working copies, are not dependent on each other's account; establishing neither leaves them dependent, and batching them anyway is the race. It licenses no merge of dependent calls, is explicitly not a rule about writing fewer and larger edit hunks, adds no permission, and leaves the commit-before-dispatch obligation for write-capable dispatches exactly where that dispatch defines it. The pre-existing site-local batching mandates in the review and implement phase files, in `skills/retrospective-weekly/SKILL.md`, and in `skills/implement/references/deferred-ac-followups.md` are left in place as the site-specific applications of it.
6. **That a verdict reaches the pull request only through Phase 4.4's emitter (section 6, issue #1629)** — a review-only section telling every standalone review run that a verdict comment it composes itself is *not* a verdict. It states that constraint independent of whether any phase reference is read, so a run that skips `phases/phase-4-4-github-post.md` and improvises a `gh pr comment` still carries the rule; it names the emitter but **not** its argument shape or outcome vocabulary, leaving `phases/phase-4-4-github-post.md` the sole owner of the procedure. It is gated on the same derived `REVIEWED_COMMIT=yes` selector as the CI and displacement sections, so `MODE=generic` (`/prflow:review-and-fix`, `/prflow:pr-description`) and `MODE=implement` never receive it.
7. **The trusted-source-displaced paths this run (section 7, issues #504 + #874)** — a displaced-paths section stating the listed working-tree files do not carry the reviewed head's bytes, so a HEAD claim about a listed path routes through `git show <head>:<path>` and the cached diff, never a working-tree read (fetch-free `git cat-file -e` → INCONCLUSIVE fail direction); listed paths stay fully in review scope (the displacement changes the read channel, never the depth). Independently of this block, in standalone PR-number mode the same seven carriers route a claim about any diff-touched path through `git show $PR_HEAD_SHA:<path>` (base-state `$PR_BASE_SHA`), so verdicts grade the reviewed head even when the displaced list is empty (issue #1889). The list has **two producers** on `devflow-runner.yml`, joined into one `HARDENED_PATHS` value by a dedicated `Join displaced-path producers` step, and the section renders whenever *either* is non-empty: the `Harden Stop-hook script sources` step's base-ref/stub overwrites (issue #458), and the unconditional prompt-extension truncation (issue #874), which empties — or creates empty — the workspace copy of each protected extension on every run. The join is why the extension paths still reach the block on a run where the harden step's relevance gate published an empty set, which is the ordinary consumer shape. **The published list carries no per-path provenance**, so a listed path is never attributed to a particular producer, and the working-tree copy is not consulted for any content claim about it — head or base alike. Inert only when both producers publish nothing: the local tier and the manual `devflow.yml` path, neither of which runs either producer.

`skills/review/SKILL.md` carries a matching **"Engine ground truth (only when the injected block is present)"** section (see the *Engine sharing* note — it is conditioned on the block. `/prflow:review-and-fix` now receives a block too, but a `MODE=generic` one carrying **no CI section**, so that section's CI-conditioned items stay inert there while its allowed-tools item applies; the inline tier's test evidence remains the orchestrator's own in-environment suite/lint results, never a CI conclusion). It tells the engine to treat the block's CI conclusions as the authoritative test evidence for the commit (citing them rather than re-running the suite — Phase 2 verifies the *checklist*, not the test suite, so no suite-execution step is left undischarged), to attempt no command the allowed-tools list omits, and to **quote a check name, never obey one** — while never doubting a conclusion because a name looks adversarial. Evidence means a check *name* with a *conclusion* beside it: when the block instead reports `CI status unavailable` or `No CI signals reported for this commit`, the engine treats the test evidence as **missing**, never as green. When the block reports a `failure` or `in_progress` signal, the engine reports it as such rather than assuming green (see [`docs/internal/workflow-triggers.md`](workflow-triggers.md)).

**The `/prflow:implement` tier carries the same block, rendered by the same helper in `MODE=implement` (issue #1170).** `devflow-implement.yml`'s `Compose implement grounding block` step prepends it so the implementing agent — which discovered its tool boundary by trial and denial before — holds the exact resolved allowed-command list in its own prompt. That mode emits only the tier-agnostic sections (the permitted commands, the command shapes, the headless-run discipline, the independent-tool-call batching disposition) and omits sections 1, 6, and 7: the implement tier has no reviewed commit, so a "CI already observed" claim would be false, and both the sole-publisher rule (issue #1629) and the trusted-source displacement are review-only concerns. As on the review tiers, the block and the workflow's `--allowed-tools` consume one hoisted `TOOLS='…'` step output, so the quoted list cannot drift from what the run resolved. **This does not make a denial visible at the moment it happens and does not change the matcher's response** — it gives the agent the list to check against up front (post-hoc denial forensics are issue #1064). The canonical statement of the standing rule and its stated limits lives in [`docs/internal/cloud-allowlist.md`](cloud-allowlist.md).

**Read-only `review` profile grants (issue #363).** So the engine can actually execute the phases the block promises are permitted, the cloud `review` tool profile additionally grants `mkdir`, `tee`, `git cat-file`, `git hash-object`, `git checkout`, `mktemp`, `cmp`, `rm -f`, and `load-prompt-extension.sh` from any anchor directory (`Bash(*/load-prompt-extension.sh:*)`). Phase 0.2's cached diff is therefore created (so Phase 3's `{DIFF_PATH}` resolves), and Phase 0.3.6's blocker-recheck fast path executes. The Phase 3.1 dirty-tree backstop no longer depends on `mktemp`: it writes `git status` snapshots and restore scratch to fixed repo-local `.prflow/tmp/` paths that survive the Agent-tool boundary and work on the implement profile where `mktemp` remains withheld. Before each snapshot write the engine removes the prior path object and validates a regular non-symlink result; restore scratch is likewise removed before reuse. It rejects truncated NUL records and failed path writes, while an object ID held only in orchestrator state detects replacement of the regular baseline during dispatch. `lib/test/extract-command-heads.py` + `lib/test/run.sh` pin every Bash command head in the **whole Review bundle** — the root `skills/review/SKILL.md` *and* every `skills/review/phases/*.md` reference (issue #529 split the engine, so the reviewed surface is every source, not just the root; both extractors take all of them in one call) — against **both** allowlists the skill runs under, so a command the skill invokes but a profile omits turns the suite RED instead of being silently denied at runtime. The extractor is quote/comment/heredoc-aware, `$(…)`-descending, wrapper-stripping, and **arm-position-aware** (issue #392: `case` arm patterns are stripped only where an arm may legally begin — after `case … in` and after each `;;` — so a command in a case *body* keeps its head; nested `case` blocks are an accepted flag-not-depth-counter limitation).

**Granted heads are not enough — the matcher also denies composite SHAPES (issues #401, #455).** A statement every one of whose heads is granted is still refused when its *shape* is one the matcher rejects, and a refused command fails **silently**: it produces no output at all. `lib/test/extract-command-shapes.py` (driven by `lib/test/run.sh`) is the desk-time pin for that class, and it carries **one rule set per tool profile**, because the read-only `review` allowlist and the read-write `devflow-implement` allowlist are separate profiles with separately-probed behavior — a shape proven on one tier is *unproven* on the other:
- **`--profile review` (default; rules R1–R4)** covers the whole Review bundle (`skills/review/SKILL.md` + `skills/review/phases/*.md`): a leading `VAR=value` assignment, a leading `cd`, a redirect (stdout or stderr) targeting `/tmp`, and an interpreter head (`python3`) are all denied in the review runner (evidence: run 29105381021 — 22 denials, no verdict). The **leading-`cd`** row of that evidence is **confounded by an independently-denied redirect (stdout or stderr) and is therefore unproven** — it is kept for a redirect-free re-probe and pinned absent in `run.sh` (see [`docs/internal/cloud-allowlist.md`](cloud-allowlist.md)); the rule enforces the leading-`cd` **authoring** rule rather than a measured refusal. (A fifth **discipline-only** rule, **R5** — a command-substitution assignment in `if`/`elif` **condition** position, issue #857 — was **retired in issue #869** once `matcher-probe.yml`'s review Shape 18 recorded that shape PERMITTED, run 30310938175.) Permitted alternatives, each with its own evidence status (issue #871): the Write tool into `.prflow/tmp/**` (probe-proven, run 29111394360), `… | tee` and `tee <<'EOF'` (probe rows 10 and 6, no per-row verdict transcribed), and repo-relative vendored-literal helper paths (the **leading-token** form is **probe-proven at the *implement* tier only, row I2, and unrecorded at the review tier**; review shape 18 measured the same path in command-substitution-condition position, which is confounding evidence, not a leading-token measurement). See [`docs/internal/cloud-allowlist.md`](cloud-allowlist.md) for the per-entry record.
- **`--profile implement` (rules IR1/IR2/IR3/IR4/IR5/IR6)** covers `skills/implement/SKILL.md` + `skills/implement/phases/*.md` + `skills/implement/references/*.md` (and, to keep the idiom uniform, `skills/create-issue/SKILL.md` + `skills/init/SKILL.md`): the implement matcher denies a `for` loop (IR1), a `while`/`until` loop (IR2), and a command substitution / `VAR="$(…)"` capture (IR3) **wrapping a label helper** (`ensure-label.sh` / `apply-labels.sh`) — measured as probe rows 4/5/6 — plus a leading `cd` (IR4, issue #855; enforced as an authoring lint) and a stdout/stderr redirect (append or truncate) to a `/tmp/…` target (IR5, issue #915), IR6 prevents agent-authored `gh` producers from redirecting into `.prflow/tmp/**`: evidence is exact-statement-specific. Run 31733588260 recorded the repo-relative row 19 PERMITTED and the absolute-workspace row 20 DENIED, so neither verdict generalizes to another head, target spelling, or statement; agent-authored scratch defaults to the Write tool unless a current exact row covers the complete statement. The older echo rows remain exact controls only (row 11 PERMITTED, row 10 `/tmp` DENIED). IR5 does not inherit R3's `cat`-headed heredoc arm (row 12 records a plain heredoc write PERMITTED on this tier). It also proved the explicit vendored-literal grant *form* PERMITTED (rows 2/3). Row 1 (the unexpanded `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR:-…}` anchor as leading token) is **not** blanket-lint-pinnable on either tier — every legitimate helper call keeps that portable anchor in source (#275) and resolves it at runtime — so the general case stays prose discipline. The one narrow exception (issue #1124): a cloud-reachable call site **enrolled** in `lib/test/lint-anchor-fallback-arm.py` must emit the granted `.prflow/vendor/prflow/scripts/…` vendored literal as its leading token first (the #1256/#1124 conditional form) and keep the anchor line only as a fallback arm — the gate fails RED when an enrolled site emits the anchor leading token with **no** vendored fallback arm. This is deliberately not a blanket fence-static rule against the anchor (which would flag every legitimate call site, colliding with #1152/#1153); the ~100 other anchor-source invocations that `extract-command-heads.py` normalizes to the granted literal stay the sanctioned single-source form until each becomes cloud-reachable and is enrolled. Rows 8/9 now disambiguate non-label and redirect-free label captures; the former is permitted and the latter denied.
- **`--profile command` (rules CR1–CR5, issue #1152)** covers the manual `devflow.yml` command tier — the `/prflow:review-and-fix` bundle (root + every `skills/review-and-fix/references/*.md`) plus the shared review-engine files it executes inline. Its denied-shape set is **inherited from the read-write `implement` tier** remapped to `CR*` ids (`command`-tier denied shapes ⊆ `implement`-tier denied shapes — the assumption the old implement-profile stand-in already rested on), so the `for`/`while`/command-substitution label-helper rules plus the leading-`cd` and `/tmp`-redirect rules carry over. As on the implement tier the unexpanded `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR:-…}` anchor is deliberately **not** a rule (issue #275 / #1124). The tier is now measured empirically as well: `matcher-probe.yml`'s `command-probe` job (baseline generated region `probe-command`) carries the shape rows including the argument-position rows adopted from #1124's closure — **DISPATCHED-PENDING** until a `workflow_dispatch` produces the verdicts. See [`docs/internal/cloud-allowlist.md`](cloud-allowlist.md)'s command-tier section.

These rule tables are additionally applied *by reachability* rather than by file glob: `lib/test/cloud_writer_contract.py`'s `check_shape_conformance()` guard (see the cloud-writer reachability contract below) runs them over every fenced command in each cloud root's closure, keyed by each profile whose own reach includes the asset.

**Recorded implement-probe table (issues #450, #455, #571, and #1514).** `.github/workflows/matcher-probe.yml` is repository-internal measurement infrastructure and `install.sh` does not ship it to consumer repositories. Rows 19–20 add exact production-shaped `gh issue view` statements with distinct target forms; their evidence is not a head- or target-class permission.

| Row | Command shape | Observed verdict |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Unexpanded `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR:-…}` anchor leading token | DENIED |
| 2 | Vendored-literal `apply-labels.sh` leading token | PERMITTED |
| 3 | Vendored-literal `ensure-label.sh` leading token | PERMITTED |
| 4 | `for …; do helper; done` | DENIED |

Rows 1–16 are recorded by [run 29623046995](https://github.com/The01Geek/prflow/actions/runs/29623046995), job `88021801138`, at head `f2162d7683bc7a352fce4efce3f092e864aab8b9`. Rows 17–20 are recorded by [run 31733588260](https://github.com/The01Geek/prflow/actions/runs/31733588260), `implement-probe` job `94559726777`, at head `8eafa06e605b0f043a1e014acb35fb27e63cc008`. Every row recorded `tool_use=yes`; row 7 recorded `shape=n/a`, every discriminated row recorded `shape=ok`, and none was REFORMULATED or UNATTEMPTED.

**No-verdict observability.** Two independent halves signal a no-verdict run so a stalled review is diagnosable rather than a frozen `🚀 Reviewing` comment:
- The **engine** stamps a terminal `❌` on its own progress comment on any path that reaches no verdict — not only a fatal mid-run error but also repeated permission denials or another establishable reason for ending without a verdict (a self-assessed budget or context state is **not** such a reason — a run cannot establish its own remaining context on any tier, so it may not name budget or turn exhaustion as the concrete cause; issue #1489) — flipping `Status` to `❌ Review failed` with a `REVIEW INCOMPLETE — <reason>` verdict line.
- The **workflow** does not rely on that step running. `scripts/surface-execution-diagnostics.sh` emits a `::warning::` when a run recorded permission denials and publishes `permission_denials_count` (a digit string, or the literal `unavailable` when the count could not be established — never collapsed onto `0`); `devflow-runner.yml` exposes it as a job output. It also publishes `claude_code_version` — the CLI build the run executed on, read from the execution file's `{"type":"system","subtype":"init"}` record and reusing `lib/probe-observation.sh`'s `devflow_probe_cli_version` — with the same `unavailable`-not-empty discipline and an in-job `::notice::` read-back, so every live run records the build it ran on with no dependency on the 7-day transcript artifact (issue #1528; see [`docs/internal/execution-diagnostics.md`](execution-diagnostics.md)). Every other init field stays type-only behind `scripts/extract-execution-shape.sh`'s redaction boundary. `devflow-review.yml`'s `finalize_check` emits an `::error::` on a no-verdict run naming the reviewed HEAD and rendering one of three clauses via `scripts/describe-denial-count.sh`. That helper is also the reference implementation of the repo's **workflow inline-shell extraction convention** (`CLAUDE.md`, the shell sibling of the inline-jq-parser rule): inline shell in a workflow that selects a branch or composes a user-facing message is extracted into a `scripts/*.sh` helper so `lib/test/run.sh` can drive each branch and its arm-order — a grep-pin on a message literal is not coverage of the selection that chooses it. Beneath that `permission_denials_count` line the check-run summary also renders the review-tier PreToolUse shape guard's visibility — its heartbeat presence, per-arm denial counts, and denied-command detail (job outputs `pretooluse_guard_fired`, `pretooluse_guard_counts`, `permission_denials_commands`) — via `scripts/render-guard-visibility.sh`, which neutralizes the un-redacted, attacker-influenced command text (backtick-strip, `::`-workflow-command neutralization, fencing, truncation) before it reaches the summary; `scripts/resolve-guard-counts-file.sh` owns the run-keyed/bare/glob selection of the guard's counts store so a fire-but-deny-nothing run reports a known zero rather than `unavailable`. **Durable denial forensics (issue #1064).** Beyond that ephemeral step-summary surfacing, both live tiers also persist a **durable** denial record — count, denied `tool_name`, and (gated by the default-on `.prflow.execution_denial_commands_enabled` key) the **scrubbed** denied-command text — into each run's efficiency record on the `prflow-telemetry` branch, via `scripts/build-denial-record.sh` handed to `lib/efficiency-trace.sh --persist` as a top-level `permission_denials` key. `scripts/build-experiment-records.py` reads that key back into the experiment store's `permission_denials_count`, restoring the durable producer #936 removed with `devflow-review.yml`. The denial-detail line's per-command bound was also raised from 200 to a pinned 500 chars and now renders the denied `.command` specifically (not the stringified `tool_input` envelope), so the ungranted head of a long pipeline is no longer cut off.

**Permissions.** The CI queries need `checks: read`; it is added to `devflow-runner.yml`, to its caller job in `devflow-review.yml` (a reusable workflow requesting a permission its caller did not grant aborts at graph-build time — a coupled pair), and to `devflow.yml`'s `command` job.

### Phase 1: Verification checklist generation
The `prflow:checklist-generator` agent (model: **opus**) reads full file contents and enumerates **every verifiable claim** the diff makes, across its categories: **dependency interactions, test-mock alignment, data-format assumptions, API contracts, string presence, and absolute claims**. Output is a JSON checklist. Key points:
- It **enumerates, does not judge** correctness.
- Files are batched (≤10 per generator; batches of 10 above that).
- **The diff reaches the generator by file reference, not inline (issue #426).** A single batch passes the Phase 0.2 cached `diff.patch` path directly; a multi-batch run authors each batch's slice with a shell-only `awk` piped through `tee` that extracts the batch's `^diff --git` sections from the cached diff into a run-scoped slice file, and passes the generator that slice's *path* — the same `{DIFF_PATH}` file-reference handoff Phase 3 uses, so the slice content never transits the orchestrator's context (the cost the shadow re-paid on every pass). The `awk` output is piped through `tee` into a `grep -c '^diff --git'` that prints the slice's section count, and a second `grep -c '^diff --git'` re-reads the written file to confirm it landed with that same count — a **two-count check**, not a size proxy alone: comparing the piped count against the file's own count catches a **non-empty but truncated** slice from a partial write (`ENOSPC`, quota, a killed `awk`), which a `test -s` size check would wave through, leaving the batch to review a thinned surface with the missing files silently unrepresented. Any observable slice-authoring failure — a non-zero `awk`/`tee` exit, a section-count mismatch, or a missing/empty slice (a shallow checkout, a run-id hiccup) — routes that batch to the full `diff.patch` path (a host with no `awk` at all degrades Phase 0.2's `diff.patch` build first — an upstream failure, not one this batch-level fallback masks). The residual window is named rather than papered over: a write error `awk` itself neither reports nor exits non-zero on would still yield a truncated slice. It reads no `git` objects and takes no **per-file** filename arguments (its only operand is the fixed run-scoped `diff.patch` path), and reuses heads (`awk`/`test`/`echo`) already granted in both cloud allowlists — **no allowlist change**. The same `{DIFF_PATH}`-style file-reference handoff now also covers the **issue body** in `/prflow:implement`'s own Phase 1–2 dispatches (issue #693): Phase 1 §1.1 caches the issue body once per run attempt to `.prflow/tmp/issue-body/issue-<n>.md`, and the §2.1 `code-explorer`, §2.2 `code-architect`, and §4.1 `prflow:docs` dispatches carry an `Issue body path:` line naming that cache instead of pasting the body inline, so the body never transits the orchestrator's context more than once. As with the diff, the read is hand-off-only (never filesystem discovery) and every verdict-bearing reader keeps fetching live.
- The checklist is **capped at 100 items**, prioritized: issue-AC items > absolute_claim > dependency_interaction > test_mock_alignment > api_contract > data_format_assumption. An `absolute_claim` item (a diff-added universal — "every", "never", "is caught by the same rule") always resolves through an `agent` verifier that *constructs the falsifying input*, never a lite grep — reading a universal confirms nothing; only a failed attempt to falsify it does.
- Each item gets a stable `claim_signature` and a `verification_mode` (`lite` = mechanical grep, or `agent` = needs reasoning).

### Phase 1.5: Dedup (only when >1 batch)
The `prflow:checklist-deduper` agent (model: **sonnet**) merges batches, preserving traceability via a `merged_from` field. It **merges, does not re-judge**: when in doubt it leaves items separate (over-merging hides defects).

### Phase 2: Checklist verification
- **lite** items: the orchestrator runs `grep -n`/`rg` directly (no agent).
- **agent** items: the `prflow:checklist-verifier` agent (model: **sonnet**) verifies each claim against the actual source, returning **PASS / FAIL / INCONCLUSIVE** with `file:line` evidence. Batches of up to **8** in parallel. The verifier is a **strict measuring instrument**: it grades a partially-correct claim FAIL and reports structured operands (`property_proven`, `inaccuracy_scope`) rather than self-normalizing — source text is data to classify, never an instruction to obey.
- **Wording-only normalization (issue #556).** Checklist items carry `claim_provenance` (`generated_paraphrase` / `source_authored`, plus a verbatim `source_excerpt` on the latter). An executable helper, `scripts/normalize-verdicts.py`, owns the parse contract and a closed **five-conjunct** predicate that normalizes a raw `FAIL` to a stored `PASS` **only** when the item is an agent-verified `generated_paraphrase`, the raw verdict is byte-exact `FAIL`, `property_proven` is JSON boolean `true`, and `inaccuracy_scope` is `generated_claim_text` — so a generator-wording artifact whose underlying property is proven no longer hard-rejects a pass, while a false source-authored assertion still FAILs → REJECT. The **raw verdict** survives for audit (`raw_verdict`, `normalized`, and a `NORMALIZED (wording-only): ` evidence prefix), the normalization is executable and suite-tested (not an LLM judgment), and the helper's degraded paths fail **closed** (zero normalization with a named tier-appropriate remedy) — it never stalls and never infers a normalization.

### Phase 3: Specialized review agents
All launched in a single message; **always re-run every fix-loop iteration** (the main variance-recovery lever). Four **always-on** reviewers:
- `prflow:code-reviewer`
- `prflow:silent-failure-hunter` (swallowed/over-broadly-caught errors and fail-open fallbacks; also audits **prompt-instruction artifacts** for inert guards — sub-classes `policy-without-mechanism` and `ordered-after-exit`)
- `prflow:comment-analyzer`
- a general-purpose final-pass reviewer invoking the first-party `prflow:requesting-code-review` skill

Each roster id is also the `prflow_review.agent_overrides` key that reprices it. Those keys are namespaced by the plugin name, and the schema declares one per **accepted** namespace, so the pre-rename spellings — `devflow:code-reviewer`, `devflow:requesting-code-review`, and their siblings — keep validating and resolving identically to the canonical `prflow:` forms; see [`docs/internal/review-agent-overrides.md`](review-agent-overrides.md).

Two **gated** reviewers, whose structural-applicability gates apply on **every** diff profile — `engine_self_modifying` included (the flag does not force them on):
- `prflow:type-design-analyzer` (only if `has_new_types`)
- `prflow:pr-test-analyzer` (only if the test-relevance predicate matches)

**Completeness-critic pass (Phase 3.1.5, forced when `detect_all_audit` is set):** when Phase 0.5 flags the diff as adding/changing a "detect-all" audit, the engine runs an extra pass that independently re-enumerates the audit's target population *by a signal other than the audit's own pattern* and emits a finding for any uncovered member (folded into the Phase 3 findings set with a `defect_signature` like any other). It **narrows, but does not close**, the circular-completeness gap — the independent enumeration is itself judgment and can share a blind spot. See `docs/internal/shadow-review.md`.

**Mechanical corroboration:** findings are matched by `defect_signature`; the matching rule is defined in `skills/review/phases/phase-3-agents.md` Phase 3.2. Corroboration across agents is a stronger calibrator than any single agent's stated confidence.

**Dirty-tree backstop (Phase 3.1/3.2).** Phase 3 review/analysis agents are *advisory* and contractually must **never mutate the working tree** — all prohibit editing working-tree source files, the index, HEAD, or branch state. The five fan-out agents perform any mutation/half-revert verification on a temporary copy made with `mktemp`, never in place; the vendored `requesting-code-review` final pass runs under narrower profiles, uses granted read-only history commands, and reports a mutation-verification limitation instead of attempting `mktemp` or a worktree. Independently of agent compliance, the shared engine snapshots the tree with `git status --porcelain -z` immediately **before** the Phase 3.1 batch and compares **after** it returns. Snapshot and restore scratch use fixed repo-local `.prflow/tmp/` paths rather than `mktemp` captures, both because that head is withheld on the implement profile and because shell variables do not survive the Agent-tool boundary between Phase 3.1 and 3.2. On an agent-introduced **modification** it records an **Important** finding (attributed to the dispatch, never silently discarded) and restores only the *snapshot delta* — paths clean at snapshot time that became dirty during the dispatch window — (a rename/copy-only or status-byte-only divergence is surfaced in a breadcrumb rather than a formal finding) computed **by path column**, so a path the orchestrator had already modified is left to the human rather than clobbered. The `-z` capture is load-bearing: it emits **unquoted, NUL-delimited** paths, so a spaced or special-character filename is a real pathspec the restore (`git checkout HEAD -- <path>`, from HEAD so a *staged* mutation is undone) can act on rather than silently skip. Both snapshots are rc-checked and fail closed (a failed before-snapshot disables the backstop for that dispatch rather than restoring off an empty baseline; a failed after-snapshot is surfaced as a distinct breadcrumb, not misattributed as a mutation). Under `/prflow:review` the agents are contractually read-only and normally leave matching snapshots; the backstop still detects a contract violation and also earns its keep in the write-enabled `/prflow:review-and-fix` and `/prflow:implement` tiers (including the Step 2.6 shadow pass, which re-runs these phases verbatim). **Residuals it does NOT auto-restore:** a **true rename/copy** (status `R`/`C`, which needs index surgery to undo safely — surfaced in a breadcrumb, left for the human) and an agent's further edit to an **already-dirty path that does not change its status byte** (identical `-z` record → the divergence test cannot see it). See [`docs/internal/shadow-review.md`](shadow-review.md).

### Phase 4: Aggregation & verdict
- **4.0** (PR mode) honor the Scope-Acknowledged Findings deferrals via `scripts/match-deferrals.py`.
- **4.1.5 Over-grade advisory annotation** (advisory for shapes 1/3 + non-inert shape 2; a deterministic verdict cap for behavior-inert prose): the **single source of truth** for the over-grade shape definitions (`/prflow:review-and-fix`'s Step 2.6 calibration gate consumes the same shapes rather than forking them). Before the verdict is computed, the engine scans the findings it will weigh and **flags** any that match an observable over-grade shape; `skills/review/phases/phase-4-verdict.md` states the numbered shapes and their fail-direction qualifications, which is what the shape ordinals in this bullet refer to. Standalone `/prflow:review` has no fixer to record a calibration, so it appends an advisory "suspected over-grade" note to the flagged finding's report line and **leaves the verdict untouched**: it never demotes a finding, never alters its severity, and never clears or downgrades a REJECT. The note only helps a human reading a bare REJECT distinguish a genuine blocker from a diminishing-returns over-grade. The full flag-and-record gate (which *requires* a recorded `severity-calibrated` evaluation before a flagged finding may drive a shadow-promotion, and still never auto-demotes) remains fix-loop-only. **One deterministic exception to "leaves the verdict untouched":** shape 2's *behavior-inert prose* sub-case — a finding whose sole observable impact is the prose itself, on prose that cannot change what the program does — is a *classification* cap: it is set to Suggestion/Minor and Phase 4.2 does **not** REJECT on it, regardless of the agent's grade, and **whether the diff touched the line is not part of the keying**. **The first conjunct is keyed on the finding's *subject*, never on the kind of file the sentence sits in:** where what the finding disputes is what a mechanism *covers* — a lint's audited population, a guard's exception net, a validation loop's type coverage, a registry's own descriptive claim about its coverage — the subject is that missing coverage rather than the line stating it, so the finding is graded on its functional severity and is never capped by this path, including when the gap is described inside a comment or docstring. **Phase 4.1.5 is the authoritative definition of behavior-inertness — its subject test and its two limbs are not restated here.** It does not reopen the #195 lenient-verdict hole because the judgment decides, from the finding's subject and that surface's stated consumers under the two limbs, whether the disputed sentence's truth value can change what the program does at runtime — never the merits or severity of the finding. `/prflow:review-and-fix`'s Step 2.6 honors the same cap by recording the required `severity-calibrated` evaluation deterministically, consuming the single-sourced 4.1.5 definition. Shape 2 also **excludes** a false-against-HEAD diff-added/modified doc line, comment, example, or command-form from the cosmetic-wording class: that is a truthfulness defect (a `documented_falsehood`), filed as a self-contradicting diff, not a demotable Suggestion — and it drives a non-demotable REJECT unless the two limbs hold, in which case the cap governs. The discriminator is single-sourced in 4.1.5 and mirrored by the shared `defect_signature` contract and the `comment-analyzer` / `code-reviewer` agents, so all six Phase-3 producers tag it from the dispatch itself.
- **4.1.6 Pre-verdict truthfulness sweep** — after the over-grade scan and **before** the verdict, this **promote-only** pre-verdict truthfulness sweep runs over **every** Phase-3 finding **regardless of severity chip** (it does *not* inherit the 4.1.5 over-grade *scan*'s Critical/Important/Major scope, because the mis-filed falsehood it closes lands at Suggestion; 4.1.5's cap already runs over every finding regardless of chip). For each finding whose subject is a diff-added/modified doc line, comment, example, or command-form, the engine verifies the flagged claim against HEAD; a **demonstrated** falsehood is routed into the Phase 4.2 self-contradicting-diff carve-out (**REJECT**) independent of the producing agent's framing and chip — unless the prose is behavior-inert, which the 4.1.5 cap governs and the carve-out's scope excludes, so the sweep never re-promotes a capped finding — while an **inconclusive** check leaves the finding exactly as filed (it promotes only on demonstrated falsity, never on suspicion). The sweep never demotes, downgrades, or clears anything, and a clean pass emits a visible `truthfulness sweep: no finding promoted` report line. Beyond the per-finding pass, the sweep also takes a **diff-scan input** (an *intra-diff contradiction scan*): independent of any finding, it cross-products the diff's added absolute claims (a universal — "every", "never", "is caught by the same rule") against its added-or-retained limitation notes about the **same symbol**, and files a contradicting pair as a non-demotable `documented_falsehood` — closing the PR #340 case where a diff published an absolute claim while retaining a limitation that contradicted it, and *no agent flagged it* so there was no finding to iterate over. Both standalone `/prflow:review` and the fix loop inherit it through the shared engine.
- **4.2 Verdict** (first match wins): any FAIL → **REJECT**; any INCONCLUSIVE → REJECT; any finding **at or above `prflow_review.verdict_severity_threshold`** (default `critical`) → REJECT; otherwise APPROVE (with caveats/notes as appropriate). The threshold moves this REJECT line and, as its complement, the APPROVE-with-notes boundary — no other Phase 4.2 rule reads it, and no verdict label changes with it. A repo can set it to `important` (Important/Major also blocks) or leave it at the default `critical` (byte-identical to the historical behavior). Read through `config-get.sh` (PRFlow's config resolver) and enum-validated inline in the skill, falling back to the default with a breadcrumb on a bad/absent value (never aborts). Both standalone `/prflow:review` and the fix loop's Phase-3 pass inherit it. **A threshold-independent self-contradicting-diff carve-out sits above the numbered rules:** a finding that a doc/release-note line, a code comment, or a test **the PR's own diff added or modified** is untrue drives REJECT at **every** threshold value (including `critical`) and regardless of the finding's severity chip — it is a correctness principle, not a severity grade. It is non-demotable (Phase 4.0's deferral match cannot rescue it) and corroboration-independent, and it shares its definition of "contradicts the diff" with the `receiving-code-review` documented-falsehood carve-out. **Its scope excludes prose the Phase 4.1.5 behavior-inert prose cap covers** (4.1.5 is the authoritative definition of that keying), so how blocking an untrue prose line is depends on whether that prose can change behavior, never on whether the diff happened to touch it — prose that *can* change behavior keeps this path unchanged. When the threshold admits Important, rule 3 also gains an in-scope qualifier (an admitted finding does not REJECT if it is genuinely pre-existing behavior the diff does not touch), which the carve-out overrides. At the default `critical`, the only behavior change is that a self-contradicting-diff finding now drives REJECT instead of being demotable to a note.
- **4.4** (PR mode only, `/prflow:review`) records a formal GitHub review: **REJECT → `--request-changes`** (blocks merge), clean → `--approve`. GitHub forbids requesting-changes-on / approving **your own** PR, so the review agent must run under an identity distinct from the PR author. The cloud tier provides this with a **dedicated `DevFlow-Reviewer` GitHub App** (repository variable `DEVFLOW_REVIEWER_APP_ID` + secret `DEVFLOW_REVIEWER_PRIVATE_KEY`): every review path — the automated runner (`devflow-runner.yml`) and the manual `/prflow:review` command (`devflow.yml`) — runs under the `DevFlow-Reviewer` token when that variable is set, else `github-actions[bot]` (`GITHUB_TOKEN`), and **never** the primary `prflow-implementer` App token that authors PRs. Since implement authors PRs as the primary app (or `github-actions[bot]` when no app is configured), the review identity is structurally distinct from the author on every configured setup, so 4.4's formal review posts instead of failing self-review. The post itself runs through the bundled `post-review-verdict.sh` (issues #1059, #1030) with a closed outcome vocabulary that names the durable channel the verdict reached. The helper takes the Phase 4.2 **verdict token** (not a REST event) and chooses the channel itself; when the review POST is refused it posts the same body as a pull-request comment and says so; when neither channel takes it, it emits a distinct no-durable-channel token and exits non-zero, and the engine posts the full report as a plain comment whose body opens with a **durable failure record** — naming the failed post, its captured error, the reached verdict, and the fact that this comment is not read as a verdict — and, on an APPROVE, runs the stale-REJECT dismissal regardless and folds its result into that record. See **The verdict marker** below for what the helper stamps. See [`docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`](cloud-setup.md) for setup and the single-app upgrade note.

### When Phase 4.4 is not reached at all (issue #1156)

Everything 4.4 records begins at the emitter's first line, so a run that **never invokes it** produces none of it. That case is real and it exits `success`: a standalone review can reach a verdict, publish it as an ordinary pull-request comment it composed itself, and finish clean — leaving the reviews API empty for the reviewed head, `reviewDecision` at whatever a superseded round left there, and every post-run backstop silent, because each is gated on the run having gone wrong. A maintainer afterwards finds a confident verdict, a contradicting `reviewDecision`, and a green run, with nothing separating *"the post was refused"* from *"the post was never reached"*.

The detection below acts *after* the gap; the *upstream* control is a review-only section of the injected grounding block (section 6, issue #1629), which states — independent of whether any phase reference is read — that a verdict reaches the pull request only through 4.4's emitter and a self-composed verdict comment is not a verdict (see *Engine ground truth: the injected block* above). It is prevention through prose and so carries no regression coverage of its *effect*; `scripts/measure-verdict-post-gap-rate.sh` establishes the per-review-run denominator that measures whether it moved the occurrence rate.

`post-review-verdict.sh` therefore writes a **run-scoped receipt** of each contract line it prints (`lib/verdict-receipt.sh` owns the path both ends compose, so they cannot drift apart), best-effort and isolated so no failure of it can perturb the helper's stdout, its line ordering, or its exit code. `scripts/check-verdict-post-reached.sh` reduces that receipt to one line from a closed vocabulary — `REACHED <outcome line>`, `NOT-REACHED`, or `UNESTABLISHED <reason>` — and always exits 0. An `always()` step in `devflow.yml`'s `command` job, gated to the standalone review command in pull-request mode, feeds that line to `scripts/describe-verdict-post-gap.sh`, which selects the arm and composes every byte it emits; the step renders those bytes and chooses nothing itself, so the suite drives every arm and its arm order rather than grepping a message literal out of YAML.

**Which pull request the step acts on (issue #1863).** The PR-ness guard tests the thread the comment sits on, and the step then acts on `TARGET_NUMBER` — the resolved command's own number. Those two are the same thread only because `scripts/resolve-command-trigger.sh` discards any trailing number a light command carries (`/prflow:review 42`) and resolves to the event's context number unconditionally, so the guard's subject and the acted-on number cannot diverge. Before #1863 a number typed elsewhere could pass the guard on one thread and write on another — recording a reach comment on a plain issue rendered as a pull request, or withholding the record from the pull request that was really reviewed. The step's own event-number fallback is unchanged and now redundant with the resolver's guarantee; the resolver still emits the command followed by a number, so the derivation keeps yielding that thread's number. When a typed number is discarded the resolver writes a `::warning::` line to the run log naming the ignored number and the used one.

`UNESTABLISHED` never collapses onto `NOT-REACHED`. A receipt that cannot be read warns naming the reason and posts nothing, because the not-reached claim is exactly what was not established — accusing a run of skipping 4.4 on no evidence, in a comment on the pull request, is the "unknown is not zero" regression this repository has already shipped once.

**And `NOT-REACHED` is an observation, not a conclusion — the same rule one level down.** The reader answers it on receipt *absence*, and absence has two causes: the emitter never ran, or it ran and its best-effort receipt write failed. So the comment states what was observed (no receipt was found), names both causes, points at the `could not write the verdict-post receipt` breadcrumb that distinguishes them in the job log, and tells the reader that a formal review in the reviews API for that head means the emitter ran. It deliberately does **not** assert that 4.4 was skipped, and it does **not** assert that the reviews API is unchanged: on the write-failure cause with a posted review, both claims would be false on a public pull-request comment. What it posts is a record of the gap and the way to close it, never a verdict.

**And the reach record now carries a job status (issue #1271).** Recording the gap is not the same as making it *loud*: about one standalone review in three finished green having reached no verdict any consumer reads (this repository measured 35 of 106 successful annotated runs).
