So the same `always()` step now also fails its job — but only on the single conjunctive shape where that is unambiguously right: the emitter was **not reached** *and* the head-scoped review oracle (`scripts/classify-head-reviews.sh`) **positively established** that no verdict exists for the reviewed head. `scripts/decide-verdict-gap-job-status.sh` owns that FAIL/PASS decision so the suite can drive every arm; the step reads its token and exits 1 only on `FAIL`, keeping `exit 0` as the terminal line on every other path and emitting no less than before (the `::notice::`, the `::warning::`, and the cause-naming comment are unchanged, on cancelled runs too — cancellation is passed to the helper as an argument, never a step condition). Every *could-not-tell* answer leaves the job status untouched: an `unestablished`/`marked`/`unmarked` oracle result, a `no-line`/`unrecognized-line` reach arm, and a missing helper on an older vendored tree all pass, honoring "unknown is not zero" (the reviews-only oracle's blindness to the progress-comment fallback channel is the disclosed residual, stated in the helper's header). Because these triggers run the workflow from the default branch, the gate is inert inside its own pull request and lands only after merge; `devflow.yml`'s `command` job creates no check-run, so no consumer pull request is wedged behind it.

**The stale-REJECT dismissal net (issue #1175).** The reach-record above tells a maintainer that 4.4 was skipped; it does not *repair* the one consequence that leaves a pull request wedged — a superseded REJECT still sitting as the `reviewDecision` after a fresh APPROVE, which only 4.4's `dismiss-stale-rejections.sh` step clears. So a second `always()` step in `devflow.yml`'s `command` job, under the same standalone-`/prflow:review` PR-mode gate, runs `scripts/dismiss-stale-rejections-net.sh` as the workflow-side net. It fires the dismissal **only when the verdict for the reviewed HEAD was positively determined as APPROVE**: it reuses the HEAD-scoped, fail-closed `scripts/derive-review-verdict.sh` and gates on its `verdict_determined` output, so a correlated GitHub API degradation that *defaults* the verdict can never dismiss a live REJECT — the failure mode that makes an unconditional workflow-side dismissal worse than none at all. Every other outcome (a determined REJECT, a defaulted or unestablished verdict, an engine error, or a missing sibling helper) refuses and says so via the step's `::notice::`/`::warning::` annotation rather than failing silent. It is a **net, not a replacement**: `dismiss-stale-rejections.sh` is idempotent and only ever clears a review whose *reviewed tree is superseded* — the tree read from the verdict marker's `head=` when the review carries one, and from `commit_id` only for a markerless review (issue #1247) — so 4.4's own run and this workflow run never double-dismiss or fight, and the agent's Phase 4.4 dismissal is unchanged. The gate arms are driven per arm from `lib/test/modules/review-trigger-helpers.sh` (the `#1175` section), and the shipped workflow block is executed there against a recording stub rather than grepped.

### The review-evidence gate (issue #2075)

Every live signal that the review engine actually ran its phases — the progress ticks, the tally lines, the telemetry — is written by the same agent being checked, so a cloud run that skips the phases and still posts a verdict passes every other check and the job ends green with a hollow, merge-gating review live on the pull request. The review-evidence gate closes that gap on the cloud tier by checking the posted verdict against machine-readable evidence the engine's phases left behind.

The engine's own entry gate now appends one line per phase entry to a run-scoped phase log at `.prflow/tmp/review/<slug>/<run-id>/phase-log` — `phase-entry phase=<id>`, written with the granted `printf … | tee -a` shape (no environment-variable expansion, so it survives the cloud matcher). Two legitimate no-checklist arms write their own record instead of the Phase 1/Phase 2 entries: the checklist generator's double-failure arm appends `checklist-skip reason=failure` (reusing the `checklist_skipped` field's existing failure spelling), and the Phase 0.3.6 blocker-recheck fast path, **on a hit only** (never on a fall-through, which runs the full pipeline and writes its own entries), appends `blocker-recheck-hit re-verdict=posted` before ending the run — the sole record the gate accepts as that fast path's green arm.

`scripts/review-evidence-gate.py` is a workflow-side, post-engine, job-level gate in `.github/workflows/devflow.yml`. It attributes this run's run root and verdict without any run key from the engine: a pre-engine inventory step snapshots the `.prflow/tmp/review/` tree and the head's review IDs immediately before the engine step, and the gate attributes to this run the directories and reviews that appear during the step — an ID-set delta on GitHub's own records, never a clock comparison against a runner clock. It screens on the resolved command the way the dead-run flip step does: it evaluates `/prflow:review` and `/prflow:review-and-fix` (the latter against the head the newest posted verdict records) and no-ops on `/prflow:pr-description`. It recomputes the diff classification from git through the same implementation `scripts/workpad.py` uses for its skipped-intentional recheck (no new copy of the classification constants), so it knows which evidence the run owed.

When a run posted a marker-bearing merge-gating verdict on the head whose recomputed classification required the checklist phases, but the attributed run root holds no phase log recording them (an engine step that created no run root at all is the same missing-record state), the gate fails the review job with an `::error::` naming each missing record, posts a durable pull-request comment through the workflow's comment helper, flips the run's progress comment to its failed state through `scripts/flip-review-progress-failed.sh --evidence-gate-fail` (a new arm taken only on a gate failure, for a run that already wrote a terminal Status; the existing interim-Status flip is unchanged), and dismisses the unbacked review best-effort through the REST review-dismissal call (the `dismiss-stale-rejections.sh` precedent). Dismissal targets only the marker-bearing review absent from the pre-engine review-ID inventory, and only when its state is `APPROVED` or `CHANGES_REQUESTED` — a `COMMENT`-state verdict is not merge-satisfying and is left to the durable comment, which also stands as the recorded residual when a dismissal is refused (e.g. a non-administrator on a protected branch). A verdict already present in the pre-engine inventory contributes to no arm of this run's evidence and is never dismissed.

The legitimate no-evidence arms end green with a `::notice::` naming the arm taken (intentional checklist skip, generator double-failure, 0.3.6 hit, no verdict posted). An evidence state the gate cannot establish ends the job green with a `::warning::` — reported neither as a pass nor a failure — covering a run root that exists but cannot be read, a vendored engine whose review root predates the phase-log instruction, the gate helper absent at both the vendored and repo-root paths (an arm the workflow step owns, since an absent script cannot report itself), a run-root delta the step cannot attribute to this run, and a diff range the classification recompute cannot resolve.

Because these triggers run the workflow from the default branch, the workflow half activates only after merge, and the change needs no capability-profile grant edit: the engine fence reuses granted `printf`/`tee`, and the gate script runs workflow-side. Residuals it does not protect against, by design: an agent that actively fabricates phase-log lines, the phase-entry Reads themselves (recorded only by the opt-in execution-transcript artifact), and a `/prflow:review-and-fix` PR that edits the gate helper or the phase-log instruction after Step 0.5's `gh pr checkout` moves the tree to the PR head — the same base-ref trust residual `devflow.yml` records for the prompt-extension loader, which routes the gate to its green unestablished arms.

### The verdict marker (issue #1030)

The reviews API carries a verdict's **decision** (`state`) and the commit it was recorded against (`commit_id`), but not **whose verdict it is**. Every consumer that must tell PRFlow's own verdict from a human reviewer's was therefore forced to pattern-match prose the reviewing agent wrote at the top of the body — and a census over the 60 most recently created pull requests in this repository measured **6 of 9** real bot `CHANGES_REQUESTED` bodies matching none of the shapes the consumers looked for. Each was a real REJECT that `dismiss-stale-rejections.sh` could never dismiss, so a later legitimate APPROVE left the pull request wedged at `reviewDecision: CHANGES_REQUESTED` while the script reported a clean no-op.

The fix is a **producer-emitted marker**. `post-review-verdict.sh` — never the reviewing agent — composes one line and stamps it on both durable surfaces:

```
<!-- prflow:review-verdict head=<40-hex head sha> verdict=<APPROVE|REJECT> -->
```

- **Placement.** Line 1 of the review body. In the run-keyed progress comment, the line immediately after the existing `<!-- prflow:review-progress run=… -->` line, so the run key stays line 1 and `seed-review-progress.sh`'s reported literal is unaffected. Readers scan only a body's first two lines, so a marker a finding quotes deeper in the body is prose, not a stamp.
- **Placement is mechanically preserved across a full-body rewrite (issue #1508).** A rewrite composes its bytes from state the caller holds, so a caller that does not retype these lines drops them — and nothing errors: a marker scan finds nothing and reads "there was no such comment". `scripts/workpad.py patch` therefore reads the live body first and re-inserts any leading marker line the composed body omits, in the live body's order, with a marker the caller *does* supply winning for its own **kind** so a same-kind re-stamp still lands (a migration that changes a marker's kind does not — it reads as a new kind, and the old marker is re-inserted beside it). The recognizer is anchored at column 0, the same predicate the readers use, and the scan window is the same two lines, so neither an indented marker nor one deeper in a body is ever hoisted into a stamp. A live body the read cannot establish — including `gh` exiting 0 with an error envelope carrying no `.body` — is *unknown*, never an empty one: the PATCH is refused unless the composed body carries its own leading marker. Observed clobbering: PR #1502 and PR #1504 lost the verdict marker at line 2, and PR #1523's run `31356552464-1` lost the run key at line 1.
- **Namespace.** `<!-- prflow:` per issue #1003, with **no** superseded `<!-- devflow:review-verdict` spelling accepted anywhere: the marker postdates the rename, so no persisted artifact can carry the old one. The dual acceptance of the `prflow:review-progress` / `devflow:review-progress` run key is untouched.
- **Verdict field.** Normalized to two tokens. The human verdict line keeps the full enum; every approve-family verdict normalizes to `APPROVE`.
- **Head field — which tree was reviewed (issue #1247).** The marker's `head=` is the field that records the commit a review actually reviewed. The reviews-API `commit_id` also names a commit, but **GitHub can change a review's `commit_id` after the review is submitted**, to a commit that did not exist at review time — so `commit_id` is not a reliable record of the reviewed tree, and a disagreement between the two keys is ordinary GitHub behavior, not a defect in PRFlow's own emitter. Observed on PR #1234: its middle review was submitted `2026-08-04T00:40:54Z`, but the `commit_id` GitHub reports for it, `b0ae621aa`, was committed `2026-08-04T01:16:07Z` — 35 minutes *after* the review — while the review's own marker `head=1db2884dd` names a commit committed `2026-08-04T00:28:25Z`, before the review and an ancestor of `b0ae621aa`. Re-derive either timestamp with `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/<sha> --jq .commit.committer.date`. So the two readers that act on the reviewed tree — `dismiss-stale-rejections.sh` and the Phase 0.3.6 blocker-recheck fast path — read the marker's `head=` when the review carries one and fall back to `commit_id` only for a markerless review.
  - **`derive-review-verdict.sh` keeps failing closed on the disagreement, by decision.** When a review's marker `head=` disagrees with the HEAD under consideration it emits `incomplete`, joining on neither key rather than guessing. The accepted cost is a re-review round each time a branch is updated after review; that cost is recorded here as a settled decision, not an open question.
  - **Marker-absent residual.** For a review carrying **no** marker — posted before #1030 landed, or posted by hand on the local tier — `commit_id` is the only key available, so a mutated one cannot be detected there. There is no second key to consult, so this residual is recorded rather than repaired.
  - On a **comment** the marker's `head=` is authoritative, because an issue comment carries no API-side head.
- **Authority.** When a HEAD-scoped review artifact exists its marker decides; the progress comment's marker is authoritative only in its absence — the same rule as "review-outcome analysis sources verdicts from the immutable bot PR reviews", which are not edited in place.
- **Consumers.** `dismiss-stale-rejections.sh` (selection), `scripts/derive-review-verdict.sh` (first signal on both surfaces), `lib/fetch-pr-context.sh`'s `verdicts_in` (and therefore `review_reject_outstanding`), `scripts/build-experiment-records.py` (with its own provenance tags), and the Phase 0.3.6 blocker-recheck precondition.
- **Producer guarantee — and its limit.** `Bash(gh pr review:*)` is withdrawn from all three capability profiles, so the helper that stamps is the sole granted *marking* post path. It is **not** the sole granted *post* path: `Bash(gh api:*)` is granted in all three profiles (it is mandated for label creation, label application and body edits, whose porcelain equivalents fail silently under a repo-scoped token), and the reviews endpoint reached through it — `POST repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/N/reviews` — creates a real, merge-blocking review that carries no marker. This was observed live: run 30860699039 posted `CHANGES_REQUESTED` review 4849248513 on pull request #1227 directly through `gh api` after the granted helper spelling was refused, and no verdict-derivation consumer read it as a verdict. A prefix-narrowed grant cannot separate that write from the reads the review engine legitimately makes against the *same* endpoint (the HTTP method is a separate token, not part of the URL), so the grant stays; the control is downstream instead (issue #1250): the Phase 4.4 reach record now classifies the reviews actually recorded on the head (`scripts/classify-head-reviews.sh`) and reports an unmarked one truthfully rather than asserting the reviews API was untouched. On the local tier the shell is ungated and a human — or a run — can post an unmarked review by hand; the transitional prose arms are what carry that case. The gap is narrowed, not closed.

#### Retiring the transitional prose acceptance — the end criterion

Every consumer keeps accepting the two pre-marker prose shapes (a `## Verdict: REJECT` / `## Verdict: APPROVE` first line, and the legacy `# Review Report` prefix) for as long as unmarked reviews can still be outstanding. Retirement is **confirmation-gated, never a timer, and carries no date and no version**. A maintainer runs this check over the repository's open pull requests, and the arms may be removed — in one change across every reader listed above — only when it prints nothing:

```bash
for n in $(gh pr list --state open --limit 200 --json number --jq '.[].number'); do
  gh api --paginate "repos/{owner}/{repo}/pulls/$n/reviews?per_page=100" \
    --jq '.[] | select(.state == "CHANGES_REQUESTED" or .state == "APPROVED" or .state == "COMMENTED")
          | select((.body // "") | strings | startswith("<!-- prflow:review-verdict ") | not)
          | "PR '"$n"' review \(.id) carries no verdict marker"'
done
```

An empty result means no open pull request still carries an outstanding review a marker-only reader would miss. A non-empty one names each review that still needs the prose arms; re-run it rather than trusting a previous result.

**The premise that unmarked reviews are a shrinking historical set is currently FALSE (issue #1250).** A live producer of unmarked reviews still exists on the cloud tiers: any run that reaches for the withdrawn `gh pr review` helper spelling can fall back to `POST …/pulls/N/reviews` through the granted `gh api` and create a real, unmarked, merge-blocking review (run 30860699039 / review 4849248513). So an empty sweep result today is a **snapshot**, not a terminal state — the next run can falsify it — and the criterion is **not** declared satisfiable while that producer remains. Issue #1250 makes such a review *observable* (the reach record classifies it and reports it truthfully) but deliberately does **not** neutralize the producer; retiring the transitional prose arms therefore waits on a separate change that stops unmarked reviews being produced, not merely on an empty sweep.

### The loop-verdict marker (issue #1212)

The same shape of problem lived one level up, between two skills rather than between a skill and a GitHub artifact. `/prflow:implement` calls `/prflow:review-and-fix` as its review loop and reads the loop's one-line **verdict headline** by exact string match to decide the single safety-relevant question: was this work independently reviewed? That headline is ordinary English prose, and the two skills sit on disk as separately-versioned plugin copies — a running implement orchestrator can call a review loop loaded from a newer, differently-worded version. Reword the headline on the writing side and a reader one version behind breaks silently, in the dangerous direction: an `APPROVE WITH UNRESOLVED SHADOW FINDINGS` run (emphatically *not* a clean approval) read as a clean approve and shipped unreviewed.

The fix follows the verdict-marker precedent above. `scripts/loop-verdict-marker.py` — never the agent in free text — composes one machine-readable line the loop emits as **line 1** of its chat output, and the same helper's `read` subcommand parses it:

```
<!-- prflow:loop-verdict result=<result-token> coverage=<full|not-verified> -->
```

- **Placement.** Line 1 of the loop's chat output, immediately before the human headline. The reader inspects that line only, so a marker a finding quotes deeper in the report is prose, not a stamp.
- **Namespace.** `<!-- prflow:` per issue #1003, with **no** superseded `devflow:` spelling accepted — the marker postdates the rename.
- **Result field.** One of the six loop-level results, as a space-free token: `approve` / `approve-with-notes` / `approve-with-caveat` / `approve-with-advisory-notes` / `approve-unresolved-shadow-findings` / `reject`.
- **Coverage field.** `full` **only** when the loop's `{shadow status}` phrase is exactly `shadow agreed, full coverage`; every other phrase normalizes to `not-verified`. The helper never over-claims full coverage.
- **Producer.** `skills/review-and-fix/references/loop-exit.md`'s *Verdict → chat output* invokes the `compose` subcommand; the vendored helper path is granted in the `implement` and `command` capability profiles (the review lock is untouched — a widen it does not).
- **Reader.** `skills/implement/phases/phase-3-fix-loop.md` §3.3 runs the `read` subcommand **first** and routes on its closed-vocabulary token (`CLEAN-FULL` / `CLEAN-NOT-VERIFIED` / `AWUSF` / `REJECT` / `NO-MARKER` / `MALFORMED`), keeping its existing exact-wording headline match as the fallback for a loop one version behind that emits no marker.
- **Safe direction.** Only `CLEAN-FULL` authorizes the clean, fully-covered completion path. A missing, malformed, or out-of-vocabulary marker is never read as a clean approval — it falls back to the prose path, and if that cannot resolve the verdict either, the run takes its existing not-clean handling.
- **Both directions across the one-version gap.** An older implement run ignores the extra first line and reads the headline exactly as before; a newer implement run that finds no marker falls back to the headline match. Version pinning is **not** proposed or required — `docs/internal/cloud-setup.md` records the no-pinning decision as a deliberate security trade-off.
- **Retirement of the caller's prose fallback is confirmation-gated** — it names no date and no version, and is removed only once a maintainer-runnable check confirms no review loop old enough to emit no marker remains in play.

Only the marker line is tool-read and covered by executable tests (`lib/test/loop-verdict-marker-test.py`, driven from `lib/test/run.sh`); the surrounding explanatory headline prose on both sides stays uncovered, per the #843/#876 decision.

### Per-subagent model & effort overrides
The `prflow_review.agent_overrides` config maps any of the **nine** review subagents (or a `default`) to a `{model, effort, iterations}`. The engine dispatches its subagents from an already-running session via the **Agent tool**, which carries a per-dispatch **`model`** override parameter but **no effort parameter**, and there is no per-dispatch `--agents` injection in an already-running session. That surface is **not closed at `model`**: the Agent tool also carries a per-dispatch **background/foreground** property, and it — not `model` — is the parameter that decides whether a dispatch returns a result at all. Upstream, subagents are **background by default** and a background dispatch's results arrive as a completion notification **in a later turn**, which a headless cloud run never reaches; the cloud engine steps therefore set `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS: "1"` to keep them in the foreground, and each engine root states the same requirement behaviorally for runtimes with no equivalent switch (issue #801). Forcing foreground also restores the full built-in tool set a background subagent would otherwise lose, so roster behavior can change independently of the stall. A per-agent **model** override is expressible only as one of the Agent tool's four accepted aliases (`sonnet`, `opus`, `haiku`, `fable`), which `scripts/resolve-review-overrides.py` validates: an in-set value is forwarded unchanged and an out-of-set value (e.g. a full or provider-routed identifier) is dropped with a warning, so the agent inherits the top-level `claude_model`. So a resolved per-agent **model** override IS delivered (as the Agent tool's `model` parameter), while a resolved per-agent **effort** override is **not** applied per-agent on this in-session path — the subagent inherits the session effort, and `scripts/resolve-review-overrides.py` reports this honestly as a `session-fallback` (a `::notice::` per resolve for the benign no-seam case, or a `::warning::` naming the model/provider for a capability-restricted one), never as an applied success (issue #554). The per-tier effort application-point matrix (`agent-definition` / `process-start-session` / `session-fallback` / `session-inheritance`) is documented in [review-agent-overrides.md](review-agent-overrides.md); a per-agent applied arm and its telemetry carrier are a spike-gated deferred follow-up. Effort enum: `low/medium/high/xhigh/max`. The optional `iterations` key (default-off; only value `first-only`) is a roster-scoping lever, not a dispatch parameter: an agent carrying it is excluded from the Phase-3 review roster on `/prflow:review-and-fix` fix-loop iterations ≥ 2 (a no-op in standalone `/prflow:review` and never applied to the Step 2.6 shadow) — see [review-agent-overrides.md](review-agent-overrides.md).

### The fix loop (`/prflow:review-and-fix`)
- **Thin root + reloadable step references (issue #530).** `skills/review-and-fix/SKILL.md` is a thin root (kept lean by authoring discipline) that retains the invocation contract, the run-scoped `### Schema`, `### Lifecycle`, the Main-Loop control pointer, a **Step-routing** table, a fail-closed **Reference-loading contract**, and the terminal verdict→chat mapping. The step procedures live in reloadable references under `skills/review-and-fix/references/` (`loop-control.md`, `pre-fix-gates.md`, `shadow-review.md`, `fixing.md`, `fix-delta-gate.md`, `convergence.md`, `loop-exit.md`, `error-handling.md`), each loaded at step entry behind a single ordered `# Reference:` / `<!-- END … -->` boundary; an unreadable **loop-step** reference takes a mapped fail-closed outcome (engine-gate/fixing → stop before mutation; shadow → `coverage: not_verified`; convergence → non-convergence; Loop Exit → persistence backstop + incomplete terminal state — none of those permits a clean approve; `error-handling.md` alone is best-effort contextual guidance, not a loop step — its absence degrades only guidance, per the root's failure map). The **durable state** that lets a compacted or resumed run recover its position — `current_step`, `current_substep`, `pending_dispatch` — is written into the run-scoped `iter-<N>.json`, and an always-resident root rule re-reads the active reference rather than trusting recall (the record, not agent memory, is the step predicate). Since issue #1470 that rule states its own firing scope, keyed on the durable stamp rather than on who is remembered to have dispatched, and since issue #1488 that stamp carries a durable `issued_by` operand (the stamping reference's own `current_step`) so the firing predicate and its absent-operand arm are each decidable from the run-scoped `iter-<N>.json` alone rather than from the orchestrator's live context — it fires on a return resolving a `pending_dispatch` whose recorded `issued_by` names the active reference, and a pre-change record carrying no `issued_by` fails closed rather than reading as loop-issued. So the dispatch returns the review engine's own phases produce while the loop executes the engine inline — the Phase-1 generators, the Phase-1.5 deduper, Phase 2's verifier dispatches and its re-asks, and the Phase-3 roster — do **not** re-read the loop's active reference; the engine's own phase procedure governs its phases. The loop's own stamped dispatches keep firing the rule, the Step 2.6 shadow fan-out among them, as does the return that ends an engine entry. This mirrors the `skills/implement/` thin-orchestrator + `phases/*.md` split. The reliability of the split's routing + fail-closed wording was measured empirically (5 fresh-context samples + a budget-RED and a no-guidance control) in [`docs/internal/review-and-fix-split-wording-study.md`](review-and-fix-split-wording-study.md).
- **Engine entries run as dispatched subagents (issue #1850).** The loop's review-engine entries — Step 1 and the Step 2.6 shadow — each dispatch an Agent-tool **engine subagent** that runs the engine's Phases 0–4.3 and fans out the Phase 3 roster from its own context, returning `dispatch_mode: fanned-out` with an entry-keyed `engine-return-*.json` by-path handoff; on a harness lacking nested dispatch it returns `dispatch_mode: unavailable` and the parent runs the engine inline exactly as before (fail-closed; any non-well-formed fanned-out outcome also falls back to inline). The subagent runs in a fresh context holding none of the loop's own text, so the parent carries Step 1's resolution steps 3 and 4 — the completeness predicate and its failure routing — into that prompt **verbatim** and the subagent reports its determination back as `root_completeness` — the parent's only view of a predicate the subagent alone applied, and a return reporting anything but established is not well-formed and takes the same inline fallback. Without that carry the dispatched path, which is tried first, would accept a truncated engine root and return a well-formed, merge-gating verdict over a fragment. This bills the ~242 KB engine prompt once inside a bounded subagent life instead of leaving it resident in the orchestrator's prefix for the rest of the run. The parent stays the sole writer of `iter-<N>.json`, and persists each entry's returned value in that record — top-level `dispatch_mode` for Step 1, `shadow.dispatch_mode` for the shadow — as `null` when the value was not established, so a run that silently flattened to inline is distinguishable from a dispatched one without raw-transcript inspection. Each is additive and conditional: `ITER_EXPECTED_FIELDS` excludes them and every existing reader takes named keys. See §9's portability constraint.
- **Configurable iteration cap (default 5, `prflow_review_and_fix.max_iterations`).** Each iteration runs the full engine, then fixes findings one at a time (using `prflow:receiving-code-review` principles), runs tests, commits (`fix:`), and continues.
- **Configurable fix-severity threshold (`prflow_review_and_fix.fix_severity_threshold`, default `important`).** The loop routes to the fixer every finding **at or above** the threshold (`critical` > `important` > `suggestion`) and parks the rest as advisory; at the default it fixes Critical + Important/Major and parks Suggestion/Minor (the historical behavior). Set `suggestion` for a more aggressive fixer or `critical` for a conservative one. Its **effective fix set additionally includes every finding that drove the engine's REJECT** — whether at/above `verdict_severity_threshold` or via a threshold-independent REJECT class (e.g. the self-contradicting-diff carve-out), even below the fix threshold — so no configuration combination produces a REJECT the fixer is configured to ignore. Read through `config-get.sh` and enum-validated inline (same never-aborting fallback as the verdict threshold). A separate `receiving_review.fix_severity_threshold` (default `critical`) governs the re-open bar only when `/prflow:receiving-code-review` is invoked **directly**; the loop never consults it.
- **Below-Important fix-loop damper (`prflow_review_and_fix.fix_below_threshold_iterations`, integer ≥ 0, default 1).** Only bites when `fix_severity_threshold` resolves to `suggestion` (at the default `important` threshold no below-`important` finding is admitted, so it is inert). Resolved once at loop start through the same resolve-validate pattern as `max_iterations` (a non-negative-integer check; a bad/absent value falls back to `1` with a warning breadcrumb naming the key; `0` is a legal value that is not coalesced to the default). Below-`important` findings the threshold admits route to the fixer during the first `fix_below_threshold_iterations` iterations exactly as before. After that window, on an iteration whose at-or-above-threshold findings include **no** Critical and **no** Important finding, each fresh below-`important` finding is parked as an advisory (decision `below-important-damper`, `skip_category` `below-important-damper-parked`) and starts **no** fix iteration; on an iteration that routes at least one Critical/Important finding, below-`important` findings still ride along and add no iteration of their own. A finding that drove the engine's REJECT always routes regardless of the window. Set the key at or above `max_iterations` to restore the prior behavior; set `0` to park below-`important` findings from the first iteration. Damper-parked findings are members of the reconciled parked population and render in the loop's final advisory output.
- **Pre-fix verification gate (Step 2.5):** single-source claims about external tools are **web-verified** (cap **5 WebFetches/iteration**), Confirmed → fix; Refuted → demote to advisory. This prevents the loop from "fixing" a hallucinated problem.
- **Pre-shadow parked-class sweep:** at both non-REJECT convergence entries, parked findings seed a bounded changed-surface enumeration before the shadow runs. The sweep registers every sibling in the triggering iteration's findings, promotes at-or-above-threshold siblings through the normal counted fix machinery, parks lower-severity siblings with a distinct marker, deduplicates by site overlap, and fails closed through a required Reflection sentinel when semantic enumeration cannot be verified.
- **Stale counted-prose post-commit pre-check (Step 3):** immediately after each fix commit, the loop runs the *same* `stale-prose-lint.py` the shared review engine runs in Phase 0.6 — here recomputing the full branch diff at the just-committed post-fix `HEAD` against the exact operand Phase 0.2 selected (`origin/$PR_BASE_BRANCH` normally or the retained `baseRefOid` after confirmed deletion in PR mode; `origin/$BASE`, the configured `base_branch` fail-closed to `main`, in current-branch mode), **not** the pre-fix diff Phase 0.2 cached. A stale claim a fix introduced under an earlier-added header is therefore caught (the earlier header is still an added line in the full-branch diff), and the invocation outcome (TSV rows, a `git diff` producer-failure note, or the helper's degradation arm) is stored in that iteration's `iter-<N>.json` under `stale_prose_check`. This item-6a pre-check is fail-closed via `set -o pipefail` + Phase 0.6 exit-code routing (it pipes straight to the lint helper — no cache to publish); the candidate/promotion/publication pipeline lives in Phase 0.2's cache producer, which checks the raw diff, filtered candidate, promotion write, and stdout publication, and on any failure or unreachable merge-base after the one-shot `--unshallow` retry removes every candidate and prior cache, becomes Blocked when observed by the wrapping run (or stop-and-report standalone), and never yields a silent `APPROVE`-eligible empty diff. A retargeted/stacked PR emits a warning naming its PR base and the different configured `base_branch`, making the residual leak observable. A fix-introduced stale counted claim is thus reconciled **within the same iteration** rather than at a later full-engine pass; it adds no new verdict or iteration-accounting rule (a surviving false-positive routes through the existing `fix_decisions` pushback shape; an unresolved STALE at the cap follows the existing unresolved-shadow-findings path).
- **Mechanism-scoped self-authored-claim re-sweep (Step 3, item 3a):** after a fix changes a *mechanism* (a guard, predicate, exclusion, or helper that comments describe), the loop re-dispatches the existing `prflow:comment-analyzer` (no new agent) over every comment describing that mechanism — located by the mechanism's identifiers across the touched files, not limited to the fix's own diff hunks — and treats a comment that still describes the pre-change mechanism as a finding. It covers the changed mechanism within touched files; it is **not** a repo-wide comment audit. See `docs/internal/shadow-review.md`.
- **Fix-delta authoring-side sweeps re-anchored on the full §2.3 index (Step 3's item 3b):** on every iteration in which Step 3 applied fixes, the loop classifies the fix delta by implement Phase 2.3's "Sweep selection (run first)" preamble, resolved from the executing skill bundle rather than the consumer repository and selects **every** warranted Phase 2.3 authoring-side sweep — the trigger-gated sweeps the delta matches plus every always-on sweep the preamble names — with no hand-enumerated subset, so adding a sweep to that index needs no selection edit here (a new implement-only routing class still requires mapping/lint vocabulary maintenance). Readable definitions run against the fix delta. A durable `sweep_defs_read` read protocol bounds the context bill (full definitions read once per run, headings re-read each iteration, adjudicated against the record not recall; an unreadable or truncated §2.3 source records `sweeps: unrunnable …` and names Step 3.5 + the shadow as backstop rather than stalling). A drift-guarded **fix-loop mapping table** resolves the imported sweeps' implement-only routings (workpad notes, `(post-merge)` retags, Phase 4.1 deferrals, desk-lint claims, the `CLAUDE.md` rulebook) to standalone-loop destinations, and a `lib/test/run.sh` routing-marker lint turns PRFlow's desk RED when a **closed-vocabulary** routing marker present in a §2.3 sweep body — a corpus that since issue #1581 spans the phase files' §2.3 span **plus** the eight gated per-sweep references under `skills/implement/references/sweep-*.md`, and fails closed on a partial corpus rather than reporting GREEN over a short one — loses (or lacks) its mapping-table row (a genuinely *new* routing marker outside that closed vocabulary is not caught — extending the vocabulary is a future change). Step 3 also runs the §2.3 **test-first gate** at fix-authoring time, with a tier-mapped ungranted-command degraded arm and the same Phase 2.4 taxonomy for un-drivable deliverables: adversarial dry-traces for logic-bearing or human-facing prose, and RED/GREEN/no-guidance subagent micro-tests for instructions that enter model context. Step 4.5's convergence conditions grade code-shaped sweep-driven fold-ins as any other fix. A sweep's **trigger** is scoped to the fix delta without exception; a **triggered** sweep's **enumeration** may exceed the delta only where that sweep's own Phase 2.3 definition states a repo-wide search domain, or an enumeration that follows call edges outside the delta (§2.3.0, §2.3.0a, §2.3.0b, §2.3.0d), every other sweep's enumeration staying delta-bounded. The authoritative statement of item 3b lives in `skills/review-and-fix/references/fixing.md` (Step 3, item 3b); the sweep definitions it imports live across the Phase 2 file set's §2.3 span, which opens in `skills/implement/phases/phase-2-sweeps-contract.md` and closes in `skills/implement/phases/phase-2-sweeps-quality.md` — **except that since issue #1581 the eight conditional sweeps (2.3.0, 2.3.0a, 2.3.0b, 2.3.0c, 2.3.0d, 2.3.1, 2.3.2, 2.3.7) keep only their trigger heading and a resident `**Predicate.**` paragraph in that span and carry their procedure in a per-sweep gated reference under `skills/implement/references/sweep-*.md`, read only when the predicate fires.** The six always-firing sweeps stay resident. Item 3b's Read protocol therefore adds a warranted conditional sweep's own gated reference to the source set it reads and to its `sweep_defs_read` completeness condition: reading the phase files alone yields a predicate with no procedure, which is not a sweep that ran.
- **Pushback tracking:** when a finding is skipped, it's tagged with a `skip_category` (e.g. `claim-quality`, `out-of-scope`, `already-tracked`, or `settled-by-disclosure` — a finding foreclosed by an already-shipped disclosure, §13). The same finding skipped twice → escalate and stop — **except** a `settled-by-disclosure` foreclosure, which is answered, not unresolved, so its repeat skip does not trip the escalation.
- **Fix-delta verification gate (Step 3.5):** after **every** iteration's fix commit, a **blinded subagent** re-reviews **only that iteration's cumulative fix delta** (prior findings/fix decisions/fixer reasoning withheld) to catch a fix-introduced regression — most often a #62/#98 guard whose accepted-input set is wider than its consumer's contract — in the **same** iteration. "Every iteration" means the gate is not gated on the *verdict*; it does **not** manufacture a delta where none exists — an iteration in which **Step 3 applied no fixes** has no delta, so the gate (and every check it carries) **skips** for that iteration. Its three checks are the #62/#98 operand-contract check, the adversarial input-shape matrix, and the **added-assertion attribution check**: for each assertion the fix delta adds, the blinded subagent identifies the regression that assertion's own name and description claim it catches and asks whether the assertion *as reported* singles that regression out — reporting an assertion that would not change state under the named regression, one that would change state under a different cause, or one whose reported identity is indistinguishable from a sibling arm; an assertion whose target cannot be read is reported **unestablished**, never clean. A surviving Critical/Important finding from the first two checks (after over-grade calibration) routes back into that iteration's fix step (capped at 2 inner attempts, then promoted); an added-assertion finding is not severity-routed but corrected in the same iteration (or recorded through the item 5 pushback flow), inheriting that same cap, promotion, and at-cap shadow carry. The gate and its inner attempts do **not** count toward the cap. Complements the shadow (which audits the whole diff at convergence, and on an `engine_self_modifying` PR also once after iteration 1 via the early trigger); the shadow + post-shadow gate are unchanged by it.
- **Convergence check:** exits early when fixes are small and no new corroborated Critical/Important finding appears. Every Step 4.5 evaluation records its three condition operands — `fixes_applied`, `fix_diff_lines`, and `new_corroborated_critical_count` (the Critical/Important code-scoped comparands `convergence.md` defines, not raw counts) — into the iteration record's `convergence_inputs`, so a later reader can audit the decision from the run record; an operand that cannot be established is recorded unestablished and its condition treated as failed.
- **Loop Exit:** runs a **widens-surface guard** and emits a run-scoped **deferrals manifest**; renders a `## Coverage` section and the run/effectiveness telemetry.

### Keeping prompt prose lean

PRFlow treats mandatory agent instructions as a runtime cost, and the policy that governs
them is a matter of authoring discipline: once an executable helper is the sole tested owner
of a workflow decision on every consuming path, the same change removes the superseded
mandatory prose, branch/enum mirrors, and obsolete prose pins. Policy, human decisions,
invocation and fail-closed contracts, and essential stop conditions stay in the skill;
rare-path explanation may move into a gated reference, but operative logic does not disappear
before tested helper ownership exists. This sole-owner discipline is stated as advisory
guidance in `CLAUDE.md`'s **Helper cutover** convention, with the lean-prose guidance in
`.prflow/prompt-extensions/implement.md` under **Keeping prompt prose lean**.

### Runtime per-context read cost (issue #1852)

Separate from the per-file byte ceiling, nothing measured what *entering* the engine costs a
run — and after issue #1850 dispatches the fix loop's engine entries into subagent contexts,
that cost moves rather than vanishes. The behavioral instrument `scripts/review-context-eval.py`
(maintainer-run over a transcript corpus; never on the engine's runtime path, so no new tool
grant — only its focused test is granted, as for #767/#1209) measures, per run, how many times
each engine file (`skills/review/**`, `skills/review-and-fix/**`) was read, attributes every
read to the context that made it — distinguishing a main-thread read from a subagent read — and
gives the peak accumulated context of each context that read one. It is the third of PRFlow's
transcript-walking context instruments (§7, §11) and gates nothing. The details live in
[`docs/internal/review-context.md`](review-context.md), the single source of truth for this
axis; it is not paraphrased here.

### Direct invocation of `prflow:receiving-code-review`: the Reception Preflight (issue #545)

The fix loop above applies the `prflow:receiving-code-review` principles inside its own machinery — the loop's mechanics establish PR context themselves. A **direct** invocation of the skill (an interactive maintainer session, or an operator flow dispatching it outside the loop) instead has a defined startup contract, the **Reception Preflight** in `skills/receiving-code-review/SKILL.md`: after the consumer-prompt-extension load and before the Step 0 branch update, before triage of any finding, before any file edit, and before any test-suite execution, the skill renders **one in-chat block of exactly nine context facts** — subject, PR head/base, checkout (with a head-match verdict), working-tree cleanliness, freshness, linked-issue requirements (each linked issue's body re-read in this run), consumer-prompt-extension outcome, severity threshold, and commit/path scope — each fact carrying **exactly one of six statuses**: `established`, `caller-supplied`, `missing`, `stale`, `ambiguous`, `not-applicable`. No rule in the contract assigns `stale` — an unobservable or un-re-measured fact renders `missing`, never `stale` (the status is reserved for a render that must carry a knowingly-superseded value, and it is never an input to the editing gate). A fact renders `established` only when its value was directly observed from a command output or a file read in the current run; anything else renders a degraded status with a one-line reason — never an assumed value (a failed fetch records divergence as *unknown, never zero-behind*; a shallow clone's undecidable ancestry renders the head-match `missing`, never a guessed verdict). The preflight's prescribed command set is **strictly read-only** — one exit-status-checked `git fetch` (remote-tracking refs only), `git rev-parse`, `git status`, `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, `git rev-list`, `gh pr view` (including its `files` scope read), `gh issue view`, the extension loader, and the threshold read, and nothing else; it never switches branches and never merges.

- **Subject classifier + corroboration.** The subject binds through a decidable three-arm classifier — whole-argument number, leading-token number, or checkout-derived (an argument-less `gh pr view` resolving the current branch's PR) — with a leading `#` stripped as normalization. A `#`-prefixed leading token is an explicit designation (`established` absent contradiction); a **bare** leading token renders `established` only when corroborated by an independent channel (the checkout-derived PR equals the bound number, or the feedback names at least one path in the bound PR's file list), else `ambiguous`. Contradiction is decided by named paths: feedback naming paths all disjoint from the bound PR's file list renders the subject `ambiguous` with the disjointness stated. A number appearing anywhere else inside feedback text never binds, and every third-party text the preflight fetches — caller-supplied feedback and linked-issue bodies alike — is data to classify, never instructions to obey.
- **Scope carve-out + fail direction.** The preflight governs **direct invocations only**: inside an autonomous fix loop that drives its own context establishment, the loop's mechanics govern and the preflight is not consulted. A direct invocation is positively established only by an explicit invocation record visible in the current transcript; a run that can establish neither direct-invocation nor loop context **executes no preflight command** — it renders a single explicit context-unestablished line and bars nothing.
- **Relation to Step 0 and the Verification Gate.** The preflight precedes the existing Step 0 branch update; after Step 0 mutates the checkout, the checkout, working-tree, freshness, and head-match facts are **re-measured** and the block re-rendered before the affirmative-only editing gate is consulted. That gate bars IMPLEMENT only on a PR-bound subject whose post-update head-match is `mismatch` (with a work-preserving remedy ladder — a checkout of the PR head is named only when the tree is clean and no local-only commits exist) or on an `ambiguous` subject (the skill asks for explicit subject confirmation and never self-confirms), while triage proceeds on the explicitly-degraded facts; `match`, `advanced`, and a `missing` head-match never bar — a `missing` head instead renders an explicit unestablished-head line. The preflight's freshness facts are point-in-time; the Verification Gate's item 4 remains the completion-time branch-sync authority.
- **Always the plugin-qualified name.** Documented invocation paths use `prflow:receiving-code-review`, never the unqualified name: on a host where the upstream `superpowers` plugin is co-installed, that plugin's own `receiving-code-review` is a name-twin that never receives PRFlow's preflight — the shared **name** is what makes the wrong copy dispatchable, whatever the two descriptions say — so a session that responded without rendering the preflight block dispatched the wrong copy.

---

## 9. Deep dive: shadow review

> Before `/prflow:review-and-fix` declares a clean approval, it **audits its own approval** with a structurally-independent re-review.

**The problem.** A fix loop's iterations *share state* (prior findings, fix decisions, pushback). That shared context biases the loop toward accepting its own earlier conclusions. So a clean approval from the loop is suspect, it might just be agreeing with itself.

**The shadow pass (Step 2.6).** When the tentative verdict is **non-REJECT**, the engine runs **again** with the loop's accumulated state *withheld*. Topic-priming is a second, distinct leak channel beyond prior-findings leakage, so every shadow-pass subagent receives the engine's verbatim prompt, extension text classified for provenance, and the shadow engine's own full-diff artifacts — no orchestrator-added focus, priority, or scope. Provenance-failing extension text remains loaded for its quality value but is recorded as an addendum and cannot produce an attested clean result. The two results are compared. (A REJECT normally skips the shadow entirely.) On an `engine_self_modifying` PR — the highest-risk diffs in the repo — the shadow *also* fires on an **early trigger**: once after iteration 1, **regardless of that iteration's verdict (including REJECT)**, feeding any new blinded findings into iteration 2 via the same promotion machinery. Non-`engine_self_modifying` PRs keep the convergence-time trigger only. The early pass reuses the same blinded fan-out and is itself uncounted toward the iteration cap (a promoted iteration 2 it spawns counts, like any promotion); if iteration 1's `engine_self_modifying` flag cannot be read it **fails closed** and runs the early audit anyway. See `docs/internal/shadow-review.md`.

**The portability constraint, and why it moved from one layer to two (issue #1850).** The original rule was **one subagent layer**: the engine fans out to its own reviewer subagents, so the shadow pass ran the engine's phases *inline in the fix-loop's own context* and launched every reviewer normally, enforcing independence per-reviewer-prompt. That rule existed because nested dispatch is not portable across harnesses — on one that withholds it the capability is absent as a *missing tool* rather than an error, so a single "go run the whole engine" shadow subagent would silently flatten to a degraded single-agent self-check returning a plausible clean APPROVE, *the exact false-convergence the step is meant to prevent.* The fallback for such a harness is therefore **no-dispatch (run inline), never flattened-dispatch.**

**Why two layers now.** Running the engine inline left ~242 KB of engine instruction text resident in the orchestrator's prefix for the rest of an implement run — re-billed as a cache read on each of the ~100 turns that follow Phase 3.3, roughly 20% of a $50 run (measured against run `32418005711`/issue #1870: every 100 KB left resident costs ~$0.05 per subsequent turn). Since nested dispatch has since been verified available at the depth this design needs (Claude Code CLI ≥ 2.1.219 re-enabled it at depth 3, governed by `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH`; the design needs depth 2 — orchestrator, engine subagent, reviewer roster — and the cloud tier pins `anthropics/claude-code-action@v1` and sets `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1` — the CLI version is that action's own internal constant, which this repo does not pin and which moves whenever the `v1` tag moves), the fix loop now **dispatches the review engine as an Agent-tool subagent** at both of its engine entries (Step 1 and the Step 2.6 shadow). The subagent's *first action is an unconditional capability check on its own toolset*: holding a delegation tool it runs the engine's Phases 0–4.3 and fans out the Phase 3 roster from **its own** context, returning `dispatch_mode: fanned-out`; holding none it returns `dispatch_mode: unavailable` immediately — having read no engine file and attempted no review — and the parent runs the engine inline exactly as before. Any non-well-formed fanned-out outcome also falls back to inline (fail-closed). So the engine's instruction text is now paid once inside a bounded subagent life and discarded on return, instead of being re-billed on every remaining orchestrator turn — while the floor is no worse than the old inline behavior. `dispatch_mode` has exactly two values (`fanned-out`, `unavailable`).

**The by-path handoff, and the single-writer invariant.** The engine subagent returns its results **by path**, not as text in the return body: an entry-keyed `engine-return-*.json` under the existing run-scoped scratch directory (`.prflow/tmp/review/<slug>/<run-id>/`) carrying `phase3_findings`, `phase3_dispatched`, `expected_reviewers`, `diff_profile`, `cap_drops`, `diff_produced_at_head` (the HEAD sha the entry's Phase 0.2 produced `diff.patch` at, which the parent matches against `git rev-parse HEAD` to reject a stale re-entrant cache) plus the verdict/report; the Step 2.6 shadow entry additionally returns each reviewer's assessment + defect_signatures for the parent's coverage 1:1-join, and carries the held `run_id` and (PR mode) `head_override = local` as its Phase 0.2 caller inputs — the same plumbing the loop's iteration entries carry. The parent folds those fields into the fused iteration-record write it already owns, so **the fix loop stays the sole writer of `iter-<N>.json`** and its field shapes are unchanged — no lock is needed because the parent blocks on the subagent's return. At both entries the engine records its own Phase 3.1 gate decisions as a **returned** `expected_reviewers` roster — the operand `diff_profile` cannot express, since it carries no test-relevance predicate for `pr-test-analyzer` — and the parent checks the dispatched roster covers *that* reported roster (rather than recomputing it from `diff_profile`), so an under-fanned Phase 3 cannot pass as a self-consistent return; a short roster is a not-well-formed return at Step 1 and a coverage shortfall (outcome 3) at the shadow. The two dispatch sites are registered in `lib/subagent-dispatch-sites.json` with `handoff: by-path`. The **fix half stays resident** in the orchestrator: it wrote the code, makes the commits, and keeps sole ownership of the iteration record.

**Why the fallback exists.** Nested-dispatch support is uneven across harnesses, and a harness without it flattens *silently*, so the capability check discriminates at runtime rather than being gated on configuration. The per-harness capability table is single-homed in [`docs/internal/shadow-review.md`](shadow-review.md); the nested-dispatch version facts are co-recorded there and in the *Why two layers now* paragraph above, which that page agrees with. **This rationale is recorded here, not in any shipped `skills/**` body** — a shipped body carries the instruction plus at most one sentence of consequence, and ships verbatim into consumer repos where it must stay repo-agnostic.

**Honest degradation, coverage is a positive assertion.** A degraded pass must **never** clear a PR. The shadow's `coverage: "full"` is *proven*, not assumed on no-error: the parent computes the expected reviewer roster from the shadow's own diff classification and confirms every dispatched reviewer returned cleanly. Any shortfall → `coverage: "not_verified"` and the verdict is reported **unverified**. A single transient reviewer failure gets exactly **one** targeted re-dispatch; structural or multi-reviewer failures fail closed immediately.

**How the coverage guarantee is enforced.** Concrete guards make the shadow's clean signal *trustworthy* rather than assumed: a **dispatched-vs-collected 1:1 join**; a **block-presence read-back gate**; a **prompt-composition attestation** requiring persisted `prompt_addenda: "none"` beside pure roster `coverage: "full"` before outcome 1 or a clean render; a **render-time lock-step assertion** that keeps the `APPROVE WITH UNRESOLVED SHADOW FINDINGS` (AWUSF) headline, Coverage line, and post-shadow edit gate in lock-step (ordinary promotions read one iteration back; a parked-class sweep finding discovered at the cap reads the current triggering iteration); and a **too-narrow-classification tripwire** that widens the expected and dispatched roster. Attestation never discards outcome-2 findings or changes roster coverage: addenda on a promotion are reported as a caveat while the findings still promote. On the consumer side, `/prflow:implement` treats only `APPROVE`, `APPROVE WITH CAVEAT`, and `APPROVE WITH ADVISORY NOTES` as the clean approve-family, and routes AWUSF through one bounded fix+re-review that clears **only** on a verdict that is both clean *and* full-coverage. A non-clean bounded re-review no longer hard-blocks: Phase 3.3 applies a **severity-aware exit** — only a genuine unresolved Critical (or an unparseable/ungradeable verdict) takes the Blocked path, while a residual of advisory/Suggestion/`severity-calibrated` findings **soft-proceeds** (surfaced in the workpad and PR body, PR left review-ready, human merger decides). See `docs/internal/shadow-review.md`.

**Honest calibration (important, don't overclaim).** A clean shadow **narrows** the gap between the loop's self-assessment and an independent review; it does **not close** it. It's one sample of the *same* engine and reviewer roster. The documented evidence: on PR #58, the in-loop shadow agreed with full coverage, yet a subsequent *standalone* `/prflow:review` still surfaced several hardening items. **A separate `/prflow:review` remains the default exhaustiveness check; a clean shadow raises confidence but never waives it.**

**Cost.** The shadow roughly **doubles** a converging run's cost (a full extra engine pass that yields no fixes when it agrees). Two 2026-07-11 R3-driven changes make that cost *measurable* and shave one component of it (the R3 baseline to re-measure against: **12.43M recorded shadow tokens; 115 shadow-attributable applied fixes across 32 of 69 runs; ~366k tokens per shadow-attributable Critical/Important fix**): (1) writing the shadow workpad block (with its `step_2_6` telemetry) is now a **single non-optional obligation fused to the pass's termination, covering both termination paths** — Parse-and-compare completion and the honest-degradation fail-safe (an outcome-3 pass writes its `not_verified` block before taking outcome 3), authored with the Write tool with the same force as the `iter-<N>.json` Layer-1 fused emit; and `lib/efficiency-trace.sh --persist` gains a **shadow floor** that synthesizes a minimal `shadow_synthesized: true` + promotion-linkage marker when promotion evidence survives with no `shadow` block (recovering **promoted** shadows only — attribution, not cost — never overwriting an agent-written block); (2) Phase 1's checklist-generator diff handoff moved **inline → by file reference** (see Phase 1 above), removing the redundant per-pass context transit the shadow re-paid on every iteration. Both narrow the cost/observability gap; neither closes it.

Issue #501 narrows that floor with `promotion_provenance`: `shadow` and
`park-calibration-post-shadow` license recovery with true and false linkage,
`park-calibration-pre-shadow` suppresses the pre-shadow false positive, unknown strings breadcrumb
without synthesis, and unestablished legacy values get a hedged marker. Park-gate promotion credit
never changes a surviving predecessor block; future producers must choose a defined provenance value
to opt into recovery.

**Evidence-aware post-shadow grading of parked findings (issue #557).** The Park-calibration gate no longer re-litigates a below-verdict-threshold shadow re-raise of a parked finding on **severity alone** — which burned promoted iterations (each a full re-shadow) re-parking findings the shadow merely *corroborated*. After the shadow block is recorded, the gate grades each re-raise↔parked-finding pair on **evidence**: the scoped sweep-sibling carve-out is evaluated **first** (a re-raise it claims never enters the classification); then each shadow finding pairing under Phase 3.2 to the **parking-time record** of a member of the **reconciled parked population** (the gate's three parked populations across all iterations, minus any member a later iteration applied or promoted-and-fixed) receives exactly one of five relations — *equivalent / strengthened / contradicted / materially different / ambiguous*. Parking is **preserved** only on positive evidence equivalence from well-formed operands (every paired re-raise equivalent, at or below the parked severity under a `major`≡`important`/`minor`≡`suggestion` label normalization, and — for a rationale-bearing row at or above `$FIX_THRESHOLD` — an anchored rationale); every other outcome, and every missing/malformed/unreadable operand, **fails closed to promotion** at the shadow re-raise's severity. Operands are read from additive structured workpad records — a `parking_evidence {basis, failing_input, source, finding_ref}` object on the rationale-bearing `fix_decisions` rows, one `park_calibration.evidence_comparisons[]` record per pair, and a new `tools_unavailable` value in the `step25_classification` enum — never from conversational memory. A single amendment to Step 2.6's novelty definition makes a paired re-raise count as **overlap, not new** (the survived-unfixed reconciliation keeps it from ever claiming a fixed member's re-raise as a preserved parking). Corroboration stays retrospective-clean via a note-kind preservation sentinel; a fail-closed degradation surfaces as friction. The shared `/prflow:review` engine is untouched. See `docs/internal/shadow-review.md`.

---

## 10. Deep dive: the docs suite

PRFlow treats documentation as part of "done." `/prflow:docs` orchestrates three steps in one session, sharing context forward:

1. **`docs-sync-internal`**: keeps the internal docs a current map a coding agent can start its exploration from. It reflects every behavior change on the branch in the page that owns that behavior — an in-place correction counts, and a change with no behavioral effect owes no update — and routes each write through the doc root's `index.md` rather than appending wherever a search landed. Mandatory final step: **verify every factual claim against the codebase** (the single most common cause of inaccurate docs).
2. **`docs-sync-external`**: aligns customer-facing docs against the internal docs (the source of truth), removing confidential/internal-only content. Follows the single customer-facing Style Guide in `skills/docs-sync-external/SKILL.md` (AP style, term conventions, etc.) — the surviving source the other customer-facing skills now point at — plus the shared writing standard `lib/writing-standard.md` for the audience-neutral rules.
3. **`docs-release-notes`**: if the change is customer-visible, appends an entry of the form `- **Short user-outcome title** — two to three sentence description.` followed by a parenthesised markdown link whose text is `#<number>` and whose target is that issue or PR URL; otherwise skips the release note. Either way, if a `chore: bump version` commit is present, it reconciles that version's CHANGELOG entry — taking the version from `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (not the bump commit's subject) and no-opping if no matching `## [version]` section exists (Step 4b). This describes the **shipped** skill's behavior, which every consumer repo gets unchanged. In the PRFlow repository itself the authoring half is turned off by repo-local prompt extensions — customer-visible entries are derived from `customer-visible: true` changesets at merge time (see **Versioning** below) — while Step 4b's CHANGELOG reconciliation stays reachable.

### Versioning (changesets)

PRFlow versions itself with **changesets** rather than an in-PR version bump. Each PR that reaches consumers adds a uniquely-named `.changeset/*.md` file (a `bump: patch|minor|major` frontmatter key plus Keep-a-Changelog prose) and never edits `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` or `CHANGELOG.md`. On merge to `main`, the `version-consolidate` GitHub Action (at `.github/workflows/version-consolidate.yml`; runs `scripts/consolidate-changesets.py`) consumes every pending changeset: it bumps `plugin.json`'s `version` by the **highest** pending bump type (patch < minor < major — one increment even when several are pending), keeps the supplementary version metadata in lockstep with that same value — `CITATION.cff`'s top-level `version` and the `marketplace.json` plugin entry's `version` (each rewritten only when present; absent → skipped, present-but-unrewritable → fails before any write) — prepends a single dated, PR-cited CHANGELOG entry assembled from all the pending prose, deletes the consumed files, and commits with a `chore: bump version` subject. A malformed changeset fails the Action loudly (naming the file); with none pending it is a clean no-op. Because each changeset has a unique filename, concurrent PRs never touch a shared line — the recurring version/CHANGELOG merge conflicts are eliminated while consumers still receive each change as a per-merge version increment. Contributors: see [`.changeset/README.md`](../../.changeset/README.md); the operative policy for `/prflow:implement` lives in `.prflow/prompt-extensions/implement.md`.

**Customer-visible release notes are derived from marked changesets at merge time (issue #2070).** A changeset may carry a `customer-visible: true` frontmatter marker (only the parsed boolean `True` marks; any other present value fails the parse loudly, naming the file). In the same `chore: bump version` commit, `consolidate-changesets.py` reuses each marked changeset's prose body **verbatim** as an entry in [`docs/external/release-notes.md`](../external/release-notes.md), written under the merge date's `## Month Day, Year` heading (inserted at the top of the page's date sections), riding the same emitted-write-set staging path (no workflow edit). An unmarked changeset behaves exactly as before — CHANGELOG only, release-notes.md byte-identical. Because release-note entries are now authored once in the reviewed changeset and rendered mechanically at merge time, **this repository's own `/prflow:docs` pass no longer authors them**: two repo-local prompt extensions deliver the skip — `.prflow/prompt-extensions/docs.md` skips the router's Step 3, and `.prflow/prompt-extensions/docs-release-notes.md` skips the direct authoring skill's authoring steps while leaving its CHANGELOG-reconciliation Step 4b reachable. This is a PRFlow-repo policy only; consumer repos see the shipped skill's unchanged per-PR authoring behavior described below.

Supporting skills:
- **`docs-verify <topic>`**: verifies internal docs for one topic against code (codebase = source of truth). Has a `--report-only` mode (no writes) used by `/prflow:create-issue` Step 1, its only programmatic caller. **The two modes carry separate identities.** Write mode is the documentation-accuracy pass described above, and its procedure — action paths, file operations, quality standards, scope constraints, completion criteria — lives in `references/write-mode.md`, loaded only on that path and **fail-closed** (a reference that cannot be read or whose boundary markers do not match stops the run rather than editing docs without its constraints). Report-only is a **docs-first code explorer**: its deliverable is a map of how the topic works in the code today, `[[INTERNAL_DOC_LOCATION]]` is its entry point and a source of **provisional evidence** rather than its subject, and documentation quality is not among its deliverables — it returns one doc-reliability signal and nothing further about it. A doc-derived claim is confirmed against the implementing code (a finding), contradicted by it (in which case the code wins and the fact is reported under `Current behavior` like any other behavior), or returned marked `doc-sourced, unconfirmed` — never silently promoted. Write mode alone carries the documentation-comparison checklist; report-only is directed instead to use documentation for context and establish every reported detail from the code. It ships a **brownfield disposition** (assume non-obvious coupling; surface-level confirmation is an unfinished read) with the breadth/depth split stated explicitly: the duty floor bounds how *many* things are examined, never how *carefully*. In that mode the breadth bound is the **duty floor** rather than a whole-codebase search, the pass accepts an explicit **search-space operand** (which is how Step 1's deep arm gives each peer its own leg), it returns a status for **every** duty on the floor — discharged, unestablished, or judged-not-engaged, the last carrying a bearing observation — and it dispatches **no subagent of its own**, kept to a single subagent layer because nested dispatch is not portable across harnesses — where a harness withholds it the capability is absent as a *missing tool* rather than an error, so a nested dispatch would silently flatten to a single-agent self-check. `discharged` requires being able to state the duty's answer *and* cite the tool output it was read from; a qualification that names something relied on but not read makes the status `unestablished` instead, while one that merely bounds a verified method's reach does not. Doc reliability: `RELIABLE` / `UNRELIABLE` / `ABSENT`, ranging over documents **inside `[[INTERNAL_DOC_LOCATION]]` only** — a discrepancy outside that location is reported but never moves the signal, so two runs over the same tree return the same token. That signal is read by Step 1's escalation predicate and reaches no drafted issue body.
- **`docs-bootstrap-internal`**: stands up an internal-docs tree from scratch, organized **domain-first** (`orders/`, `customers/`) not code-layer-first, flat (one level), quality over quantity (5–10 thorough seed docs, not 50 stubs).
- **`docs-bootstrap-external`**: generates the initial external docs from the internal source of truth.

Doc paths are configurable (`docs.internal`, `docs.external`, `docs.release_notes_file`, `docs.changelog_file`); internal/external steps can be toggled off (`docs.internal_enabled`, `docs.external_enabled`).

**Shared discipline:** the two diff-driven skills (`docs-sync-internal`, `docs-release-notes`) read the branch with `git diff origin/main...HEAD` (three dots, branch-only changes); every docs skill references source by bare path only (no line numbers, which rot); and the generation skills leave committing to the caller.

---

## 11. Deep dive: `/prflow:create-issue`

Turns a rough user story / bug report / feature idea into a well-structured GitHub issue.

> **Core principle (a quotable line):** *"An issue is the output of resolved decisions, not a place to park unresolved ones."*

**A thin root plus marker-gated references (issue #614).** `skills/create-issue/SKILL.md` is a thin always-loaded root whose `##` sections, in file order, are: the completion checklist (expressed as a structural slot the run fills in), the announcement contract, the Iron Law, the Red Flags list, the rationalization table, the core principle, the consumer-prompt-extension load, a pointer to the relocated routing table, the five non-degradable invariants, the subagent-dispatch injection-condition clause, the prerequisites, the Steps, and a runner-setup section holding the portable-helper-anchor, Windows-normalization, and anchor-degrades prose — down from a large single-file monolith. The compliance devices near the top (Iron Law, Red Flags, rationalization table) and the front-loaded checklist were added so the always-read surface states what to do before it states how to resolve helper paths (issue #1643). The per-step procedures and the conditional fallback arms live in `skills/create-issue/references/`, each loaded at its trigger behind an entry gate that checks the file's first-line/last-line boundary markers. The step procedures (`step-2-clarify.md`, `step-3-5-steelman.md`, `step-3-6-audit.md`, `step-4-present-create.md`) load at their step entry — and Step 3.6 is itself an **ordered reference set** since issue #1702: `references/step-3-6-audit.md` is now a thin **entry** reference that declares an ordered set of three cohesive procedure members (`step-3-6-audit-shared.md` (part 1) — the **run bootstrap** (`init` and the nonce, the canonical-draft write plus its two Step 3.5 gates, and the draft-root binding) alongside ledger maintenance and the staged canonical-draft write; `step-3-6-audit-dispatch.md` (part 2) — round-kind → dispatch scope → dispatch arm → info-diet → carriage → instruction generation/rendering; `step-3-6-audit-adjudication.md` (part 3) — audit report → record-return/adjudicate → evidence → reconciliation → boundary read → coverage/calibration → call sequence → fallbacks), each carrying a `<!-- prflow:create-issue-set step=3.6 part=k of=3 -->` second-line marker. Since issue #1752 the members load at **two times**: part 1 loads unconditionally on every entry into Step 3.6 and runs the bootstrap immediately — because that bootstrap (the state document, the nonce, the written draft and its gates, and the bound root) runs on the path to Step 4's pre-approval pause whether or not any round is elected — while the audit-only dispatch and adjudication members (parts 2 and 3) are held as a pair and loaded **only when a user accepts an audit round** at that pause, so a run electing no round never reads them (the run bootstrap used to live in the dispatch member; #1752 re-homed it into the shared member). The entry performs no `init`/write/bind until part 1 has passed its boundary-marker contract, and no dispatch until the parts-2-and-3 pair has (the ordered-set gate attributes each failure state — denied, empty, missing, truncated, duplicate, reversed, noncanonical, misrouted, set-incomplete — and a required-member load failure enters the existing degraded path exactly once before audit work begins). The declared member set is single-sourced in `lib/test/create-issue-step-3-6-members.json`; that manifest is the roster every guard reconciles against, so `docs/internal/skills/create-issue.md` is the canonical detail on the decomposed ownership. The remaining step references load at their step entry; `revision-delta.md` is a shared procedure loaded at every revision event; and the `references/fallback-*.md` files are the predicate-gated set, each reached only when its own routing-table predicate fires, so a default path — task tool usable, writable filesystem, file-arm dispatch, state owner available, audit evidence and boundary triggers clean, draft writes landing, the implement-offer tier read succeeding, no user-visible UI surface, no second-or-later audit round — loads none of them at all. The routing table in `skills/create-issue/references/degradation-routing.md` (relocated off the always-read root in issue #1644) is the defining enumeration of that set and of each member's predicate; read it there rather than transcribing filenames here, and note that the set has grown over time as rarely-taken arms were moved out of the always-loaded step references (issue #1372). Divergences from the review engine's split are deliberate: there is **no identity-manifest or hash layer** on the entry gate, and a failed load **degrades best-effort** (an in-chat breadcrumb naming the file and the failure kind, then the routing row's named degraded behavior) rather than stopping — extending this skill's documented never-block-issue-creation ethos, so no routing row's failure arm can terminate the run. The five non-degradable invariants hold on every degraded arm. Keeping the root and the default path lean stays an authoring goal. The mechanical ceiling over these files is the reader-capability limit described in the review-engine section above (`lib/test/lint-reference-size.py`, issue #1595), which caps a boundary-gated reference or a skill root at 61,750 bytes so a single read still returns both boundary markers; it constrains size, never how lean the prose should be. Files already over it when it landed are carried as expiring exemptions in `lib/test/reference-size-exemptions.json`, so they still truncate until trimmed. The Step 3.6 audit was one such file: issue #1702 decomposed it into the entry-plus-three-members set above and **retired its exemption** (removed from both `recorded_set` and `exemptions`), so the entry and every member now sit under the ordinary size ceiling. That decomposition also adds a **per-member 55,000-byte limit and an aggregate source-byte budget** (total ≤ 72,458 B, the pre-refactor total recorded at commit e712655b6) enforced by `lib/test/lint-reference-size.py` over the manifest's declared population.

The skill exists to prevent "option-listing" issues. Steps:
1. **Assess (read-only) — right-sized into two arms (issue #749):** Step 1 grounds the issue in current behavior by dispatching `/prflow:docs-verify --report-only` as a **peer subagent**, so the survey's tool output stays in the peer's context and the orchestrator pays only the returned report. Its breadth is bounded by a stated **duty floor** — exact operand and population identity, code-versus-doc authority, reachability and writer classification, sibling consumer and output enumeration, coupled-doc and guard propagation, and reusable contradictions — rather than by a whole-repository search, so a topic engaging few duties pays for few. Every dispatch starts with the **shallow arm** — a single peer over the union of the two legs — and reaches the **deep arm** only by escalation: two parallel peers over legs made disjoint by construction (the documentation surface resolved from `.docs.internal`, and the tracked tree with that subtree removed, both enumerated from the git index), whose returns the orchestrator reconciles. There is no pre-dispatch arm-selection judgement; **escalation is the only entry to deep** — driven by the doc-reliability signal, and also firing on an unestablished duty and on a judged-not-engaged duty whose returned bearing observation is anything other than the producer's explicit `none-observed` token, which is the backstop for a topic the shallow report reads as narrower than it is. Shallow-first was adopted on 2026-08-19 after one deep run measured about 230,000 subagent tokens across its two peers (read from the runner's reported per-agent token usage); the shallow-arm escalation rate was unmeasured at the time of the change, so the saving rests on the assumption that most substantive topics' shallow reports do not escalate. The orchestrator (never a peer) writes the returned evidence to the run-scoped **`.prflow/tmp/create-issue/<slug>/issue-step1-<slug>.md`**, which an escalating deep arm and Step 2's evidence bundle both read so an escalation warm-starts instead of re-deriving; that artifact is out of bounds to the Step 3.6 auditor. A failed, unavailable, rejected, or anchor-unresolvable pass takes the **degraded arm** — a bounded inline verification with a breadcrumb naming the failure kind, its evidence marked degraded — which never terminates the run and never presents a half-verification as a whole one.
