Step 1 is also where the run's kebab-case **slug** is bound — before anything is dispatched, and no later step binds one — so a single stem spans the Step 1 evidence artifact, the Step 2 derivation artifact, and the Step 4 draft file; Step 1 records it in the fixed, slug-independent pointer `.prflow/tmp/create-issue/issue-run-slug` that later sites read when they no longer hold it (a pointer that is absent, unreadable, empty, whitespace-only, or not a single kebab-case slug on one line is recorded unestablished and routes to Step 4's retained title-derived fallback). Both `.prflow/tmp/` paths are deleted **on entry**, on every path including the degraded one, so a leftover from a prior run on the same deterministic slug cannot be read as this run's; both are anchored to the working directory rather than the main-root the draft file uses, and in a read-only sandbox the write degrades to a visible inline-in-chat block with the on-disk copies distrusted.
2. **Clarify until Definition of Ready:** an unconditional **independent-derivation pass runs first on every run** — the orchestrator re-derives the full Definition of Ready (problem/beneficiary, behavioral forks, edge/error cases, acceptance criteria) from the problem and the Step 1 findings *before* weighing the user's supplied criteria, then drives clarification from the delta plus conflicts; user-supplied acceptance criteria are challenged on completeness *and* correctness, so a comprehensive-looking story gets the same scrutiny a terse one does. The pass writes its derived list to an **observable, gitignored artifact** (`.prflow/tmp/create-issue/<slug>/issue-derivation-<slug>.md`, deleting any same-slug leftover first so the file can only be *this* run's) before any clarification round; a **gate** — anchored before the first clarification question and, unconditionally, before Step 3 drafting, with a per-round re-check mirroring it — confirms that artifact is present *and* holds this run's derivation (freshness, not mere presence), so a skipped pass is surfaced rather than silently waved through. In a read-only sandbox the write falls back to a visible inline-in-chat block (and on-disk files are distrusted as possible stale leftovers). The guarantee stays an **ordering-based self-check with an observable artifact, not subagent isolation**. The Definition of Ready itself (the independent-derivation pass above being its first item): problem + beneficiary, single coherent scope, one decided behavior per fork, **solution-space expansion before convergence** (independently generate the mechanism space, rate each candidate on both the guarantee it enforces and the long-term cost it leaves behind, and surface both a categorically stronger guarantee class than the user proposed and the simplest mechanism that reliably solves the problem), one implementation approach, concrete testable acceptance criteria, known prerequisites (cross-issue ordering), a **visual specification** for user-visible UI changes (skipped entirely otherwise), and — for a story the agent reads as reporting a defect — the **reproduction facts** the issue template's `Current Behavior` requires (triggering steps or input, observed result, expected result, and environment; skipped entirely for non-defect stories). Both the visual-specification and reproduction-facts rows add a question only when they apply, and an unanswered reproduction fact reaches `## 🚫 Blocked` through the existing unanswered-readiness-item path rather than a new one. Uses the runner's user-question tool (`AskUserQuestion` on Claude Code, the canonical example; `ask_user` on runners like GitHub Copilot CLI), with batching conditional on the tool and clarification capped at a runner-neutral total-clarifying-question budget.
3. **Draft + no-options gate:** outside an explicit `## 🚫 Blocked` section, no unresolved-decision language — the canonical no-options vocabulary and its category structure are owned by `skills/create-issue/references/issue-template.md` and are not restated here. Unresolvable decisions go into exactly one Blocked section, never invented defaults.
4. **Self-steelman (Step 3.5, mandatory — issue #304):** before the user sees the draft, the skill stress-tests it against the *actual code* — fresh targeted reads/greps, never the ambient context the draft came from. It verifies every load-bearing claim, file reference, and acceptance criterion; runs a **universal-quantifier sweep** (every "never/always/each/every/all/cannot" grounded — pinned per-arm, scoped, or removed — with a planted-defect positive-control obligation on detector-coverage claims); runs a **request-origin sweep** (every acceptance criterion and named Testing-Strategy assertion whose asserted guarantee the request did not name and which no failure the change introduces requires is reported for revision under the step's existing revise-and-re-gate loop — a criterion resting on a change-introduced failure is not reported, and the sweep refuses no draft); grounds every **occurrence count and coupled-site list** by an executed whitespace-normalized search or the cited evidence records, never recall; reads a "code does X" premise with its **enclosing gates/conditionals and their defaults**; hunts for missed ACs, missed edge cases, wrong assumptions, unstated scope, and an unreconciled multi-state contract (a within-text check that no summary or table form lists fewer causes for a state than the per-state ACs specify); it also flags an **AC mutual-consistency** conflict (no AC forbids a surface another AC's discharge must touch) and a **trust-boundary closure** gap (a protected executable-artifact set defined over the transitive source/exec/import closure of its entry points); binds every **stated-but-unbound input** (a mechanism input named only by role) to a named referent with a cited code reference or an implementer-obligation AC; revises the draft itself and re-runs the no-options gate on the revision; then — at every revision event, per the shared **Revision-delta verification** procedure (stated once, referenced by every revise-and-re-gate site) — walks the revision's edit-batch delta across six classes (mechanisms, lifecycle rules, execution-tier assumptions, dependencies, universal guarantees, and a total-making residual class) and verifies each non-empty class against the code, so audit rounds and the declined-re-audit filing path see delta that has been walked and verified rather than only language-gated; and reports a one-line outcome summary either way, including the universal-quantifier sweep's zero arm (a silent pass is indistinguishable from a skipped step) — **and persists that summary as a numbered `### pass <n>` entry to a `## Steelman record` section of the run's `.prflow/tmp/create-issue/<slug>/issue-derivation-<slug>.md` derivation artifact before the step returns, so the step leaves a durable trace a maintainer can read rather than only chat text** (append-only across passes, one line per summary field, riding inside the already-out-of-bounds derivation artifact beside its `## Evidence bundle`). A genuinely new decision fork routes through the existing Step 2 question machinery, not a new path.
5. **Fresh-context audit (Step 3.6, mandatory — issue #443, extended by #522):** after Step 3.5 passes (which now also **self-checks the draft against the same audit dimension checklist** before any dispatch), and on any round the user elects at the Step 4 pause, the skill — **first running a Step 3.6 entry confirmation that reads a bounded tail of the derivation artifact to verify this run's latest `## Steelman record` `### pass <n>` entry is present and fresh, **reporting the confirmed/missing/stale outcome as an in-chat breadcrumb before it routes** (issue #1695) so a skipped Step 3.5 is transcript-distinguishable from a compliant one, and stopping to run Step 3.5 when it is missing or stale (the remedy runs at most once per entry, blocks only the audit dispatch, and never blocks issue creation)** — dispatches **one synchronous audit subagent** whose entire value is that it did **not** draft the issue — the mechanism is *information removal* (separated-context critics outperform same-session self-review on critical errors). On the normal **file arm**, the orchestrator writes the current rendered draft to the canonical `issue-draft-<slug>.md` before each round and the auditor **reads that file as the sole draft source** (closing the condensation-drift channel a hand-embedded copy opened) with a carriage/identity check; the drafting conversation, Step 1 findings, and the *reasoning* artifacts stay out of bounds while the draft file itself is the artifact under audit. When the write fails (read-only sandbox), a fallback **embed arm** carries the full body verbatim with its own sentinel carriage check, and the on-disk draft path stays out of bounds. The auditor runs an **adversarial pre-mortem** audit-prompt: a per-finding bar, scope exclusions at issue altitude, one assessed "Quiet Killer" slot, no cap on the number of findings paired with a per-finding length discipline, and a mandatory verdict line with three legal values — `VERDICT: FILE`, `VERDICT: REVISE`, or `VERDICT: DRAFT-UNREADABLE` (file arm only). Since **issue #1751 every fresh-context audit round is offered to the user before it opens**, at the Step 4 pre-approval pause after the rendered draft is on screen: a satisfied user elects none, so the **default run cost is zero audit rounds** and the run files unaudited on the user's explicit approval. Re-audit is a **user election at the Step 4 pause**, never an automatic loop after a `REVISE` verdict. On `REVISE` the orchestrator verifies each finding against the code, revises, re-runs the no-options gate, runs the shared **Revision-delta verification** procedure over the revision's delta (the same procedure the Step 3.5 and Step 4 revise loops reference), then offers — never spends automatically — one more round. The skill **offers user-chosen rounds** (up to 3, `_funded_rounds` funding only rounds a recorded `record-offer --accepted` election paid for) via the question tool — the user, not the skill, spends the tokens. A single run can therefore open at most **four** discovery-class rounds — three elected plus one confirming — against the six reachable before this change; a zero-round `user-decline` grounds eligibility, binds a decline-bound creation epoch, and emits the body, so a run that elects nothing is an ordinary run rather than an impossible one. Every accepted round's findings are **adjudicated** into must-revise / advisory / invalid; since issue #743 the advisory and invalid grades are no longer bare counts but carry a **durable per-finding record** — a one-line summary and rationale, an impact-class tag, and the auditor's returned finding block byte-preserved — recorded through the state owner (`record-adjudication --advisory-records-file/--invalid-records-file`, refused when a count and its records disagree), read back with `query-adjudication-records`, and rendered to the user **before** the approval election. A **calibration** layer (`query-calibration`, a `calibration=` sibling of the coverage boundary offer) surfaces an advisory grade on an impact-bearing finding (`implementation-correctness`/`scope`/`safety`/`verifiability`) that carries no recorded evidence, so an under-evidenced grade is named to the maintainer rather than silently converged past — disclosure only, **never a filing block**. Full evidence record: [`docs/internal/advisory-adjudication-calibration.md`](advisory-adjudication-calibration.md).

**The no-options gate carves out a fixed set of surfaces (issue #1112).** The gate that bars choice/hedge/deferral language outside `## 🚫 Blocked` exempts a fixed carve-out set — the canonical enumeration of those surfaces and their conditions is owned by `skills/create-issue/references/issue-template.md` and is not restated or counted here. The carve-out set reaches **Stage-B-authored** issues through that same `references/issue-template.md`, which Stage B follows verbatim while running none of create-issue's Step 3.5/3.6 seams, and Stage B's own Implementation Notes skeleton carries the matching floor-declared `Relevant files` placeholder so the template and the skeleton agree.

**The lifecycle itself is owned by a tested state-owner CLI, not by prose (issue #546).** Every deterministic rule above — transition legality, round numbering, the automatic budget and the bounded retries, arm routing and its three embed markers, digest computation and comparison, sentinel generation and comparison, the offer triggers, override records, presentation eligibility, and the audit-summary field set — is executed by `scripts/issue-audit-state.py`; the skill records lifecycle events through it and **obeys its answers** rather than re-deriving them each turn. Its two-class contract is what the prose branches on: queries always exit 0 once the arguments parse (an argparse usage error still exits 2) with a decided answer line, except for the multi-line read-back queries `query-findings`, issue #704's `query-finding-evidence`, `query-coverage`, and `query-adjudication-records`, which print one decided line per record, and issue #795's composite `query-boundary`, which prints one decided line per boundary component; all of them are strictly read-only, while mutations exit non-zero with a named breadcrumb on an illegal transition or an unpersistable state. Since issue #795 most subcommands print a `next_call=` line naming the next legal invocation, with every state-derivable operand filled and every caller-supplied one bare in a `needs=` field; it is a generated suggestion the caller reviews before running, never an instruction. Since issue #1803 that stdout is a **three-part output contract**: the decided answer line stays first, a **summary-block** line follows it — a compact fixed subset of the fields `query-summary` reports (the subset enumerated in the tool's own `--help`), so a caller reads the state it needs from the call it just made rather than issuing a standalone `query-*` read-back — and the `next_call=` line becomes the final line. That enrichment let the audit references drop the clean path's standalone `query-summary` read (its answer now comes from the preceding call's summary-block line), lowering the per-run mandated `unconditional_call_count` from 13 to 12 (derived live by `lib/test/check-audit-lifecycle-contracts.py`); `record-finding-evidence` also gained a batched `--finding-evidence-records-file` form recording a whole round's finding evidence from one JSON file, each entry keeping its own completeness verdict. The same issue lets the five subcommands whose round the state uniquely determines resolve an omitted `--round` from state, while every subcommand where the flag selects an operation or names a caller-chosen round keeps it required. Run state persists to a cwd/worktree-anchored `.prflow/tmp/create-issue/<slug>/issue-audit-state-<slug>.json`, replacing the markdown event log the offer used to read — the skill still writes the observable audit artifact (`.prflow/tmp/create-issue/<slug>/issue-audit-<slug>.md`, same gate/read-only-stand-in convention as the derivation artifact), and both state paths plus the **retired** `.md` leftover stay declared out of bounds so an auditor with repository read access cannot re-anchor on this run's prior verdicts. **Writes to that state document are serialized (issue #1040).** Every mutating subcommand runs inside an exclusive-create sentinel critical section — a `.lock` file beside the state document, created with `os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL` (standard-library only) — so two concurrent invocations for the same slug produce a document reflecting one of them entirely and then the other, never an interleaved mixture; each writer also persists through its own unique `tempfile.mkstemp` temporary path (with a bounded `os.replace` retry over `PermissionError`) so two writers never share and truncate one temp file. **Read-only subcommands acquire no sentinel and stay unserialized** (`query-*`, `emit-body`), and a fail-closed transitive call-graph check proves `save_state` is unreachable from every read-only-classified subcommand. An **abandoned sentinel is recovered by age**, and the threshold that decides it is `stale_after_s` — *not* the longer `acquire_window_s` a contending writer is willing to wait: a sentinel whose mtime age exceeds `stale_after_s` is unlinked, and the exclusive create is then re-attempted exactly once before control returns to the ordinary acquire loop. Because the shipped `stale_after_s` is strictly shorter than the shipped `acquire_window_s`, a writer contending with an abandoned sentinel always reaches that break inside its own acquire window, which is what makes a crashed writer unable to wedge the slug permanently; the acquire-window expiry is the fail-closed arm for a host whose overrides invert that relation, and it refuses the mutation rather than proceeding. That relation is the load-bearing part — an override that sets `stale_after_s` above `acquire_window_s` trades permanent-wedge immunity for the refusal. **A broken holder releases nothing**, and the check that guarantees it compares an **owner nonce written into the sentinel**, not the sentinel's `(st_dev, st_ino)`: a writer unlinks on release only a sentinel whose recorded owner is still the nonce it wrote at acquire, so a holder whose sentinel was age-broken breadcrumbs instead of stripping the new holder's exclusion. The identity form of that check was unsound and is not what ships — the breaker unlinks the old inode and creates its own file at the same path, and an inode-reusing filesystem may hand it the very `(st_dev, st_ino)` the earlier holder recorded, so an identity comparison can match a file the releasing process does not own. The remaining residual is the one named above and is unchanged by this: exclusion is heartbeat-free, so a holder that occupies the section for longer than `stale_after_s` may still have its sentinel age-broken and overlap with the breaker — the nonce bounds what a broken holder can destroy, not whether it can be broken. This guarantee is **document integrity only**. The **decision channel is a distinct quantity with a named residual**: because `_emit_next_call` re-reads the state after the critical section has released, and every `query-*` reads unserialized, a batch of concurrent mutations renders each `next_call=` line and each query answer against whichever post-image that process happened to observe — so those answers are **not authoritative under concurrent invocation**, even though the persisted document they read is always internally consistent. §11 is the canonical home for this system contract; the skill states only the operative rule the batching caller follows, and since issue #1190 removed its internal-doc references it carries no back-pointer here (a shipped skill body installs verbatim into a consumer repository, where this path does not resolve). Only the current draft counts as audited: eligibility is grounded on a completed clean-verdict round whose recorded identity still holds — byte-digest equality against the canonical file on file-arm epochs, revision ordering on the embed and inline arms where no trustworthy file exists — or on an explicitly recorded override that no later revision has invalidated, and every `eligible` answer carries a deterministic token bound to the answering digest or revision ordinal (matching the ground that answered) which the summary line quotes verbatim. That **narrows** the prose-compliance gap and makes a skipped eligibility check detectable in the transcript; it does not close it, since no in-process component can force an orchestrator that never invokes it. Where the tool cannot run at all, a named bounded fallback runs one round, asks once, and marks the summary line `state-owner unavailable` — distinct from `degraded`, which keeps its meaning of the inline audit arm.

**Runtime main-thread context (issue #767).** Separate from the skill's *structure* and *static shipped size* discussed above, a long create-issue run accumulates a large **runtime main-thread context** across its many turns. The behavioral instrument `scripts/create-issue-context-eval.py` (maintainer-run over a transcript corpus; never on the skill's runtime path, so no new tool grant) measures it, and the determination of which appended-content classes are authoritative versus safely-removable redundant additions — with the reduction that removes the primary safely-removable class (re-emission of an already-produced block, replaced by a pointer) — lives in [`docs/internal/create-issue-context.md`](create-issue-context.md). That doc is the single source of truth for this axis; it is not paraphrased here.

**Reproducible finding evidence (issue #704).** An additive payload extends the same state owner, so it survives a context compaction and reads back at every later stage. The Step 3.6 auditor's per-finding bar now requires a locator, the exact command, the observed output, and the baseline revision it read (resolved by the auditor itself — the state file stays out of bounds to it). That payload is multi-line and field-shaped, which the one-line `--ledger-stdin` ledger-summary transport refuses by contract, so it rides a **dedicated per-finding channel keyed by finding id** (`record-finding-evidence` / `query-finding-evidence`) with its own bounded encoding: fields are capped with disclosed truncation and JSON-encoded at the print boundary, so record-splitting auditor text cannot forge a line, and the decision fields (`finding=`, `completeness=`, `conflict=`) cannot be forged because they precede every auditor-controlled value and are drawn from closed domains — the same *data, never protocol* identity rule the ledger enforces by refusal, achieved by neutralization instead. The trailing evidence values are quoted rather than delimited, so the line is parsed by its JSON quoting, never by whitespace splitting. Evidence missing a required field — or carrying the literal `unestablished`, the spelling the auditor bar tells an auditor to use for a field it could not establish — is recorded `incomplete`, never verified. Adjudication is then **proportionate in scope, never in whether the conclusion is checked**: a low-risk finding whose evidence is complete and conflict-free has its **conclusion re-derived from the locator** by a bounded read-only check — confirming the defect holds, not merely that the cited bytes exist — instead of a fresh whole-repository investigation, while a **high-risk** finding (one that would change scope, an acceptance criterion, or a trust/security boundary), one whose evidence **conflicts** with another probe, and one whose evidence is absent or incomplete each get full independent verification. Conflicting probes are surfaced with both values intact and resolved by verification, never auto-resolved to either. The orchestrator reconstructs every replay from the **locator** using its own reads and **never executes an auditor-supplied shell string**. The coupled invariant is unchanged: a resolution is recorded only from that site's own per-finding verification, never from the auditor's say-so. Every arm is best-effort — staleness, incomplete evidence, a conflict, a read-only sandbox, a git-unavailable baseline, a denied probe, or a compacted context degrades with a disclosed marker and **never blocks issue creation**.

**A `stale-override` refusal names its own recovery (issue #611).** That refusal is fail-closed and correct, but it used to state only the reason token, so an agent that hit it rediscovered the way out by trial — costliest at `emit-body`, where the creation epoch is already recorded. The tool now writes an arm-selected recovery breadcrumb to stderr beside the refusal at its **two refusal surfaces only** (`query-eligibility` and `emit-body`), never from the shared evaluation they both call and never on `query-summary`, which is a *rendering* surface and would otherwise grow an unplanned stderr line on every summary render of a stale-override-shaped state. The stdout token line stays byte-identical — the remedy is purely additive. The arm is selected by the **staling operand observed on the newest current-ordinal override, never by the epoch's query-time arm**: an override's digest binding is fixed at record time while the epoch arm is keyed at query time, so the two legitimately diverge (a file-write failure and embed retry landing between the record and the query leaves a digest-bound override on an embed-arm epoch), and keying on the epoch arm would name the wrong remedy on exactly that divergence. A current-ordinal override whose recorded digest differs from the draft leads with `record-revision` (the revision is not yet recorded); an already-recorded revision that invalidated an earlier override does **not**, since that would send the caller to re-record state it already holds; and every shape whose cause could not be established — an uncomparable digest binding, a future-ordinal or hand-edited record — makes no claim about the revision state at all. No arm names a bare `record-revision`-then-`record-override` pair: that sequence would re-arm a user election the user never made. Every arm therefore ends in the same clause — re-present the revised draft and record a new override only on a fresh explicit user election through the offer surfaces, a fresh clean audit round being the other eligibility ground.

**Axis-complete evidence bundle and finding actionability (issue #548).** Two additions gate approach recommendations on recorded evidence rather than inline judgment. **(a) The evidence bundle.** The Step 2 independent-derivation pass writes a read-only `## Evidence bundle` section into the derivation artifact on every run — one entry per axis on an effective list (a generic floor: producers, consumers, execution tiers, persistence, lifecycle states/termination paths, migration/coexistence surfaces, coupled tests/docs — extended by a consumer `## Evidence axes` extension section) in one of three forms (`Verified:`, `N/A — checked …`, or `unestablished — …`), opened by a self-describing header restating the rules and the pass-time axis list. A **bundle-coverage gate** fires at the same two sites as the derivation gate (before the first clarification question and unconditionally at the Step 3 drafting precondition), the per-round Definition-of-Ready re-check keeps it current against three triggers, and the implementation-approach fork question's `(Recommended)` marking must cite a bundle entry by axis name whose named check was executed (a `Verified:` row, or a `N/A — checked` row whose check was run — but not a row batch-marked by scope inference, which records no executed check) while disclosing any unestablished axes — so an evidence-thin recommendation is visibly thin at approval time; the marking itself goes to the weakest mechanism class whose stated single-failure consequence the problem statement tolerates, prices the strongest passed-over candidate in one clause, is overridden by a consumer extension's own recommendation policy, and is not reopened toward a stronger class by the later steelman and audit passes except on a verified must-revise defect. The bundle rides inside the already-out-of-bounds derivation artifact, so the audit information diet and its pinned out-of-bounds lists are unchanged. **(b) Finding actionability.** After each accepted audit round, every finding is adjudicated into **must-revise** / **advisory** / **invalid-unverified**; the state owner records this via `record-adjudication`, accepting the payload only when the adjudicated verdict and unresolved-must-revise count agree (`VERDICT: FILE` may carry advisories; `VERDICT: REVISE` requires ≥1 verified unresolved must-revise finding). The **T1** offer trigger and a new **`query-convergence`** query consume that post-adjudication unresolved-must-revise count (a converged run = final adjudicated `VERDICT: FILE` with zero unresolved must-revise axis-attributable findings; an unestablished count is not converged — unknown is not zero) instead of the raw verdict token, while **T2**'s **other** fail-closed arms are unchanged (it gains one new `unadjudicated-round` arm preserving the offer the raw-REVISE T1 token used to fire — firing on a completed REVISE round whose post-adjudication unresolved-must-revise count is absent, whether un-adjudicated or adjudicated with an unestablished count). The Quiet Killer becomes one assessed slot (`Quiet Killer: none` is legal on `FILE`), and the Step 3.5 and Step 4 summary lines report axis coverage plus post-adjudication counts by actionability class. **(c) Post-revision finding resolution (issue #603).** The counts in (b) froze at round close, so a drafter who revised and verified every finding fixed still read `T1: hold` and `converged=no reason=unresolved-must-revise-remain` forever — the audit informed but deadlocked filing. Adjudicating a round `REVISE` with a settled count now additionally records a **per-finding ledger** under a required `--ledger-stdin` flag (one status-prefixed one-line summary per must-revise finding, piped through a quoted-delimiter heredoc so the shell never expands auditor-derived text, and refused when a summary forges a `<field>=` word of the tool's own printed protocol vocabulary — ledger text is identity data, never protocol). Three post-close channels then move an entry: `record-resolution` binds named entries to a recorded revision ordinal (cross-round, so a late fix clears the round that found the defect), `record-reopen` marks a resolved entry unresolved again when the fix did not land, and `record-invalidate` retires a misclassified finding with a mandatory reason rather than laundering it through resolution as a fix that never happened. `record-adjudication` is correspondingly **write-once** per round, with those three as the sole sanctioned post-close channels, and a FILE adjudication **supersedes** every prior unresolved entry (terminal), so an auditor-accepted clean round still converges the run exactly as it did before. T1 and `query-convergence` consume the **run-wide effective** count derived from those ledgers — derived by the tool rather than accepted from the caller, with one disclosed AC5 boundary: an earlier `REVISE` round that recorded no ledger contributes its at-close `--unresolved-must-revise` figure, which the caller supplied — `query-convergence`'s `unledgered_revise=` field names exactly those rounds so the gap is visible rather than silent — and convergence now reports its **basis and currency**: `basis=adjudicated` when the auditor's own FILE verdict vouches for the state, `basis=resolution` when the effective count reached zero through self-verified post-close changes, and `basis=resolution-stale` when any entry's settling provenance predates a later revision (judged per entry). The read-only `query-findings` prints one line per ledger entry — the first of the tool's multi-line read-back queries — and is the **durable** read-back the orchestrator reconciles a later round's findings against, never context recall, so the mechanism survives a context compaction. `query-summary` exposes the same two operands as `effective_unresolved=` and `convergence_basis=`, each placed **before** the contractually-trailing `attestation=` field so nothing follows it. Eligibility is deliberately untouched: revised bytes still refuse `approve` as `unaudited-revision`, so self-attested resolution never reaches the creation gate.

**(d) Positive per-dimension coverage evidence (issue #708).** A clean `VERDICT: FILE` with zero findings used to be structurally indistinguishable from a cursory read — carriage proves which draft *bytes* the auditor read, never the *scrutiny* those bytes received. So the audit now records a **positive, falsifiable, per-dimension coverage assertion**. The renderer gains an `enumerate-dimensions` mode emitting a canonical, keyed, count-stable effective-dimension list, which the **orchestrator** runs to hold the authoritative operand; **issue #729 made those keys first-class declared data rather than a scrape of the rendered prose** — each generic dimension declares its key on a `<!-- dim-key: <lowercase-kebab> -->` line above its checklist bullet (emitted `g:<declared-key>`, stripped from the auditor-facing prose, fail-closed on an undeclared bullet, an orphan declaration, a malformed key, or a duplicate), and a consumer dimension keys off its own declaration, else its bold-lead name slug, else a hash of its text (`c:<key>`), retiring the positional `c:<n>` that reshuffled whenever a consumer inserted a bullet mid-section. Rewording a **generic** dimension therefore leaves its key byte-identical; a consumer dimension gains the same guarantee only by carrying its own declaration, since the bold-lead-slug fallback rekeys on a name edit and the hash fallback on any text edit. Every declaration arm — template and consumer alike — fails closed on the render path as well as the enumeration path, so the two projections cannot drift apart silently. **Migration is a no-op:** the state owner treats coverage keys as opaque strings and checks a round's totality against the `coverage_expected` keyset persisted in that round, never against a fresh enumeration, so a run recorded under the previous derivation stays readable and keeps its backing without any rekeying step. The auditor runs the same deterministic render and returns, per dimension, one outcome from the closed set **`exercised`** / **`valid-N/A`** / **`unestablished`** / **`skipped`**, joined to the orchestrator's list by the shared key. **Applicability is decided before the finding hunt (issue #1686):** the auditor classifies each dimension first and routes a dimension that plainly does not apply straight to `valid-N/A` with a specific, draft-grounded reason (no finding hunt under it), while an applicable **and** an uncertain dimension alike receive the full examination — uncertainty is not inapplicability, and the draft's own claim that a dimension is irrelevant is not by itself sufficient evidence for `valid-N/A`. The trust model splits by where the operand lives: the **auditor** self-reports outcomes and anchors as untrusted identity data; the **state owner** (`record-coverage`) enforces a text-only floor on the anchor alone (non-empty, no forged `<field>=` protocol token, no record-splitting byte, a hard per-anchor length cap — a floor-failing `exercised`/`valid-N/A` downgrades to `unestablished`, unknown never collapsed onto backed); and the **orchestrator** runs the two data-dependent checks (the anchor is not byte-identical to the dimension's rendered prompt text, and any quoted draft line provably appears in the digest-verified re-read of the draft it wrote) and adversarially adjudicates each `exercised` anchor **and each `valid-N/A` reason** for substance exactly as it adjudicates findings (a generic or prompt-paraphrasing anchor, or a `valid-N/A` reason that is generic, a prompt paraphrase, or unsupported by the draft, becomes `skipped`). A run is **coverage-backed** only when every required dimension resolved to `exercised`/`valid-N/A` with adjudication-surviving evidence, derived from the final accepted clean round via `evaluate_coverage` / `query-coverage`. A clean audit whose per-dimension coverage the Step 3.6 procedure mandated but did not deliver fires a new offer trigger — a sibling of T1/T2 (`query-triggers` now answers `coverage=hold|not-hold`) contributing to the **single** boundary offer, bounded by the same user-round cap, firing coverage-only **at most once per run**. Two grounds fire it: an unbacked **full-render** clean audit (`not-backed` + `full`), and — since **issue #1694** — a clean `FILE` round that recorded **no coverage at all** (the `no-coverage-recorded` arm `evaluate_coverage` reports as `unestablished`/`none`), which another audit round can recover; the offer *routing* widens to that named arm while its backing/render/reason tokens are unchanged (absent coverage is never relabelled `not-backed`/`full`). A legitimately narrowed **degraded** render still discloses but never fires, and the other `unestablished` arms — no clean round, and unreadable/corrupt/foreign state — stay disclosure-only. Coverage-backing is a **distinct axis from convergence** — it never redefines `evaluate_convergence` and never gates `emit-body`/`query-eligibility`; the offer is its only teeth, so **filing is never blocked**. The mandatory audit summary line carries `coverage_backing=` and `coverage_render=` on every arm and outcome. The honesty scope is bounded and stated: `coverage-backed` certifies per-dimension evidence of the required shape survived the structural floor and generic-adjudication — it does **not** certify genuine scrutiny, and the residual thin-but-plausible-anchor gap is disclosed, not implied away.

**(e) Final-byte audit coverage — did anyone audit the bytes we are about to file? (issue #792).** Coverage-backing (d) certifies the *scrutiny* an accepted round applied; it says nothing about whether the round that earned it saw the bytes that actually get **posted**. Three sanctioned paths file bytes no auditor has ever read: a `user-decline` override at the approval election, a run that converged on the drafter's own self-verified fixes (`basis=resolution`) rather than an auditor's clean verdict, and a creation attestation — whose comparand is deliberately the digest of the bytes actually posted, so a legitimate override filing attests `match` against bytes the audited round never covered. **The attestation proves identity; it never proved audit coverage.** So the lifecycle now reports coverage of the final bytes as its own axis: `evaluate_final_byte_coverage` answers `covered` / `uncovered` / `unestablished`, rendered on `query-summary` as `final_byte_coverage=` immediately before `bound_root=`. `covered` requires all four terms of the shipped clean test — the newest completed verdict-bearing round carries `VERDICT: FILE` (a newer completed `REVISE` revokes it), its recorded dispatch digest equals the current canonical-file digest, no recorded revision postdates it, and its steering-absence was **established** — because the axis reports what the engine would actually ground on. **Four things never set it**, and the complement of that set is what `covered` means: an attestation (tamper evidence, not coverage), a `cap-reached` override (a ceiling record, not a verdict), a `user-decline` override (a user's election, not an auditor's reading), and a clean round whose steering was never established. `unestablished` is **not** `uncovered` and covers exactly four states — no readable, owned lifecycle state exists at all; no completed file-arm verdict-bearing round exists; the canonical file could not be digested; or no draft digest was supplied — so a pass whose pre-dispatch write fails and lands on the embed arm cannot downgrade a known `uncovered` and consume the safety pass on a read-only host.

**The exact-byte pass, funded from its own slot.** Where coverage reports `uncovered`, an **exact-byte audit round** is offered at the last moment it can be true — immediately before the Step 4 approval election, when the bytes are final. It is answered on its **own** `query-final-byte`, deliberately not appended to `query-triggers`, whose boundary consumer applies *while any holds* at the **pre-presentation** pause where the bytes are not yet final. The offer is **suppressed** (`final_byte_trigger=not-hold reason=resolution-settled`) when the run converged `basis=resolution` on a steering-established REVISE round whose findings the drafter self-verified and resolved (issue #1771) — a second user pause there only duplicates diligence already done, so the pass is withheld while the coverage axis still reports the bytes `uncovered` truthfully; the offer still fires when independence was not established or the basis is stale. The pass draws on a **dedicated slot outside the user-round cap** (`record-final-byte-offer`), so a run that legitimately spent every discovery round — and a run carrying a `cap-reached` override — still gets its safety pass; `record-dispatch`'s funding test accounts for that slot, and the derived automatic-re-audit spend is suppressed for a pass so an accepted one cannot fund a phantom round. The slot is **spent per canonical digest**, not per run: a recorded revision that changes the bytes re-arms it (bounded by a per-run cap on **honoured** passes, whose exhaustion the summary line discloses, plus an absolute grant ceiling that stops a refund→re-arm loop on a host where every pass degrades), so a pass taken on bytes the user then edits never leaves the bytes actually filed unofferable. A pass that closes **without** a file-arm verdict — a failed pre-dispatch write, an unparseable verdict, an exhausted `DRAFT-UNREADABLE` retry — **refunds** the slot, because the offer's own precondition is that an accepted round could honour it. The pass is an **ordinary whole-draft round** through the existing file-arm machinery (no new dispatch vocabulary), but it is carved out of the iterate loop's return handling: on `REVISE` its findings are adjudicated with a ledger and disclosed at the election rather than forcing a revise-and-re-present cycle that would change the digest and re-arm the slot off the mechanism's own return. It is excluded from the coverage-round and calibration-round selectors, so an accepted pass retires neither axis. Like (d), this is a **distinct, non-gating axis** — off `query-triggers` and off `query-convergence`, and it never gates `emit-body` or `query-eligibility`. **Filing is never blocked:** the user's explicit election remains the documented path by which a determined filer files. What changed is that the lifecycle stops representing that path as equivalent to an audited one, and the one line the maintainer reads before approving says so.

**Tiered canonical-draft-root binding (issue #562).** The draft file the auditor reads (and the file-arm posting sources from) has always anchored to a *different* root than the three cwd/worktree-anchored reasoning artifacts (the derivation artifact, the audit report, and the state `.json`) — the draft is written under the **main** repo root via `resolve-main-root.sh` so its displayed path opens from a worktree editor, while the reasoning artifacts stay cwd/worktree-anchored; that divergence is deliberate and must not be unified. This PR lands the **state-owner half** of a tiered binding: on a run whose main root is unwritable while the active worktree is writable (an isolated/background session), a **successfully-writable** canonical draft root can be bound instead of the draft write degrading straight to the embed arm. The tool gains that capability here; the **skill-side tier-selection ladder** that will consume it — tier 1 the resolver's main root (taken only on a clean, non-empty resolver answer whose `.git` entry is a directory, a bash-builtin test), tier 2 the active-worktree root (`git rev-parse --show-toplevel`), tier 3 the existing embed fallback, with the **first landed write** binding that root for the rest of the run — is the paired follow-up (issue #569). Issue #569 lands the **binding-reuse half** of that ladder: the skill now records the immutable binding at its first landed canonical-draft write (`record-draft-binding … --tier main-root`), reads the bound root back from `query-draft-binding` at the write/display/overwrite sites that run in a **later turn** (never re-resolving or recalling it there; the same-turn presentation write keeps the root already in hand, and an unbound `bound=none` answer deliberately falls back to a fresh resolution), and forwards the bound canonical path to `record-dispatch --write-path`, which the state owner cross-checks against the recorded binding (`write-path-mismatch` on divergence). That option is deliberately **layered** (issue #1695): it stays **optional at the `record-dispatch`/state-owner CLI boundary** for compatibility (omitted, or on an unbound run, the dispatch proceeds unchanged; a present-empty value is refused `write-path-empty`), while the **live create-issue file-arm caller is required to forward the bound canonical path** once the draft root is bound — omission there bypasses only the reported-path cross-check and is not a sanctioned opt-out for that workflow. The full **tier-2 active-worktree / tier-3 embed selection** and the strict **binding-required** file-arm enforcement (fail-closed when a file-arm dispatch has no recorded binding, with its coupled reconciliation of every pre-binding file-arm test's bound-first reader setup) remain a further follow-up — a `main-root`-tier binding is what the skill selects today, so the bound root equals the resolved main root and the write still degrades to the embed arm on a write failure. The binding is recorded once through the state owner (`record-draft-binding`: the bound absolute path, a bound-tier token `main-root`/`worktree-root`, and the divergent non-bound root when both exist) and is immutable; the tool's digest, `approve`-eligibility, and body-emitting operations resolve the draft file from that recorded binding rather than a caller-supplied path a compacted context could drift, and a `query-draft-binding` query plus the `bound_root`/`bound_tier` summary fields expose it so a future display marker (`draft bound to worktree root` on a worktree-root binding) and the divergent-roots out-of-bounds enumerations can obtain their operands from the tool. A post-revision write-failure closure lands with it: revision records may carry the revised bytes' stdin digest, a write failure at the bound path is recorded, and the file-arm `approve` eligibility ground now refuses bytes a recorded revision has staled but that never landed (`unaudited-revision`) instead of waving them through on stale byte-identity — re-enabling once a fresh clean-verdict round that no revision postdates is recorded (the separate `latest_revision_landed` presentation field reports independently as a three-way token — `yes` when a subsequent landed write at the bound path carries a digest equal to the revision's recorded stdin digest, `no` only when a recorded write-failure for that revision's ordinal proves the bytes did not land, and `unestablished` when the recorded state proves neither, as on the common `basis=resolution` terminal path or a revision with no recorded stdin digest). The state `schema_version` is 3 (issue #709 bumped it from 2 for the additive steering and dispatch-instruction records).

**Durable staged canonical-draft write, and the #562 write-failure closure reached from the skill for the first time (issue #705).** The write-failure closure above shipped in `issue-audit-state.py` but was **inert in the skill** — no create-issue recipe recorded a revision's `stdin_digest`, called `record-write-failure`, or read `latest_revision_landed`, so `record-revision --stdin-digest` and `record-write-failure` were producers the skill never invoked. Issue #705 wires them in through **one shared *Staged canonical-draft write* procedure** every canonical-draft write site (Step 3.6's pre-dispatch write and its re-dispatches, Step 4's presentation write, and Step 4's iterate-on-feedback overwrite) references by name. A new bundled helper, `scripts/stage-draft-write.py`, owns three single-process modes: **stage** lands the intended title-and-body bytes atomically at a staging artifact keyed on this run's nonce **and, since issue #793, on the staged bytes' own digest** — `issue-draft-<slug>.<nonce>.<digest>.staged.md`, resolved by the helper from the caller's base path and reported back, since the digest is computed from stdin inside the helper and each shell fence is a fresh process (a temporary sibling plus a rename, so an interruption after the run's first staging write leaves a complete copy on disk); **emit** carries those staged bytes into `record-revision --stdin-digest` without a heredoc; and **apply** copies the staged bytes onto the canonical file via `os.replace` (never renaming the staging artifact, which therefore survives for the recovery arm), re-digests the canonical file, and reports whether it agrees with the caller's **declared** `--expect-digest` — the answer the run reports to `query-arm` as `--write-landed`. A disagreement after a revision write records `record-write-failure`, presents from the in-context revision bytes, and re-attempts the replace exactly once; a **cross-turn landed re-check** (canonical file re-digested against the latest revision's recorded `stdin_digest`) is what detects an interruption the in-process comparison cannot see, since a killed process leaves no in-process signal. The tool half is enforced: `record-revision` now **refuses** a file-arm-latest-round revision that carries no `--stdin-digest`, so the digest guarantee is owned by the tool rather than by prose a compaction can evict. A verified multi-finding revision wave applies as exactly one replace and one revision record, with per-finding traceability unchanged (`record-resolution --resolved-ids`). The staging artifact is added to both audit out-of-bounds enumerations (after a failed replace it holds bytes the canonical file does not), and a read-only sandbox arm degrades with a reduced-durability report — filing is never blocked. Nothing about the schema changes at that time: `schema_version` stayed **2** — the historical value it carried when #705 landed — and no new state key was added. (The current value is **3**, stated in the #562/#569 paragraph above; #709 bumped it after this change.)

The Step 4 presentation carries a one-line audit summary read from the tool's summary query (findings, revisions, verdict, rounds run, an embed-arm marker, a declined-further-audit note when applicable, the eligibility token, and a degraded-arm marker whenever the attempt-first dispatch fell to its inline degraded arm) plus the **skill-composed** criterion-count marker described below under issue #1341 (which the summary query does not report), and a consumer `## Audit dimensions` prompt-extension section is re-loaded fresh at dispatch time and forwarded verbatim to the auditor. **Since issue #600 the audit prompt is rendered by `scripts/render-audit-prompt.py`, not hand-emitted:** the audit-prompt template, the generic dimension checklist, and the heading-extraction rule live in the committed template file `skills/create-issue/references/audit-prompt-template.md`, and on the normal path the auditor runs the renderer itself (a positional two-marker `render-status:`/`render-end:` delivery check, with a template-file Read fallback ladder). The information diet and out-of-bounds policy stay prose in the skill; the prompt *text* is renderer-owned.

**The dispatch instructions are generated and hash-verified too (issue #709).** #600 made the audit-*instruction* block observable and left the orchestrator composing the wrapping **preamble** freehand — an unobserved surface through which a parent could topic-prime the auditor (focus, prioritization, reassurance, scoping) and still reach a coverage-backed clean audit. The preamble is now generated by the same renderer's `dispatch-instructions` mode (deterministic, reading the draft **title from the draft file** — no drafter free text on a command line), written to `.prflow/tmp/create-issue/<slug>/issue-audit-dispatch-<slug>.md`, and the Agent-tool prompt is a generated pointer to that file and the draft file and nothing else. The auditor `git hash-object --no-filters`es the instruction file exactly as it already does the draft file and quotes the object ID; `issue-audit-state.py` **regenerates** the canonical instructions from the round's recorded closed inputs and compares — regenerating rather than re-reading its own write-time digest is what catches a hand-written steered file that bypassed the generator. The clean (`VERDICT: FILE`) eligibility ground now additionally requires that match **and** the auditor's `extra-dispatch-content: no` affirmation, and the state owner records the outcome as the round-bound `steering` / `steering_reason` pair, exposed by `query-summary` as two space-free tokens placed **before** the contractually-trailing `attestation=` field. **What this proves is scoped, and the summary line says so:** the file hash proves the instruction *content* was canonical; the Agent-tool prompt *string* is not a hashable artifact, so it is covered only by canonical generation plus the auditor's best-effort affirmation — a positive report withholds the clean ground, but its silence is not a proof. The mechanism **narrows** that one channel rather than closing it, the same withhold-then-disclose posture §11 already uses for a skipped eligibility check. On every non-established path — a mismatch, a reported extra-content flag, a generator failure, and the embed / inline / read-only-sandbox arms where no instruction file can be written, so the property is **unestablished by construction** — the audit summary line carries an `audit independence unestablished` marker (distinct from the reserved `degraded` token), a new `evaluate_triggers` arm fires the existing re-audit offer (so even a zero-finding `VERDICT: FILE` round is offered one unsteered re-dispatch rather than silently withheld), and **filing is still never blocked** — the user's explicit approval files through the same documented override election an unresolved `REVISE` already uses. The enforcement is always-on: no `.prflow/config.json` key gates it and no new workflow tool grant is involved. **The transport is named exactly (issue #768):** the orchestrator writes the generator's stdout to `.prflow/tmp/create-issue/<slug>/issue-audit-dispatch-<slug>.md` with a **shell redirect** (the redirect truncates the target before the generator runs, so no delete-first step exists; the write has landed when the generator exits zero and leaves a non-empty file), and obtains the generated `dispatch-pointer:` line **from the generator's own stderr** (issue #795): the standalone read-back extraction this paragraph used to prescribe is retired, because a line the generator folds onto stderr in the same invocation that writes the file needs no second extraction step at all — and the old recipe's own constraint (never `grep`/`sed`/`awk`, since the extracted line becomes the entire Agent-tool prompt and an emitted result must not depend on a tool outside the preflight-guaranteed set) is satisfied by construction once no extraction runs. The stderr line is asserted byte-identical to the `dispatch-pointer:` line inside the stdout the same invocation wrote. The instruction bytes therefore never traverse the orchestrator's context. This is a local/interactive-tier skill: no cloud profile grants these heads, so the cloud matcher's redirect-shape denial does not bind it, and the dispatch blocks on the completed result rather than arming any fallback wakeup.
6. **Review then create:** show the **complete rendered issue** in chat (never summarized), get **explicit confirmation**, then — once approval is established and before any creation command runs — ask a **post-confirmation self-assignment election** (*"Assign this issue to you?"*): an explicit **yes** adds `--assignee "@me"` to the `gh issue create` call (self-assignment inside the same atomic create, no post-create edit), an explicit **no** creates it unassigned, and silence or any non-yes/non-no reply pauses and re-asks (mirroring the Step 2 silent-non-response rule) — no creation command runs until the answer is an explicit yes or no. A draft-only request never reaches this election. Then `gh issue create`. After creation, present a **gated** offer to start implementation — posting the bare `/prflow:implement <n>` comment only when the issue has no open dependencies/blocked decisions and the cloud tier is enabled, else printing a one-line reason (see the gated-offer hardening below).

**Tool-owned round KINDS, and the durable byte history they need (issue #793).** Every Step 3.6 round used to be the same round: a cold, whole-draft, whole-repository derivation. The state owner recorded an `arm` per round and nothing about what the round was *for*, so a later round re-established from scratch the repository facts an earlier one had established, over sections no revision had touched, while the findings it should have been re-checking sat untouched in the ledger. The round **kind** is now the second tool-owned per-round dispatch dimension beside the arm, resolved by the same query-then-obey-then-cross-check shape: `query-round-kind` answers one of exactly two kinds — `discovery` (a cold whole-draft round) or `targeted` (a claim-scoped re-check) — with the reason token that selected it, and `record-dispatch` **requires** `--kind` and refuses any kind other than the one the tool selects at that moment (`kind-mismatch`), exactly as it refuses a `write-path-mismatch`. The orchestrator never chooses a kind, because the context that would choose it is the anchored context the audit exists to remove. **Selection fails toward the expensive kind**: `targeted` requires all five of a revision postdating the last completed round, a `file`-arm latest attempt, dispatch bytes recoverable from the byte history with a matching digest, a non-empty enumerated claim set, and a non-empty computed changed-section set — and every other input, including every unestablished one, selects `discovery` and names the failing condition. An empty delta and a delta that errors both select `discovery`; neither is read as "nothing changed".

That delta has no operand without a byte history, so `stage --path` became a **base** the helper completes with the staged bytes' own digest, reporting the resolved path (`record-staged-write` records it durably; `query-staged-write` resolves it back). A second stage of different bytes lands beside the first instead of overwriting it, re-staging identical bytes resolves to the same path, and the nonce stays in the leaf so the write-failure recovery arm can still name its artifact after an interruption — it re-applies the artifact **that write recorded**, never the newest on disk. **A `targeted` auditor learns what to check, never what was concluded:** the enumerated claims (id plus one-line summary) and the changed-section set travel in **one dispatch-scope file whose path AND content digest both join the closed recorded regeneration tuple**, frozen at dispatch. That freezing is forced rather than tidy — the instruction file is regenerated and digest-compared over a closed tuple and a divergence is sticky, so a payload passed as an unrecorded render argument, or read from the live ledger, would make every scoped round diverge (post-close status mutations would give the return-time regeneration a different ledger than dispatch saw). The identity-data floor sits at the single state-owner write site, so the closed protocol-token vocabulary stays in one module; the renderer refuses an empty claim set independently, so a scoped round cannot pass vacuously. Absent/unreadable and tampered scope files take **distinct** named steering reasons, because they send a reader to opposite remedies.

**The byte history is a precondition the tool enforces, not a step the prose asks for (issue #1104).** A history the run never recorded makes the recoverability condition above fail, and that failure is silent by construction — a missing operand degrades the selection to the expensive kind rather than aborting — so a run whose canonical write went unrecorded paid for a cold whole-draft round on every round after the first and nothing said why. `record-dispatch` now refuses a **fresh file-arm** dispatch whose draft bytes it cannot recover from the recorded history (`file-arm-requires-staged-write`), before any state write, naming the staged-write call as the remedy; the refused round stays dispatchable, so the caller records the write and re-issues the identical call. It is the sibling of the `file-arm-requires-stdin-digest` refusal `record-revision` already carries, and its predicate is the same reader the selection uses — not a digest-membership test, which would admit a recorded pair whose artifact is gone or whose bytes no longer hash to it and leave the selection failing on the same run. The scoping is what makes the requirement safe: the file arm is selected only when the canonical write landed, and a retry re-dispatches an already-open round whose bytes may legitimately have moved. Recording the pair is **necessary, not sufficient** — the check reads the artifact at dispatch time, so one later overwritten or swept still strands the record.

**A `targeted` round re-checks resolved claims, and records the draft-line span that makes the trade measurable (issue #1105).** The `#793` enumerated claim set above was `unresolved`-only, but the shipped revision discipline records a resolution for every confirmed fix *before* the next round's kind is selected — so a run that fixed what it was told about and confirmed the fixes emptied the very set a scoped round requires and dispatched every round cold: the better a run behaved, the more certainly it was ineligible. The enumeration is now **every earlier-round ledger entry regardless of status** — a resolved entry is the drafter's own attestation that its own fix worked, produced by the same context that wrote the defect, which is exactly the claim a fresh-context auditor is best placed to falsify, so it becomes the input the round audits rather than a filter that skips it. The information diet is unchanged and load-bearing under the widening: only the id and one-line summary travel, so a re-checked resolved claim arrives as something to check, never as an answer already given. Condition 4 stays a real gate — a run with **no** earlier-round entries at all still selects the cold kind (`empty-claim-set`). The widening trades against the fail-toward-the-expensive-kind property, so it ships with its measurement: `record-dispatch` now records a `draft_lines` span on a targeted round's frozen `scope` — the **convex hull** `[min_start, max_end]` over the changed sections' draft-line extents (a single `(start, end)` the `#889` scope-escape proxy tests with `any(s <= line <= e)`, deliberately over-approximating a disjoint changed set so it over-counts escapes rather than under-counting them). That fills the proxy's only comparand the repository lacked a producer for, converting the safety trade from argued to observed; a pre-#1105 round, or a wrong-typed or inverted span, still drives the proxy to `unestablished` rather than a number.

**The selecting reason is recorded durably, and the fall-off announces itself (issue #1103).** The reason token that says *why* a round's kind is what it is used to be printed on a query's stdout and discarded, so a cold round taken because it was the run's first was, in the run's own durable state, indistinguishable from a cold round taken because a `targeted` precondition failed — and the `#889` census that joins rounds to kinds could draw the same distinction: none. `record-dispatch` now records the reason on the round record **beside `kind`**, validated against the same closed vocabulary at the write boundary and read with a default at every reader (an absent reason on a pre-#1103 round reports `unestablished`, never a guess). The schema version is **not** bumped — the loader refuses a differing version with no migration path, so the field is additive under the unchanged version exactly as the round kind itself was. The old shared `no-completed-round` token is split into two facts: `no-round-dispatched` (the genuine cold first round — nothing dispatched yet) and `no-completed-round` (the fall-off — a round *was* dispatched but never completed). Two additive stderr breadcrumbs, each beside a closed stdout contract line it does not touch, make the quiet paths loud: `record-return` names the **carriage cause** (`carriage-absent` vs `carriage-mismatch`, distinct from an ordinary unparseable return) when an operand slip classified a parseable verdict `no-parseable-verdict`, naming the remedy (supply `--carriage-object-id` with the object id of the draft the auditor audited); and `record-dispatch` announces the **accepted-discovery fallback** — a `discovery` kind selected for any failed-condition reason, silent only for the genuine `no-round-dispatched` first round — so the expensive whole-draft path is named at the moment it is paid for rather than only in a later census. Both stay breadcrumbs, never refusals: falling back to the cold kind is correct behavior on every run that legitimately cannot take a scoped round.

**A clean `targeted` round is confirmed, not trusted.** It never grounds `evaluate_eligibility`'s clean scan, never backs the coverage axis, never wipes the calibration signal and never reports `basis=adjudicated` — `_last_discovery_round` was widened rather than duplicated, exactly as its own docstring said a second non-discovery kind should extend it. Teaching the scan to merely *skip* a clean scoped round would land it on the preceding `REVISE`, break, and refuse, so while the confirming budget remains the tool instead selects a **confirming whole-draft round** through a new `confirm-whole-draft` next-action token, funded from a **counter of its own**. That selection covers a usable `targeted` round closing `FILE`, and an unusable return carrying no per-claim block regardless of whether its terminal token is `FILE` or `REVISE` — the latter is recorded UNUSABLE, and confirmation is scheduled precisely because it established nothing. Once the dedicated confirmation slot is exhausted, an unusable return proceeds to the existing disclosed boundary election; it does not silently inherit ordinary scoped-round approval or spend the unrelated automatic re-audit pool. `record-dispatch` funds on the same predicate `next_action` schedules on, deliberately not on the narrower all-addressed sweep, so the error-recovery path can never be directed to open a round the tool then refuses as unfunded. That separation is forced too: the automatic pool is shared, and walking it at the shipped ceiling of one shows the confirming round already refused at any position. `_MAX_AUTOMATIC_REAUDITS` is therefore left alone — it has a second reader in `next_action`, so raising it would move a whole round out of the user's decision on every run, including the embed-arm, inline-arm and empty-delta runs that can never take a scoped round; issue #827, which proposed raising it, was closed as not planned in favour of that position. A scoped round records **no ledger of its own**: its per-claim return updates the existing entry each claim names (`not-addressed` reopens or leaves unresolved, `addressed` changes nothing, since resolution stays the drafter's own recorded verification), so a mechanism built to reduce rounds cannot inflate the very count that fires the offer for more of them. A claim the round dispatched but the return omits, and a claim returned outside the closed `addressed`/`not-addressed` set, are both recorded `not-addressed`. The Step 4 audit summary reads the same way: its verdict and class counts ground on the latest **whole-draft** round rather than `last_completed`, so a scoped re-check never renders as the run's verdict, and the scoped round is reported beside them under its own `scoped_round=` token rather than dropped — a reader sees that it ran without being told a whole draft came back clean. That selector differs from `_last_discovery_round` by exactly one clause: a final-byte exact-byte pass *is* whole-draft evidence here, whose verdict issue #792 renders on purpose.

The issue is created **only after the user explicitly confirms**: a pending confirmation is a valid waiting state, not a reason to create.

**Verified-or-obligation for mechanical claims (issue #380).** A mechanical prediction in an issue — an assertion of a concrete outcome such as "running X reports Y", "the extractor/grep/command emits Z", or "this must fail RED reporting W" — must take exactly one of two decided forms: **(a) verified** — the drafter actually ran the extraction/grep/command while drafting and cites its *observed* output, or **(b) an obligation** on the implementer ("the pin must cover X"), never a prediction of the specific result the drafter did not execute. An unverified mechanical prediction reads exactly like a decided requirement and sends the implementer to encode a falsehood; both the Step 3.5 steelman verification loop (`skills/create-issue/SKILL.md`) and the Testing Strategy guidance in `skills/create-issue/references/issue-template.md` enforce this. The same discipline governs `Relevant Classes/Files` line anchors — cite the symbol or section, not a `file:line` number that rots between drafting and implementation.

**Named instruments for quantitative acceptance criteria (issue #1215).** An acceptance criterion that expresses a number names the exact command or counting rule that produces the measured value. The shipped guidance gives Python `str.split()` over named UTF-8 file contents as a concrete example because naming only a unit is insufficient: GNU and BSD `wc -w` can disagree on the same corpus. When the instrument cannot be established, the draft records `unestablished` and omits the unnamed quantitative criterion. The repository-specific evidence-axis extension points to this shipped rule instead of maintaining a second copy. If the issue template is unavailable, a pointer from the loaded extension does not establish the rule; the create-issue fallback preserves the same fail-closed disposition.

**Re-derivation handles for `Verified:` premises, re-checked at implement time (issues #868 and #1675).** A `Verified:` bullet is what licenses an implementing run to **skip its own investigation**, so `/prflow:create-issue` requires a self-contained re-derivation handle: a repository path in backticks followed by the source sentence verbatim inside a recognized ASCII or typographic double-quoted span. The helper classifies each bullet independently by handle (`path-quote` / `path` / `quote` / `command` / `none`) and state (`holds` / `refuted` / `unestablished`), and both dimensions are routed: `holds` needs no evidence action, `refuted` and usable `unestablished` results restore ordinary investigation, incomplete handles are repaired, and body-supplied commands are never executed automatically. Refuting remains deliberately hardest because it discards a premise; unreadable, escaping, ambiguous, or otherwise unprovable operands become `unestablished`, never false clean evidence. The issue body is third-party text, so the helper performs read-only file reads and no network or subprocess calls. Its recognized marker spellings remain a floor; the non-adjudicating ungraded pass reports collocation-family verification phrases outside that marker set without changing exit status or inventing a state. Per-arm detail is in [`implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md); the helper's vendored literal is granted on the `implement` and `command` capability profiles, leaving the `review` lock unchanged.

**Acceptance-criteria parseability gate at drafting time (issues #1111 and #1675).** The implementing run reads exactly one issue section as its definition of "done" — `## Acceptance Criteria`, mirrored into the workpad by `scripts/parse-acs.py` — and when that parser reads zero criteria the Phase 3.4 acceptance-criteria gate passes trivially, so a drafted section the parser cannot read ships an unenforceable specification. `/prflow:create-issue` runs the **same shipped parser** over the canonical draft at Step 3.6's pre-dispatch write as `parse-acs.py --body-file <the canonical draft> --format json`. Its consumer parses both typed result fields: a true `acceptance_criteria_unreadable` repairs the unreadable section without spending the genuinely-empty counter, while an empty `acceptance_criteria` array rewrites the rows into the parser's accepted shape and spends that counter. Both repair routes are bounded; three consecutive genuinely-empty rewrites preserve their exact historical ceiling, while repeated unreadable results reach a separate exhaustion disclosure. An unreadable result whose repair budget is exhausted, or three genuinely-empty results, proceed only through an explicit file-anyway election that carries the unestablished count and failure kind into Step 4 — unknown is never read as zero or clean, but the best-effort gate still cannot deadlock issue creation. An **unavailable** parser — an unreadable helper, a non-zero exit, a denied invocation, or unparseable stdout — is not an input to that election: it emits an in-chat breadcrumb naming the failure kind and proceeds to ordinary presentation with the criterion count rendered unavailable. The gate asserts **non-emptiness, not completeness**, and one residual is disclosed rather than closed: two same-level `## Acceptance Criteria` headings yield only the first section's rows. The obligation is stated in `skills/create-issue/references/step-3-5-steelman.md` and executed at the pre-dispatch write in `step-3-6-audit.md`; the parser's typed producer is unit-tested in `lib/test/test_python_scripts.py`.

**The `Documentation Needed` contract with `/prflow:implement`.** When an issue names specific files that must be documented, the create-issue template renders them under `## Implementation Notes` in a canonical `- **Documentation Needed** — …` bold-bullet list item. That block is the input to the deterministic path extractor (`scripts/extract-doc-needed-paths.sh`), which `/prflow:implement` Phase 4.1 reaches through `scripts/read-doc-needed-deliverables.sh` — the read boundary that prints an outcome token and the extracted paths — turning the named files into mandatory doc-pass deliverables. To keep a differently-rendered body from silently skipping that gate, the extractor accepts **three** scope-opening shapes — the canonical `- **Documentation Needed**` list item (issue #185), a bare blank-line-preceded `**Documentation Needed**` bold paragraph (issue #309), and a `### Documentation Needed` level-3 heading (issue #380) — all emitting the same paths. The template canonically emits the bold-bullet form; `lib/test/run.sh` pins the template literal and the extractor's accepted shapes as a coupled pair so the producer and consumer cannot drift. Within the scoped block, command and grant literals are **not** deliverables (issue #644): a backtick span yields a path only when its content is a single bare-path token or several extension-bearing / in-tree bare-path tokens, while a `:`/`*`/`(`-bearing span (`` `Bash(x.sh:*)` ``), a bare command word (`` `bash lib/test/run.sh` ``), an un-backticked `Word(...)` call group, and a fenced code block are treated as scope markers that contribute no tokens (a suppressed backtick *span* leaves a one-time stderr breadcrumb; `Word(...)` call groups and fenced blocks are removed silently). Because the extractor ships to consumers via the `prflow_version` vendor fetch, a consumer should normalize its open issues' Documentation Needed blocks to one bare backticked path per span before bumping, since a multi-token span authored under the older tokenization can otherwise be reclassified from a deliverable to a suppressed literal.

**A silent non-response is never disengagement.** During Step 2 clarification, a question-tool timeout (`No response after 60s`) or the user stepping away does **not** engage the draft-from-decided / Blocked path — the skill pauses and re-asks the unanswered question in its final chat message rather than proceeding, drafting, or guessing a default. Only an *explicit* reply ("just create it", "you decide", "I don't know") or an exhausted clarification budget of **answered** questions engages that path. This mirrors the Step 4 confirmation gate: with no response, the pipeline stays paused rather than moving on without the user. (The ~18-question budget counts *answered* questions, so silence never consumes it.)

**Visual specification for UI changes.** When Step 2 infers an issue involves user-visible UI changes, it checks the user-provided resources (pasted images, attached files, URLs, design-tool links such as Figma) for an existing screenshot or mockup and records it in a dedicated visual-specification section — embedded when a hosted URL exists, otherwise referenced with a note on how the implementer can obtain it. When none is provided it asks for one; and when the user has none, it **verbally verifies** the visual details — placement & layout, visual states, responsive behavior, design-system match, plus any task-specific dimension — before finalizing the draft. A screenshot is **preferred, not mandatory**: verbal verification is an accepted substitute, and an unresolved placement detail at disengagement flows to the existing `## 🚫 Blocked` section. Non-UI issues skip the whole path and gain no new questions. This is **prose guidance, not a new gate** — inline LLM inference in the same Step 2 clarification that already assesses scope and forks.

**Four authoring-pipeline hardenings (issue #446).** (1) **Cross-issue `## Dependencies`.** The issue template gains an optional top-level `## Dependencies` section, rendered above `## Problem Statement` and included only when a prerequisite is still open at drafting time; each entry is a `Blocked by #N — <reason>` line. Both the heading and the `blocked by #N` phrasing are forms `/prflow:implement`'s early Phase 1 dependency preflight already recognizes as a declared sequencing dependency, so the section feeds the existing implement gate with **no recognizer change** — giving create-issue-authored and human-authored issues a deliberate producer for a gate that previously had none. Step 2's Definition of Ready gains a known-prerequisites item that infers candidates from the story and Step 1 findings and confirms them with the user (no new question when none apply); and `/prflow:implement` Phase 4.0's deferred follow-ups render the same section with a `Blocked by #<parent>` line — a documented human-visible redundancy alongside the mandated `Follow-up to #N` opener that the early Phase 1 dependency preflight already recognizes.
