It stays distinct from `## 🚫 Blocked` (unresolved *decisions*) and the `Technical Context` → `Dependencies` bullet (service/module/library). **After creation, each prerequisite declared inside the `## Dependencies` section is additionally registered as a GitHub-native blocked-by dependency on the created issue (issue #1011), so the blocker shows in GitHub's own issue header, issue list, and project views without anyone reading the body.** Both post-creation producers stamp it — `/prflow:create-issue` Step 4 and `/prflow:implement` Phase 4.0's deferred follow-up — through the best-effort helper `scripts/apply-issue-dependencies.py`, the third member of the post-creation REST-stamp family beside `ensure-label.sh` / `apply-labels.sh`: it fetches the created issue's body itself (so it takes only the number), derives the prerequisites through the same **section-scoped** recognizer factored out of `scripts/preflight.py` (deliberately narrower than the reversible implement gate — a persistent GitHub write consumes only the reserved section, and any number that resolves to a pull request or to the issue's own number is skipped), always exits 0, never blocks or reverses the creation, and leaves a specific `apply-issue-dependencies.py:`-prefixed stderr breadcrumb on every path (a number it cannot resolve or link is named in that breadcrumb and reported in the run's final outcome; the remaining numbers are still linked). A number the section-scoped recognizer dropped because its `## Dependencies` line reads as an OUTBOUND relation (this issue is the prerequisite, not blocked by it) is now surfaced under that same prefix rather than dropped silently or misdescribed as "no prerequisites" (issue #1268): the recognizer returns the outbound-skipped numbers to the helper through the `(found, skipped)` accessor `dependency_section_scan` while keeping its own section-only path stderr-free, and the helper names each skip on both the every-entry-dropped and the some-dropped-some-kept paths. The POST is `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/{n}/dependencies/blocked_by` with the blocker's database `issue_id`; a run in which every registration attempt was refused with the same status collapses to a single breadcrumb naming that status. (2) **Relied-on third-party docs ladder.** The never-assume discipline broadens from "an AC's mechanism" to every third-party behavior the issue *relies on* (load-bearing for the Desired Behavior, an AC, or the Implementation Notes Approach), with a decided fallback ladder — official docs via `WebFetch` → `WebSearch` → ask the user for documentation — terminating either in a `## 🚫 Blocked` vendor-behavior question (when nothing verifies it and no in-repo example proves it) or a flagged `— assumption, confirm before implementing` line citing an in-repo example; incidental third-party mentions stay light. The Step 3.5 self-steelman carries the broadened class (the drafting-side surface that states it self-containedly). (3) **Gated implement offer.** The post-creation implement-comment offer is presented only when the created issue has no `## Dependencies` entries and no `## 🚫 Blocked` section **and** `workflows.prflow` reads as exactly `true` from `.prflow/config.json` (mirroring the cloud gate's fail-closed `.workflows.prflow // false`, read through `config-get.sh` with a direct `python3`/`jq` fallback on classifier denial, compared with bash builtins); a withheld offer prints a one-line reason naming the failed condition (declared dependencies present, blocked decisions present, tier disabled/unconfigured, or config unreadable) instead of posting a comment that fires nothing on a tier-disabled repo. (4) **Step 1 completion-wait.** Step 1's `/prflow:docs-verify --report-only` carries the same synchronous completion-wait discipline as Step 3.6 — its findings report must be complete and captured before the first Step 2 clarification question (and, on a zero-question run, before Step 3 drafting), so users are never interrogated before the code findings that ground the questions exist. Since issue #1695 a malformed reserved leading `Dependencies` heading — one spelled at a Markdown level other than two, above `## Problem Statement` — is treated as unknown rather than an empty prerequisite set: the shared `scripts/preflight.py` positional detector `malformed_reserved_dependency_heading` makes the reversible implement preflight fail closed with `UNAVAILABLE` naming the canonical `## Dependencies` spelling and resolve nothing, and the native `apply-issue-dependencies.py` stamp emits its prefixed malformed-heading breadcrumb and registers nothing instead of claiming the issue declared no prerequisites; the detector is positional and bounded (never promoting a later nested `Dependencies` heading), not a general Markdown parser.

**Three authoring-discipline rules (issue #462).** Class-level prevention of three defect shapes that PR #447 (issue #446) surfaced only as review round-trips. (1) **Value-comparison type-semantics pinning.** An AC or Testing-Strategy assertion that compares a produced value against a literal must state the comparison in the producing surface's **observed-output terms, in the AC's own language**, grounded either by a drafting-time probe cited in the issue (the *verified* arm — which requires the cited probe to exercise the **boundary fixtures the comparison distinguishes**, e.g. a JSON string `"true"` vs. a boolean `true`; a probe silent on the distinguishing axis does not ground it) or by a named implementer *obligation* stating the decided semantics or the exact fixture-and-observed-output command. A probeable fact whose direct probe is classifier-denied routes through the documented `python3 <path>` / `jq` fallback rung before the obligation arm is legal; a specification *choice* axis routes to Step 2's user-question machinery instead. Adjective-only language ("explicit `true`", "reads as exactly `true`") — including the #446 probe-present-but-silent-on-the-axis shape — is named non-conforming. (2) **Convention-matrix reconciliation.** When a Testing Strategy *enumerates* an input-shape or case matrix for a convention-governed surface, the issue carries the full convention matrix or an explicit named-and-justified narrowing, plus a one-line `governing conventions consulted: <sources by path, or "none found; searched <bounded list>">` discharge record whose search is bounded to `CLAUDE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, and the configured internal-docs path (the consumer prompt extension is the override point). The record is a claim the Step 3.6 auditor independently re-runs — flagging only a governing matrix at a path the line omits — not an attestation to accept (the degraded inline audit arm cannot deliver that verify-not-attest property, which its "degraded" summary marker signals). (3) **Unstated-mechanism-dependency sweep.** Step 3.5's steelman gains a mandatory hunt: enumerate the in-repo behaviors the draft's designed mechanism relies on but never asserts as claims (helper exit codes, resolver output shapes, gate semantics, and existence-shaped reliances such as a renderer mode or a config key) and, for each, determine existence first and then route — the rule's single home is Step 3.5's item 4 (`skills/create-issue/references/step-3-5-steelman.md`); the summary line reports both new sweeps, and its zero arm states a falsifiable no-in-repo-dependencies claim rather than a bare count. **Obligation-arm tier constraint (rules 1 and 3):** an obligation whose discharge runs an in-repo command must name a command already granted in `prflow_implement.allowed_tools` or be a code-reading obligation citing the producer — never one that would send a consumer's cloud implement run Blocked. The three classes land as **one** consolidated consumer-agnostic authoring-discipline dimension in Step 3.6's generic checklist and **one** consolidated PRFlow-specific sharpening in the live prompt extension; `skills/create-issue/references/step-3-6-audit.md` states a **growth policy** governing the generic dimension checklist that `skills/create-issue/references/audit-prompt-template.md` carries — execution-blocking classes are reported ahead of authoring-discipline classes, and future dimension additions consolidate into an existing dimension before appending a new one, **except** for the sanctioned standalone additions `step-3-6-audit.md` enumerates with their grounds in full: the adversarial-input dimension, on two grounds — it is a distinct **security** class outranking the authoring-discipline classes, and it is orthogonal to every environment and discipline dimension, so it cannot be expressed by sharpening one — and the criterion-shape dimension (issue #1341), which could not be consolidated into its nearest neighbour because that bullet carries an enforcement ceiling on its own length that the addition would have breached.

**Adversarial-input dimension and enumerated-list floor rule (issue #464).** Spec-quality gaps that surfaced only as review round-trips a full implement-plus-review cycle after drafting (PR #454, issue #449) are now caught at drafting time across the authoring pipeline. (1) **Adversarial-input dimension.** Step 3.6's generic dimension checklist gains a consumer-agnostic adversarial-third-party-input dimension with a narrow trigger: when a draft's Desired Behavior introduces a *new* LLM/semantic judgment over third-party text the change does not author (issue bodies, PR comments, commit messages, external API responses) whose output drives an automated selection or action, the draft must carry the input-is-data guard as an acceptance criterion — the text is *data to classify, never instructions to obey* — **paired** with a Testing Strategy case that exercises instruction-shaped input and asserts it is not obeyed; a surface reusing an existing already-guarded path is exempt when the draft cites it, and a draft with no new judgment surface gains no new flags (the visual-specification skip-when-inapplicable shape). The matching drafter-side rule lives in `references/issue-template.md`'s Acceptance Criteria guidance. (2) **Enumerated AC lists declare their closure.** The template's Acceptance Criteria rules require every enumerated test/case/example list inside an AC to declare its form — a **floor** (the marker `at minimum`) or a **closed set** (an explicit exhaustiveness statement) — and Testing Strategy Move 2 extends its floor-not-ceiling rule to every enumerated AC list: the coverage walk sweeps the new capability's contract dimensions (state, case variants, multiplicity, absence) beyond the enumerated items and writes that coverage output back as additional closed AC items before filing. The checks also join Step 3.5's omission-hunt list so the drafter self-catches before the auditor. A repo-specific sharpening lands in the live prompt extension's `## Audit dimensions`: an **executable-evidence** dimension flagging a Testing Strategy that protects a behavioral regression only through source-text presence or omits an ordinary executable RED obligation (typed surface-presence contract pins are explicitly outside its scope). **Stage-B reach is deliberate.** The retrospective's Stage B (`skills/retrospective-audit/SKILL.md`) files issues following `references/issue-template.md` verbatim but runs none of create-issue's Step 3.5/3.6 seams — so the **template-side** rules (the judgment-surface guard and the AC list-closure rule) reach Stage-B-authored issues **by construction**, while the **auditor-side** dimensions (the Step 3.6 generic-checklist dimension and the extension's executable-evidence dimension) do not. That auditor-side non-coverage is a **deliberate deferral**: Stage-B issues are human-reviewed before filing and re-derived by the implement run's Phase 1, so extending the audit seams into Stage B is a separate change if evidence shows leakage.

**Criterion shape, grounded-once apparatus, and a reported criterion count (issue #1341).** The Acceptance Criteria section had become the longest section in a drafted issue, and also the hardest to read, from two independent causes the drafting rules addressed neither of: sets so numerous nobody can hold them (measured 2026-08-05, issues #1273–#1278 carried 69, 46, 55, 46, 59 and 59 criteria — six consecutive issues, none below 46; a snapshot of that date, re-derivable with `scripts/parse-acs.py --format json` over each issue body), and individual criteria so long that each rule which fires expands one criterion into a paragraph, because the template required every criterion to carry its own supporting apparatus inside itself. A third defect cut across those causes — criteria describing what the diff must contain rather than what is true once it lands, which duplicates the `Relevant files` map and pins one solution, so an implementer who reaches the same outcome by a cleaner route reads the criteria as failures. Three changes, and **no rule the section enforced was dropped**. (1) **Post-change truth.** `references/issue-template.md`'s Acceptance Criteria guidance requires each criterion to state the fact true after the change, and **permits** the diff-shaped form where the criterion's subject is a surface the change must *not* touch ("the literals the test module pins are present verbatim after the change") — that shape is itself a post-change fact about the diff's boundary, so the rule describes the permission rather than banning the shape. (2) **Grounding stated once.** An optional prose **grounding block** may open the section (the opening sentence admits it) and carries the apparatus whose only readers are the drafter and the audit step; apparatus the *implementer* reads — a quantitative criterion's measurement instrument, an enumeration's `at minimum` floor marker or closed-set exhaustiveness statement, an obligation's named command — stays inside its own criterion. The rules state the reason the boundary falls exactly there: the implementing run mirrors this section into its workpad through `scripts/parse-acs.py`, and per `scripts/section_parse.py`'s contract only checkbox items are guaranteed to make that crossing, so an instruction placed in the block is unenforceable by the workpad-reading acceptance-criteria gate — while the issue **body** stays separately visible to the implementing run, so the block is never the only place such an instruction appears. The block sits inside the no-options gate's scan and carries no unresolved-decision language, so it needs no third carve-out beside `## 🚫 Blocked` and the `Relevant files` block. (3) **A reported count.** Step 4's audit summary line carries the criterion count as a **skill-composed** marker (`<n> acceptance criteria`), read from the length of the `acceptance_criteria` array produced by the Step 3.6 pre-dispatch parseability gate's `parse-acs.py --format json` invocation — its last invocation whose array was non-empty — which the run already performs, so there is no second parse and `scripts/issue-audit-state.py`'s reported field set is untouched. It is **reported, never enforced**: it changes no verdict, refuses no draft, and gates nothing, so an unsplit scope becomes visible to the approver at the moment splitting is still cheap without any count becoming a threshold. The trigger for the unavailable rendering is stated as the **absence of an established `acceptance_criteria` array from this run's own gate invocation** — not as a list of ways the invocation can fail, so an arm nobody enumerated still routes there: a run that could not run the invocation, one whose bounded rewrite loop exhausted without ever producing a non-empty array, and any other unestablished state alike render `acceptance criteria count unavailable` rather than a zero, and a hand count is never substituted. The count is also **as of the bytes that invocation read**: when any revision landed afterwards the line carries the residual inline — `<n> acceptance criteria (as of the audited draft; later revisions not re-counted)` — so the number is never read as a fact about the bytes being filed. Two further points are decided rather than incidental. **Placement is sorted by consequence, not readership**: a statement belongs in the block only if deleting it changes no criterion's truth value, so anything that narrows, bounds, quantifies, defines a term used by, or names a verification route for a criterion stays inside it however much that repeats — the four instruments above are that floor's commonest members, not its closed set, and the earlier readership-based wording was what let a depended-on term definition be sorted into the ungated block. And **the block opens the section**: placed after the criteria and indented, the same prose is welded onto the last criterion by `parse_checkboxes` and crosses into the workpad *with* it, so the gate reads a corrupted row rather than a clean one. The mirrored restatements in `references/step-3-5-steelman.md` (a criterion-shape check in the omission hunt) and `references/audit-prompt-template.md` are reconciled in the same change; the audit restatement lands as a **Criterion shape** dimension, and the dimension-growth policy's consolidate-before-appending rule was amended in the same change to enumerate it as the second sanctioned standalone addition with its ground — `authoring-discipline-defects`, its nearest neighbour, carries an enforcement ceiling on its own payload length whose stated margin the shipped fourth shape already nearly exhausts, so consolidating there would have breached that bound rather than honored the policy. The change ships **no new test pin** over the prose it adds; the three pre-existing generic-dimension *count* assertions (one in `lib/test/modules/create-issue-contract.sh`, two in `lib/test/test_render_audit_prompt.py`) moved with the added bullet because counting it is what they do. The repository's recorded position is that agent-executed prompt prose carries no automated regression coverage by design, with the review pass as the compensating control.

**The minimum-sufficient implementation brief, and the transfer boundary its over-retention audit shares (issue #1676).** The issue template already splits a draft into an **implementation brief** (the issue body) and an **investigation record** (a separate comment), but the routing rule was stated negatively — a reserved-content list that gave `Verified:` bullets unconditional body retention and an over-retention audit that only reported exact restatements and repository-inferable passages with a cited surviving home. The contract is now stated **positively**: the body transfers the decisions a competent implementer cannot safely derive — the problem and impact, observable desired behavior, non-inferable scope boundaries and decisions, behavior-altitude acceptance criteria, genuine hazards and dependencies, load-bearing premises, and the required machine-read sections — and does **not** transfer the investigation used to reach them. Investigation narrative, confirmatory evidence, audit history, lower-severity hazards, repeated prose, implementation play-by-play, mutable censuses, exhaustive path lists, and repository-inferable prose are **candidates** governed by a single test, never automatic removal classes: material moves to the record only when the **vanish test** establishes that removing it cannot change the implementation contract *and* the reserved-surface check establishes that no consumer requires it in the body. Severity, mutability, list shape, and implementation-detail form give no independent removal reason, and a load-bearing item stays regardless of its category and regardless of the resulting body length. `Verified:` bullets are now governed by that same test rather than unconditionally reserved — a load-bearing premise stays in the body while a confirmatory one routes to the record — and the drafting examples (an investigation-narrative passage among them) are inputs to the shared test, not a taxonomy. The **over-retention audit dimension** applies the same transfer boundary instead of treating its former finding types as the full population: it reports a repeated claim only when the copy serves no distinct consumer and no distinct enforcement role, and it **never** reports the required Desired-Behavior→Acceptance-Criteria projection or a copy a parser, gate, presentation surface, filing surface, or implementation consumer requires. Audit-driven and drafting revisions edit the brief as a document — a finding replaces prose it makes false or supersedes, consolidates copies that serve no distinct role, and deletes content whose decision already survives at every required home, while required projections, machine-consumer copies, and load-bearing findings remain even when consolidation would shorten the body. The contract carries **no measurement threshold** — no word-count, estimated-size, criterion-count, or proportionality gate decides whether content survives; the boundary is solely whether removal risks a wrong implementation and whether a consumer requires the content at that location. The rules live in `skills/create-issue/references/issue-template.md` (brief/record routing), `skills/create-issue/references/audit-prompt-template.md` (the over-retention dimension, consolidated in place under its existing payload ceiling rather than appended), and the shared `skills/create-issue/references/revision-delta.md` (the replace/consolidate/delete discipline every revision site invokes); the fresh-context audit, correctness checks, and the parser-consumed body surfaces are unchanged, and this change adds no acceptance-criterion provenance sweep.

**Hardening against the four dominant review-REJECT seed classes (issue #467).** Class-level prevention of the four recurring seed classes behind create-issue-authored issues that later drew review REJECTs, each landing as a consumer-generic rule in the skill and `references/issue-template.md` with PRFlow-specific sharpening in the live prompt extension. (A) **Universal-claim grounding.** The template's Acceptance Criteria guidance, Step 3.5's steelman, and Step 3.6's *Load-bearing assumptions* dimension all gain a universal-quantifier rule: every "never/always/each/every/all/cannot" the body asserts about the system under change (outside `## 🚫 Blocked`) is grounded one of three decided ways — pinned per-arm/per-element (an accepted-loss/suppression claim pinned by a fixture in which the suppressed input is *present*), scoped to the mechanism's supported form, or removed — with only mandated-verbatim boilerplate and rule-text-shipped-as-artifact-content exempt (an AC or Desired-Behavior sentence is never exempt however imperative), and a detector/guard coverage claim additionally carrying a planted-defect positive-control obligation. (B) **Executed enumeration.** An in-repo occurrence count or coupled-site/mirror-site list is a premise class: verified against the tree before it is asserted by an **executed whitespace-normalized search** (where the tier grants one), or — for a count not derivable from a text search, or a denied search — by the specific evidence records consulted record-by-record; never a recall count, and with no requirement to cite the executed command or its hit list in the issue body. Step 3.5 adds a paired **AC mutual-consistency** check (no AC forbids a surface another AC's discharge must touch). (C) **Verification depth.** A "code does X" premise is verified with the enclosing gates/conditionals and their defaults on the path to X (a claim holding only under a non-default configuration states that precondition inside the claim); Step 3.5's unstated-mechanism-dependency sweep extends to **stated-but-unbound inputs** (a mechanism input named only by role is bound to a named referent with a cited code reference, or carried as an implementer-obligation AC); and a **trust-boundary closure** rule requires an AC protecting executable artifacts to define the protected set over the transitive source/exec/import closure of its entry points (or state the residual unprotected surface). (D) **New-parser matrix.** The regression quality group's specialized-test-matrix rule (now stated in full in `references/quality-group-regression.md`) fires on *introduction*, not only narrowing: an issue introducing a **reader of input the repo does not itself produce** (historical records, user/reporter-controlled text, an external structured format, agent/human-mutable markdown) enumerates that input's malformed/boundary shape matrix with at least one production-realistic fixture, and a blanket testing-scope waiver is non-conforming. The best-effort-parser convention is widened at its three lockstep sites (`CLAUDE.md` gotcha, implement Phase 2.4, review-and-fix fix-delta gate) to name parsers over agent/human-mutable markdown and readers of new external structured formats as governed surfaces — each under the malformed-shape matrix appropriate to *its* input type, with the six-shape set staying the config-JSON matrix — and the extension's authoring-discipline dimension is sharpened to flag a matrix narrowed below the governing matrix appropriate to the surface's input type.

**Testing Strategy is a residual-risk supplement, and criterion-lifecycle accounting moved downstream (issue #1674).** The four hardening rules above accumulated a Testing Strategy that mirrored the formal contract: the template required every acceptance criterion to map to at least one named assertion and every assertion to map back, so a clear behavioral criterion was restated as a test that added no implementation guidance. That mirror is **retired** — do not read the §467 New-parser-matrix D-clause or the §1341 criterion-shape rules as still demanding a named assertion per criterion. `references/issue-template.md`'s Testing Strategy records **the concrete decided test plan its two drafting moves produce, including the cases that add information beyond the criteria** (issue #1729 retired the separate residual-risk selection step this §1674 point introduced, folding its surviving obligations into the two-move Testing Strategy and the quality-group references) — the residual risks a competent implementer in this codebase is most likely to get wrong: bug reproduction, non-obvious boundaries and failure modes, hostile third-party input paired with the input-is-data guard, the new external/human-mutable/agent-mutable input reader's specialized-test matrix carrying its `governing conventions consulted:` record, guarantee-class paths where the cooperative step was skipped (with idempotency where repeated enforcement is possible), and retry behavior — each naming the residual risk it covers and the contract it protects, never becoming a second criterion list. When no such case exists, Testing Strategy is one concise statement that the acceptance criteria fully express the verification contract. The residual-risk-only framing does **not** relax the surviving cases: a quantitative criterion keeps its exact command or counting rule (issue #1215), and the regression group's specialized-test matrix, hostile-input pairing, and skipped-cooperative-step floors remain intact. **The ownership split is the load-bearing point:** the Acceptance Criteria remain the exhaustive, merge-gated specification; **create-issue owns residual-risk guidance**, while **implement Phase 2 owns the exhaustive criterion-level verification-lifecycle accounting** — before implementation code, the Phase 2 test-first gate (`skills/implement/phases/phase-2-sweeps-contract.md`) enumerates every resolved workpad criterion row and records each one's existing lifecycle route (testable → named RED/GREEN assertion, with one assertion allowed to cover several rows it proves; genuinely-untestable Phase 2 deliverable → Phase 2.4 trace; documentation criterion → Phase 3.4 deferral then Phase 4.1 discharge; genuinely-live → the existing `(post-merge)` disposition) through the existing workpad note channel, so an uncovered criterion is visible before code is written. The producer side is described in [`implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md); the change adds no acceptance-criterion provenance sweep and leaves issue #1672's separate request-origin check independent.

**Code exploration, yes, just not the `code-explorer` subagent.** `/prflow:create-issue` *does* explore the codebase, that's precisely what lets it ask deep, well-grounded questions instead of generic ones. Step 1's `/prflow:docs-verify --report-only` surfaces current behavior and the **relevant files**, and the Step 2 clarification reads those (and adjacent code) to find **similar existing patterns and features** and understand **how the new feature would fit**: which is exactly how it surfaces real implementation forks ("where the codebase admits more than one way to build it") and recommends the best option. What it does **not** do is dispatch the heavyweight `prflow:code-explorer` subagent; the exploration is done inline by the orchestrator. The "do not re-explore the whole codebase" rule in Step 3 is a *draft-time* discipline, by then it works from what it already learned, doing only targeted confirming reads. The dedicated `code-explorer` deep dive is reserved for `/prflow:implement` **Phase 2.1**, where the goal shifts from *what/why* to *how*. The pipeline dispatches subagents at exactly two seams, and neither is a `code-explorer`. Step 1 dispatches its docs-verification **peers** (issue #749) — one on the shallow arm, two in parallel on the deep arm — so the survey's tool output stays in a peer's context and the orchestrator pays only the returned report. Step 3.6 dispatches the fresh-context **auditor** (issue #443) — the exact opposite of an exploration pass: deliberately information-starved (it sees only the rendered draft, never the drafting context), its whole value is *not* having explored the way the drafter did. In between, question-grounding clarification and drafting stay inline.

**Value-semantics grounding for `Verified:` premises (issue #2083).** The re-derivation handle rule above governs only a bullet's *form* — path plus verbatim quote — and says nothing about which code site a claim about a value's *meaning* may cite. `skills/create-issue/references/quality-group-premises.md` now adds a fourth premises obligation for that one claim class: a `Verified:` bullet asserting what a data value means — an on/off pair, the wider set of states it can hold, an enumeration's admitted values, nullability, or units — must be grounded in a code site that reads the value and branches on it, since a definition site alone (a schema column, a field declaration, a form binding) is consistent with the claim without establishing it. Before writing such a bullet the drafter searches the value's consuming sites and withholds the claim when a found site distinguishes more states than the claim admits. The rule is mirrored into the Step 3.5 steelman pass, which re-applies it to the assembled draft exactly as it already re-applies the enclosing-gates obligation. Every other Verified-bullet obligation is unchanged.

---

## 12. Deep dive: the retrospective loop

> **This is PRFlow's "it learns" story, the strongest differentiator for a keynote.** It is an **evaluator/optimizer** self-improvement loop for the `/prflow:implement` automation.

**The idea.** Every bot-authored PR leaves an evidence trail: review comments, post-bot human commits, CI signals, the workpad's final state, and the bot's own `## Devflow Reflection` friction notes. Once a week, `/prflow:retrospective-weekly` reads the accumulated trail, finds **patterns that recur**, and opens a **human-reviewed** PR proposing the smallest change that would prevent the next occurrence, a CLAUDE.md tweak, a skill rewrite, a missing doc, a new lint rule, a tightened issue template. Humans approve or reject.

**Detection (how a PR is selected).** `lib/scan.sh` selects a merged PR for retrospection via a **union predicate** — a PR qualifies when **any** of these holds, deduplicated by number:
- **(a) the reserved `PRFlow` provenance label, or its superseded `DevFlow` spelling** — PRFlow stamps the literal `PRFlow` label on every issue and PR it creates, and every selector accepts BOTH spellings so no pre-rename history is dropped (issue #1003; the API-side filter uses one `--search "… label:PRFlow,DevFlow"` qualifier, because `gh pr list --label A --label B` is an AND and would silently return zero candidates) (`/prflow:create-issue`, `/prflow:implement` at PR create, Stage B). The label is *created* via the idempotent best-effort `scripts/ensure-label.sh` (`/prflow:init` pre-creates the label at setup so it exists from day one) and *applied* via the shared best-effort `scripts/apply-labels.sh`. Both helpers transport through repo-scoped REST `gh api` calls — `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/labels` for creation and `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/{n}/labels` for application (a PR is an issue, so the same endpoint applies labels to both) — rather than the GraphQL-resolving porcelain (`gh label create`, `gh issue edit`/`gh pr edit --add-label`) they replaced. The porcelain's org-scoped GraphQL repo resolution needs `read:org`, which repo-scoped tokens (GitHub App installation tokens, fine-grained `repo`-only PATs) lack, so under those tokens the porcelain silently dropped the label; the REST path needs only the `repo` scope. The `{owner}`/`{repo}` placeholders are what address the repository on **both** tiers — `gh` fills them from the git remote, so a helper or skill body that could run outside Actions never interpolates `$GITHUB_REPOSITORY` (empty there, collapsing the path to `repos//…` while `gh` writes the 404 body to stdout for a best-effort capture to mistake for a result — issue #664). Only Actions-only surfaces (`.github/workflows/`, `.github/actions/`), where a checkout-less job has no remote to resolve from, address the repo through the environment. `lib/test/lint-gh-api-repo-path.py` enforces the split over the tracked-and-unignored tree, minus those two Actions-only prefixes and minus the corpora that quote the rule's own text (`lib/test/`, `docs/`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `.changeset/`, `.prflow/logs/`, `.prflow/learnings/`) and minus `.claude/worktrees/`, whose sibling checkouts belong to another branch and which this scanner's *working-tree* population would otherwise sweep in (issue #711) — that scanner's module docstring is the authoritative exclusion list and gives each entry's reason. Each helper leaves a specific stderr breadcrumb on `gh` failure — and since issue #455 `apply-labels.sh` breadcrumbs on **success** too (`devflow: applied label(s) '…' to #N`), so a caller reading the tool result can tell the outcomes apart: applied → the success line, API failure → the warning line, a caller arg-slip (a missing/blank/non-numeric number, or an empty label list — the #480 review) → its own warning line naming the slip, and **refused by the permission matcher → no output at all** (a denied command prints nothing, so without the success line "applied" and "denied" would be byte-identical). `apply-labels.sh` **always exits 0 and has no silent path**, which is what makes that no-output inference sound; `ensure-label.sh` likewise always exits 0 and has no silent path — a missing/empty `<name>` emits its own arg-slip warning to stderr and still exits 0, so a harness refusal stays the only silent outcome (its already-exists check is a bash `case` match, never `grep`, so a `grep`-less host still reports a benign already-exists as success). Since issue #1855 `apply-labels.sh` also reads the label list **from configuration itself** in a `--config-key <key> --config-fallback <value>` mode and prints exactly one **stdout outcome token** — the closed set `applied | nothing-to-apply | arg-slip | api-failure | config-unreadable` — as the primary channel callers route on; the stderr breadcrumbs above are unchanged for a human reading a log. Its label list is derived with bash builtins only — never a `tr`/`sed`/`grep` pipeline — because a missing PATH tool would empty the set silently and a caller that reads silence as a denial would then record one that never happened. This path is **author- and branch-agnostic**, so it works in any repo with **zero branch-naming configuration**. **Cloud implement-tier coupling (issue #455).** On the read-write cloud `/prflow:implement` tier, actually *applying* these labels requires two halves that ship on **independently-updated artifacts** and must be upgraded together: the `devflow-implement.yml` workflow must grant `apply-labels.sh`/`ensure-label.sh` in the explicit vendored-literal leading-token form the implement-probe table proved PERMITTED (this half arrives by **re-running `install.sh`**), *and* the skill must emit permitted command *shapes* at all four label call sites (Phase 3.1's provenance apply, Phase 4.0/4.0.5's deferred applies, Phase 4.1's docs apply) — single leading-token agent-level calls, never a `for`/piped-`while read` loop or a `VAR="$(…)"` capture wrapping a label helper (this half arrives by **bumping `prflow_version`**). Take only one half and the applies are **silently denied** — the PR and filed deferred issues carry none of the configured labels and the retrospective's label-first detection arm goes dark on cloud-authored PRs (see [`install.md`](install.md) → *the cloud implement-tier label grants are a TWO-halves upgrade*); **The same two-halves coupling covers the Phase 4.0.5 deferral-discovery helper (issue #555):** `devflow-implement.yml` must grant `Bash(.prflow/vendor/prflow/scripts/discover-deferral-manifests.py:*)` (re-run `install.sh`) *and* the §4.0.5 fence must invoke that helper instead of the old multi-root `find` (bump `prflow_version`). Unlike the label class, a skew here fails **loudly**: with only the skill half the discovery statement is refused and produces no output, which the reader routes to its fail-closed `discovery=[]` exit — a `dropped-failed` reflection and nothing filed, rather than the masked-exit-status silent loss the `find` shape produced;
- **(b) a watched author that closes an issue** — a watched-author PR with a non-empty `closingIssuesReferences`. This is the branch-naming-independent fallback that makes detection work even when the label step was skipped (e.g. PRFlow's own `issue-<N>-<slug>` branches, which match no prefix);
- **(c) the configured `implementation_branch_prefix`** — *only* when it is set non-empty (an empty/unset prefix excludes nothing and never degrades to a match-all glob).

The label (a) and closes-issue (b) paths mean the loop can no longer silently no-op when an adopter's bot uses an unrecognized branch prefix.

If any candidate-source fetch or jq-reshape hard-fails while assembling the weekly candidate set (a `gh pr list` error or a reshape failure on the provenance-label or watched-author batch), the scan sets a degraded flag and **exits non-zero with an `::error::` breadcrumb naming the gh/jq cause** rather than substituting an empty batch and reporting a partial set as "0 new PRs to retrospect" — a partial GitHub outage must not masquerade as a clean no-op (the same fail-loud posture the idempotency read path takes below). In the operator-driven `--prs` ad-hoc mode, a per-PR predicate *evaluation failure* (the jq `select` aborting, e.g. on a non-array `labels` shape) is reported with a distinct `predicate evaluation failed` breadcrumb carrying the jq diagnostic, kept separate from the `matches no retrospection path` breadcrumb that means the PR was evaluated and legitimately excluded.

**The workpad lives on the issue.** `lib/fetch-pr-context.sh` reads the `<!-- prflow:workpad -->` comment from the **linked issue's** comments (where `/prflow:implement` writes it), not the PR conversation thread. From it the bundle derives `workpad_final_status` (stripping only a leading canonical 🚀/🎉/👎/💥/🛑 glyph — by the known glyph set, kept in sync with `workpad.py`'s `_STATUS_GLYPHS` — so a multi-word status such as `In Progress` survives intact) and a `reflections[]` array of the `## Devflow Reflection` bullets. The bullets are now grouped by kind under `### ⚠️ Action required` / `### 💡 Improvements` / `### ℹ️ Notes` sub-headings (see the workpad-sections note above); the parser captures every kind bullet — glyph, and the bold-label prefix on the labeled kinds (a glyph-only `note`/`improvement` bullet is captured identically; useful signal for the retrospective LLM) — excludes the `### ` sub-headings, and parses a legacy flat block identically, so the `reflections[]` array's **count and contents are unchanged**. Alongside it the parser now emits a top-level `reflections_friction_count` — how many of those bullets are **friction** (every reflection kind EXCEPT the informational `note`: an `ℹ️` bullet under `### ℹ️ Notes` is exempt, but an `issue-accuracy` `📝` bullet sharing that section, any `### ⚠️ Action required` / `### 💡 Improvements` bullet, and any bullet under an unrecognized or missing heading all fail closed to friction). The `### ℹ️ Notes` heading literal and the `ℹ️`/`📝` glyphs are a coupled invariant hard-copied from `workpad.py`'s `_REFLECTION_SUBSECTIONS`/`_REFLECTION_KINDS` (pinned by `lib/test/run.sh`). `lib/cheap-gate.jq` forces LLM analysis of any run that left **at least one friction reflection** — a run whose only reflections are informational `note`-kind bullets is treated as clean (the note is still recorded verbatim by `lib/clean-entry.jq` into the clean entry's `reflections` field, not dropped) — that self-reported friction is precisely what the loop exists to learn from. The gate **fails closed** when `reflections_friction_count` is absent (an older bundle, or a bundle whose emission failed): it falls back to the legacy "any reflection trips" count, and `fetch-pr-context.sh` substitutes a friction sentinel when the reflection block is present but unparseable, so a missing or broken signal over-analyzes rather than silently skipping a friction PR. The issue-sourced `workpad_final_status` makes a non-`Complete` run — `Blocked`, or a stall-backstop `Failed` (💥) — gate correctly instead of being mis-read as clean (`cheap-gate.jq`'s clean condition is `workpad_final_status == "Complete"`, so a dead run gates non-clean with reason `workpad status not Complete`).

**Absent-workpad fail-closed (issue #626).** The cheap gate's clean set is `workpad_final_status == "Complete"` **only** — an *absent* workpad no longer launders past analysis. `fetch-pr-context.sh` emits a non-empty sentinel on each absent path — `NoIssue` (no linked issue resolved) and `Absent` (the issue resolved but carried no `<!-- prflow:workpad -->` comment), mirroring the present-but-corrupt `Unparsed` sentinel — each with a `::warning::` breadcrumb, so all four producer paths emit a non-empty value; a leaked `""`/`null` still gates non-clean at the gate with the distinct reason `workpad absent or status unknown` (defense-in-depth), while any non-empty non-`Complete` word keeps `workpad status not Complete`. The bundle also gains a top-level boolean `pr_devflow_provenance` — `true` iff the `PRFlow` provenance label (or its superseded `DevFlow` spelling) is on the PR **or** the resolved linked issue. A suite-driven helper `lib/dispatch-disposition.jq` then makes a **mechanical pre-dispatch disposition** with **zero LLM cost**: a non-clean bundle is *skipped* only when the gate reason is a workpad reason, the status is a sentinel (`Absent`/`NoIssue`), and provenance is `false` (a foreign, non-PRFlow PR whose only non-clean signal is a missing workpad audit trail — note `Absent` means the linked issue *did* resolve but carried no workpad comment, so the skip is not restricted to issueless PRs) — otherwise it is *dispatched* to Stage A exactly as today, so a bundle non-clean on any non-workpad signal (REJECT, an unreadable review-verdict signal, CI failure, post-bot commits, review comments) is always analyzed. Stage A analyzes a dispatched sentinel bundle under a **workpad-absent analysis rule** (a PRFlow run that lost its audit trail is analyzed from the remaining evidence, the absence recorded as friction, default verdict `imperfect`) and re-keys its two defined skips (interim, `Cancelled`) to a top-level `"skip"` key the orchestrator recognizes **by that key alone**, distinct from a genuine `"error"`. Skips are recorded as one-line report records (never silent); a `kind: "skip"` marker entry is appended for the **permanently-terminal** skips (the mechanical no-provenance skip and `Cancelled`) so the processed-PRs filter sees them handled, while the **transient** interim skip appends no marker and is re-scanned inside the 7-day lookback.

**Review-verdict arm — the durable-review union (issue #895).** The cheap gate's first (most-blocking) arm reads `signals.review_reject_outstanding`, `true` when a review REJECT has not been superseded by a later APPROVE; the gate reports the reason `outstanding /review REJECT` when it fires. That signal is derived by `fetch-pr-context.sh` from the **union of two sources**: the PR conversation comments (the historical scrape) *and* the durable bot PR reviews already fetched into `pr_reviews`. Reading comments alone failed open — when a `/review` run leaves no progress comment its whole verdict lives only in the posted review body, so a PR that merged over an un-cleared REJECT read as clean and was silently dropped from analysis. Both legs read the issue-#1030 producer marker first (see *The verdict marker*) and fall back to recognizing the verdict heading with the **same grammar** (1–6 `#`, an optional `/review —` wrapper, `Verdict:`, an optional `**` bold marker, then `APPROVE`/`REJECT`, case-insensitive), and the verdict token always comes from the body — never from a review's state. **A third rung (issue #1443) sits below that heading grammar** and is consulted only when the marker and the heading both yield nothing for an artifact: it reads only a `[bot]` author's first 30 lines, drops commented-out, fenced and blockquoted lines, requires a `Verdict`/`Review` anchor on the line it reads, or a `Verdict` heading immediately above it, and contributes at most one verdict — closing four shapes measured in this repository's own merged pull requests. Beside the union the bundle carries `review_verdict_unparsed_count`, an upper bound on the artifacts that survived every rung with no verdict yet carry a token in that window; it feeds nothing, so `review_reject_outstanding` stays derived from `review_verdicts` alone. A review contributes an entry when its state is anything other than `PENDING` (a plain equality test) and its body carries a heading; a `DISMISSED` review still contributes (the retrospective deliberately inverts the merge gate's dismissal rule, because a merge over an overridden REJECT is what the loop exists to study — a REJECT a later APPROVE cleared is handled by that APPROVE ordering after it). The union entries carry `{verdict, createdAt, source}` where `source` is `pr_comment` or `pr_review`; timestamp-less entries can only raise non-cleanliness, never lower it. Separately, the gate now **fails closed** on an unreadable review-verdict signal: when `.signals` is not a JSON object, or `review_reject_outstanding` is absent/null/non-boolean (a truthy JSON string like `"false"` must not read as an outstanding REJECT), the gate reports the distinct reason `review-verdict signal unreadable`, evaluated **before** every other arm so the unreadability is named rather than masked by the workpad-absent reason a non-object `.signals` would otherwise trip. That reason is neither workpad reason literal, so `dispatch-disposition.jq` routes such a bundle to `dispatch` (and it now guards its own `.signals` index against a non-object shape). Note the sibling framing above: the clean set being `workpad_final_status == "Complete"` **only** scopes the *workpad* axis — the review-verdict, CI, post-bot-commit, review-comment, and friction-reflection arms are co-equal siblings, each able to gate a run non-clean on its own.

**CI-failure arm — real red conclusions only, read across every page (issue #1441).** The `signals.ci_failures_during_pr` field counts check-runs on the PR's head SHA whose conclusion is a genuine red signal. It is a **denylist**, not a failure allowlist: `success`, `neutral`, `skipped`, a still-running `null`, and — since this change — the superseded conclusions `cancelled` and `stale` do not count, while `failure`, `timed_out`, `action_required` and any conclusion the filter does not recognize do, so an unrecognized future conclusion fails closed into analysis rather than being read as a pass. Excluding the superseded pair matters because a new push cancels the in-flight run for the previous head **by design**, so ordinary iteration was manufacturing "CI failures" and forcing paid LLM analysis on PRs that were never broken. In the other direction the read is now paginated (`--paginate` with `per_page=100`), since the endpoint serves a bounded page per request and a head with a larger CI matrix was silently truncated — the flag alone would not have sufficed, because `gh --paginate` concatenates one JSON object per page, so the filter merges them with `jq -n '[inputs]'` before counting rather than reading only the first page's object. The existing fail-safe posture is unchanged and extended: a transport failure, an empty body, a body carrying no check-run pages, a `check_runs` that is not an array, and a check-run element that is not an object each set `ci_status_unknown=true` with `ci_failures_during_pr` at the non-clean sentinel `1`, so an unreadable head is never reported as a clean zero — and the three limbs that can fire (a non-zero `gh` exit, an rc-0 empty body, and an unusable count) now emit their own **single-line** stderr breadcrumbs (newlines collapsed, because a caller splits this stderr into records), so a rate-limited query, an unparseable body and a genuinely green head are distinguishable. `gh`'s stderr is deliberately no longer folded into the captured body: under `--paginate` a non-fatal notice on any one request would otherwise poison the filter and report a healthy head as unreadable. The canonical field description lives in `lib/cheap-gate.jq`'s signal header and `skills/retrospective/SKILL.md`'s signal table.

**The LLM/heuristic split (a key efficiency claim).** Deterministic scripts handle *all* scanning, fetching, signal computation, gating, pattern math, and git/PR/issue mechanics. The LLM is invoked at **only two genuine-judgment points**:
- **Stage A** (`/prflow:retrospective`), a per-PR retrospective, **only for PRs that fail a mechanical "clean gate."** Clean PRs are processed deterministically at **zero LLM cost.**
- **Stage B** (`/prflow:retrospective-audit`), per-pattern issue-spec drafting.

```
scan.sh
  → fetch-pr-context.sh  (per PR)
    → cheap-gate.jq
      [clean]  → clean-entry.jq   (deterministic, no LLM)
      [not clean] → Stage A: retrospective subagents (≤3–4 concurrent)
  → materialize-retrospectives.sh
  → pattern-state.sh  (migrate v1→v2→v3 + reconcile lifecycle vs live issue state)
  → actionable-patterns.sh  (uses compute-patterns.jq)
    → Stage B: retrospective-audit subagents (return a ranked findings[] array)
      → select-findings.sh (compose/alias/rank/cap: which findings become filings)
      → meta-issue.sh  (one issue per selected finding + overrides.json lifecycle record)
  → build-experiment-records.py  (best-effort; joins cost ↔ outcome per merged PR)
  → open-state-pr.sh
  → post-status.sh
```

**Stage A** classifies each non-clean PR into a **fixed category vocabulary** (never coins new slugs): `doc-accuracy`, `fabricated-claim`, `outstanding-reject`, `lenient-verdict`, `deferred-verification`, `unmet-acceptance-criteria`, `incomplete-edit`, `convention-violation`, `unverified-assumption`, `issue-quality`, `tooling-gap`, `other`. Categories drive pattern detection.

**Stage B** re-derives the root cause from primary sources (it does *not* trust Stage A's summary), does a counterfactual analysis (false positives / over-broad application), and returns **one JSON object carrying a ranked `findings` array of one to three sub-patterns** — each with its own `subslug`, `title`, `body`, `evidence_prs`, and `rationale`, dominant first (issue #893). It reads each occurrence's own `summary`/`descriptors`/`suggested_interventions` (now carried on the pattern object, handed to it by path on disk) to cluster the sub-patterns, and it makes **no** edits and runs **no** worktree. `lib/select-findings.sh` owns which findings become filings **on this path** (the bare-`{title, body}` coexistence path below never reaches it and derives its own cap verdict): it composes each finding's opaque `<category>-<subslug>` filing key through the #891 composer, aliases a churned subslug onto an existing lifecycle record of the same category (equal **subslug** token set — the signature is taken over the subslug, never the composed key, so a subslug repeating one of its category's own tokens stays distinct instead of merging onto that category's record), re-checks the final key against the `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+` grammar after aliasing, ranks by descending evidence-PR count and truncates to the top three — naming the pattern and the dropped count in the run report — and asks the shipped `devflow_filing_cap_verdict` for each finding's cap decision. The orchestrator then files **one GitHub issue per selected finding** via `meta-issue.sh` (stamping the `PRFlow` and `Retrospective` labels and recording a `filed` `overrides.json` lifecycle record keyed by the finding's filing key — see the lifecycle subsection below). A human triages each filed issue and runs it through the normal `/prflow:implement` → review pipeline. A deployed Stage B subagent that still returns a bare `{title, body}` is filed under the bare category key, the coexistence path.

**Propose, don't dispose (a self-governance safeguard).** Stage B never edits the repo. Each selected finding of an actionable pattern — whatever surface its fix would touch (a skill, an agent, `lib/`, `scripts/`, docs, CLAUDE.md, config, or application code) — becomes **one GitHub issue** filed via `meta-issue.sh`, carrying the `PRFlow` and `Retrospective` labels and a re-derived spec of create-issue quality. A human triages it and runs it through the same `/prflow:implement` → review pipeline every other change gets, so loop-proposed engine changes get the same review — not a weaker autonomous-edit path. (Earlier versions auto-opened intervention PRs on a fixed audit branch for "safe" surfaces and only filed issues for engine files; that split, its worktrees, and the old audit retrospection kind are all retired — #152.)

**Recurring intervention targets (report-only, issue #520).** The weekly report carries a **Recurring intervention targets** section that surfaces the files/areas the accumulated retrospectives repeatedly point at, independent of the pattern-tag clustering above. `lib/recurring-targets.sh` (a thin arg-taking wrapper over `lib/recurring-targets.jq`, resolving `jq` via `resolve-jq.sh` and emitting `[]` for an empty/absent store) reads `.prflow/learnings/retrospectives.jsonl`, groups every entry's `suggested_interventions[].candidate_targets[]` by **exact target path**, and emits `{target, pr_count, prs, representative_summary}` for each target named in **≥2 distinct PRs**, sorted by descending `pr_count` then target ascending (both `.suggested_interventions` and `.candidate_targets` are `// []`-guarded; non-string targets are dropped via `strings` and empty-string targets by the separate `select(. != "")`; the intervention `.summary` is likewise `strings`-guarded so `representative_summary` is always a string — a wrong-typed one would otherwise abort the consumer's `gsub` render). Step 9 of `/prflow:retrospective-weekly` wires that output into the report summary JSON under the `recurring_targets` key, and `lib/render-report.sh` renders it under the omit-when-empty idiom (no section when nothing recurs). This view is **purely diagnostic**: it files no issue and writes no `overrides.json` dismissal state, so it surfaces recurring targets **regardless of category cooldown/dismissal** — a target a human has dismissed as a pattern still shows here as long as it keeps recurring. It is a **local/interactive retrospective-tier** feature only — no cloud-tier change.

**The pattern lifecycle (issues #788, #891).** `.prflow/learnings/overrides.json` is `schema_version: 3` with two top-level maps: **`patterns{}`**, the *machine-owned* lifecycle map, and **`dismissed{}`**, the *human-owned* durable escape valve. **Each lifecycle record now carries an explicit `category` string field and its map KEY is an opaque filing key** (issue #891) — the key is no longer required to equal a category slug, so a single attribution category can be spread across several sub-pattern records under distinct keys. Every consumer that used to re-derive the category from the key now reads the stored `category` field instead: status derivation (`lib/compute-patterns.jq`), the per-category filing cap, and the run report. The **cooldown lookup is the deliberate exception**: `lib/actionable-patterns.sh` keys it on the entry's own opaque filing key and never reads the stored `category`, so cooldown suppresses re-filing per record, not per category — which is what lets several sub-pattern records under one category cool down independently. `lib/pattern-state.sh` (invoked in Step 6, before `actionable-patterns.sh`) migrates an older file forward on read — v1→v2 converts only the loop's own former `dismissed` entries into lifecycle records and preserves every hand-written key, and **v2→v3 stamps each record's `category` field** — then **reconciles** each pattern's `filed`/`fixed`/`declined` state against the live state of **every** meta-issue on its lifecycle record, deriving the record state from the resulting entry set: an open issue → `filed` (`fixed_at` cleared); closed `COMPLETED` → `fixed`; closed `NOT_PLANNED`/`DUPLICATE` → `declined`; every closed transition stamps `fixed_at` from the issue's `closedAt`. `lib/compute-patterns.jq` then derives each pattern's status through six ordered arms — **`dismissed` → `regressed` → `declined` → `filed` → `fixed` → `open`** — with the lifecycle record's `fixed_at` taking precedence over legacy audit rows, so a pattern whose issue was closed but that recurs afterward becomes `regressed` and re-enters the eligible pool. Lifecycle-derived suppression therefore lasts exactly as long as the fix it waits on; only a hand-written `dismissed{}` entry suppresses permanently.

**Opaque filing keys (issue #891).** Because the record key no longer has to be a bare category slug, `lib/compose-filing-key.sh` composes a **collision-resistant opaque filing key** from a `category` argument and a `subslug` argument: the result is always `≤40` chars and matches `[a-z0-9-]+`, and when the naïve `category-subslug` composition would exceed 40 chars it is truncated and given a `python3`/`hashlib` sha256 digest suffix (width 8) so distinct inputs stay distinct. Both `lib/compute-patterns.jq` and `lib/compose-filing-key.sh` resolve their `slugify` function from the shared **`lib/slugify.jq`** module (included by each), so slug normalization is single-sourced across the jq derivation and the shell composer. `lib/meta-issue.sh` takes a required `--category` argument (slug grammar `[a-z0-9-]+`) and records it on the `filed` lifecycle entry it appends.

The **machine writer** of `overrides.json` is `meta-issue.sh` (it appends a number-keyed `filed` lifecycle entry per filing) and `pattern-state.sh` (migrate + reconcile). No **filing** path writes a `dismissed{}` entry: the filing and first-run-stub paths only ensure the map exists (`meta-issue.sh`'s lifecycle write carries a `.dismissed = (.dismissed // {})`, and each writer's first-run stub seeds an empty one). The only machine path that alters entries is the one-time v1→v2 migration: it converts the loop's own former entries (`dismissed_by: retrospective-weekly`) into lifecycle records — **removing** them from the map in the process — and preserves every hand-written key verbatim. That map is the one **durable off-switch**: a maintainer adds a slug key there by hand to permanently stop a pattern the lifecycle would otherwise re-raise, and a pattern closed `NOT_PLANNED` that keeps recurring is re-raised (as `declined` → `regressed`) and named in the run report alongside this off-switch, so the won't-fix decision is re-raised visibly rather than overridden silently.

**Run-report structure.** `lib/render-report.sh` (`devflow_render_report`) is the **source of truth for the report's section list** — read it rather than transcribing the sections here, since a hand-copied enumeration of a mutable set rots. Beyond the summary counts, analyzed-PR and pattern views, and the recurring-targets section above, issue #894 added surfaces that answer "did anything regress?", "was any evidence truncated?" and "how full is the filing queue?" outright: a **Regressed patterns** section lists every pattern whose derived status is `regressed` (a cumulative `newest occurrence > last fix` state over the whole history, not a this-run event, derived from the same `.patterns` view), a **Stage B evidence truncated by the audit bundle cap** section lists every *dispatched* pattern for which Stage B was delivered fewer occurrence bundles than the pattern has occurrences (a pattern excluded by the no-dispatch floor is reported through its blocker instead, never as truncated evidence), and a **Filing queue** line reports the aggregate `filing queue: N/M open` state (N = open filed meta-issue entries via `devflow_open_filed_total`, M = resolved `max_open_issues`, ` — at capacity` when both are established and N ≥ M, followed by a note that the ceiling is not absolute — a `regressed` pattern bypasses it, so a run can file while at capacity). The queue line reports state, not blame — it deliberately does not say "blocked" and does not read the cap token, and an unestablished count renders `unavailable`, never `0`.

**Filing back-pressure & eligibility config.** `min_occurrences` (default 2) is the occurrence-count gate — a `regressed` pattern always bypasses it. `cooldown_days` (default 3) skips re-filing a pattern whose youngest open filed issue is younger than that. A set of cap keys bounds filing volume: `max_issues_per_run` (default 3), `max_open_issues` (default 10, counted from `filed` lifecycle entries across all records; a `regressed` pattern bypasses this ceiling), and `max_open_per_category` (default 2, counted by `lib/filing-decisions.sh`'s `devflow_open_filed_for_category`, which sums the `filed` meta-issue count across **every** record whose stored `category` matches — not just the single record keyed by this pattern's filing key, issue #891). Separately, `audit_bundle_cap` (default 10) bounds the **Stage B fetch, not the filing volume** (issue #894): for each actionable pattern, Step 8a fetches context bundles for at most this many occurrence PRs, most-recent-first by occurrence timestamp, and `lib/audit-bundle-selection.sh` owns the cap validation, the most-recent-N selection, and the no-dispatch floor. When the cap (or a fetch failure) leaves a *dispatched* pattern with fewer bundles than it has occurrences, the run records a truncation entry (pattern tag, delivered, total, selected) that the report surfaces under a dedicated section. When **no** bundle is delivered at all — or the selection itself fails — `devflow_audit_dispatch_ok` clears that pattern's `dispatch` carrier and Step 8a records a blocker: it is dispatched to no Stage B subagent and files nothing, rather than having Stage B re-derive a root cause from metadata alone and file an evidence-free issue. A `0`/negative cap is rejected because a bundle cap of zero would starve Stage B of all evidence. A liveness `::warning::` fires when nothing is eligible while a pattern has occurred at/above `min_occurrences` and is currently suppressed (`dismissed`/`declined`/`fixed`), so a silently-exhausted loop is caught on the run it breaks. `occurrence_count` is cumulative, so this reads as "has occurred", not "recurs": a suppressed pattern that genuinely recurred would derive `regressed` and be eligible.

**Cost-weighted pattern ranking (issue #1828).** Frequency is the admission gate, but it is not the rank — the most-*reported* category is not the most-*expensive* one, so ordering effort by occurrence count spent the fixed filing budget on the loudest failure rather than the costliest. `lib/compute-patterns.jq` joins pattern occurrences to their PR's `efficiency_runs[].iterations` in the sibling `experiment-records.jsonl` and derives a per-pattern `cost_mean_iterations` (mean over the occurrences that have coverage) plus the `covered_occurrence_count` it was computed from; `lib/actionable-patterns.sh` reads that sibling and emits the actionable stream **ranked by descending cost** — covered patterns first, ordered by cost aggregate with occurrence count as the tiebreak, then every zero-coverage pattern after them ordered by occurrence count as before. The `min_occurrences` admission gate is **unchanged** — cost reorders, it never admits or excludes. The cost source is best-effort and fails safe to no-coverage: an absent/empty artifact (a repo that has not built one yet) or an unparseable one degrades to occurrence-count-only ranking with a `::warning::` breadcrumb, and — because coverage is sparse and a producer-schema regression that still parses as JSON (a non-number `pr`, a renamed `efficiency_runs`) would silently collapse coverage to zero — a producer-drift heartbeat `::warning::` fires when the real experiment file was present, non-empty and parseable yet yielded zero cost coverage for every pattern. An uncovered occurrence contributes nothing to the mean and an uncovered pattern carries a null cost (never a fabricated `0`), ranked by the explicit zero-coverage fallback. The cost is derived from `efficiency_runs[].iterations` and **never** from `post_bot_commits`, which is a coarse human-rework cheap-gate trigger — counting only positively human-attributable commits, with blank-login commits classified agent-side (#1440) — not a run-cost measure. This is distinct from `select-findings.sh`'s ranking of a pattern's Stage B sub-patterns by evidence-PR count (above): the cost weight orders *which patterns* reach Stage B, the evidence-PR count orders *which sub-patterns* of one pattern become filings.

**Suite-runtime supply side (issue #2010).** Beyond the per-PR pattern loop above, the weekly pass carries two agent-executed steps that keep the test suite itself within the cloud tier's per-command execution ceiling — the *supply-side* counterpart to the repository's fewer-tests-by-default demand-side stance. A **profiling step** runs the existing opt-in profiler `lib/test/profile-suite.py` (`run --with-modules`, so it ranks the whole run — sections, modules, and the Python pool — not just the default `monolith` shard) as a local-tier diagnostic launch, then files targeted follow-up issues to retire, speed up, or extract the top offenders. A **ceiling-tripwire step** reads the coordinator's latest `run-parallel: elapsed <N>s` figure from CI job logs and compares it against a named threshold — 85% of `BASH_MAX_TIMEOUT_MS` (owned by `devflow-implement.yml`; read by name, never copied), leaving headroom to act before a run drops to the shard-decomposition fallback — filing a suite-runtime maintenance issue on breach, or recording the new reading on the already-open one and filing nothing new. Both steps only read figures and file issues: neither gates any run on suite duration, because a duration gate flakes under host contention and this repository treats every FAIL as a real failure to diagnose. Both follow the loop's observe-then-file shape and dedup against the open queue, and both are skill prose in `skills/retrospective-weekly/SKILL.md`.

**Data (all in git, append-only ground truth):**
- `.prflow/learnings/retrospectives.jsonl`, one JSON object per processed PR (every record carries a `pr` field).
- `.prflow/learnings/overrides.json`, the two-map lifecycle state (`schema_version: 3`): a machine-owned `patterns{}` lifecycle map (each record carries an explicit `category` field under an opaque filing key, issue #891) and a human-owned `dismissed{}` escape valve (see the lifecycle subsection above). Unlike the append-only stores, this file is rewritten in place by `pattern-state.sh`/`meta-issue.sh` and staged into the state PR.
- `.prflow/learnings/experiment-records.jsonl`, one JSON object per **merged** PR joining that PR's run cost to its review outcome (written by `scripts/build-experiment-records.py`, above; unlike the two stores above it is **rewritten**, not appended — a re-run replaces a PR's line, keyed by PR number). See [`docs/internal/efficiency-trace.md`](efficiency-trace.md) for the schema.
- Pattern status (`dismissed`/`regressed`/`declined`/`filed`/`fixed`/`open`) is computed **on demand** by `lib/compute-patterns.jq` from the retrospectives corpus and the reconciled `overrides.json` lifecycle records.

All three files are staged by `lib/open-state-pr.sh` and land on the human-merged state PR, so `main` is clean entering Stage B. Because the corpus is **tracked and published**, `lib/materialize-retrospectives.sh` redacts operator home-directory prefixes (`/Users/<name>/`, `/home/<name>/`, `<drive>:\Users\<name>\`) to `~` on the merge write path before records are committed — a privacy backstop that preserves `/home/runner` CI-runner paths and every other string, and **fails closed** (aborts rather than commit an unredacted corpus if the redaction pass fails over valid JSONL). The primary guard is still the no-owner-identifying-data rule (see `SECURITY.md`), not this transform.

**Idempotent:** re-running processes only PRs not already recorded — `lib/scan.sh` reads `retrospectives.jsonl` from the base branch via the GitHub Contents API and subtracts the already-processed PR numbers from the candidate set. A `404` is the legitimate first-run case (the file isn't on the base branch yet) and leaves the processed set empty. Any *other* failure to decode the processed set under `HTTP 200` — an unparseable Contents-API envelope, a base64 decode miss, a `jq` parse failure, a body carrying neither inline content nor a `download_url`, a failed `download_url` fetch, or content that parses but yields zero `pr` records — **fails loud (exit 1) with a specific breadcrumb** rather than collapsing the processed set to empty. A silent collapse would re-queue the entire backlog and create duplicate retrospectives, so genuinely-corrupt state aborts the scan instead. The same guard (`_decode_existing`) covers both the inline `≤ 1 MB` content transport and the `> 1 MB` `download_url` fallback. **Never auto-merges and never auto-implements**: the maintainer merges the state PR manually and triages each filed issue manually — the loop never starts an implement run on its own filings.

---

## 13. The Scope-Acknowledged Findings contract

A structured handoff that prevents a deliberately-deferred Critical finding from being re-raised as a fresh REJECT by the next review run. The handoff, in order:

1. **`/prflow:review-and-fix` Loop Exit** runs a **widens-surface guard** (if the PR diff overlaps the deferred finding's file within ±10 lines, the deferral is disqualified, catches "refactor around a pre-existing bug then defer it"), and emits a **run-scoped** deferrals manifest.
2. **`/prflow:implement` Phase 4.0.5** discovers those run-scoped manifests with `scripts/discover-deferral-manifests.py` (per-root, status-bearing — see Phase 4.0.5 above; a failed or refused discovery files nothing and is recorded, never read as "no deferrals"), merges them into one slug-level aggregate, and files **one follow-up issue per source file** (with a `PR #<N>` cross-link).
3. **`/prflow:pr-description`** renders a Scope-Acknowledged Findings block in the PR body (a human-readable table + a hidden machine payload).
4. **`/prflow:review` Phase 4.0** validates each deferral against **three guards** and demotes matched findings to **Informational** before computing the verdict.

**The three guards (any failure rejects the deferral):** **trusted filer** (PR author in `prflow.allowed_bots`); **mutual cross-link** (the follow-up issue exists, is open, and references the current PR); **widens surface** (re-checked at review time). The contract is repo-agnostic.

**Settled-by-disclosure foreclosure (issue #621).** A non-blocking finding whose deliverable is an **already-shipped disclosure** — one below the run's `verdict_severity_threshold` and not a REJECT driver — can be recorded once with `skip_category: "settled-by-disclosure"` and a verifiable disclosure citation `{path, phrase}`, instead of being re-litigated every pass. A foreclosure files **no** follow-up issue (the disclosure is the deliverable), so it rides the manifest → PR-body block through the same implement-driven writers, and `match-deferrals.py` honors it through a **disclosure-verification guard** that **replaces the mutual-cross-link guard for this category only**: the guard passes when the PR's cached diff parsed to ≥1 hunk, the `disclosure.path` resolves to a file under the repo root, the `disclosure.phrase` is found in that file (whitespace-normalized), and `disclosure.path` is **not** a file the PR's own diff touched (a PR-authored disclosure never self-forecloses). It fails closed with reason `disclosure-unverified` (plus a `detail` naming the failed arm) on every degraded operand, so a disclosure that later drifts (reworded/moved/deleted) flips standing foreclosures back to a full-strength re-raise — accepted-and-surfaced, not mitigated. A honored foreclosure demotes a matched finding to Informational **only below the consuming run's `verdict_severity_threshold`** (there is no tracked follow-up behind it), and the honored report quotes the disclosure phrase for the human merge gate. **Disclosed limitations:** (1) cross-pass demotion applies only to foreclosures recorded during an **implement run's inline fix loop** (Phase 3.3), whose manifests Phase 4.0.5 merges in the same environment; a **standalone** `/prflow:review-and-fix` run's foreclosures are **in-run only** — they persist in an environment-local run-scoped manifest no later pass reads, a permanent limitation since the implement-side writers run once, before any post-PR fix cycle. (2) The locally-installed marketplace plugin and a consumer's pinned `prflow_version` are independently-versioned producer/consumer copies; that skew degrades visibly and fail-closed — an **old** vendored matcher rejects a new-shape foreclosure entry as `missing-follow-up-issue` until the pin is bumped.

---

## 14. The cloud tier: GitHub Actions architecture

Three PRFlow workflows remain in the tree — `install.sh` ships the first two into a
consumer repository (plus the repo's own `ci.yml`, whose **required** status check is the **`lib + python tests`** job, *not* `CI`, which is only the workflow `name:` and never resolves as a check — since issue #877 that job is an **aggregator** that recombines the pass/fail/skip tallies of a concurrent `shard` matrix (`monolith` + `python-pool` + `modules-*`) and fails the required check if any shard fails, is cancelled, is skipped, or is missing; the required-check name is unchanged):

| Workflow | `name:` | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `devflow.yml` | `DevFlow` | Light command listener: `/prflow:review`, `/prflow:review-and-fix`, `/prflow:pr-description` (event-driven only) — **shipped** |
| `devflow-implement.yml` | `DevFlow (implement)` | Runs `/prflow:implement` on a bare command comment — **shipped** |
| `devflow-runner.yml` | `DevFlow Runner (reusable)` | Reusable read-only runner. Its only caller was the withheld auto-review workflow, so nothing reaches it now; retained, not shipped |

`devflow-review.yml` — the auto-review caller that ran `/prflow:review` as a required PR
gate — was **withdrawn from this release (issue #936) and is not in the tree**. The
always-available review path is a repository collaborator commenting `/prflow:review`; a
consumer can *additionally* opt into an automatic CI-green `/prflow:review` request via the
documented snippet in [`workflow-triggers.md`](workflow-triggers.md). See
[Withheld from this release](#withheld-from-this-release-the-automatic-pull-request-triggered-review-tier).

**Key architectural facts (the engineering-deck details):**

- **The runner is split into its own file** because GitHub validates a called reusable workflow's permission ceiling against the caller's grant *across the whole called graph, before any `if:` runs*. Keeping the read-only runner separate from the high-privilege command job let the read-only reviewer call it without a `startup_failure`. That caller is withheld, so nothing calls the runner today; the split is why the file exists in its own right.
- **Coexists with Anthropic's Claude GitHub App.** PRFlow never creates or overwrites `claude.yml`. Every PRFlow trigger negates `@claude` so the two never double-fire (the *partition invariant*, enforced by tests). Anthropic's app owns plain `@claude` mentions, Q&A, and `/security-review`.
- **Triggers fire on real comments only, never descriptions.** A `/prflow:*` phrase in an issue/PR body or title must never start a run. Trigger text comes solely from comment bodies — since issue #1163 dropped the light path's two review-triggered subscriptions, no gate reads `github.event.review.body`.
- **`devflow-implement.yml` is issues-only.** The heavy `/prflow:implement` listener subscribes to `issue_comment[created]` alone, and its gate `if:` requires `github.event.issue.pull_request == null` (with `scripts/resolve-implement-trigger.sh` re-checking via an `IS_PULL_REQUEST` backstop). Because a PR comment is *also* an `issue_comment` in GitHub's API, this filter is what keeps a comment on a pull request — including the weekly retrospective's audit-report comment, which quotes the literal `/prflow:implement` phrase in prose on the state PR — from ever starting an implement run. `/prflow:review` and `/prflow:pr-description` in `devflow.yml` stay PR-aware and are unaffected.
- **A light `/prflow:*` command fires only when *issued*, never when *quoted*.** The light path (`/prflow:review`, `/prflow:review-and-fix`, `/prflow:pr-description`) stays PR-aware by design, but a command merely quoted in prose (a human review that says "as `/prflow:review` flagged…", PRFlow's own review narrative) must not be mistaken for the command being issued — a quoted `/prflow:review` in a PR **review** body was the reported self-trigger vector. `scripts/resolve-command-trigger.sh` (the authoritative gate; the workflow `if:` stays a coarse `contains()` pre-filter) closes this two ways: (1) **anchoring** — a command triggers only when it is the sole content of its own line (≤3 leading spaces, not inside a fenced or indented code block, remainder at most an optional `#`-number), via a shared markdown-aware POSIX-`awk` scanner (`scripts/detect-standalone-command.sh`) that is CRLF-tolerant, most-specific-first, and **fail-closed on an unbalanced fence**; and (2) a **self-marker guard** that declines any body carrying a PRFlow self-comment marker (the `<!-- prflow:review-progress` progress-comment prefix or the `<!-- prflow:workpad -->` marker) before authorization. See [`docs/internal/workflow-triggers.md`](workflow-triggers.md) for the full contract.
> [!IMPORTANT]
