> **The `devflow-review.yml` tier described in the rest of this section is WITHHELD from this release (issue #936).** The workflow is not in PRFlow's tree and `install.sh` ships none of the three tier files, so a fresh installation has no automatic PR-triggered review workflow — the always-available path is a collaborator commenting `/prflow:review`, and a consumer can additionally opt into having that comment posted automatically once their CI is green, with the documented snippet in [`workflow-triggers.md`](workflow-triggers.md). What follows describes the tier **as it behaved when it shipped**, and is retained as the reconstruction reference; a repository that installed it earlier still runs exactly this. See the withheld-tier note near the top of this document for the reasoning and the removal procedure.

- **`devflow-review.yml`** triggers on `pull_request` + `pull_request_target` `[opened, reopened, ready_for_review, synchronize]`, `check_run[rerequested]`, and — since issue #304 — the CI-completion re-trigger events `workflow_run[completed]` (Actions CI, which **must be named** in the trigger's `workflows:` list — a GitHub platform requirement, no wildcards; the list must cover **every** first-party workflow that runs on PR events, since `require_ci_green` waits on all of them but a deferred review re-fires only when a *listed* workflow completes — a gating workflow left off the list that finishes last strands the review at a neutral "waiting" check, issue #579; the `lib/test/check-review-retrigger-coverage.py` guard that enforced this was retired with the tier in issue #936, so a reconstruction must restore it too; consumer repos replace the list with their own PR-gating workflow names when installing) and `check_suite[completed]` (external, non-Actions CI apps; GitHub's recursion prevention means Actions-created suites never deliver this event), and — since issue #310 — the `status` event (legacy commit-status-only CI — classic Jenkins, legacy CircleCI — which reports via the commit-status API and emits neither `workflow_run` nor `check_suite`; the precheck filters to a green `state == 'success'` before a runner spins, and the route resolves the PR from the status head SHA since the payload carries no PR ref — it cannot self-trigger because this workflow posts check-runs, never statuses). It listens on `opened`/`reopened` (not just `ready_for_review`) because a PR opened directly non-draft never emits `ready_for_review`, without those events the required check would never run on such a PR. Bot actors route to `pull_request_target` (so secrets resolve); humans route to `pull_request`, runs exactly once.
- **Review-trigger preconditions (issue #304).** Before the first-review, `synchronize`, or CI-completion paths run the engine, the precheck evaluates two config-gated preconditions via `scripts/derive-review-preconditions.sh` (fail-closed on unverifiable queries — the check then says "preconditions unverifiable", never a condition the script did not observe; a consumer repo missing the vendored script fails **open** and reviews unconditionally; no hardcoded job names): `prflow_review.require_up_to_date` (the PR branch must not be behind the configured `base_branch`) and `prflow_review.require_ci_green` (every other CI signal on the head — non-self Actions runs, legacy statuses, external check runs — completed without failing; success/skipped/neutral count as green, and a head with **no** other CI is satisfied, so a CI-less repo is never wedged). The non-self Actions runs are first **collapsed to the latest run (highest `run_number`) per `(workflow_id, event)` group** (issue #351), so a superseded run — an approval-gated re-dispatch, a double-fire, a cancelled sibling — never gates the review once a newer run of the same workflow+event exists; a non-self run missing a numeric `workflow_id`/`run_number` or a string `event` makes the collapse unverifiable and fails closed. Signal sets (2)/(3) need no collapse — GitHub already returns the latest per name server-side. A surviving run awaiting manual approval (conclusion `action_required`, in either the Actions-runs or external-check-runs set via the shared green-gate) defers with the distinct reason `ci-approval-required`. On an unmet precondition the run **defers**: `create_check` posts the required `Devflow Review` check pre-completed **neutral** with a "waiting: branch behind base" / "waiting: other CI not green" / "waiting: CI approval required" title (each title is selected from the skip reason by `scripts/describe-skip-title.sh`, invoked in the `precheck` job and consumed by `create_check` via the `skip_title` output — the last is the `ci-approval-required` reason's own title), the engine never runs, and `finalize_check` is deliberately skipped (a skipped job check-run is excluded by the exactly-once gates — a finalize that ran would leave a `success` job check-run that reads as an already-run review and would wedge the re-trigger). Repeated deferrals are **de-duplicated**: a branch that stays behind base (or whose CI stays red) across several CI-completion events re-enters the deferral path each time, so instead of POSTing a fresh completed-neutral check per event, `create_check` looks up any existing neutral `Devflow Review` check for the **same head + same reason** (title) and PATCHes it in place (refreshing its details URL/summary), only POSTing when none exists — a lookup failure falls through to the POST path so the required check is never lost. The review then auto-re-triggers on the next `synchronize` or CI-completion event; the check's Re-run button forces a review past the preconditions (the `check_run` path is deliberately ungated). A neutral required check does not itself block merge — the freshness gate is a cost optimization; pair it with branch protection's "require branches up to date" if staleness must block. Setting either key to `false` restores the unconditional trigger for that precondition. When one of the script's `gh api` queries (the branch-freshness compare, the workflow-runs, combined-status, or check-runs fetch) fails, its fail-closed "query failed" stderr breadcrumb captures `gh`'s own stderr, so an operator debugging a permanently-deferred auto-review sees the real cause (rate limit, 403 token-scope, 5xx) rather than a bare "query failed". **Known limitation:** the deferral re-evaluation triggers are all head-scoped, so a `require_up_to_date` (behind-base) deferral is **not** re-evaluated when the *base* branch advances with the head untouched — it clears only on a `synchronize`, a CI-completion event, or a Re-run. The **producer-side mitigation** for PRFlow's own runs is the base-branch update checkpoints (`prflow_implement.update_branch_checkpoints`, on by default — see the config table below and [`docs/internal/implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md)): an `/prflow:implement` (and standalone `/prflow:review-and-fix --push-each-iteration`) run merges `base_branch` into its feature branch and pushes at its checkpoints — including pre-ready (Phase 4.3) — so it emits a fresh, non-behind head *before* the review fires — which in the common case avoids this behind-base deferral, and also satisfies branch protection's "require branches up to date" rule. It does not, however, re-clear a deferral that has *already* stranded on a base that advanced afterward — nor guarantee freshness when the base advances in the narrow window between the checkpoint push and the review firing — either of which still needs a `synchronize`/CI event or a Re-run. See [`docs/internal/workflow-triggers.md`](workflow-triggers.md) for the full trigger + preconditions policy.
- **Third-party model provider routing (opt-in, §17 `providers`).** Each of the three model-running workflow sections — `devflow` (`devflow.yml`), `prflow_implement` (`devflow-implement.yml`), and `prflow_runner` (`devflow-runner.yml`) — can route Claude Code through a named provider entry instead of the default Anthropic OAuth path — either an Anthropic-compatible HTTP endpoint (`auth: bearer`\|`api_key`) or Amazon Bedrock (`auth: bedrock_api_key`). A **single-sourced inline `jq` resolver** (delimited by `devflow-provider-resolver BEGIN/END` and kept **byte-identical** across all three workflows — `lib/test/run.sh` pins that identity; only the `--arg section` value differs) reads `.prflow/config.json` and emits a per-section decision: the named `providers.<name>` entry's `base_url`, `auth` (`bearer`\|`api_key`\|`bedrock_api_key`), `timeout_ms`, `effort_supported`, and `env` map, plus the resolved model. A `run:` step then injects into `$GITHUB_ENV` **only when a provider is active** (gated on `provider != ''`): on the `bearer`/`api_key` arms `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` (plus `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` for `bearer`); on the `bedrock_api_key` arm the secret as `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` and **no** `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`/`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` (Bedrock is reached via the `AWS_REGION` the `env` map must carry, not a base URL); and on every arm `API_TIMEOUT_MS` / the provider `env` map. So the default path carries no empty `ANTHROPIC_*` vars and renders today's OAuth wiring unchanged for a given `claude_model` (one deliberate change: an empty resolved model now fails loud instead of rendering `--model null`). The resolver **fails the job loud** on an incomplete provider entry (`auth` outside `bearer`/`api_key`/`bedrock_api_key`, or a `bearer`/`api_key` entry missing `base_url`); the inject step **fails loud** on a `bedrock_api_key` entry whose `env` map sets no `AWS_REGION` (naming the section and provider) and **warns** (never fails) if such an entry carries a stray `base_url`; the `claude_args`-building step fails loud on an empty resolved model, on a flag-shaped (leading-dash) model value or one carrying any character outside a conservative model-id allowlist, and on an effort value outside the schema enum (the flag-injection guard — those values are spliced unquoted into the CLI string, which the action tokenises shell-style, and a positive allowlist stays closed even where a bytewise whitespace blacklist would miss Unicode spaces under a non-UTF-8 locale); the inject step fails loud when the fixed `DEVFLOW_PROVIDER_API_KEY` repo secret is empty, it rejects an `env`-map key that is not a valid environment-variable name (the key is interpolated into the `$GITHUB_ENV` heredoc header, which the newline-safe value form does not protect), and it rejects a key whose *name* is on the deny list — credentials, job/Actions plumbing, interpreter and loader hooks, and `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` (the names and the reasoning are canonical in `docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`) — never silently degrading. `--model` is always passed; `--effort` is dropped only for a provider whose `effort_supported` is `false` (the default path resolves `effort_supported=true`, preserving effort). On the `bearer`/`api_key` provider path the action authenticates via `anthropic_api_key` (the fixed secret) with an empty `claude_code_oauth_token`; for `bearer` auth the same key is also exported as `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`. On the `bedrock_api_key` arm the action instead receives `use_bedrock: true` and an **empty** `anthropic_api_key`, authenticating via the `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` export (the Bedrock switch travels as that action input, not a job-env export, because the action's step-level `env` would blank a job-env `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`). **Security:** `devflow-runner.yml` (the privileged reviewer, which runs on the PR head under a write token) resolves its provider decision from the **trusted base-ref config** — the same source as `provision_env` / `setup` / `allowed_tools`, never the PR head — so a malicious PR cannot add a rogue provider and redirect the review to an attacker endpoint to exfiltrate the API key; and on the runner the provider endpoint (including, for `bearer`, the key exported as `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`) is injected **after** the opt-in `provision_env` build step, so a `provider` + `provision_env` combination never exposes the key to PR-author-controlled build code. When that trusted base config is unreadable (fetch failure or malformed), the runner reverts to the Anthropic default with a loud warning that names the provider-routing revert (not just `provision_env`), and the OAuth-default guard reports the unreadable config rather than falsely claiming no provider is configured. The runner's `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` secret is therefore `required: false` (it still fails loud when absent *and* no provider is configured), and its dead `model` input was removed. See §17 `providers` / `docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`.
- **`finalize_check` job is named `Devflow Review`** so the required-status-check matcher resolves the ruleset context name to a workflow *job* name (otherwise the check never links and the PR's merge state stays BLOCKED).
- **Stall backstop (`prflow_implement.stall_backstop`).** A headless single-shot `claude-code-action` run can end mid-lifecycle yet report `success` — the SDK ends the session the moment the model emits a tool-call-free turn, so a run that stops at (say) the Phase 2→3 boundary leaves the workpad frozen at an interim `Status` while the job goes green. The agent-side terminal-status self-check cannot fire once the session has ended, so a **workflow-level backstop** runs after the `claude` step and keys on the issue workpad `Status` (via `workpad.py status`, **never** PR draft state): a terminal `Status` is no longer uniformly a no-op (issue #1025): 🎉 `Complete` remains a no-op (the job stays `success`), a stale 🛑 `Cancelled` read (on a non-cancelled job) is left green — never converted to a failure — while 👎 `Blocked` and 💥 `Failed` conclude the job **non-`success`**, the step emitting a `::error::` that names the issue number and the workpad `Status` and exiting non-zero, so `gh run list` plus the annotation distinguish a blocked/failed run from a completed one without opening the workpad (the workpad is left unchanged — its 👎/💥 is already truthful — and `workpad.py status` names each terminal glyph with its own decision class rather than collapsing them, so this split is decided on a parsed class, not a downstream glyph match); an interim `Status` (🚀) auto-resumes `/prflow:implement <n>` up to `stall_backstop.max_resume_attempts` (default `2`) with a distinct audit comment per attempt (the resumed run does **not** resume Phase 2 in place — the `code-explorer`/`code-architect` artifacts are non-persistent, so the fresh process re-enters Phase 1 and the **Phase 2 §2.0 resume-idempotency gate** builds on the committed `## Plan` instead of re-running discovery/architecture, per the Phase 2 section above), and an exhausted cap or an unreadable status fails the job loud with a distinct comment — "unreadable" covers a missing/empty `Status` line **and** a present `Status` word that isn't in the canonical vocabulary (`workpad.py status` exits 1 for either shape, exit 2 for no workpad, never misreading a corrupted word as a healthy in-progress stall). A distinct **`auth-failure`** class handles a `gh`-api/transport/auth failure reading the workpad (`workpad.py status` exit **3**, e.g. an expired App installation token, or a failed comment-list fetch): it fails loud with an auth-specific comment **without consuming a resume attempt** and is never mislabeled `unreadable`, so a healthy workpad behind an expired token is not misclassified as corrupt (issue #287). To keep that read from failing on a long run, the backstop mints a **fresh** App token just-in-time immediately before it runs, rather than reusing the token minted at the start of the (possibly >60-minute) `claude` job. `stall_backstop.enabled` (default `true`, safe-direction on any unrecognized value) is the master switch; disabled or absent, the job behaves exactly as before. The decision is a pure, unit-tested helper (`scripts/stall-backstop-decide.sh`) and the comments go through the best-effort repo-scoped REST `scripts/post-issue-comment.sh`, so the backstop can detect-and-fail-loud honestly rather than masquerading a stalled run as green. The thin `Stall backstop` step (`if: always()`, after the observability-persist backstop in the `claude` job) wires these together, counting prior attempts via the `<!-- prflow:stall-backstop-audit -->` marker each resume comment carries; only the resume comment carries the trigger phrase. Auto-resume additionally requires the optional `DEVFLOW_APP_ID` App token (a `GITHUB_TOKEN`-authored comment never re-triggers a workflow — a resume decided without the App token posts its comment and then fails the job loud rather than reading as green) and that App's bot login in `prflow.allowed_bots`; the fail-loud and audit-comment behavior works under either token. **Dead-run `Status` flip (issue #356).** On every fail-loud exit taken after successfully reading a genuinely interim `Status` — cap exhausted, the `mktemp` abort, the dropped-resume-comment abort, and the resume-posted-but-no-App-token abort — the step first best-effort-flips the workpad to the new terminal word **`Failed`** (glyph **💥**, added to `workpad.py`'s closed status model) with a note linking the dead run, then exits with its existing code, so a dead run stops lying at `🚀 Implementing`. The flip is guarded on the `interim` class (a terminal/unreadable/auth-failure `Status` is never clobbered) and never runs on the green resume path (a terminal `Failed` written before a resume would make the resumed run's own backstop read a decided terminal end — pre-#1025 a silent `terminal→noop` disarm, post-#1025 a spurious `fail-blocked` — so the flip must never precede a resume). It is best-effort — a failed flip emits a `::warning::` and never changes the exit code — and 💥 has no reaction equivalent. **Cancelled-run exclusion + flip (issue #498).** A cancelled run (job.status=cancelled) is a decided ending, not a stall: the backstop reads `job.status` and, only on the exact value `cancelled`, resumes nothing — an interim `Status` flips to the new terminal word **`Cancelled`** (glyph **🛑**, the sibling of 💥) with no comment and no resume attempt consumed, while terminal/unreadable/auth-failure classes log and exit green (no fail-loud diagnostic). Every other `job.status` value leaves the table above byte-identical (fail toward resume). 🛑 joins 💥 as a workpad-only glyph with no reaction equivalent; a fresh `/prflow:implement <n>` re-trigger on a `Cancelled` workpad resumes through Phase 1's terminal-`Status` re-trigger arm, exactly like a `Failed` one. **Prevention layer (issue #415).** The backstop above is the deterministic *convergence* net; a coordinated *prevention* layer reduces how often the early-quit fires in the first place, mirroring the review tier's #408/#410 fix. **(1) Headless-wait discipline (prose).** The injected engine-ground-truth block (`scripts/render-grounding-block.sh`, rendered in `MODE=implement` for this tier) tells the orchestrator this is a headless run: ending the turn ends the process; never end the turn while any dispatched agent (a Phase-2 `code-explorer`/`code-architect`, the Phase-3 inline `review-and-fix` agents, the Phase-4.1 `prflow:docs` subagent, any Agent-tool subagent) has not returned; poll to keep the turn alive; treat `ScheduleWakeup`/future task-notifications as unavailable. It first lived as an always-resident cross-phase-rules section of `skills/implement/SKILL.md`, cloud-conditioned on `GITHUB_ACTIONS`; the block is now its sole home, so it arrives ahead of the skill body rather than depending on that body surviving nested-skill eviction, and the block's implement-mode clause names the Phase-3 inline review pass among the dispatch points it binds. A one-line mirror rides inside the stall-backstop resume comment (`devflow-implement.yml`), so a resumed run receives it in the comment as well as in its own injected block. **(2) Probe-verified `ScheduleWakeup` denial.** `.github/workflows/matcher-probe.yml` carries a `schedulewakeup-probe` job that runs a `claude-code-action` session with `--disallowedTools ScheduleWakeup`, has the model attempt one `ScheduleWakeup` call (bracketed by two positive controls, with the tool granted in `--allowed-tools` so the flag under test is the only removal cause), and derives a deterministic DENIED/AVAILABLE/REMOVED/INCONCLUSIVE verdict from the execution file (never the model's text). The verdict gates whether `devflow-implement.yml`'s `claude` step ships `--disallowedTools ScheduleWakeup`: a tool-removed/denied verdict ships the flag plus a `lib/test/run.sh` pin; a tool-still-available verdict ships no flag and records the omission rationale on the PR — the same probe-before-grant discipline the matcher-probe corpus already uses. Local/interactive runs are untouched: the rule is cloud-scoped and the flag is added only to the cloud `claude` job. **Executed (issue #418); verdict WITHDRAWN and awaiting re-measurement (issue #1527):** the earlier **AVAILABLE** reading across real cloud runs 29140791165 and 29138117625 was produced by the pre-#1527 false-positive attempt match — the verdict helper substring-matched `schedulewakeup` against each `tool_use`'s *input* JSON, so a `ToolSearch` query naming ScheduleWakeup registered as an attempt when **no** real `ScheduleWakeup` `tool_use` was ever recorded on either run. Under the corrected name-scoped helper those transcripts (no ScheduleWakeup `tool_use`, no denial) read INCONCLUSIVE, not AVAILABLE, so that verdict measured nothing and the ship/no-ship question is unresolved — it awaits a fresh run of the `schedulewakeup-probe` job. No flag or `--disallowedTools`-flag `lib/test/run.sh` pin shipped (the unrelated #408/#415 ScheduleWakeup prose/verdict-helper pins are untouched). At that point the early-quit prevention rested on the headless-wait prose alone; **issue #801 added a deterministic harness floor beside it** — each cloud engine step (`devflow-runner.yml`, `devflow-implement.yml`, `devflow.yml`) now sets `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS: "1"`, which the vendor documents as keeping subagents in the foreground, so a dispatch's results are in hand before the turn continues rather than arriving on a later turn a headless run never reaches. Unlike the `--disallowedTools` flag above it ships **unconditionally rather than probe-gated**, a deliberate divergence recorded as such: that flag *removed* a tool, so shipping it on an unconfirmed premise risked breaking a working run, whereas this variable is **inert when ignored** — a runner that does not honor it leaves today's behavior exactly as it is. **Its documented scope is wider than subagents:** upstream it disables *all* background task functionality, which includes the `Bash` tool's `run_in_background`. No engine surface asks for a backgrounded `Bash` today, so that breadth is inert here — but a future step that wants one would find it blocked on the cloud tier, and the remedy is a scoped design decision on this variable, not a local workaround. Beside it, the injected block states the requirement **behaviorally** rather than by naming a mechanism (a launch acknowledgment is never the return), naming the per-dispatch `run_in_background: false` lever the engine itself controls; the cloud tier supports Claude Code headless only, so it names that one lever outright rather than hedging across runtimes. The requirement is stated as **collect-before-proceeding** — first in the two engine roots (the implement root since issue #1254, the review root since issue #1365) and, since those copies were consolidated into the renderer, in the block alone: every dispatched result is collected before the orchestrator proceeds past the dispatch point and before the turn ends, and more than one dispatch may be outstanding at a time provided all are collected within the turn — the meaning the review engine already relies on when it batches up to eight verifiers under this same barrier, so concurrent dispatch rests on a rule that plainly allows it. **Executed (issue #812):** whether the variable takes effect inside `claude-code-action` is now **observed**, not assumed. `matcher-probe.yml`'s `background-tasks-probe` job runs a `claude-code-action` session carrying `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS: "1"`, dispatches one subagent, and derives a FOREGROUND/BACKGROUNDED/NOT_DISPATCHED/INCONCLUSIVE verdict from the execution file (`scripts/background-tasks-probe-verdict.py`; the model's text is never the measurement). It measured **FOREGROUND** across real cloud runs 30210679122 and 30211584731: in each, the top-level session echoed the subagent's own returned marker back through Bash in the same turn — harness-recorded evidence, under the probe's cooperative-model assumption, that the completed result was in hand before the turn continued — with both bracketing positive controls run and no `permission_denials`. So the harness floor is **observed effective** on this action version, and the early-quit prevention no longer rests on the headless-wait prose alone. The run also recorded the dispatch tool under the name `Agent` rather than `Task` — which the probe's both-names grant already covered, and which its name-agnostic input match is designed for. **Both** verdicts in this bullet are **version-dependent** — each is a dated observation of one `claude-code-action` version, not a platform contract — so **re-probe the ScheduleWakeup result via the `schedulewakeup-probe` job, and this one via the `background-tasks-probe` job, after a `claude-code-action` upgrade before trusting either**. An additional sibling job in the same `matcher-probe.yml`, `implement-probe` (issue #450), applies the same execution-file measurement to the read-write `devflow-implement` tool profile — a separate allowlist from the read-only `review` profile the existing `probe` job measures, so a command shape or grant form proven on one tier is unproven on the other — establishing the human-dispatched implement-tier evidence of record for the label-helper grants; like the rest of `matcher-probe.yml` it is repo-internal and not shipped by `install.sh`.
- **Local-tier Stop-hook backstop (`lib/implement-stop-guard.sh`, issue #362).** The stall backstop above is cloud-only — **not** because `Stop` hooks are unavailable there (`claude-code-action` removes `.claude/` and then **restores it from the base branch**, so a base-registered `Stop` hook executes inside the action: observed **`FIRED`** by probe run `29224205805`, recorded in [`docs/internal/execution-file-shape.md`](execution-file-shape.md) and relied on by the Stop-hook trusted-source floor below), but because the cloud net must read the workpad `Status` *after* the `claude` step ends, which no hook inside the session can do — and because this guard is repo-local and ships to no consumer. So an unattended *local* run that dies mid-phase has no deterministic net. This repo wires a second `Stop` hook that is **marker-gated**: Phase 1.3 writes a gitignored `.prflow/tmp/implement-active-<issue>` marker — recording the run's owning session id as the marker's first line when the runner exports one (`CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, the same value the Stop payload carries as `session_id`), empty otherwise — and the always-resident *Outcome reaction* block removes it at every terminal `Status` transition. With no marker present the guard exits on a pure-bash glob, having spawned no interpreter and made no network call (only the one local `git rev-parse` its repo-root resolver runs), so ordinary sessions are unaffected; with one, it reads that issue's live workpad `Status` (`workpad.py status`, the source of truth; the marker only gates *whether* to ask) and, on an `interim` class, blocks **only** when the marker is owned by the stopping session or carries no usable owner — exiting **2** (the documented Stop-hook blocking code) with a stderr instruction naming the issue and status word. A marker whose first line is a well-formed session id belonging to *another* live session never blocks the stopper (issue #1222): a non-owner cannot drive that run's workpad terminal, so the guard prints an issue+status breadcrumb, writes no sentinel, and keeps scanning — the block is reserved for the run that can actually act on it. Ownership is compared like with like (the recorded owner and the stopper's id each non-empty and filename-safe), so every absent/blank/malformed/unreadable owner — including today's zero-byte markers — fails **closed** and blocks as before. A `session_id`-keyed sentinel bounds it to at most one block per session, and every ambiguous path (unreadable workpad, `gh` transport failure, unparseable hook input, failed sentinel write) fails **open**. A stale marker self-heals regardless of owner: a live status that reads terminal deletes it. The guard is **repo-local by design** and ships to no consumer repo. It complements three always-resident cross-phase rules in `skills/implement/SKILL.md` that prevent the death rather than catch it — a generalized mid-phase re-anchor after every Skill-tool return, a non-interactive self-answer rule for a nested skill's user-facing question, and a subagent-dispatch path for convention-mandated *interactive* skills (a mid-phase Skill invocation is a tail call: the run ends on the nested skill's final approval step). Full treatment: [`docs/internal/implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md).
- **Resume detects an existing PR (`agents/branch-setup.md` §1.4, issue #362; the §1.4 branch procedure moved into the dispatched branch-setup subagent by issue #1582).** Before the linked-worktree signal, a run reads the workpad `**Branch:**` line and queries the issue's open PRs (by head branch *and* by body reference), then continues that PR's head branch — inside an existing linked worktree when one holds it — instead of forking a duplicate branch and PR. With no workpad `Branch` line and no open PR, §1.4 is unchanged.
- **Stale-checkout guard for adopted branches (`agents/branch-setup.md` §1.4, `phase-1-setup.md` §1.6, `phase-2-implement.md` §2.1, issue #429).** A run that *adopts* a pre-existing branch (the worktree/`USE_CURRENT` path) used to skip the base fetch, so every later verification read a tree that could be days behind the base — the #325 incident falsely refuted a *true* "already shipped in PR #319" claim against a 43-hours-stale checkout and re-implemented merged work into a dirty merge. Four bounded prose rules close it (no helper, workflow, allowlist, or config change; consumers inherit through the shared skill): a **freshness guard** on §1.4's adopted-branch arm fetches the base, records how far behind `origin/$BASE` the tree is (behind-by-0 included, so freshness is *checked* not assumed), and on a fetch failure records a freshness-unverified reflection and continues; a **read-target rule** (§1.6 + §2.1 coupled mirrors) points shipped-work verification reads at `origin/$BASE` state when behind, when unverified, or when no freshness record exists at all (the workpad write is best-effort, so a missing record fails closed as unverified rather than reading as behind-by-0); a **cross-pass coherence rule** (same two mirrors) refuses to REFUTE a "shipped in PR #N" claim on tree reads unless a read-only `gh pr view` shows PR #N MERGED *and* `git merge-base --is-ancestor` confirms the merge commit is in the checkout — MERGED-but-absent or any indeterminate outcome yields "checkout stale — refresh and re-verify", never "code wins"; and a **sibling-PR annotation rule** (§4.0) requires a composed already-shipped annotation to name the sibling PR and its merge state at filing time.
- **Ahead-of-base branch-state preflight — Verdict B (`agents/branch-setup.md` §1.4.0.5, `scripts/preflight.py branch-state`, issue #576; the Verdict-B procedure moved out of `phase-1-setup.md` into the dispatched branch-setup subagent by issue #1582).** The freshness guard above derives only the *behind*-by count, so a branch that is not behind base can still carry unrelated **ahead-only** history that every downstream step treats as the run's own — §1.5 publishes those foreign commits into the PR diff (the PR #524 incident: four unrelated files forked from an unpushed local-`main` commit that read "behind-by-0 / up to date"). On the adopted-branch arm (`USE_CURRENT`) and — since issue #780 — the landed-resume arm (`LANDED`, the population #779 deliberately left unwidened even though the checkpoint below it was already arm-independent), after the freshness record on the adopted arm and, on either arm, **before** the end-of-§1.4 update-branch checkpoint and the §1.5 push, §1.4.0.5 writes the state it holds to `.prflow/tmp/branch-state-$ISSUE_NUMBER.json` (Write tool) and invokes the `scripts/preflight.py branch-state` subcommand, which derives the ahead-of-base count (shallow unshallow-once-then-rederive), recorded-branch existence, and published-tip reachability, then emits **one verdict token + matching exit code** (mirroring `update-branch-checkpoint.sh`'s one-token-stdout contract): `FRESH`/`VALIDATED_RESUME` → 0 (proceed), `AMBIGUOUS`/`DECISION_BLOCKED` → 2 (needs a human decision — flip the workpad `Blocked`), `UNAVAILABLE` → 3 (an unestablished measurement — fail closed to `Blocked`). Any non-zero exit stops the run before the end-of-§1.4 checkpoint invocation and §1.5. The subcommand is **read-only w.r.t. git history** (rev-list/rev-parse/check-ref-format/merge-base plus a single shallow deepen; it never resets, rebases, checks out, commits, merges, pushes, or deletes), so a stop verdict makes no history mutation. **Ahead history may be vouched for by more than one provenance source (issue #780):** the **workpad** (`provenance_established`), and the **open-PR linkage** — an open PR in *this* repository whose head branch is the working branch, which is not cross-repository, and which is tied to this issue either by closing it or by having been selected by the pre-check's head-branch query. The second is what makes the landed-resume arm classifiable at all, since that arm has ahead history by definition while its workpad provenance is unestablished for a cloud run whose §1.3 `HANDOFF` is `unknown` and for a local resumed run that created no workpad of its own — a workpad-only gate turned each of those populations into a terminal stop. The workpad wins wherever the two sources overlap; on the PR-vouched path the untrusted workpad is **neutralized, not consulted**; and a partial gather of the PR operands is refused rather than read as a refutation. The canonical statement of the admission and its threat model — including the honest population-overlap caveat and the narrower scope the screen has on the landed-resume arm — is [`docs/internal/implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md)'s *Two provenance sources for ahead history* section; this bullet is its coupled summary. Adds the `preflight.py` subcommand and the §1.4.0.5 prose only — no workflow, allowlist, or config surface; consumers inherit through the shared skill and vendored helper.
- **Review-progress dead-run upsert (issues #356, #1054 and #1154).** The review engine maintains a run-keyed `<!-- prflow:review-progress run=<id>-<attempt> -->` comment at `🚀 Reviewing`. On a cloud run, `scripts/seed-review-progress.sh` derives the marker from `GITHUB_RUN_ID` and `GITHUB_RUN_ATTEMPT`, writes it as line 1 of the created body and reports the exact literal the engine reuses for later rewrites. The engine composes a marker only for the timestamp-based local fallback or when the seed helper never executes; the helper-absent recovery reauthors the body with that marker before creating the comment. The comment's **`Run:`** run link is owned the same way (issue #1536): `scripts/compose-run-url.sh` is the single place it is composed — bash parameter substitution only, failing closed to `_(local run)_` when any of `GITHUB_SERVER_URL`/`GITHUB_REPOSITORY`/`GITHUB_RUN_ID` is empty or whitespace-only, exit 0 on every path — and `seed-review-progress.sh` rewrites the created body's `**Run:**` line to that composed link in the same `normalize_body()` pass that normalizes line 1, then reports the literal on a new `RUNLINK <literal>` stdout line after each successful outcome (`RESUME` and `CREATED`). The engine re-emits that reported literal in the seed body and every later full-body rewrite rather than composing its own URL, so a wrong-owner or unexpanded-`$GITHUB_…` link the agent would otherwise guess never reaches the comment. `/prflow:review-and-fix` inherits it through the shared engine. The skill flips the comment to `❌ Review failed` on an *agent-side* fatal abort, but when the run ends without a verdict the agent never acts. A workflow-level backstop writes that state instead: `scripts/flip-review-progress-failed.sh <pr_number> <marker> <cause>` uses the workflow's independently rebuilt cloud marker to locate this run's comment through `workpad.py`, and **upserts** it. An existing comment is changed only when its Status is still the interim `🚀`; a confirmed clean absence — a run that died before the Phase 0.3.5 seed — is *created* in the terminal state carrying the same run-keyed marker on line 1, so a later lookup for that run resolves it and the retry takes the already-terminal arm instead of posting a second comment. A read that merely *failed* never authorizes a create, a create failure is diagnosed as a create failure rather than as a read failure, and the helper still always exits 0.
  **The shipped call site is `devflow.yml`'s `always()` step, and its decision to act no longer depends on the run's outcome (issue #1154).** The pre-#1154 gate fired only on a failed Claude step, a cancellation, or a final engine result carrying `is_error`, so a run that exited cleanly having written no verdict matched none of them — and, having died before the seed, had no comment to flip either. The Claude step's raw outcome and the parsed engine `is_error` are now inputs to the **cause**: they partition the run-end space into four modes, and `scripts/describe-dead-run-cause.sh` owns that selection and its arm order so the suite can drive each arm (the `scripts/describe-denial-count.sh` extraction pattern). The step screens out a command that seeds no progress comment, and degrades with a warning when a consumer's vendored pin predates the cause helper. **Its decision to act no longer being outcome-gated meant it fired on a clean-exit run that had actually posted a verdict — a false `❌ Review failed` banner (issue #1172: measured 16 false banners against 15 real verdicts in one day, 0 observed precision). The step now gates the write on a fresh verdict-presence check:** `scripts/dead-run-verdict-present.sh` reuses the HEAD-scoped, fail-closed `scripts/derive-review-verdict.sh` (consulting both channels `post-review-verdict.sh` writes — the formal review and this run's run-keyed progress comment) and prints present/absent. A **positively-determined** verdict suppresses the flip; every other outcome (no verdict, an engine error, an unresolvable HEAD, a query failure, an absent helper) still writes the banner, so a genuinely verdict-less run keeps getting it. This also makes `derive-review-verdict.sh` reachable in PRFlow's own tree again, the withheld tier no longer being its only caller.
  After the upsert, review and review-and-fix commands run `scripts/run-review-progress-diagnosis.sh`; its sibling diagnosis helper reads comments through the `gh` resolver. A foreign active bot-authored `🚀 Reviewing` comment emits the possible-mismatch warning. Exact matches and clean absences remain silent. An unestablished read emits a notice. Non-review commands skip diagnosis. Repositories that retained the withheld `devflow-review.yml` keep its installed `finalize_check` call site, but that workflow is not present or updated in this tree. Runtime tests compare the seed helper's cloud marker with the workflow rebuild instead of treating `skills/review/SKILL.md` as the sole producer.

  **The upsert survives a `command`-runner death via the out-of-job `review_finalize` job (issue #1174).** Every review post-run handler above is an `always()` step *inside* the `command` job, so a runner death (OOM, eviction, infrastructure loss) takes all of them down with the job — the one failure mode they exist to cover, leaving the pull request frozen at `🚀 Reviewing`. `devflow.yml`'s `review_finalize` job does not share a runner with the `claude` step: it `needs: [config, review_dedupe, gate, command]`, runs `always()` under the same enable/should-run/dedupe gate (plus a review-family screen covering both `/prflow:review` and `/prflow:review-and-fix`, and `needs.*.result == 'success'` on the upstream chain so an upstream failure never draws a spurious banner), and reads `needs.command.result` — a job output that survives a dead runner — through `scripts/describe-command-job-arm.sh`, which partitions it into `completed-normally` / `cancelled` / `did-not-report` (a JOB-level decision distinct from `describe-dead-run-cause.sh`'s cause clause). On `completed-normally` it produces nothing, so the happy path is byte-unchanged. On the other arms it reuses the *same* idempotent `flip-review-progress-failed.sh` (so it never stacks a second banner and never fights the in-job copy on an alive failure) gated by the *same* `dead-run-verdict-present.sh` verdict-presence check, leaving a terminal "did not report" banner naming the run URL. The two promoted `command`-job outputs `claude_outcome`/`engine_is_error` let it name the richer `describe-dead-run-cause.sh` cause on an *alive* failure (where the outputs survive); on a runner-death path a dead job emits no outputs, so both read empty and the job states the generic "did not report" cause — the honest limit that a dead job cannot reproduce the full cause selection. The five `execution_file`-dependent handlers and the re-dispatching stall backstop deliberately stay in-job (each annotated in the workflow), because `execution_file` is a path on the dead runner's filesystem that no downstream job can read, and the stall backstop's out-of-job coexistence is the open question #1027 owns.

- **Run-completion Blueprint row and the termination-time re-read (issue #1367).** The progress comment's `## Blueprint` checklist ends with a final row, *Run complete — everything this run owed*, ticked after the verdict-aggregation row. Before this row existed, the same write that ticked *Aggregate & verdict* also flipped `Status` to its terminal glyph, so a run that aggregated a verdict and then failed, skipped, or never entered delivery presented a fully-ticked checklist and a finished status while nothing durable carried the verdict — measured four times across two pull requests on 2026-08-06, in three of which the delivery helper was invoked zero times. One row literal serves both execution paths, because its subject is run completion rather than verdict delivery. On the **standalone `/prflow:review`** path it is ticked only when Phase 4.4's delivery helper reported `POSTED review <event>` or `POSTED comment <event>`; every other reading — `FAILED no-durable-channel`, any `SKIP <reason>`, or no output at all — leaves it unticked. A ticked row therefore asserts that a durable marked verdict exists and **not** that a merge signal exists, since the `POSTED comment` arm leaves the reviews API and `reviewDecision` untouched. On the **`/prflow:review-and-fix`** path, which skips Phase 4.4 and posts no verdict to GitHub at all, the row is ticked at Loop Exit and asserts only that the loop reached its terminal work; a run whose Loop Exit steps were dropped — the interactive/inline-orchestrator drop mode recorded in that skill's *Common Mistakes* — leaves the row unticked, which on that path is a known false negative rather than a delivery gap. When `/prflow:implement` drives that loop, the same tuple-defined rows are rendered under the issue workpad's **Review** phase and the loop ticks them there instead of seeding a PR progress comment. The tick is issued as its own `patch`, never fused to the Phase 4 write, so **`Status` still goes terminal at exactly the point in the run it did before this change**. Before terminating — and before the terminal-glyph stamp that remains the engine's final action — the standalone path re-reads its own progress comment and reads the last Blueprint row. An unresolved re-read (comment unfetchable, or no final row in the body) is read as unticked, never as ticked. On a recorded `FAILED no-durable-channel` reading and only there, the run makes exactly **one** bounded corrective attempt by re-entering the Phase 4.4 delivery helper; a reaching attempt ticks the row, any other reading leaves it unticked and states what the helper reported, and the bound is what keeps a delivery failure from becoming a non-terminating run. Every other reading is report-only, because a `SKIP` and a harness refusal are both properties of the invocation this run supplied and re-invoking reproduces them. The fix-loop path makes no corrective attempt at all. On the PR-comment surface, when the engine holds no progress-comment handle — `prflow_review.live_progress_comment_enabled` off, a failed seed, or a non-pull-request run alike — no row is rendered and the run instead reports at termination, on the chat narrative channel that configuration already uses, whether a durable marked verdict was reached. On the fix-loop path the aggregation phase runs once per iteration, so the window between the first iteration's terminal `Status` and Loop Exit is an in-flight state; the unticked-row reading is scoped to a run that has **ended**. The mechanism is agent-executed prompt prose whose only reader is the runtime agent, so per the repository's recorded decision it carries no automated regression coverage; the generated cloud-writer contract pins the edited files' content and is regenerated on `main` (issue #1445), not in the feature-branch change.

  **The workflow's dead-run banner stays suppressed on this failure class, and this change does not make it fire.** The `always()` step gates its write on `scripts/dead-run-verdict-present.sh`, and on a run that aggregated a verdict but delivered nothing that oracle answers *present* for two independent reasons on two different channels: its run-keyed progress-comment channel accepts this run's own verdict prose, which the engine writes into the comment before delivery is attempted, and its reviews-API channel is HEAD-sha-scoped rather than run-scoped, so a verdict from an earlier round at an unchanged head satisfies it too. Correcting either would change `derive-review-verdict.sh`, which also drives the `finalize_check` blocking merge-gate in consumer repositories that installed the withheld review tier, so it is deliberately out of scope here and belongs to its own issue. The unticked row is the only new signal this change adds; it is not a replacement for the banner and does not restore it.

- **Review no-verdict auto-resume backstop (`prflow_review.stall_backstop`, issue #408).** The died-flip above makes a dead review *visible* but leaves it a dead-end — a human still has to re-trigger. A headless review run can end `success` with **no verdict**: the SDK session ends the moment the model emits a tool-call-free turn (e.g. after a `ScheduleWakeup` while a Phase-3 agent is still pending), so the required `Devflow Review` check fails "incomplete — re-run needed". Two coordinated layers fix this. **(1) Headless-wait discipline (prose).** The injected grounding block (`scripts/render-grounding-block.sh`, rendered once and now the discipline's sole home — the `skills/review/SKILL.md` section that mirrored it has been removed) tells the engine this is a headless run — ending the turn ends the process; never end the turn while a dispatched agent has not returned; poll to keep the turn alive; treat `ScheduleWakeup`/future task-notifications as unavailable. This reduces the frequency of the early-quit. **(2) Bounded auto-resume backstop (deterministic).** The **auto-review path** (`devflow-review.yml`'s `finalize_check`) derives the verdict for the head via `derive-review-verdict.sh` and, when it concludes the run incomplete **with no verdict positively observed for the head** (exactly the success-with-no-verdict early-quit above), invokes `scripts/request-review-backstop.sh` — the pure decision helper that owns the whole fire/no-fire choice (config read, verdict guard, per-head attempt count, App-token guard, marker construction), so `lib/test/run.sh` drives every arm — to decide whether to fire. The **manual `/prflow:review` comment path** (`devflow.yml`) has no verdict-derivation step, so it invokes the same helper on the **`#356` dead-run signal** it already computes — the engine ending `is_error` or the `claude` step ending `failure` (a hard death), **not** a clean success-with-no-verdict. So a manual-path run that early-quits benignly is covered only by Layer 1's headless-wait prose, not the deterministic backstop; the auto-review path — which handles the vast majority of reviews and is the one the early-quit was diagnosed on — is fully covered. (Closing the manual-path success-with-no-verdict case deterministically would need a verdict-derivation step ported into that comment-triggered job; deferred as a follow-up.) On a fire, the workflow posts a `/prflow:review` re-trigger comment carrying a `<!-- prflow:review-backstop head=<sha> attempt=<n> -->` marker, authored by a **freshly minted App token**, so the review re-runs without human action. Each workflow path posts and annotates through the shared `scripts/post-review-backstop-comment.sh` helper (extracted from the two near-identical inline blocks, issue #414): it composes the marker + re-trigger body, POSTs it via `post-issue-comment.sh`, and selects the fired-`::notice::`-vs-failed-`::warning::` annotation on the POST success breadcrumb — so `lib/test/run.sh` drives that fail-closed selection instead of only presence-pinning a YAML literal. The cap is `stall_backstop.max_resume_attempts` (default `2`) per head, counted from the marker comments for the current head; `stall_backstop.enabled` (default `true`, safe-direction on any unrecognized value — a real JSON `false` disables it) is the master switch. When the cap is exhausted, or no App token is configured (`DEVFLOW_APP_ID` unset — a `GITHUB_TOKEN`-authored comment never re-triggers a workflow), the backstop reports **no-fire** with a breadcrumb naming why, and the run degrades to exactly the dead-end flip above. Because the re-trigger re-enters the same manual `/prflow:review` comment gate, auto-resume also requires the minting App's bot login to be in `prflow.allowed_bots` (the same prerequisite the implement backstop carries) — otherwise the comment posts but the gate declines it. The backstop never fires when a verdict exists (APPROVE or REJECT), never fires beyond the cap, and never fires when disabled — it is purely additive. This reverses the earlier "no auto-retry backstop" decision, which predates the transcript evidence characterizing the residual failure. **That residual is not a timing race (issue #801).** Run `30138268273` established it as the harness's *documented default dispatch mode* meeting a headless runner: subagents are background-by-default upstream, a background dispatch's results arrive in a **later turn**, and a headless `claude -p` session ends at the first tool-call-free turn — so all eight dispatches returned only launch acknowledgments and the run ended `end_turn` with no verdict after a full-fleet spend. A race is a scheduling coincidence a re-run wins; this is the documented default dispatch mode meeting a headless runner, which run `30138268273` reproduced on a full fleet. The prevention layers are correspondingly deterministic rather than probabilistic — `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS: "1"` on each cloud engine step, plus the barrier statement the injected grounding block carries into every cloud engine prompt. It is the review-side sibling of `prflow_implement.stall_backstop`.

- **Long-run credential refresh (issue #487).** A GitHub App installation token expires 60 minutes after minting and cannot be renewed, so a writer-job run that outlives the hour used to spend its remainder on dead credentials (`git push` and agent-side `gh` both 401). Both writer jobs — `devflow-implement.yml`'s `claude` job and `devflow.yml`'s `command` job — now start a detached refresher (`scripts/refresh-app-credentials.sh`) after checkout that, on a 45-minute cadence, re-mints a fresh installation token and rewrites both repo-controlled credential surfaces in place: the checkout-persisted `http.<server>/.extraheader` credential the in-run `git push` authenticates with, and a mode-0600 token file that the `gh` wrapper (`scripts/gh-fresh.sh`, installed ahead of `gh` on `PATH` by the checked-in, seven-output-validated `scripts/install-gh-wrapper.sh` — which publishes `DEVFLOW_GH_REAL` but deliberately **no** process-global `DEVFLOW_GH`, so the override seam never leaks into later steps such as the repo test suite; issue #533) resolves at call time. **`mode-0600` holds only where POSIX mode bits apply: on Windows they constrain neither that token file nor the installer's sibling fingerprint file, so the installer's mode gate relaxes there with a stderr breadcrumb and access is left to the filesystem's ACLs (issue #690 — see [`docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`](cloud-setup.md#windows-posix-mode-bits-do-not-constrain-the-credential-files)).** An `if: always()` step retires it via `scripts/stop-refresher.sh`, whose **first** action is a cross-job **sweep** — it expands a job-scoped-pidfile glob across the whole temporary directory and reaps any live refresher orphan that is not this step's own — and which then kills this step's refresher by pidfile, tails its detached log, and re-emits one live `::warning::` when the refresher was defeated (suppressed when the self-test marker is present). It derives the sweep glob from the temporary directory normalized through `lib/normalize-path.sh`, and reaps nothing (empty pattern plus a `::warning::` naming the unusable value) when that value cannot be expressed as a POSIX glob root — a Windows-form path with no `wslpath`/`cygpath` and no WSL/MSYS signal, or a UNC path — while an explicit `DEVFLOW_REFRESH_REAP_GLOB` is used verbatim (issue #1925). The Stop step derives the self-test marker path itself from the Start step's handle; neither writer workflow publishes `DEVFLOW_REFRESH_SELFTEST_FAILED` job-wide, mirroring the `DEVFLOW_GH` decision. The whole mechanism is gated on `vars.DEVFLOW_APP_ID != ''` — unconfigured, it is a byte-identical no-op — and the `/prflow:review` read-only path (which never pushes) is excluded. See [`docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`](cloud-setup.md).

### Vendoring / runtime materialization
- **Why a workspace path, not a marketplace install?** Inside the `claude-code-action` runner, `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}` is unset, the bash sandbox can't read `~/.claude`, and `$`-expansion is blocked, so workflows reference helpers at the literal path `.prflow/vendor/prflow/scripts/…`. The plugin must physically be there when a job runs.
- **Why `.prflow/vendor/` and not `.claude/`?** On every PR, `claude-code-action` runs a security step that `rm -rf`s sensitive paths (including `.claude`) and restores them from the *base* branch, which would wipe a plugin vendored under `.claude/`. `.prflow/vendor/prflow/` is outside every sensitive path and survives.
- **Thin install (default):** the bulky plugin tree is **not** committed; the `vendor-plugin` composite action fetches it at runtime, pinned to `prflow_version` in `.prflow/config.json`. A thin install **refuses to run without a pinned `prflow_version`** (it never silently tracks mutable `main`).
- **Workflow grants arrive via `install.sh`, not `prflow_version`.** `install.sh` copies `.github/workflows/devflow-*.yml` into the consumer repo; `prflow_version` fetches only the plugin tree (`skills/`, `scripts/`, `lib/`). A grant wave on the implement/review profiles therefore reaches an adopter by **re-running `install.sh`** — bumping `prflow_version` alone leaves the older workflow copy without the grants. The #484 wave grants the helpers used by cloud implement runs: Phase 3's inline review engine calls `stale-prose-lint.py`, `match-lint-adjudications.py`, and `load-prompt-extension.sh`, while the implement-owned surfaces call `react-to-trigger.sh` in the trigger-reaction fence and `extract-doc-needed-paths.sh` in Phase 4.1. The exact issue-mandated grant set also contains `dismiss-stale-rejections.sh`; that helper appears in the shared review source's standalone-only Phase 4.4, which inline implement runs skip after Phase 4.3. The normal inline flow therefore does not invoke it, although the grant makes the capability available to the inline session; the source guard deliberately over-approximates the shared fenced source and requires the grant for parity. The wave also grants `cmp` and `git hash-object` for the inline review engine, plus `gh pr checkout` in `devflow.yml`. Without re-running `install.sh`, the Phase 3 stale-prose gate and the four other runtime helpers stay silently denied. The `lib/test/run.sh` #484 head guard pins the implement-profile surface.
- **Vendored install (`DEVFLOW_VENDOR=1`):** commits the full tree; nothing fetched at runtime.

### Runtime provisioning (the `setup` block)
Before Claude runs, the runner provisions the toolchain in order **Python → Node → PHP → service containers → `install` lines**. Languages are gated by version keys (empty = skipped). Service containers (databases/caches) start via `docker run` and are reachable on `127.0.0.1:<host-port>`. Node dependency caching is automatic when a lockfile is present. `/prflow:init` auto-fills the deterministic parts from detected language markers.

### Selecting the runner (`DEVFLOW_RUNNER`)
Every job in the two consumer-shipped workflows (`devflow.yml`, `devflow-implement.yml`) resolves its `runs-on` from an optional GitHub **repository/organization variable** `DEVFLOW_RUNNER` — infrastructure (which machine runs the job), set in GitHub Settings (Actions → Variables), deliberately **not** in the versioned `.prflow/config.json`. It accepts three shapes: **unset or empty** → `ubuntu-latest` (byte-for-byte the previous behavior); a **bare single label** (e.g. `windows-latest`) → that runner; a **JSON array** (e.g. `["self-hosted","windows","PRFlow"]`) → a runner matching that label set. A value that begins with `[` but is not valid JSON fails the job **loud** at evaluation time (a visible `fromJSON` error), rather than silently degrading to `ubuntu-latest`. Each workflow also declares a top-level `defaults: run: shell: bash`, forcing bash for `run:` steps on a non-Linux runner. **`setup.services` Docker caveat:** the service containers above run via `docker run`, and Docker is **preinstalled on `ubuntu-latest`** but **not** on a self-hosted non-ubuntu runner — so `setup.services` and any `Bash(docker:*)` path can break there unless Docker is installed on the runner. **Dispatch-enabled, not certified:** this variable makes a self-hosted / Windows runner *selectable* and forces bash, but does **not** certify that every inline bash body runs correctly on a Windows filesystem — an adopter must run at least one full consumer-shipped workflow end-to-end on the target runner before treating it as production-ready. Full prerequisites (toolchain, the `python3` shim, `DEVFLOW_GH`/`DEVFLOW_JQ`/`DEVFLOW_BASH`, the queue-forever label-match gotcha, the smoke-test boundary) are in [`docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`](cloud-setup.md).

---

## 15. Security model

A strong slide for security-conscious buyers, PRFlow is explicit about its threat model.

- **One secret by default.** The cloud tier needs only `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (plus the built-in `GITHUB_TOKEN`) — no GitHub App is **required**. Routing a workflow section through an **optional** third-party model provider (§17 `providers`) adds exactly one more secret, the fixed, greppable `DEVFLOW_PROVIDER_API_KEY`; with no provider configured the cloud tier keeps the OAuth-only default (unchanged for a given `claude_model`). An **optional** App (`DEVFLOW_APP_ID` variable + `DEVFLOW_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` secret, with `Contents: write` + `Workflows: write` + `Pull requests: write` + `Issues: write` + `Actions: read`) does two things: it lets the cloud writers edit `.github/workflows/` files (which the built-in `GITHUB_TOKEN` is hard-blocked from), and it puts **the non-review user-visible cloud posts under the App identity** — the trigger reactions and the notice comments (the workpad comment is marker-detected by design, so its authorship never matters) — each site minting its own short-lived token downscoped via `permission-*` inputs to exactly what that site does. The **review** agent's posts (`devflow-runner.yml`'s progress comment, verdicts, approvals, and rejections) run instead under the separate **`DevFlow-Reviewer`** App (`DEVFLOW_REVIEWER_APP_ID` + `DEVFLOW_REVIEWER_PRIVATE_KEY`, `contents: read` so the review path cannot push), never this primary App — that separation is what keeps Phase 4.4's formal review from being a forbidden self-review (see 4.4 above). Unset, behavior is unchanged. See `docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`.
- **Authorization gate.** A run only starts if the sender is an allowed bot (`prflow.allowed_bots`) **or** a human in `prflow.allowed_users` (default `*` = any collaborator) **who also holds write/admin/maintain access**. Resolvers fail **closed**.
- **Base-ref trust boundary.** The automated reviewer reads the `provision_env` flag, the `allowed_tools` list, the `setup` block, the pinned **`prflow_version`**, **and** its own appended **prompt extensions** (`.prflow/prompt-extensions/review.md` and `requesting-code-review.md`) **only from the trusted base branch**, never the PR head. A malicious PR therefore cannot enable provisioning for its own review, grant itself tools, inject install commands, choose which plugin commit reviews it, or write instructions into the reviewing agent's own prompt. The last two arrived together in issue #874: the review job checks out the PR head, so a repo-root read of an extension would have appended PR-author-editable bytes to the merge-gating reviewer's prompt — a strictly more powerful lever than the tool grants this boundary already covered. The job now truncates the workspace copy of each protected extension and exports `DEVFLOW_PROMPT_EXTENSION_ROOT` **unconditionally**, then populates that `$RUNNER_TEMP` closure from the base ref inside the fetch-success branch alone; the arms on which population does not happen — a failed base-ref fetch, an empty base ref, no trusted source for the materialization helper, a per-name read failure or unwritable target, a helper usage defect, a traversal-shaped protected name, and a non-blob object at a protected path — therefore each degrade to an empty closure rather than to the PR-head path. **The same boundary now binds the shipped command tier (issue #1075).** `devflow.yml`'s `command` job — the only review path reachable in a consumer, running `/prflow:review`, `/prflow:review-and-fix` and `/prflow:pr-description` — carries its own `promptext` step that establishes a `$RUNNER_TEMP` closure and exports `DEVFLOW_PROMPT_EXTENSION_ROOT` **unconditionally**, then populates it from the PR's base ref through the same `scripts/materialize-trusted-prompt-extensions.sh`, inside that step's own base-ref fetch-success branch alone. Its protected set is the whole closed set those three commands can load (`review`, `requesting-code-review`, `review-and-fix`, `receiving-code-review`, `pr-description`), declared once as a job-level `env:` and reconciled against the skill trees by a drift guard. **The mechanism there is not the runner's, and the difference matters:** as of issue #1163 `devflow.yml` subscribes to `issue_comment` alone and every job pins `ref: github.event.repository.default_branch`, so that job's own checkout is **always the default branch**. The untrusted tree is therefore never what the workflow checked out but only what the *agent* produces when `/prflow:review-and-fix`'s Step 0.5 branch sync runs `gh pr checkout`, which happens before the engine loads `review` and before Phase 3.1 loads `requesting-code-review`. Standalone `/prflow:review` never moves the tree at all: it reads the PR through `gh pr diff` / `gh pr view` and verifies claims about diff-touched paths against the reviewed head's bytes through `git show $PR_HEAD_SHA:<path>`, the tree staying on the trusted default branch throughout (issue #1889). Where the command tier diverges from the runner it does so deliberately, never by oversight. It **does not truncate** the workspace copies (it is write-capable — a truncation dirties the tree `gh pr checkout` requires clean, and any that survived would be committed to the contributor's branch by the fix loop), replacing that belt with a loud skew warning when the vendored loader predates the variable; and it uses **no three-rank trusted-source ladder** for the materialization helper, because the workspace is the trusted default branch at checkout and turns untrusted only when Step 0.5's `gh pr checkout` moves the tree to the PR head — at which point a PR that can edit that helper can equally edit the `load-prompt-extension.sh` that consults the closure, which no ladder reaches. **Residuals, stated rather than glossed:** on the **command** tier the plugin tree is **no longer PR-selectable** as of issue #1163 — `./` in the composed `plugin_marketplaces` input and every `uses: ./.github/actions/…` composite action resolve from that job's checkout, which is now always the default branch, so a PR-branch edit to `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, to the vendored tree, or to a composite action is not exercised by the workflow on the PR introducing it. What remains on that tier is the *agent*-introduced untrust: once `/prflow:review-and-fix`'s Step 0.5 runs `gh pr checkout`, every byte the engine subsequently reads from the working tree — the vendored plugin tree included — is PR-head content, which is the same route the prompt-extension closure above exists to bypass. The PR-head marketplace/composite-action selection **does** still apply to the withheld automated runner (`devflow-runner.yml`), whose checkout is `inputs.ref` — the PR head — by design (filed separately). The deeper adversarial residual is that any actor with **write access** to the repository — including a prompt-injected agent run authoring a branch — can obtain **every repository secret** by editing or adding a workflow file in its own branch, because GitHub reads the workflow definition (the `on:` block included) from the pull-request ref for the `pull_request` event family: the trigger removal and checkout pins in issue #1163 are controls that live *inside* `devflow.yml`, and a branch can omit a pin or re-add a deleted trigger in its own copy (PR #404's REJECT — *a floor the pull request controls is no floor* — applied to the workflow file itself). Environment-scoped secrets with a branch deployment policy are the only mitigation, and that mitigation is tracked in **#1300**; issue #1163 closes the **accident** class — a PR's content executed by a review-triggered run with nobody intending it — not this adversarial one. A consumer whose pinned `prflow_version` predates issue #874 ships a loader that ignores the variable and reads the working tree, warned about but not prevented; a local or interactive `/prflow:review-and-fix` has no dispatching environment to materialize anything, so its Step 0.5 checkout lands in the very tree the loader reads (the advisory provenance checks in `skills/review-and-fix/references/shadow-review.md` are the sole control there); and the added bytes still reach the reviewer through the *reviewed diff*, where the engine-ground-truth block's untrusted-data framing is the compensating control. **Reconciled with Phase 0.5's engine-surface classification (the prompt-extension and `CLAUDE.md` arms):** Phase 0.5 classifies a prompt-extension diff and a `CLAUDE.md` diff as `engine_self_modifying` (see §14 and `skills/review/phases/phase-0-setup.md` §0.5), so such a diff reaches the full checklist and the early shadow rather than the lean config-only path — a defence-in-depth strengthening of this boundary, scoped honestly per arm. For the **prompt-extension arm** that strengthening is no longer load-bearing against the self-nullification it was framed against on the cloud command tier, because the bytes the engine now loads there come from the base ref rather than the diff it is classifying; it remains the compensating control on the residual paths enumerated above, where it still **narrows, never closes** the gap. The **`CLAUDE.md` arm does not** carry that self-nullification on the cloud review paths — `claude-code-action`'s restore pass loads `CLAUDE.md` from the base branch (see `docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`), so the reviewing agent's context carries the base branch's copy, a *merged* root-instruction file governs every future review, and that arm is a review-thoroughness gain rather than a self-nullifiable one (a unified sentence asserting self-nullification for each arm alike would contradict `docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`). The strengthening is also **exposure-increasing**, stated rather than glossed as neutral: restoring Phases 1 and 2 adds checklist-generator, deduper and verifier dispatches, and arming the early shadow adds its fan-out, while every one of those subagent prompts is composed with consumer prompt-extension text (`skills/review-and-fix/references/shadow-review.md`) — so an adopter trades one orchestrator prompt carrying the author's bytes for many.
- **Deny-list floor (authoritative, enforced at consume-time).** Regardless of config, the runner strips catastrophic-tier tools before appending the build allowlist: tree-mutation tools (`Edit`, `Write`, `MultiEdit`, `NotebookEdit`) and raw-shell/eval/privilege Bash (`bash`, `sh`, `zsh`, `eval`, `exec`, `source`, `sudo`, …). The tree-mutation tools are matched by tool **name** — the token before the first `(`, compared case-insensitively — so a **parameterized** entry (`Write(**)`, `Edit(src/**)`) is stripped exactly like the bare name, not just the bare form. Legitimate entries whose *subcommand* is a deny word are kept (`Bash(docker exec:*)`), and read-only file tools (`Read(src/**)`) are kept. The filter lives in the suite-driven helper `scripts/filter-runner-tools.sh`, and the workflow executes it **only from a trusted source** — the review job checks out the PR head, so a floor whose code the PR can edit is no floor at all: the `baseprovision` step materializes the helper from the **trusted base ref** (`git show FETCH_HEAD:…` into `RUNNER_TEMP`, outside the PR-writable workspace — trying the committed-vendor path first, then the repo's own `scripts/` copy gated on the base ref actually being the PRFlow plugin repo), and when the base ref carries none, the vendored copy is accepted **only when `vendor-plugin` reports it was freshly fetched this run** at the pinned `prflow_version` (official-repo content — a PR can at most select an official-repo commit). A committed or self-copied vendor tree — PR-head content — is never consulted for floor code, and when no trusted source resolves the workflow fails **closed** (appending nothing, with a warning naming the rule). Each strip emits a per-entry warning naming the entry; the review continues. **The floor is as current as the base ref's helper (or the pinned fetch):** a consumer must land a **re-vendor on the base branch** for a later tightening (e.g. the parameterized file-tool strip) to take effect on reviews; an *absent* trusted helper fails closed (nothing appended), while a *present-but-stale* base-ref copy enforces its own older rules.
- **Stop-hook trusted-source floor (issue #458).** The deny-floor above covers *tool grants*; a second, parallel channel bypasses it entirely — **base-branch `.claude/settings.json` `Stop` hooks execute inside `claude-code-action`** (confirmed FIRED by run `29224205805`). `claude-code-action` restores `.claude/` (the hook **configuration**) from the base branch, so the hook *commands* are trusted — but those three commands (`bash lib/efficiency-trace.sh --persist`, `bash lib/implement-stop-guard.sh`, `bash …/scripts/stop-hook-probe.sh`) exec **script files under `lib/` and `scripts/`, not under `.claude/`**, which the review job's **PR-head checkout** (`devflow-runner.yml` checks out `ref: inputs.ref` = the PR head SHA) supplies. A PR that edits any of those three targets would otherwise obtain unmediated shell at session end inside a secrets-bearing CI job — the exact `#404` class ("a floor the PR controls is no floor"), reached through hooks rather than tool grants. **Hardening only the three entry scripts is not enough:** each of them `source`s / `exec`s / `python3`-runs further PR-head-editable files under `lib/`+`scripts/` (and `source` resolves relative to the sourcing script's own path in the PR-head workspace), so a base-copy entry would still pull in the PR-head copy of its dependency — the hole is one `source`/`exec` hop deeper. The fix therefore hardens the **full transitive source/exec closure** of the entries (thirteen files: the three Stop-hook entries plus the PreToolUse guard `scripts/pretooluse-shape-guard.py`, `lib/resolve-jq.sh`, `lib/config-source.sh`, `lib/resolve-bin.sh`, `lib/telemetry-branch.sh`, `scripts/config-get.sh`, `scripts/config_fingerprint.py`, `scripts/workpad.py`, and the guard's importlib closure `lib/test/extract-command-shapes.py` + `lib/test/extract-command-heads.py`), mirroring the deny-floor: **before** `claude-code-action` runs, `devflow-runner.yml`'s *Harden Stop-hook script sources* step overwrites each closure target in the PR-head workspace with the **trusted base-ref copy** (`git show FETCH_HEAD:…`), and where no base copy exists overwrites it with a **fail-closed no-op stub** — so the PR-head copy is not executed **through the base-hook channel this floor closes** (a scope the residuals below make precise; the floor closes the checkout-supply channel, not every path by which PR-head code could run). Fail-closed treatment differs by file class so a missing copy never breaks a legitimate hook: an **inline-sourced library** (`resolve-jq.sh`/`config-source.sh`/`resolve-bin.sh`/`telemetry-branch.sh`) runs *inside* the sourcing entry, so a missing trusted copy neutralizes the **entries** to stubs rather than installing a mid-`source` `exit 0` that would break them; an **entry** or a subprocess-**exec'd** dep is safe to stub directly. The installer is the suite-driven helper `scripts/harden-stop-hooks.sh` (whose `HOOK_TARGETS` closure a `lib/test/run.sh` drift-guard — the shared walker `scripts/detect-hook-closure-edges.py` — walks: a future added `source`/exec of a new helper turns the suite RED, including a **command-position** source edge such as a negation-guarded `if ! . "$dep"` or a brace-grouped `{ . "$dep"; }`, and a positive-control test asserts each such form is reported), run **only from a trusted source** (rank 1: base-ref materialized copy; rank 2: the vendored copy gated on `vendor_source == fetch`), and when no trusted helper resolves — **or when the helper reports a wholly-unwritable target it could neither trusted-copy nor stub (a non-zero exit)** — the step fails **closed** by stubbing every target inline (trusted workflow shell); and if even that inline stub write cannot land (a wholly-unwritable target), the step **fails the job** (`exit 1`) **before** `claude-code-action` runs, so no PR-controlled hook ever fires — the last-resort arm is genuinely fail-closed, never a warn-and-proceed. **Residuals (out of scope, not hardened — disclosed so the "closed channel" scope is not read as "no PR-head code can ever run"):** (1) the jq *program* `lib/efficiency-trace.jq`, fed to `jq -f`; `jq` is sandboxed (no shell/exec/arbitrary-write), so a PR-head `.jq` is not a shell/RCE vector, and it lies outside the `source`/`.`/`exec`/`python3` edge set this floor closes. (2) **In-session restore of the PR-head copies.** The review profile grants `Bash(git checkout:*)` and `Bash(tee:*)` (the Phase 3.2 dirty-tree backstop uses `git checkout`), so a *prompt-injected* review session could re-materialize the PR-head hook scripts (`git checkout HEAD -- lib/ scripts/`) after this step and before the Stop hooks fire. The floor closes the checkout-**supply** channel, not this agent-mediated one; the review token being read-only bounds the *GitHub-API-write* damage, but **not** secret exfiltration — the restored PR-head hook runs at Stop time inside the CI job, whose process environment carries the model API key and any other job secrets, so that exfiltration surface is the residual this channel genuinely carries. (3) **Reviewer-fidelity for a PR that edits a closure file (issue #504).** The step reverts the closure files to base bytes *before* the engine reads them, so a PR that legitimately edits one is reviewed against the base version of that file while `gh pr diff` still shows the head version. The refinement this issue ships surfaces the displaced set to the engine so those paths' HEAD verification no longer reads the base-ref/stub working-tree copy: the harden step publishes the displaced-path set (the `HOOK_TARGETS` members) plus a disposition token (`displaced` on every displacement arm, `skipped` + empty on the relevance-gate arm) as step outputs; a new `Compose CI summary` step runs *before* harden (so `summarize-ci-checks.sh` — which sources the closure member `lib/resolve-jq.sh` — runs pre-displacement, never against an `exit 0` stub); and the slimmed `Compose review prompt` step runs *after* harden, forwarding the displaced paths to `scripts/render-grounding-block.sh` as `HARDENED_PATHS`. The rendered engine-ground-truth block then carries a displaced-paths section stating the working-tree copy is base-ref/stub bytes (not the reviewed head) and that a HEAD claim about a listed path routes through `git show $PR_HEAD_SHA:<path>` and the Phase 0.2 cached diff, never a working-tree read — with a `git cat-file -e` → INCONCLUSIVE fail direction that is fetch-free on the review tier. The engine's seven claim-verification surfaces (Phase 2.1a lite-probe, Phase 2.1b `checklist-verifier` dispatch, the shared `defect_signature` truthfulness contract, Phase 4.1.6 sweep, and the `code-reviewer`/`comment-analyzer`/`checklist-verifier` agent mirrors) carry the routing rule — and, since issue #1889, the standalone diff-touched arm that routes a claim about any Phase-0.2-cached-diff-touched path through `git show $PR_HEAD_SHA:<path>` even when the displaced list is empty, while an untouched path keeps the working-tree read; Phase 0.1 attributes displaced-path `git status --porcelain` output (content and mode-only deltas) to the floor instead of the generic uncommitted-changes warning. The displaced list is re-derivable in-session (Phase 0.1.5 persists it to `.prflow/tmp/`), so a compacted long run keeps the routing at the far end where the sweep executes; a run with no displaced list (local tier, manual `devflow.yml` path, consumer relevance-gate skip, fix-loop iteration) behaves exactly as today.
