The floor's displaced working-tree copies stay in place through session end — verification is read-only via `git show`, and neither the skill nor the workflow restores a displaced path's PR-head bytes (that would re-arm PR-head Stop-hook scripts inside the secrets-bearing job). **A newly widened reviewer-fidelity exposure (issue #805).** Since the PreToolUse guard joined the closure, that closure reaches into `lib/test/` for the first time — `lib/test/extract-command-shapes.py` and `lib/test/extract-command-heads.py` are the guard's importlib dependencies. Unlike the pre-#805 members (stable resolver/config helpers edited rarely), these two are **test helpers edited routinely**, including by the very pull requests that change the shape rules, so the displacement-to-base-ref-bytes described above now fires on a materially larger share of pull requests. The routing rule handles it correctly — a HEAD claim about a displaced path goes through `git show $PR_HEAD_SHA:<path>` — but the *frequency* of that route, not just its existence, is now the operative fact. The alternative, having the guard vendor its own arm predicates rather than importing a test helper at runtime, would trade this exposure for a duplicated predicate chain (the very duplication the one arm table exists to remove) and is deliberately not taken. The floor's unconditional `chmod +x` also surfaces a mode-only `100644`→`100755` delta on every closure member tracked non-executable (`lib/resolve-jq.sh`, `lib/resolve-bin.sh`, `lib/telemetry-branch.sh`, and — since the issue-#805 guard closure — `lib/test/extract-command-shapes.py` and `lib/test/extract-command-heads.py`) every run, even on a PR that touches none of them; Phase 0.1 attributes that mode-only delta to the floor too, so the engine no longer reports it as an uncommitted change. **Consumer relevance gate (issue #460 review):** `install.sh` ships `devflow-runner.yml` to consumers, but PRFlow's `.claude/settings.json` Stop hooks do not; the step therefore **no-ops** when the trusted base `.claude/` does not wire these hooks (decided via the trusted helper's tested `--wired-check` mode, keyed on the base ref, reading `.claude/settings.json` **and** `.claude/settings.local.json` — the two base-tracked files that can wire a Stop hook — so a wiring split across them cannot silently un-gate the floor), so a consumer's review never has the PRFlow-layout closure paths stubbed or created over same-named files. **One disclosed residual (FP-S1):** the gate lives on the *fetch-success* path, so a transient base-ref **fetch failure** in a consumer repo bypasses it and the step falls through to fail-closed stubbing — stubbing/creating the PRFlow-layout closure paths over any same-named consumer files during that (read-only, nothing-persisted) review. This is the deliberate *fail-closed-over-consumer-fidelity* trade: a fetch failure must never drop **PRFlow's own** protection, and it is far rarer than the common success path the gate covers. **A second disclosed assumption (Obs B):** the gate's correctness rests on *gate base == the base `claude-code-action` restores `.claude/` from*. `BASE_REF` is `github.event.pull_request.base.ref || github.event.repository.default_branch` (mirroring `baseprovision`); on the secrets-bearing CI-completion re-triggers (`workflow_run`/`status`/`check_suite`) the `pull_request` payload is absent, so it falls back to the **default branch**. The Stop-hook wiring is therefore assumed to live on the **default branch** (it does for PRFlow — the three hooks are wired on `main`); a repo that wired these hooks on a *non-default* target branch but not on the default could have the gate read "not wired" (default branch) and skip while `claude-code-action` restored the wiring from the actual PR base — the hooks would then fire from PR-head scripts. Not reachable for PRFlow or any consumer following the default-branch convention; documented so a future settings split does not silently un-gate. **Reachability & scope (verified against the workflows):** the exposure is confined to the **review runner** — the sole PR-head-checkout `claude-code-action` job — and is reachable by an untrusted PR chiefly via the secrets-bearing CI-completion re-trigger (`workflow_run`/`status`/`check_suite`), which runs in the default-branch/secrets context yet still checks out the PR head. The **implement** job needs no such floor: it checks out the **default branch** (never a PR head) and is authorization-gated, so its Stop-hook scripts — and its writable App token — are already trusted and never in reach of PR-author code. (Live-probe caveats, documented as unverified boundaries: that `claude-code-action` leaves the base hooks active in the review *profile* specifically, and that the pre-run workspace overwrite survives into the Stop-hook execution. On the *helper-resolution* axis the floor fails closed — no trusted source resolves ⇒ stubs, never the PR-head copy. The *survival* boundary is different in kind: if `claude-code-action` were to reset `lib/`/`scripts/` from the PR head after this step, the overwrite would be undone — but the action restores only sensitive paths (`.claude/`), not the general worktree, so survival is the expected behavior.)
- **Capability policy is generated from one versioned manifest, with the reviewer boundary locked (issue #561).** The hand-maintained allowlist literals that scattered across the cloud workflows and the matcher-probe baselines are now **generated** from a single source of truth, `lib/capability-profiles.json` (an integer `manifest_version`, named token groups, and the `review`/`implement`/`command` profiles). `python3 lib/generate-capability-profiles.py` compiles the manifest into exactly six regions — the `devflow-runner.yml` review `TOOLS`, the `devflow.yml` command `TOOLS`, the `devflow-implement.yml` `--allowed-tools` base list, and the `matcher-probe.yml` `REVIEW`/`IMPLEMENT`/`COMMAND` baselines (the `COMMAND` baseline backs the `command-probe` job, issue #1152) — each stamped with a banner comment carrying `manifest_version` + the sha256 of that region's resolved token list (the skew diagnostic in a support question). A `--check` mode wired into `lib/test/run.sh` (the required `lib + python tests` CI job) turns any manifest↔literal drift RED before merge, with a token-level directional diff so a grant hand-added to a workflow is never silently reverted by a blind regenerate. Because the generated review literal **is** the read-only reviewer allowlist (the deny floor above filters only appended consumer extras, never the base), the review profile is a security boundary: `lib/review-profile.tokens` locks its exact resolved token list, the generator never writes it, and any manifest edit — including to a shared group — that would widen the resolved review list fails generation and `--check` closed until that lock is updated in the same PR, so widening the reviewer always requires a deliberate, visible diff. The generator is python3 stdlib-only, reads no git history, and has no runtime footprint — generation happens at desk/CI time on the repo's own tree; consumer runs still resolve their baked literal shipped by file-copy, so no runtime workflow ever compiles policy from a reviewed PR's checkout. The probe's candidate rows, verdict tables, and command-shape verdicts stay hand-maintained — the manifest states policy, never measurement.
- **Read-only by default.** With `provision_env` off (the default), the reviewer is byte-for-byte read-only, it inspects the diff and cannot compile/lint/test. Turning it on is a documented opt-in that accepts running untrusted PR build code under a write token.
- **Self-trigger guard.** The implement workpad quotes the literal `/prflow:implement` phrase; the resolver declines any trigger text containing the workpad marker so PRFlow can't trigger itself in a loop.
- **Duplicate-run dedupe.** A thread-scoped dedupe ensures only the oldest concurrent `/prflow:implement` run on a thread proceeds. A parallel gate covers the command path: `devflow.yml`'s `review_dedupe` job suppresses a duplicate standalone `/prflow:review` while a review of the same **commit** is already in flight **and has published** its seeded `prflow:review-progress` live-progress comment — that comment is the sole in-flight signal (`🚀 Reviewing`, bot-authored, liveness-bounded), read via the bundled `scripts/dedupe-review-command.sh` helper (issue #989). The scope is the commit (issue #1010): the engine stamps the head it is reviewing into that comment at seed time as a distinct machine-only producer key, `<!-- prflow:review-seeded-head <sha> -->` — distinct because the comment's `Reviewed HEAD:` line means "a review *finished* at this head" and two consumers depend on that meaning — and detect mode requires an exact match against the head the requesting job resolves, so a review requested after a mid-review push proceeds. The recorded value is the API `headRefOid` captured before any caller head-override, which preserves the second of the two accepted costs `docs/internal/workflow-triggers.md` enumerates; an in-flight comment carrying no such key (an installed copy predating the change) fails open. It fails open in every direction, never suppresses a `/prflow:review-and-fix` or a `prflow:review-backstop` auto-resume, and no-ops when the live-progress comment is disabled. Because that comment is seeded inside the peer's agent job (Phase 0.3.5), a request arriving in the **pre-seed window** before the peer seeds it fails open — a transient timing exposure measured once at 141 s on PR #1469 (2026-08-09), and deliberately not a numbered accepted cost (issue #1479). Since caller-scoped progress routing (issue #1657), an implement-hosted `/prflow:review-and-fix` binds its progress surface to the issue workpad and seeds **no** PR progress comment, so a `/prflow:review` issued during one fails open for that review's **whole duration**, not just the pre-seed window — another accepted fail-open exposure, likewise not a numbered cost. See `docs/internal/workflow-triggers.md`.

---

## 16. Observability: efficiency traces & telemetry

PRFlow instruments its own review runs so teams can see which reviewers actually earn their cost.

- **Per-iteration workpads** (`.prflow/tmp/review/<slug>/<run-id>/iter-<N>.json`) record each Phase-3 agent's findings, corroboration counts, and fix decisions, plus per-phase cost telemetry.
- At **Loop Exit**, `/prflow:review-and-fix` derives a per-subagent **effectiveness trace** and classifies each reviewer with a deterministic 4-way taxonomy: **unique-effective** (found a defect no one else did, and it was fixed), **corroborating** (found a fixed defect ≥2 agents agreed on), **noise** (raised findings, none applied), **null** (silent / out-of-scope only).
- **Decomposing the `null` residual (issue #1849).** `null` is a **derived residual**, so a bare `null` verdict collapses three opposite states — an agent genuinely **silent**, one whose real findings were all **deferred / severity-calibrated / foreclosed**, and one that **failed** or returned an unusable result. `lib/efficiency-trace.jq` adds two additive per-agent fields beside the unchanged `verdict` (no new verdict, no derivation change): a **`disposition`** (`returned` \| `failed` \| `silent` \| `unestablished`) that separates a failed return from silence — `returned` when the agent is in `phase3_findings`, `failed` when it is in the established failed set (the new `phase3_failed_agents` sink written by Phase 3, or a shadow `per_reviewer_assessment` entry marked not-returned), `silent` when dispatched, absent from findings, and the failed set is established without it, and `unestablished` when the split cannot be read (roster or failed set absent/malformed) — **never mapped onto `silent`** (unknown is not zero); and a **`fix_decisions`** roll-up (the agent's sorted-unique `fix_decision` values, `[]` in review-mode) that separates an all-deferred/calibrated agent (verdict `null`, non-empty roll-up) from one that raised nothing (verdict `null`, empty roll-up). The residual is decomposed only over an **established roster** (`phase3_dispatched_present`); a roster-absent iteration reads `unestablished` per non-returning agent rather than shrinking the null denominator, and a `phase3_failed_agents_present` flag on each durable iteration record lets a historical row lacking the field read as disposition-unestablished. The shadow join's per-reviewer assessment is now **persisted** with the iteration record rather than held in memory. **Denominator confound:** a per-agent rate's denominator is *the defects that existed to be found*, not the agent's quality — a `null` or low-yield rate on a config-only / out-of-domain diff is correct silence, so **raw per-agent rates are not reviewer quality until that shipped-defect denominator is characterised** (the prerequisite for the cross-run cut-candidate analysis `efficiency_cut_candidate_min_dispatch` anticipates). See [`docs/internal/efficiency-trace.md`](efficiency-trace.md).
- **Recurring defect kinds (issue #1903).** `lib/efficiency-trace.jq` also derives a per-run **`recurring_defect_kinds`** field — every `defect_signature.kind` that appeared in the `phase3_findings` of **three separate iterations**, each paired with the list of iterations it appeared in — and renders it as a "Recurring defect kinds" section of the trace. A finding whose `defect_signature` is absent or malformed is counted under an explicit **`unknown`** label rather than dropped, and a run whose iteration records cannot be read renders as **`unestablished`** rather than an empty set. The count keys on a kind's presence across iterations, never on whether its findings were fixed, so a kind whose findings were all pushed back still counts. `skills/review-and-fix/references/fixing.md` reads this signal and tells the fixer that a recurring kind means the fix should model the artifact rather than extend an enumeration.
- One **durable** JSON record per run is persisted to a dedicated **telemetry branch** (`prflow-telemetry`, configurable via `telemetry.branch` — issue #441) at `.prflow/logs/efficiency/<slug>-<run-id>.json`, keyed by the run's `<run-id>` (the same discriminator that scopes the workpad directory), so it is one file per run and parallel writers never collide. The write goes through git plumbing to a long-lived orphan branch that shares no history with `main` — **never touching the current branch, `HEAD`, or the working tree** — so a local run no longer diverges local `main`, and durability no longer depends on whichever branch the run sat on being pushed and merged. The run-id key (not a fresh timestamp) is also what lets the persistence backstop be idempotent, tested by record presence **on the branch** (next bullet).
- **Non-droppable persistence (layered).** The per-iteration `iter-<N>.json` emit is a **non-optional obligation on every iteration, regardless of how the loop was executed** — whether `/prflow:review-and-fix` ran as a `Skill` invocation or was hand-run via direct `Agent` dispatch on a degraded path (a cloud `claude-code-action` permission/sandbox denial is never license to leave the instrumented loop) — written with the Write tool (the dependable authoring path into `.prflow/tmp` from this loop's profile, and the one this emit must use), and **fused to the fix-commit moment** (Layer 1: capture the fix commit's SHA, then Write, as one step — the one mechanical point every fix iteration passes through, so an inline-driven loop has no seam in which to lose the emit). On top of that emit, the record and the durable workpad copy survive even when the loop is driven inline by an orchestrator that skips the Loop Exit bookkeeping: a warn-only **self-check** at Loop Exit flags a dropped persist (Layer 2), and a deterministic `lib/efficiency-trace.sh --persist` backstop re-derives the artifacts from the on-disk `iter-<N>.json` workpads and persists them to the telemetry branch (Layer 3). And when even the workpads are gone — a run that left **zero** `iter-*.json` — `--persist` **synthesizes** minimal iteration records from the branch's `fix: address review findings (iteration N)` commits (Layer 3+, the synthesis floor): `iter` / `fix_commit_sha` / `fix_files` / `loop_role` / `synthesized: true`, guarded against double-counting and misattribution, telemetry-gated, and marked `synthesized` in the derived record so a reconstructed floor is never mistaken for an agent-written record. Symmetrically, `--persist` also stamps `synthesized: false` onto the **durable** copy of any agent-written `iter-<N>.json` that lacks the key (issue #534, the emitted-provenance backfill) — moving the emitted record's provenance stamp off the agent's decision path, so an emitted record (`.synthesized == false`) and a synthesized one (`.synthesized == true`) are told apart by a deterministic JSON boolean rather than by field-absence, and a skipped emit surfaces as the absent record. The floor recovers only the effectiveness skeleton — never license to skip the emit, which alone carries the checklist / findings / cost detail. This makes the **effectiveness** half of the telemetry (dispatch counts, findings, verdicts, fix decisions) recoverable; the **token/wall-clock cost** half is captured *live* by the loop. Issue #437 observed that the cloud `execution_file` carries the tokens, wall-clock, the subagent dispatch roster, and cost with **zero agent cooperation**, and — on the local tier — that the `Stop`-hook transcript's per-message token counts are **real** figures, not streaming placeholders (wall-clock and the dispatch roster were *not* measured locally; see [`docs/internal/execution-file-shape.md`](execution-file-shape.md)), so an agent-independent cost floor is buildable. **Issue #475 builds the cloud half (Layer 4, the harness-side cost floor):** on each writable cloud workflow, `--persist` reads the harness-written `execution_file` (via `scripts/extract-execution-cost.py` + `scripts/prepare-harness-floor.sh`) and lands its cost as a per-run `harness_cost` record field — the first floor NOT fed by an agent-volunteered operand, so a cloud run that dropped every telemetry emit still contributes a cost record. Two boundaries remain: a hard-death run that never produced an execution file stays uncovered (announced by a named breadcrumb, never silent), and the read-only auto-review tier is cost-unmeasured by this floor (no persist path there yet). On the **local** tier the cost half is still captured only by the live loop. See [`docs/internal/efficiency-trace.md`](efficiency-trace.md).
- **Auto-review telemetry relay (cross-workflow, consumer-shipped — issues #489, #502).** The read-only auto-review tier runs on the PR head with `contents: read`, so it cannot push: instead its `devflow-runner.yml` collect step **stages** its observability records and uploads them as a workflow artifact, and a separate **trusted** workflow — `telemetry-push.yml`, triggered by `workflow_run` off the auto-review's completion so its own code comes from the default branch — downloads that artifact, validates it as untrusted PR-influenced input, and pushes the records to the telemetry branch. This is the GitHub-secure pattern for handling data a PR-triggered run produced: the auto-review job never gains a write credential. The relay **ships to consumers** — `install.sh` copies `telemetry-push.yml`, and both the collect step and the pusher resolve their bundled helpers at the vendored `.prflow/vendor/prflow/scripts/…` path (materialized by the `vendor-plugin` action, with a repo-root fallback for PRFlow's own checkout) rather than a repo-relative `scripts/` path that would be absent in a consumer's vendored install.
- A configurable `efficiency_cut_candidate_min_dispatch` (default 3) governs when a persistently unhelpful agent is flagged as a candidate to cut (consumed by a follow-up cross-run analyzer).
- **Live progress comment:** in PR mode, `/prflow:review` authors a single run-keyed comment that updates incrementally, a blueprint up front, then per-phase results as they land.

All telemetry is gated by config (`prflow_review_and_fix.efficiency_telemetry_enabled`, `prflow_review.live_progress_comment_enabled`, both default `true`) and is **best-effort**: a telemetry failure never aborts the loop.

**The measurement substrate: the unified experiment record.** A run's *cost* (tokens, iterations, agent verdicts) lands on the `prflow-telemetry` branch under `.prflow/logs/efficiency/`, while its *outcome* (the first independent review's verdict and Important-finding count) lives in GitHub's review history — two stores with no per-PR join, so a before/after comparison of a config change could not be measured. `scripts/build-experiment-records.py` (invoked by `/prflow:retrospective-weekly` between Materialize and the state PR, on the local/interactive tier only, after it fetches the telemetry branch) assembles the join: one tracked line per merged PR in `.prflow/learnings/experiment-records.jsonl`, joining **all** of the PR's efficiency records — read as a `(slug, run-id)`-keyed **union** of the telemetry branch and any legacy tracked `.prflow/logs/`, branch-wins — (both slug families, as a per-run cost list — never newest-wins) to its retrospective entry, its shape-selected review **verdict** (with a progress-comment fallback and a null-verdict arm), the **Important-finding count** (parsed from the run-keyed progress comment via the engine's own `review.commit_id` ↔ `Reviewed HEAD:` join), the **permission-denial count** (made durable and API-retrievable by including it verbatim in the `Devflow Review` check-run summary — `unavailable` stays `unavailable`, never coerced to `0` — with a positive-count-only annotation fallback for historical PRs), and a **config fingerprint** naming the config variant that produced the run. A `telemetry_complete` flag on each entry in `efficiency_runs[]` lets analyses exclude degraded runs rather than silently averaging them, and a `provenance` map keeps a `null` field distinguishable from an unqueried one. The assembler is idempotent (keyed by PR), incremental (never a full-history sweep), and missing-source-tolerant. See [`docs/internal/efficiency-trace.md`](efficiency-trace.md) for the store schema and the abandoned-run cost-side bias.

**Local workflow flight recorder.** For interactive performance R&D, Claude's native project JSONL is the transcript source of truth. A fail-open `UserPromptSubmit` observer writes metadata-only start manifests under `.prflow/tmp/workflow-manifests/`; a read-only inventory selects only sessions whose first authoritative user message invoked a registered workflow, including approved short aliases and corroborated embedded invocations; and an explicit session-ID import creates or refreshes the sensitive, byte-verified bundle under `.prflow/tmp/workflow-runs/`. Restart active Claude Code sessions after changing the local hook configuration. Generalized `.prflow/tmp/workflow-runs/` and legacy `.prflow/tmp/implement-runs/` bundles remain readable, while Claude owns native transcript retention. Analysis is read-only; recurrence requires at least two distinct sessions with the same workflow, mode, and prompt fingerprint, never pools two occurrences from one session as independent evidence, and never files issues automatically. See [`docs/internal/workflow-flight-recorder.md`](workflow-flight-recorder.md).

**Verification-launch baseline (Wave 1, issue #527).** An offline analyzer
(`scripts/verification_baseline.py`) extends this recorder foundation into a
read-only, source-provenanced baseline of actual verification launches from local
native transcript events, plus a local + cloud lifecycle census (eligibility +
source missingness) that is independent of transcript survival. It is pure Python
standard library with no subprocess, no network, and no model/provider call;
`workspace_state` coverage comes from explicit source-event results, not
analyzer-time inspection. Repeated launches are classified conservatively as
`single`, `candidate_transport_retry`, `intentional_rerun_evidence`,
`independent_lifecycle`, or `unclassifiable` — never as automatically proven
duplicates — and a deterministic SHA-256-seeded manual-review sample selects
relationship groups for human adjudication. Cloud launch analysis is excluded in
Wave 1; cloud rows are census/missingness-only from an explicit Actions snapshot
(`scripts/export-workflow-lifecycle-census.py`, the sole networked step). Output
is local and gitignored under owner-only `0700`/`0600`. See
[`docs/internal/workflow-flight-recorder.md`](workflow-flight-recorder.md#verification-launch-baseline-wave-1).

---

## 17. Configuration reference

The local tier needs **no config**. To customize, `/prflow:init` scaffolds `.prflow/config.json` (JSON, read by a single python3-based resolver, no `yq`/PyYAML/Node prerequisite for config) and a `config.schema.json` your editor uses for autocomplete. Top-level keys:

| Key | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `base_branch` | Review/merge base (default `main`). |
| `claude_model` | Default model (default `claude-opus-5`). |
| `providers.<name>` | Cloud-tier (opt-in): third-party model routes — `base_url`, `auth` (`bearer`\|`api_key`\|`bedrock_api_key`), `timeout_ms`, `effort_supported`, and an `env` map whose keys are name-filtered before export (a denied name refuses the run; separately, a beside-it warn block prints one `::warning::` and **continues** — not refuse — when the map names any of four watched keys `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `API_TIMEOUT_MS`, `HOME`, `RUNNER_TEMP`, whose map value still wins). A `bearer`/`api_key` route is an **Anthropic-compatible** HTTP endpoint (requires `base_url`, exported as `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`); a `bedrock_api_key` route reaches **Amazon Bedrock** with a long-lived Bedrock API key (no `base_url`; exported as `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_BEDROCK` with `use_bedrock: true`, and requires `AWS_REGION` in the `env` map). The API credential is never named in config — it is always the fixed `DEVFLOW_PROVIDER_API_KEY` repo secret, reused by both route kinds. See §14 / `docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`. |
| `prflow.provider` / `prflow_implement.provider` / `prflow_runner.provider` (+ each section's `claude_model`) | Cloud-tier (opt-in): route that workflow section through a named `providers` entry and/or override its model, independently per section. Unset → today's Anthropic-OAuth default. |
| `prflow_version` | Cloud-tier: git ref the workflows fetch the plugin from (thin install). |
| `prflow.allowed_bots` / `allowed_users` | Trigger authorization (also the trusted-filer allowlist). |
| `prflow.workpad_marker` | Workpad comment marker. |
| `prflow.effort` / `allowed_tools` | Light command path settings. |
| `prflow.execution_diagnostics_enabled` | Gates cloud-tier surfacing of a `claude-code-action` run's execution diagnostics — the run summary (`is_error`, `num_turns`, `duration_ms`, `total_cost_usd`, `permission_denials_count`, `claude_code_version`) plus permission-denial detail — to the job log and `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` via `scripts/surface-execution-diagnostics.sh`. A read-only diagnostic: never changes a job's pass/fail result, adds no token scope, uploads no artifact. Default `true`. (Read by the `Surface execution diagnostics` step in each cloud-tier workflow.) |
| `prflow.execution_denial_commands_enabled` | Gates whether the **scrubbed text** of the engine's denied Bash commands is persisted into each run's efficiency record on the `prflow-telemetry` branch (durably), via `scripts/build-denial-record.sh` in the `Persist … (backstop)` step of both live tiers (issue #1064). The scrub (`scripts/scrub-credentials.sh`) is an **incomplete blocklist**, so the record discloses `scrub.blocklist_incomplete: true`. **Default `true`** — an upgrading repository begins persisting scrubbed denied-command text without opting in; set `false` to disable. Deliberately the **opposite** default from `execution_transcript_artifact_enabled` (default `false`): that key gates the whole transcript (prompt text, repo content, a possible env dump), this one only a bounded scrubbed command-only field — they are not harmonized. The denial **count** and denied **`tool_name`** are always persisted and are **not** gated by this key (a number and a fixed-vocabulary tool identifier carry no credential risk). Read at runtime via `config-get.sh` (live in the same run). This restores the durable producer for the experiment store's `permission_denials_count`, whose `Devflow Review` check-run producer #936 removed. |
| `prflow.attribute_commits_to_triggerer` | Opt-in (default `false`): on the cloud-tier **writer** runs (`/prflow:implement`'s `claude` job and `/prflow:review-and-fix`'s `command` job), author the agent's git commits as the human who triggered the run, so `git blame`/history show the responsible human — parity with local runs. Read at TRIGGER time from the trusted default-branch checkout (never the PR head), so its effect is **post-merge-only**. Humans only, fail-safe: identity is emitted only for a `.type == "User"` login without the `[bot]` suffix; any other type, a `[bot]` login, or an unestablished type falls back to current authorship with a warning. Fail-open — advisory, never gates the run — and needs no new credential (commit author/committer is git metadata, independent of the push token). The read-only review tier never commits and is untouched. Resolved by `scripts/resolve-committer-identity.sh` via the `Resolve committer identity` step in each writer workflow. See §14 / [`docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`](cloud-setup.md). |
| `prflow_implement.effort` / `allowed_tools` | `/prflow:implement` settings. |
| `prflow_implement.update_branch_checkpoints` | Base-branch update checkpoints (boolean, default `true`; disabled exactly when the value serializes to the string `false` via `config-get.sh` — an explicit JSON `false`, the JSON string `"false"`, or `[false]`; anything else leaves them enabled — issue #312 valid-falsy). When on, `/prflow:implement` brings its feature branch up to date with `base_branch` at four checkpoints — the end of Phase 1.4, pre-draft-PR (Phase 3.1), each fix iteration of the review-and-fix loop under `--push-each-iteration` plus a Loop Exit checkpoint (Checkpoint 3) that `skills/review-and-fix/references/loop-exit.md` gates **only in PR mode with `--push-each-iteration`** — not on the flag alone — and pre-ready (Phase 4.3) — through the shared `scripts/update-branch-checkpoint.sh`, so every state the merge gate or an auto-review consumes (including the terminal pushed state) is current. Since issue #779 the Phase 1 checkpoint is **arm-independent**: it is the last step of §1.4 and runs on the new-branch, adopted-branch, and landed-resume arms alike (so Phase 1 reconciles with the base on **every** path reaching Phase 1.5 — a resumed run can no longer proceed on a stale base), while §1.4's freshness record **remains adopted-arm-only** (§1.4.0.5's Verdict B classification was widened to the landed-resume arm by issue #780). Correspondingly, Phase 4.3 gates publication and the `Complete` flip on a clean checkpoint-4 token — see [`docs/internal/implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md). This key **also governs the checkpoint inside a standalone `/prflow:review-and-fix --push-each-iteration` run.** See §14 and [`docs/internal/implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md). |
| `prflow.publish_model_effort` | Gates the model+effort clause of the shared provenance line `_Generated via <command> (v<version>[, <model>][, <effort>])_` (Markdown italics), which both the `/prflow:implement` draft PR and the `/prflow:create-issue` issue emit (boolean, default `true`; disabled **only** by the JSON boolean `false` read directly in Python — the string `"false"` and the array `[false]` do **not** disable it, unlike the `config-get.sh` valid-falsy rule; anything else leaves it enabled). When on, `scripts/render-pr-provenance-line.py` names the model (from the session transcript's most-recent assistant record) and effort (`CLAUDE_EFFORT`) alongside the version when each can be established; when off it names the plugin version alone. The **version is always emitted** whatever this key holds. The renderer takes the command name as a required `--command` argument (exiting non-zero only on its absence) and one key now serves both commands. Read at run time from the working tree (not the trigger-time extract step of `devflow-implement.yml`), so a repository that changes it sees the change in the same run. Supersedes the former per-command key in the `prflow_implement` section, which is read by nothing. See [`docs/internal/implement-skill.md`](implement-skill.md). |
| `prflow_runner.provision_env` / `effort` / `allowed_tools` | Automated-reviewer build-environment opt-in + tools. |
| `prflow_review.live_progress_comment_enabled` / `agent_overrides` | Review engine: live comment + per-subagent model/effort. |
| `prflow_review.verdict_severity_threshold` | Moves the REJECT line of Phase 4.2 and, as its complement, the APPROVE-with-notes boundary (enum `critical/important/suggestion`, default `critical`): a finding at or above it, excluding deferral-demoted ones, drives REJECT, and a run whose findings all sit below it lands on APPROVE with notes. Those two rules are the only Phase 4.2 rules that read the key; every other Phase 4.2 rule and verdict label is untouched. Both standalone `/prflow:review` and the Phase-3 pass of `/prflow:review-and-fix` inherit it. A bad/absent value falls back to the default with a breadcrumb (never aborts). Threshold-independent carve-outs sit above the numbered rules and are unaffected by it — see §8 (and the Phase 4.1.5 behavior-inert prose cap, which fixes a covered finding's severity at Suggestion so the default threshold does not REJECT on it). |
| `prflow_review.require_up_to_date` / `require_ci_green` | Auto-trigger preconditions (default `true`): defer the review — a neutral "waiting" `Devflow Review` check — while the PR branch is behind base / other CI on the head is pending or red; `false` restores the unconditional trigger. `require_ci_green` collapses the non-self Actions runs to the latest per `(workflow_id, event)` group before gating (a superseded run never wedges the review), and a run awaiting manual approval (`action_required`) defers with the distinct `ci-approval-required` reason. See §14. |
| `prflow_review.stale_prose.enabled` / `severity` | Phase 0.6 deterministic stale counted-prose lint (`enabled` default `true` — only an explicit `false` disables; `severity` default `important`, enum `critical/important/suggestion`). Each STALE row becomes a Phase 4.2 finding at `severity`; a refused/absent/errored/disabled helper degrades to a recorded note, never a stall or silent skip. |
| `prflow_review.stall_backstop.enabled` / `max_resume_attempts` | No-verdict auto-resume backstop (default `enabled: true`, `max_resume_attempts: 2`): when a cloud review ends with no verdict for the head, post a bounded App-token-authored `/prflow:review` re-trigger so it re-runs without a human; degrades to the dead-end flip when the cap is exhausted, disabled, or no App token is configured. The review-side sibling of `prflow_implement.stall_backstop`. |
| `prflow_review_and_fix.fix_severity_threshold` | The `/prflow:review-and-fix` loop routes to the fixer every finding at or above this severity (enum `critical/important/suggestion`, default `important`); findings below it stay advisory. Independent of `prflow_review.verdict_severity_threshold`, except that every REJECT-driving finding is always fixable regardless of this key — so a Phase 4.1.5 capped Suggestion still reaches the fixer at `suggestion` while driving no REJECT at the default verdict threshold. A bad/absent value falls back to the default with a breadcrumb (never aborts). |
| `prflow_review_and_fix.fix_below_threshold_iterations` | Below-Important fix-loop damper (integer ≥ 0, default 1). Bites **only** when `fix_severity_threshold` resolves to `suggestion`; inert at the default `important` threshold. Sets how many leading iterations route below-`important` findings to the fixer; after the window, an iteration with no Critical/Important finding parks each below-`important` finding as advisory (decision `below-important-damper`, `skip_category` `below-important-damper-parked`) and starts no fix iteration, while an iteration routing a Critical/Important finding still carries below-`important` findings along. Resolved once at loop start via the `max_iterations` resolve-validate pattern; a bad/absent value falls back to `1` with a breadcrumb, and `0` is a legal value (park from iteration 1) that is not coalesced to the default. At or above `max_iterations` restores the prior behavior. REJECT-drivers always route. |
| `prflow_review_and_fix.efficiency_telemetry_enabled` / `efficiency_cut_candidate_min_dispatch` | Telemetry. |
| `receiving_review.fix_severity_threshold` | Consulted **only** by a direct invocation of `/prflow:receiving-code-review` (the `/prflow:review-and-fix` loop governs its own routing via `prflow_review_and_fix.fix_severity_threshold` and does not read this section). When the verdict is already non-blocking, the diff is re-opened for a finding at or above this severity (enum `critical/important/suggestion`, default `critical`). The demonstrable-correctness-defect / documented-falsehood carve-out re-opens the diff at every threshold value — a correctness principle, not a severity grade — with behavior-inert prose excluded from its scope (graded by severity like any other finding). A bad/absent value falls back to the default with a breadcrumb (never aborts). |
| `telemetry.enabled` | Master switch for the five enrolled optional telemetry sub-keys plus the telemetry-branch push (boolean, default `true`; issue #2035). `prflow.execution_transcript_artifact_enabled` is deliberately not enrolled — it already defaults to `false`. Disabled **only** by the JSON boolean `false` — every other state (wrong-typed value, string `"false"`, `0`, `null`, missing, corrupt config, resolver error) fails safe to telemetry-**on**, matching the fail-safe direction the individual gates already use. Read at runtime from the checked-out tree via `scripts/config-get.sh` + `scripts/telemetry-master-off.py`. When `false`, the five enrolled default-`true` sub-keys **inherit** it on their resolver miss path (when their own key does not resolve to a value — absent, JSON null, or an empty string) — `prflow_review_and_fix.efficiency_telemetry_enabled`, `prflow.execution_diagnostics_enabled`, `prflow.execution_denial_commands_enabled`, `prflow_review.live_progress_comment_enabled`, `create_issue.investigation_record_enabled` — and the two telemetry-branch push-path helpers skip: `lib/efficiency-trace.sh --persist` (the workpad-copy push) and `scripts/collect-staged-telemetry.sh`. A sub-key value that **resolves** always wins over the master, in both directions. Rollback is removing the key; mixed plugin versions are safe (a consumer whose vendored copy predates the change ignores the key and keeps telemetry on until upgrade). |
| `telemetry.branch` | Name of the dedicated orphan branch every writable run persists observability artifacts to (default `prflow-telemetry`, issue #441); auto-created on first use, verified logs-only before appending. Keep workflow `push:` triggers `main`-filtered so a push to it runs no CI. |
| `setup.*` | Cloud-tier runtime provisioning (versions, services, install lines). |
| `docs.*` | Doc paths, enable flags, release-notes file, CHANGELOG file, labels. |
| `create_issue.investigation_record_enabled` | `/prflow:create-issue` publication gate (boolean, default `true`; disabled exactly when `config-get.sh` reads the value as the literal string `false`). `/prflow:create-issue` always sorts a draft into the implementer **brief** (the issue body) and a separate **investigation record** (rejected designs, refutation prose, confirmatory evidence, deliberation, lower-severity hazards); this key gates **publication only** — the sorting runs on every run regardless. When `false`, no record file is written and the record comment (posted via `scripts/post-issue-comment.sh` as the issue's first comment, ahead of the implement-offer comment, with workflow-trigger tokens neutralized) is not posted. |
| `prflow_retrospective.*` | Weekly loop settings (watched authors, min occurrences, cooldown, etc.). Detection is by the reserved `PRFlow` label (its superseded `DevFlow` spelling is still accepted) + a closes-issue fallback (see §12); `implementation_branch_prefix` is an **optional** extra match path, not the detection mechanism, and may be left empty. |
| `workflows.*` | Per-workflow enable/disable toggles. |

`/prflow:init` auto-detects languages (Node, Go, Rust, Java, Ruby, PHP, .NET, Make, Docker) and merges matching build/test/lint tools into three independent allowlists, plus the `setup` block, idempotently (your values always win).

Re-running `/prflow:init` (or `install.sh`) also **backfills** newly-added keys into an existing `.prflow/config.json` without clobbering your values. Backfill is add-only, so it cannot propagate a key *removal*; for the one case where that matters — a `prflow_review.agent_overrides` entry pinning Claude Haiku must not carry an `effort` key, which Haiku rejects with HTTP 400 — re-scaffold runs a separate idempotent cleanup that strips `effort` from any Haiku-pinned override (see [review-agent-overrides.md](review-agent-overrides.md)). An already-clean config is left byte-identical.

After scaffolding and the dependency preflight, and immediately before the project-memory check below, `/prflow:init` runs a **documentation-state check**. It reads the two documentation locations from `.docs.internal` (default `docs/internal/`) and `.docs.external` (default `docs/external/`) — through the same bundled config helper the other docs skills use, and deliberately **not** the `docs.internal_enabled`/`docs.external_enabled` flags, whose published scope is a different pass — and sorts each into exactly one of four states: holds real content (at least one on-disk file under it at any depth whose name is not `.gitkeep`, tracked or not), exists but empty, absent, or could-not-establish. When both locations hold real content, or when the repo root cannot be resolved, it says nothing. When the internal location is empty or absent it explains, in plain language, what internal vs external docs are and why written internal docs pay for themselves (every later `/prflow:implement` and `/prflow:review` run then starts from a map instead of re-exploring the codebase, and bootstrapping means reading the whole codebase and takes a while), then asks whether to create internal docs now. On an explicit yes — and only when the runner offers a subagent-dispatch tool — it dispatches **one** subagent running `/prflow:docs-bootstrap-internal`, scoped to write only under the internal docs location and forbidden every version-control command (the scoping-plus-after-the-fact-re-read substitutes for the usual commit-before-dispatch rule, since init has itself left uncommitted config writes). After the subagent returns it reports the generated files as uncommitted, tells the owner to review and commit them, and reports any file init wrote earlier in the run whose contents changed. On a decline, a non-interactive run, or a runner with no dispatch tool it writes and dispatches nothing and just prints the `/prflow:docs-bootstrap-internal` command; a dispatch failure is reported rather than raised, and init still completes. The step **never** runs the external bootstrap: when the external location is empty or absent it prints one line naming `/prflow:docs-bootstrap-external`, adding the "usable once internal docs exist" precondition only when the internal location is itself empty or absent. `/prflow:init` commits nothing on any path.

Then `/prflow:init` runs one final **advisory project-memory check**: if the repo root has no `CLAUDE.md` it nudges you toward the built-in `/init` (project memory measurably improves PRFlow's review/implement results), and it points any agent-instruction files you already keep for other tools (`.github/copilot-instructions.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `.cursorrules`) at `CLAUDE.md` `@`-import reuse. The check is strictly advisory — it never writes or edits any file and never blocks init (which has already succeeded), and stays silent when nothing is actionable.

---

## 18. Installation & updates

**Local tier (one line):**
```bash
claude plugin marketplace add The01Geek/prflow \
  && claude plugin install prflow@devflow-marketplace
```
PRFlow declares **zero companion-plugin dependencies** (every external asset it once dispatched is now first-party — see §6), so `/plugin install` resolves on its own with no `claude-plugins-official` prerequisite. PyYAML is a separate, manual extra (`python3 -m pip install PyYAML` — by package name; a relative `-r requirements.txt` resolves against the user's working directory, not the plugin cache); `/plugin install` never runs `pip`. On the local tier it is advisory rather than a hard prerequisite — a missing PyYAML degrades one runtime helper and `bash lib/preflight.sh` reports it but exits 0 (it stays required for the test suite, CI, and the cloud tiers).

The plugin install above runs **no installer script** — `install.sh` belongs to the optional cloud tier only, and a local-tier-only user never runs it.

**Cloud tier (optional, from repo root)** — download, read, then run, with both refs pinned to a release tag:
```bash
curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/The01Geek/prflow/v2.36.2/install.sh -o devflow-install.sh
# review devflow-install.sh, then:
DEVFLOW_REF=v2.36.2 bash devflow-install.sh
```
The URL ref fixes which installer bytes you review and run; `DEVFLOW_REF` (default `main`; a tag, SHA, or branch) fixes which ref the installer clones its payload from — pinning the URL alone leaves the payload on `main`. Substitute a newer tag in both places to move the pin; every version is tagged, so the [Tags page](https://github.com/The01Geek/prflow/tags) names the current one, while the [Releases page](https://github.com/The01Geek/prflow/releases) announces the feature releases — see [`docs/internal/install.md`](install.md#pinning-the-installer). Piping the download straight to `bash` works but forfeits the review step. Thin by default (installs workflows, actions, a local marketplace, a config scaffold, and pins `prflow_version`). `DEVFLOW_VENDOR=1` commits the tree instead.

**Updates:** local tier, `/prflow:init` provisions the project `.claude/settings.json` — it registers `devflow-marketplace` under `extraKnownMarketplaces` with `autoUpdate: true` and enables the plugin under `enabledPlugins`, so Claude Code keeps the plugin updated (additive and non-clobbering, idempotent on re-run); it never sets `permissions.defaultMode`. This project-scope write is **ungated**: unlike the consent-gated auto-mode step described next, it is performed directly by `/prflow:init` with no separate opt-in, in the same run the command is typed, into a committed file collaborators inherit — the write is diff-visible and the breadcrumb ends "Review the change before committing." The registration it writes carries **no version pin** (`ref`/`tag`/`sha`/`version` are all absent), so `autoUpdate: true` tracks the marketplace repo's default branch. Selectable `auto` mode is provisioned **separately, at user scope, only with explicit consent, and only on a third-party model provider**: `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_AUTO_MODE` is honored only from user scope (`~/.claude/settings.json`) or managed settings, so the project provisioner skips it (writing it there is a silent no-op) and a dedicated helper (`scripts/provision-auto-mode.sh`, run by `/prflow:init`) merges it into `~/.claude/settings.json` after asking the user — making `auto` **selectable, never on** (no `permissions.defaultMode`, plan/model/admin gates still apply), preserving a deliberately-disabled `"0"`, idempotent/atomic/fail-closed, and printing the one-line setting instead of writing if the user declines. The env var has **no effect on the Anthropic API** (auto mode is already available there by default) and only does anything on Amazon Bedrock / Google Vertex AI / Microsoft Foundry, so the whole step is gated on the provider: it runs only when one of `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_BEDROCK`/`VERTEX`/`FOUNDRY` is truthy and is skipped entirely on Anthropic-direct. The gate is two-layer — the skill pre-checks the provider and skips the prompt silently, and `provision-auto-mode.sh --apply` enforces the same check as a deterministic backstop (it leaves the settings file unchanged and exits 0 with a breadcrumb on Anthropic-direct). This is local-tier only — the cloud tier neither needs nor receives a `.claude/settings.json`. Cloud tier, bump `prflow_version` or re-run `install.sh` (re-running only re-stamps `prflow_version` itself when eligible — a hand-set non-SHA value is preserved; see `docs/internal/cloud-setup.md`).

---

## 19. Repository layout

```text
.claude-plugin/
├── plugin.json          # plugin manifest (declares dependencies)
└── marketplace.json     # this repo is its own marketplace
skills/                  # one SKILL.md per skill
├── implement/phases/    #   /prflow:implement's per-phase reference files
├── implement/references/ #  /prflow:implement's predicate-gated references
└── review/phases/       #   the review engine's gated per-phase references (§8)
agents/                  # first-party subagents: the review-engine trio (checklist-generator /
                         #   -deduper / -verifier), the implement discovery/planning pair
                         #   (code-explorer, code-architect), the five vendored review agents,
                         #   and the implement-phase helper agents (branch-setup, ac-*-verifier, …)
scripts/                 # branch-for-issue.py, config-get.sh, ensure-label.sh,
                         #   apply-labels.sh, file-deferrals.py, match-deferrals.py, parse-acs.py,
                         #   workpad.py, …
lib/                     # retrospective-loop helpers (*.sh, *.jq), preflight.sh, writing-standard.md, test/
.github/                 # cloud tier: workflows + composite actions (incl. vendor-plugin)
.prflow/                # config.example.json + config.schema.json (+ learnings/, logs/),
                         #   prompt-extensions/, tracked live config.json
.changeset/              # pending version-bump changesets (consumed on merge to main)
docs/
├── internal/            # developer/agent reference — start at docs/internal/index.md;
│                        #   deep flat-root records (DEVFLOW_SYSTEM_OVERVIEW.md, implement-skill.md,
│                        #   cloud-allowlist.md, cloud-setup.md, install.md, …), the categorized
│                        #   layer (architecture/, skills/, agents/, workflows/, operations/,
│                        #   improvement-loops/), and historical cutovers/
└── external/            # customer-facing docs site (Mintlify), release-notes.md
install.sh               # one-command cloud-tier install/update
```

Skills reference bundled helpers via the portable single-statement anchor (`$CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR` preferred, runner-reported base directory fallback — see §6) so they resolve from any install location and runner. The complete suite runs with `bash lib/test/run.sh` (CI runs it on every PR). For local iteration, cohesive test blocks may also be registered under `test_modules` in `scripts/workflow-flight-recorder-registry.json` and run explicitly with `bash lib/test/run-module.sh <module-id>`.

**Cloud-writer reachability contract (issue #543).** `lib/test/cloud_writer_contract.py` is a machine-auditable source of truth for the three cloud execution roots (implement / light-command / review) and their transitive skill/phase closure. Its `check_closure()` guard fails when a root or dispatch edge reaches an asset that is not classified (or a classified asset that is missing on disk), and its `verify` CLI pins the `devflow-cloud-writer-contract-v1` runtime manifest (`scripts/devflow-cloud-writer-contract.json`, which SHA-256-pins every reached skill asset — `SKILL.md`, `phases/*.md`, `references/*.md`, and reviewer prose — plus every required helper) against that same closure. As of issue #1445 that pinning is a **main-side** property, not a per-branch suite gate: the manifest is written on `main` alone, so it derives from the closure at the moment `main` publishes it and a feature branch's checked-in copy may legitimately lag the branch's own closure until it merges. The merge-to-main job `.github/workflows/version-consolidate.yml` regenerates it with `python3 lib/test/cloud_writer_contract.py generate` immediately before its bump commit, so the published digests derive from the merged tree that ships them. **Known residual:** that regeneration sits *after* the job's no-pending-changesets early exit, so a merge that edits a pinned closure file but ships no changeset leaves the manifest stale until the next changeset-bearing merge, and nothing mechanically catches the window — the branch-side `verify` gate is gone and the retention check below flags hand-*mutation*, not staleness-vs-closure. The invariant it rests on (every engine-surface edit mandates a changeset) is a review-gate convention, not a mechanical gate; a changeset-less engine merge is already a broken state, since it also produces no version bump. A feature branch never regenerates it and is not gated on it — the branch-side `verify` drift gate and the `lib/test/regenerate-artifacts.py` batched-pass row were both removed, so two concurrent prompt-surface PRs no longer conflict in the manifest. The other half of that contract, that a branch must not mutate the artifact by hand, is reported by the CI-side merge-base check `lib/test/cloud-writer-retention-check.py`, which fails RED on any difference from the merge-base manifest — so hand-authored divergence is surfaced before merge, which is that check's whole scope; the staleness window above is not closed by it. That check runs in `ci.yml`'s `lint` job, so whether a red result also *blocks* the merge is a branch-protection question for the repository, not a property of the check — state it as detection, never as an impossibility. A second guard, `check_grant_sync()` (exposed as the `grant-sync` subcommand and driven by the suite), maps every helper literal reachable in that closure to the three current cloud profiles — implement, light-command, and review — and fails when a reachable literal lacks an explicit vendored grant, or when any other grant *covering* a reachable helper widens the executable trust boundary beyond that literal — an absolute or repo-root path, a basename or directory wildcard, a PATH-resolved bare name, or any other shape that still reaches the helper (the guard's own docstring is the authoritative class list; deliberate exceptions are registered **per profile** in `SANCTIONED_WILDCARD_GRANTS`, since a globally-scoped exemption would re-open the widening class for whichever profile does not carry the exempted grant). The grant source it reads is scoped to each profile's own grant-bearing region — `devflow-implement.yml`'s `--allowed-tools` block, the `TOOLS='…'` line elsewhere — located with `lib/test/extract-command-heads.py`'s scopers, so a vendored literal named only in a `run:` echo or a doc string cannot satisfy the granted arm for a helper the profile does not actually grant; a region that cannot be located fails closed as an unavailable grant source rather than falling back to the whole file. Its remaining documented scope limits — interpreter/wrapper heads, which every live profile legitimately grants, and grants spliced in by consumer config — are recorded at the guard, which is the authoritative enumeration. A third guard, `check_shape_conformance()` (exposed as the `shape-conformance` subcommand and driven by the suite), applies `lib/test/extract-command-shapes.py`'s probe-anchored rule tables to every fenced command in that closure, keyed by each profile whose own reach includes the asset — so a denied command shape in a reached asset is caught at the desk even when no file-glob scan names it. The profile→table mapping is explicit: a profile with no probe baseline of its own (`light-command`) is declared as carrying no table rather than borrowing another tier's evidence, and a profile with no declaration at all is reported, never silently unaudited. A fourth guard, `check_helper_boundary()` (issue #701; built on `extract-command-heads.py`'s `helper_boundary_violations`), requires the vendored helper path to be the first executable token of every cloud-reached fenced command that names a per-helper-granted bundled helper — rejecting, by classified reason, an unexpanded anchor, an absolute or repo-root path, a helper behind any other prefix, or a helper behind a granted launcher head (`env`/`xargs`/interpreter/process-wrapper) whose broad grant would otherwise mask the per-helper vendored grant; the launcher table is generated from each profile's parsed grants so it can never drift from the tier it governs. The decision record — including why AC2/AC3 were restated against the emission-time surface rather than amending the portable-anchor convention — is [`docs/internal/cloud-writer-boundary.md`](cloud-writer-boundary.md). `scripts/validate-cloud-writer-contract.py` is the companion pre-agent manifest validator whose rejection matrix is closed at exactly seventeen classes; its runtime pre-agent wiring is deferred to follow-up work.

**Focused review-and-fix iteration.** For the local review-and-fix contract loop, run the direct leading-token form `lib/test/run-module.sh review-and-fix-contract`. Selection is explicit: consulting the coverage map (`lib/test/modules/coverage-map.json`) to identify a candidate module is part of explicit selection — record the entry consulted and confirm the ID in the registry — while changed files never auto-route to a module. Cloud runs use `lib/test/run-module.sh <module-id>` when the tier grants it and a registered module covers the fix; otherwise they use the already-permitted complete suite. Focused verification is the iteration default: a focused pass covering the changed surface is sufficient for an intermediate commit or push. Two tiers of focused test qualify (issue #789): a registered shell module, and — for a `scripts/*.py`/`lib/*.py` unit — the `lib/test/test_*.py` file its coverage-map entry names in a `focused_test` field, invoked as a direct leading token (`lib/test/test_python_scripts.py`) rather than `python3 <path>`, the interpreter-head shape the cloud matcher denies, so the same command runs on both tiers. Focused-first is a **precondition** on the mid-iteration complete-suite launch, not merely routing advice: before a run launches the complete suite mid-iteration, every touched surface that has a covering focused test invocable on the tier has already been run this cycle, answered by the two coverage-map fields together (a `focused_test` for a Python unit, or a module id in `owner` for a shell surface). The exempt set is total over four grounds — no coverage-map entry at all (the majority case; the map covers only `lib/` and `scripts/`), a declared exempt subtree, an `unmodularized` entry with no `focused_test`, or a covering test the tier cannot invoke — and a covering test that ran and **failed** discharges the precondition for launching the suite to diagnose it. The precondition binds the **mid-iteration** launch only; the final completion gate is not gated on it, and a run that cannot establish which covering tests it already ran re-runs them (an unestablished record is not satisfied) rather than being blocked. The precondition now names a **sink** for what the run did (issue #1229): `scripts/focused_selection.py` is a round-trippable producer/reader whose per-surface entries carry either the discharging focused result (the coverage-map entry consulted and the target selected) or the exemption ground that applied, plus a `single_flight_consulted` field recording whether the `scripts/verification-flight.py` flight was consulted before a full-suite relaunch. An implement run sinks the `<!-- prflow:focused-selection … -->` marker it emits as a `## Progress` note through `scripts/workpad.py`; a standalone fix loop stores the record as `iter-<N>.json`'s `verification_evidence.focused_selection`. It is a record of what the run did — never a launch counter, ordinal, or changed-file-to-module routing table. The prompt extensions (`.prflow/prompt-extensions/{implement,review-and-fix,receiving-code-review}.md`) are its source of record; this paragraph is a non-authoritative summary. The complete suite runs mid-iteration only for a surface no focused test covers, and then only on its first cycle: a second mid-iteration cycle on that same uncovered surface extracts a durable module instead. A run that takes the complete suite mid-iteration records a `## Devflow Reflection` bullet naming which closed-set case applied, and a mid-iteration issue-#434 stale-prose `blocking-gate` skip on a dirty tree is expected rather than a reason to re-run. The final gate is preserved, and since issue #1607 this repository's **local/interactive implement** tier discharges it by reading CI for the head the run leaves rather than by a local complete-suite pass; `CLAUDE.md`'s tier ladder carries that rule in one copy and this sentence is a pointer to it, not a second statement of the procedure. The standalone fix-loop and reception tiers are **not** changed by it — `.prflow/prompt-extensions/{review-and-fix,receiving-code-review}.md` continue to govern those runs, and a loop still does not cite CI for its own progress. A nonzero failure tally, a nonempty skip tally, or a run that never started is still not a completion. The local run remains the signal a contributor **troubleshoots** from, because its failure detail is richer than CI's. Skips are not clean. The cloud `/prflow:implement` in-env gate (issue #405) is unchanged: such a run verifies in its own environment and never waits on, polls, re-checks, or cites CI for its own progress.

**Disposable verification-rig reuse (issue #754).** The focused-module iteration default above covers *pre-registered* test modules, and Phase 2.2.4's *Reuse & Altitude* plan gate covers *production* code. A third case falls between them: a throwaway rig a run builds mid-iteration purely to exercise code in isolation — a scratch repo to drive a git-consuming helper, a fixture config to drive a config reader, an interpreter/CLI wrapper that calls a helper with crafted argv (an illustrative floor, not a closed list). Three verification/fix-iteration surfaces now name that case in per-surface-adapted wording: `skills/implement/phases/phase-2-sweeps-contract.md` §2.3, `skills/review-and-fix/references/fixing.md` Step 3, and the vendored `skills/receiving-code-review/SKILL.md` verification gate (whose copy is strictly repo-agnostic). Each says: keep the rig under an already-ignored scratch path (`.prflow/tmp/…` internally; a consumer-ignored scratch path in the vendored body), which no explicitly-scoped checkpoint or final commit ever names, so it is never staged — a nested `git init` rig left elsewhere lands as a gitlink — record its location on the channel that surface actually has (a workpad `--note` when implement-driven, the run-scoped `.prflow/tmp/review/<slug>/<run-id>/…` record on a standalone review-and-fix run, a run-persisted channel where the consumer flow offers one), and reuse it on the next iteration instead of rebuilding it — but **only after confirming it still exercises the current code shape**. When that shape has changed, rebuilding is the correct move: a rig keyed to a superseded shape drives paths that no longer exist and yields a false GREEN. This is advisory prose at the same altitude as the two rules it sits beside; it adds no gate, command, config key, or tool grant.

**Focused create-issue iteration.** For the local `/prflow:create-issue` contract loop, run `bash lib/test/run-module.sh create-issue-contract`. It carries the extracted Step 3.6 audit, state-owner cutover, authoring-discipline, and revision-delta contracts (with a provenance map in `lib/test/modules/create-issue-contract.inventory.md`). The same explicit-selection and focused-default-plus-final-gate discipline applies as for review-and-fix above.

**Focused capability-profiles iteration.** For the local #561 capability-profile-generator loop, run `bash lib/test/run-module.sh capability-profiles`. It carries the extracted `lib/generate-capability-profiles.py --check` coverage — the manifest and region adversarial matrices, planted-defect controls, idempotency/determinism, no-runtime-read, and the PR #588 hardening rows (provenance in `lib/test/modules/capability-profiles.inventory.md`).

**Focused installer/workflow-wiring iteration (issue #695).** For the local installer and workflow-wiring loop, run `bash lib/test/run-module.sh installer-wiring`. It carries the `install.sh` and cloud-workflow wiring coverage extracted as a syntactic unit from `lib/test/run.sh` — the gh-wrapper/token-refresher workflow wiring and its step ordering, the installer's output validation and guard recipes, fingerprint-comparison symmetry, workflow-token and secret-file permission pins, and the Windows mode-probe arms (provenance in `lib/test/modules/installer-wiring.inventory.md`). The same explicit-selection and focused-default-plus-final-gate discipline applies as for the modules above.

**Focused harness Python-guard iteration (issues #707 and #810).** For the monolith-only Python guards whose subject is a single code unit and whose verification is self-contained, run `bash lib/test/run-module.sh harness-python-guards`. It carries the driver blocks extracted from `lib/test/run.sh` for the `#600` create-issue audit-prompt renderer, the `#527` verification-launch baseline analyzer, the `#528` single-flight verification ledger, the `#668` reception-identity producer, and the `#591` coverage-map ratchet guard — the last as both its live-tree invocation and its unit test, plus a planted coverage-map drift as a positive control that the module goes RED when that guard breaks. It also drives the focused `pin-corpus-lint.py` unit suite: typed declaration parsing, path-aware complete-site scoping, helper/raw wording-pin parity, classification-preserving moves, registry population closure, and injected fail-closed setup failures. The raw-presence coverage includes positive fixed-and-quiet shell `grep` guards over repository files and direct file-text assertions in tracked or untracked `lib/test/test_*.py` leaves; an uninspectable typed target is a finding, not an exemption. The production pin scan remains a whole-tree required gate in `run.sh`; the focused module tests its logic without running the maintainer census or reading its inventory (provenance and deliberate exclusions are in `lib/test/modules/harness-python-guards.inventory.md`). The guards that test the module system itself (`lib/test/test_module_runner.py`, `lib/test/test_module_harness.py`) remain unextracted because a module running them would be circular. The same explicit-selection and focused-default-plus-final-gate discipline applies as for the modules above.

**Focused workflow-flight-recorder iteration (issues #563/#566).** For the local workflow-flight-recorder loop, run `bash lib/test/run-module.sh workflow-flight-recorder`. Authored natively alongside the selectable-module machinery rather than carved out of `lib/test/run.sh`, it drives `test_workflow_flight_recorder.py` and `test_workflow_analyzer.py` through `devflow_run_focused_python_test` (provenance in `lib/test/modules/workflow-flight-recorder.inventory.md`). The same explicit-selection and focused-default-plus-final-gate discipline applies as for the modules above.

**Focused regenerate-artifacts iteration (issue #619).** For the local generated-artifact regeneration loop, run `bash lib/test/run-module.sh regenerate-artifacts`. A new module (not an extraction), it covers `lib/test/regenerate-artifacts.py` — building each per-assertion fixture from the git index alone (see *Tracked-only module fixtures* below) and pairing its bash builder with an independent `python3` oracle (provenance in `lib/test/modules/regenerate-artifacts.inventory.md`). The same explicit-selection and focused-default-plus-final-gate discipline applies as for the modules above.

**Focused iteration over the first extraction tranche (issue #746).** Four further modules were carved out of `lib/test/run.sh` as a measured tranche, chosen by dependency cost rather than line span: `review-trigger-helpers` (eleven consecutive sections covering the review/implement trigger helpers, from the `#249` verdict deriver through `resolve-command-trigger.sh`), `experiment-records` (the `#431` unified experiment-record join), `review-stall-backstop` (the `#408` no-verdict auto-resume backstop and the `#414` post-and-annotate helper), and `prompt-extension-reader` (the consumer prompt-extension reader). Run each with `bash lib/test/run-module.sh <module-id>`; provenance is in the paired `lib/test/modules/<module-id>.inventory.md`. The `experiment-records` extraction is **deliberately partial** — the section's trailing `#431 producer pins` tail stays in `lib/test/run.sh` because it asserts against files that helper does not own, so the `#431` coverage-map label correctly remains `unmodularized`. Some contract points generalize from this tranche. A module's `minimum_assertions` floor is an **equality** check against the extraction-time executed count (a floor seeded below the real count lets assertions vanish silently), and a module re-deriving a repo root must spell it `REPO_ROOT="$LIB/.."` rather than the monolith's command-substitution form, because `lib/test/pin-corpus-lint.py`'s resolver cannot see through a substitution and every pin targeting an unresolved path is silently exempt from the meta-guards while the suite still reads clean. `lib/test/module-harness.sh` carries the spelling requirement as an authoring note, and `devflow_module_build_bundle` lives there too — the shared bundle builder that reports each unusable member as its own named RED. The same explicit-selection and focused-default-plus-final-gate discipline applies as for the modules above.

**Focused iteration over the review/implement/workpad subjects (issue #1934).** Three further modules were carved out of `lib/test/run.sh`, one per largest ungrouped subject: `review-contract` (the `skills/review` assertions), `implement-contract` (the `skills/implement` assertions), and `workpad-cli` (the `scripts/workpad.py` assertions). Run each with `bash lib/test/run-module.sh <module-id>`; provenance — including each label that deliberately stayed resident in `lib/test/run.sh` because it belongs to a self-skip, a module-system self-test, or a whole-population meta-guard — is in the paired `lib/test/modules/<module-id>.inventory.md`. The same registration surfaces apply as for every extraction above (coverage map, flight-recorder floor, `ci.yml` shellcheck list, and the pin-census populations a module joins wherever it carries audited pins); the checklist is in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../../CONTRIBUTING.md). The same explicit-selection and focused-default-plus-final-gate discipline applies as for the modules above.

**Failure recap (issue #789).** On a run with one or more failures `lib/test/run.sh` prints a named `Failure recap` after its terminal summary, re-listing each failing assertion's identifier from an on-disk sibling record (`<results>.names`) that every tally-writing site appends to — including a module or pooled worker, whose private record is folded into the parent's. That record is what makes the recap cover both streams — most of run.sh's FAIL sites write their detail to stderr — so recovering *which* assertion failed reads the recap plus the stderr-merged capture (the issue-#719 `2>&1` log locally, the Actions runner log on cloud) instead of relaunching a ~10-minute suite. On zero failures no recap prints and the terminal summary is byte-identical to the issue-#456 renderer's output; the suite's exit status is preserved through the recap, which `scripts/verification-flight.py` reads to record the single-flight terminal state.

**Coverage map and ratchet (issue #591).** `lib/test/modules/coverage-map.json` records the owning module for every git-tracked depth-1 code unit under `lib/` and `scripts/` (the literal `unmodularized` marks units no *shell module* covers; an optional `focused_test` field, issue #789, additionally records the `lib/test/test_*.py` file that covers such a unit, which the guard validates as git-tracked, `test_*.py`-named, and executable in the index — the exec bit being what makes it invocable as a direct leading token on the cloud tier), for the guard-derived `run.sh` assertion-name blocks (keyed by issue label), and lists the non-code depth-1 files and the exempt subtree (`lib/test/`). A coverage guard (`lib/test/coverage_map_guard.py`, driven by the complete suite through `git` and `python3` only) turns the suite RED when a new depth-1 unit ships without a coverage decision — at any depth, with an out-of-set extension, in a new subdirectory, or misfiled as non-code — and when the map or the registry is stale, misfiled, or wrong-shape. The `run_sh_blocks` half is **derived and enforced by the guard**, not hand-maintained: it derives the issue labels asserted by `lib/test/run.sh` and by every `lib/test/modules/*.sh` — anchored on assertion-name position, so a label appearing only in a comment derives nothing — and fails RED on a `run.sh` label with no map entry, and on a **fully extracted** label (carried by a module and asserted nowhere in `run.sh`) whose entry is absent or still names `unmodularized`. A **partially extracted** label, one a module carries while assertions remain in `run.sh`, correctly keeps `unmodularized`, because a single `owner` string cannot describe split coverage. The remedy is the guard's own hand-invoked `--fix` mode (`python3 lib/test/coverage_map_guard.py . --fix`), never a hand-transcribed label list; it is deliberately not wired into the batched generated-artifact pass, where the map stays a `by-hand` judgment row the pass leaves byte-unchanged. The guard additionally fails RED when the map on disk is **not byte-identical to its canonical serialization** (issue #1065) — ordering/formatting drift that leaves the parsed value unchanged, so every ownership arm passes but a later unrelated `--fix` would silently rewrite it; the writer and this checker share one `_serialize_map` definition, and `--fix` re-canonicalizes an order-only drifted map so its remedy actually repairs the violation. A **merge conflict** in the map is a distinct case (issue #1194): the two string-sorted objects conflict textually when two branches each add a distinct adjacent key, and `--fix` cannot restore a key a take-one-side resolution dropped, so `--fix` is **not** the merge-conflict remedy. Two mechanisms close that class — a JSON-aware git merge driver (`lib/test/coverage-map-merge-driver.py`, declared for the map in `.gitattributes`, registered per clone with `--register` and verified with `--check`, and — since issue #2025 — additionally self-registered by `scripts/update-branch-checkpoint.sh` before its base merge, guarded on the `.gitattributes` declaration and fail-soft, so an adjacent-key map conflict no longer Blocks a cloud implement run whose ephemeral checkout was never registered by hand) that unions the objects per key and conflicts only on a genuine same-key divergence, and a CI-side key-retention check (`lib/test/coverage-map-retention-check.py`, wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`) that fails when a key or its `note`/`owner` content disappears relative to the merge base, covering the curated keys no arm inspects and the web-conflict-editor path the driver cannot reach. That check applies *unknown is not zero* to its own comparand: it reports three outcomes — `0` clean, `1` a dropped key measured against a SOUND comparand, and `3` a base comparand it could not establish (a shallow or partial clone, where `git merge-base` fails or names a boundary commit whose tree predates the map) — so a degraded comparison can never be read as a clean pass, and the explicit `--allow-degraded-base` acknowledgement is the only way to exit 0 from one. The soundness qualifier on `1` is load-bearing: when no merge base can be computed the check compares against the base ref's own **tip**, so a key that ref added *after* the branch forked reads as absent from head there. That is a substituted comparand, not a dropped key, so such a difference is reported as unconfirmed — naming the substitution — and takes the `3`/acknowledged-`0` path, rather than a `1` no acknowledgement could reach. A loss against a real merge base keeps `1` and stays un-acknowledgeable. That is also what makes CI's `fetch-depth: 0` self-enforcing: removing it turns the step RED rather than silently dropping the protection. So every PR that adds an assertion named for a new issue owes that label a map entry — see [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../../CONTRIBUTING.md)'s *Coverage-map block ownership*. When you register a new focused module, follow the module-authoring checklist in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../../CONTRIBUTING.md) (which also records the coverage-map ownership update), so the map keeps every future extraction ticket a ranked to-do list.

**Shared harness-helper contract.** `lib/test/module-harness.sh` exposes a namespaced static-pin API — `devflow_module_pin_count` (a fixed-string occurrence counter), `devflow_module_pin_unique` (an exactly-one presence pin), and `devflow_module_pin_present` (an at-least-one presence pin, for values that legitimately recur) — so a sourceable module carries no private copy of the pin machinery. Behavioral regressions use ordinary executable tests; the former mutation-taking harness helper is retired.
