Alongside the pin API the harness owns the shared execution helpers the tier runners reuse: `devflow_run_full_suite_module` (supervise one sourceable module in its own process group, fold its private tally into the shared one), `devflow_run_focused_python_test` (run one standalone `python3` suite with `PYTHON_COLORS=0` — one application of the rendered-output-probe colour convention, whose single statement is [`test-suite-probe-conventions.md`](test-suite-probe-conventions.md), not this clause — capturing and pure-bash-indenting any traceback on failure, and asserting `rc == 0`), `devflow_run_sharded_python_test` (issue #870 — its concurrent sibling: run ONE `python3` unittest file as many bounded-concurrency selector processes and fold every one of them into a **single** `assert_eq`, so a module's emitted tally stays independent of the host's `cpu_count` and the registry/`run.sh`-operand/tally equality triple is unaffected; it enumerates the file's tests by collection, schedules them through a dynamic work queue bounded by the same `_devflow_pool_resolve_width` the pool uses, parses each unit's executed count from that unit's **stderr** capture alone, and fails closed on every arm — an unestablished enumeration, an enumeration establishing zero tests, a unit that records no exit status, a non-zero unit, a unit reporting anything other than exactly one executed test, a dispatched/executed total short of the enumerated one, or (issue #890) an unrecognized value of its optional fourth argument, the **population mode**: `full` — the default when the argument is absent or empty — enumerates every test, `smoke` enumerates only the first test of each class the loader produced a test for, and any other value is refused before anything is enumerated or dispatched. A `smoke` run states the bound in the same statement as its tally, so a bounded run and a full one are never byte-identical; `lib/test/run-module.sh` and `devflow_run_full_suite_module` each *assign* that mode unconditionally rather than defaulting it off the environment, so `smoke` is only ever selected by `run-module.sh`'s explicit `--heavy-units full|smoke` flag and never acquired from an ambient variable), `devflow_fold_module_failures` (add the boundary-failure channel into the terminal failure count), `devflow_module_allocate_owned_directory` (allocate a validated private scratch root), and the bounded concurrent Python-suite pool (`devflow_pool_open`/`devflow_pool_join`, described below). The counter runs a **checked** `python3`: it returns an established non-negative count only after a successful readable scan and otherwise returns `unestablished` (never `0`), so a failed count surfaces as a RED assertion instead of a vacuous pass. The harness is also the single home of the shared fixture helpers `mint_blk`, `probe_tmp` and `probe_assert` — promoted out of `lib/test/run.sh` (issue #695) so exactly one definition of each exists tree-wide, with the monolith obtaining them by sourcing the harness. On entry the harness clears an inherited `DEVFLOW_GH` (announcing it on stderr) before any module body is sourced, so a focused run started with that override exported gets the same fixture isolation the complete suite's preamble already guaranteed; tests of the override contract set their own value per invocation, which the clear does not affect.

**Bounded concurrent Python-suite pool (issue #720).** The complete suite runs its long-tail Python suites through a bounded concurrent pool rather than strictly serially. Five suites — `test_module_runner.py` and the four parts `test_python_scripts.py` was split into for concurrency (`test_python_scripts.py`, `test_python_scripts_part2.py`, `_part3.py`, `_part4.py`; issue #2007) — are launched into a pool whose in-flight limit is `min(os.cpu_count() or 1, 4)` (`devflow_pool_open`/`devflow_pool_join` in `lib/test/module-harness.sh`); the coordinator's `POOL_RESERVATION_CEILING` (`lib/test/run-parallel.sh`) is 5, the member count, so it never reserves a slot the pool cannot fill. Their membership, tally modes, and self-tally reconciliation have exactly one definition — `devflow_python_suite_pool_open`/`devflow_python_suite_pool_join`, also in `lib/test/module-harness.sh` — because two drivers consume it: `lib/test/run.sh` (a local full run, and the overlap described next) and `lib/test/run-python-pool.sh` (the `python-pool` CI shard, described under the shard matrix below). On a local run the pool opens at the `test_module_runner.py` driver site — after every preceding `devflow_run_full_suite_module` call (those boundaries are complete, so no pooled suite races the main shell on them) — stays open across the last module boundary (`harness-python-guards`) and the intervening main-shell assertions, and joins **before** the `RESULTS_FILE` tally is counted, so the long pole overlaps both the last module and the shell tail. The pooled `test_module_runner.py` itself drives `harness-python-guards` through its own `run-module.sh` subprocess — since issue #890 with `--heavy-units smoke`, so that end-to-end proof no longer re-runs the module's heaviest unit in full — and that boundary therefore runs in the main shell and in the pooled suite concurrently; the overlap is safe because each execution allocates its own isolated owned scratch root, not shared mutable state. Pooling overlaps the long-pole suite with the last module boundary and the shell tail, measurably shortening wall-clock on a multi-core host. In CI the overlap is no longer how these suites are paid for at all: profiling the `monolith` shard (`lib/test/profile-suite.py`) measured the shell sitting **idle** at the join for about a fifth of that shard's wall-clock, having run out of assertions to hide the Python work behind — so the shard runner sets `DEVFLOW_SKIP_PYTHON_POOL=1` on the monolith and the same pool runs concurrently, over the same membership, on its own `python-pool` shard. Neither the assertions nor their count change; only which shard counts them does. The width is overridable by the single named environment variable `DEVFLOW_POOL_WIDTH` (a positive integer takes precedence over the `cpu_count` probe); because the cap decides a selection it is derived through the preflight-guaranteed `python3` (never a non-preflight PATH tool), and a non-positive-integer probe falls back to width 1. The cap of 4 bounds oversubscription on a cgroup-throttled CI container, where `os.cpu_count()` reports host CPUs and ignores the enforced quota. Each pooled suite runs with its own `TMPDIR` under a private scratch root allocated through `devflow_module_allocate_owned_directory`, writes its verdict(s) to a private tally validated and appended to `RESULTS_FILE` after the suite is reaped (a self-tally suite like `test_python_scripts.py` emits one `PASS`/`FAIL` line per assertion), and `test_module_harness.py` and its `--signal-matrix-capability` probe stay serial on the main shell — the supervisor forks its worker with job control disabled and that suite asserts on the inherited `SIGINT` disposition. `_devflow_full_suite_signal` is generalized from a single scalar child slot to a run-wide registry of live children, so one delivered signal terminates every in-flight child's process group before the handler exits, and a supervisor PID-rendezvous timeout under pool saturation is absorbed by re-running that suite serially rather than recorded as a failure. The suite reports byte-identical tallies whether the pool runs at its core-derived width or is forced to width 1.

**Memoized per-source parses (issue #881).** `lib/test/pin-corpus-lint.py` and `lib/test/mutation-pin-census.py` hold process-global `functools.lru_cache` memos over their per-source parses, so a source re-presented within one process is reused rather than re-parsed, for as long as it stays inside each memo's bound (those bounds are sized in the two modules, against the working set each one actually repeats over; an evicted entry is recomputed, never answered wrongly). That matters here because `lib/test/test_pin_corpus_lint.py` is dispatched twice concurrently — once on the main shell's `harness-python-guards` boundary, in full, and once inside the pooled `test_module_runner.py` real-runner meta-test, since issue #890 as a bounded `smoke` subset rather than a second full population — and its heaviest class is sharded across bounded-concurrency selector processes, so a memo that captured repository state would make an outcome depend on ordering. The two-limb argument for why none of them does — key purity for the per-source memos, module statelessness for the argument-less `_load_mutation_census_module` — is stated once in `lib/test/test_pin_corpus_lint.py`'s module docstring and mirrored at the shard-dispatch site in `lib/test/modules/harness-python-guards.sh`, which are a coupled pair; it is not restated here. It is executable rather than prose: `MemoizedParseContractTests` and the `StaticPinWorktreeCompositionTests.test_leaked_*` probes are named there as the tests that hold it.

**Single-EXIT-trap registry contract.** `lib/test/run.sh` installs exactly one `trap _suite_cleanup EXIT` over an append-only registry of temporary files and directories; `_suite_cleanup` removes every registered entry on exit. Helpers in `lib/test/module-harness.sh` — the per-module boundary and the concurrent pool included — install **no** `EXIT` trap of their own: they only save, install, and restore the temporary `HUP`/`INT`/`TERM` handlers, and clean their own scratch roots and private tallies inline on every return path. Re-registering a longer `trap … EXIT` would replace the sole handler rather than extend it (the failure mode PR #539 hit, losing `SKIPS_FILE`), so the pool adds no second `EXIT` trap and leaves `_suite_cleanup` the only one installed.

**Tracked-only module fixtures (issue #714).** A module that needs a repository image builds it from the **git index** — every tracked blob `git ls-files -s -z` reports, reproduced file by file at its own relative path, with each file's mode taken from the index rather than from the working tree — never by `cp -R`-ing a whole top-level directory. The directory form is the trap: one tracked file inside an otherwise-untracked directory drags the entire directory in, and because `.claude/settings.json` is tracked the `regenerate-artifacts` module used to copy the whole untracked `.claude/` tree — including any `git worktree` checkouts under `.claude/worktrees/` — into every per-assertion fixture copy, which on a developer checkout was the dominant cost of a full local suite run (CI, behind a fresh `actions/checkout`, never carried the payload and is unaffected). Untracked state cannot enter such an image by construction, so no build-cache prune step is needed; three skip arms — the two non-blob index modes (a gitlink `160000` and a symlink `120000`) plus a blob the working tree does not carry — are each taken with their own named stderr breadcrumb and subtracted from the completeness denominator by name, a copy or mode failure is counted on its own channel rather than vanishing into that gap, and unmerged paths contribute once rather than once per stage. `regenerate-artifacts` pairs its bash builder with an independent `python3` oracle that re-reads the index itself — a deliberate coupled mirror, edited together — and both fail closed on an unestablished measurement rather than reporting a vacuous zero.

**Selectable-module interruption contract.** On POSIX hosts with signal and process-group support, both `lib/test/run-module.sh` and the complete-suite boundary supervise a selected module in a dedicated process group. Their temporary `HUP`, `INT`, and `TERM` handlers forward the signal, escalate resistant helpers within a bound, reap the group, and remove the boundary-owned tally and scratch root; the full-suite boundary restores the caller's signal traps after a normal return without replacing its `EXIT` trap. Modules nest ordinary `TMPDIR` allocations under the validated boundary root, while `create-issue-contract` reuses that owned root and keeps idempotent signal/exit cleanup as its fallback. `lib/test/test_module_harness.py` exercises each signal against parent-only, module-only, and process-group delivery for both boundaries, including launch-window and resistant-helper paths; `lib/test/run.sh` records a visible `host-capability` skip when the host cannot express that matrix.

A module run through the focused runner may not self-skip: `lib/test/run-module.sh` overrides `skip` to a fatal `exit 1` inside the module worker, so a skippable gate must stay in the complete suite (which owns the `#456` skip accounting) rather than be smuggled into a focused module that would silently neither pass nor fail.

**In-run parallel full-suite coordinator (issue #1086).** CI's shard partition existed only for CI: an agent's final verification gate ran `lib/test/run.sh` as one serial process and pushed the whole assertion stream back into model context. `lib/test/run-parallel.sh` is that partition run *in one checkout*. Before launching any shard it runs a read-only, sub-second generated-artifact drift preflight (issue #1244) and refuses to launch on a positively-attributed drift, failing open (warn-and-proceed) on an inconclusive result; the standalone `lib/test/run-parallel.sh --preflight` mode (issue #1288) runs that same check with the same verdict contract and launches nothing, so the #1132 shard-decomposition route can run the identical pre-launch check before its own shard loop. It derives its launch population from `lib/test/run-shard.sh --list-shards` (never a copied shard list, so a shard added to the dispatcher is launched here with no second edit), allocates a fresh run root under `.prflow/tmp/parallel-suite/` — falling back to `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/devflow-parallel-suite/` when the checkout cannot hold one (read-only, full, or name space exhausted), and refusing by name when neither can — and gives every launched shard a private tally directory and its own captured log there, plus a private `TMPDIR` allocated **outside** the checkout — a shard's own `mktemp -d` fixtures must not land inside a git working tree, which is what a whole class of this suite's non-git-tree assertions is premised on. Concurrency is bounded by one process budget resolved through the preflight-guaranteed `python3` (`DEVFLOW_SUITE_PROCESS_BUDGET` overrides it when it is a positive integer; an unestablished probe fails closed to a serial-but-complete width 1), capped at eight; the nested Python pool's width is *reserved out of* that same budget — one to four slots, exported to the `python-pool` shard as `DEVFLOW_POOL_WIDTH` — rather than added to it, so the total process count is what the scheduler thinks it is. Each shard is launched as its own process-group leader behind a pending-signal replay guard across the fork-to-PID-registration window, so a `HUP`/`INT`/`TERM` — including one delivered inside that window, which is parked and replayed rather than dropped — terminates and **reaps** the launched population before the parent exits. The parent waits for the complete launched population, then hands `lib/test/shard-tally.py combine` the **explicit tally paths of this run only** — never a `--scan` of the shared parent, which would let a stale sibling run satisfy the missing-shard floor. It also passes `--require-shards "$SHARDS"` (issue #1289), reconciling the recombined tallies against the true `run-shard.sh --list-shards` partition **by name** — a missing, unexpected, or duplicated shard fails closed naming the gap — rather than trusting `combine`'s bare `--expect` count, which a run over one shard with `--expect 1` could satisfy while covering the wrong partition.

Two properties are load-bearing for how its output is read. First, **retention is not truncation**: the aggregate renders at most the number of entries per detail class that `lib/test/run-parallel.sh` sets in its own `DETAIL_CAP` constant and passes as `--detail-cap`, and announces the omitted count; `shard-tally.py`'s own default is uncapped, so CI's aggregator — which omits the flag — renders everything exactly as before, while every launched shard's complete log stays readable under the printed retained-log root — so a failing gate is diagnosed from the retained logs rather than by relaunching. Second, **this timing is not CI's**: CI isolates each shard on a separate runner, whereas these shards share one host's CPU, memory, checkout and process namespace, so the coordinator's `real` time is the slowest shard *under contention with its siblings*. The coordinator reports that wall-clock time itself — a `run-parallel: elapsed <N>s` line (bash `SECONDS`, no external program) printed to stdout before the clean/failed branch, which therefore cannot skip it (issue #1808) — so a launch's runtime is captured in the run's own `Verification evidence:` record instead of being recoverable only by hand from an Actions transcript. `lib/test/run.sh` remains the serial primitive — the `monolith` shard runs it, and the documented uncovered-surface fallback still names it — and focused modules remain the iteration default; a real-population invocation from *inside* a shard is refused by name, because that would fork a second suite underneath the first. Mid-iteration on a tier where this coordinator meaningfully exceeds a single shard (the cloud implement tier, measured under issue #1253), that same `monolith` shard may stand in for the whole suite on a `run.sh`-resident surface via `lib/test/run-shard.sh monolith` — a cheaper whole-file run that never discharges the completion gate; the operative rule and its limits live in the prompt extensions. Coverage is `lib/test/modules/parallel-suite-runner.sh`, which drives the coordinator against synthetic dispatchers (the real population is never launched from a registered module) with `shard-tally.py` unmocked.

**Bounded heavy units on the focused runner (issue #890).** `lib/test/run-module.sh` takes `--heavy-units full|smoke`, the only channel that ever selects a bounded population for a module's heaviest unit (the mode's meaning is defined once at `devflow_run_sharded_python_test`, above). The runner *assigns* `full` unconditionally rather than defaulting it off the environment and applies `export -n`, so an inherited `MODULE_HEAVY_UNIT_MODE` can neither shrink what a run executes nor leak a bound into anything launched underneath it; the flag is refused when repeated, when its value is anything but `full` or `smoke`, and when its value is missing, so a typo can never read as a bounded pass. Because the reduction is deliberate, the runner also makes it visible in both directions. When `full` was requested it scans the module's own log — with a bash `case`, never a non-preflight `grep`, since the result decides the emitted failure tally — and **fails** the run if the log nevertheless carries a bound, which is the last link no earlier guard can see: a module that forwards a literal `smoke` or defaults to one would otherwise drop a full population from CI while every tally stayed green. When a bound *was* requested it prints a separate notice line naming the mode, deliberately not folded into the `Module <id>: N passed, M failed` summary, whose shape is a machine-consumed contract (`lib/test/shard-tally.py` anchors a regex on it, and the step-8 real-runner meta-tests assert it as an exact `splitlines()` member). The notice reports what was *requested*, and says so: only a module that reads the mode bounds anything, so whether a unit actually bounded its population is the driver's own tally line above it in the same log. The recombined gate summary cannot express a bound at all — the unrequested-bound failure is what makes an unintended reduction gate-visible, the same "a reduced run is never a clean pass" rule issue #456 established for skips.

**The one accounted exception, and it is tier-asymmetric (issue #838).** A module may declare that *this host* cannot express a condition — the `host-capability` kind alone — through `module_host_capability_skip <name> <reason> <assertions-covered>` in `lib/test/module-harness.sh`. It is a wrapper, not a second producer: it delegates to the same `skip` helper, so the reserved `  NOTE ` emit still has exactly one writer. Under the **complete suite** the boundary binds the module child a *private* skip tally plus a credit record (the `RESULTS_FILE`/`.names` pattern) and folds them back after `wait`, folding only `host-capability` records and crediting each arm's declared assertion count against the module's `minimum_assertions` floor via a computed effective minimum — so a host that cannot express the condition reports a **visible skip** instead of tripping the floor with a count mismatch that reads like a regression. Every degraded shape fails closed: a malformed or floor-meeting credit grants zero, and a skip record that never reached the shared tally forfeits every credit so the raw floor stands. Under the **focused runner** the same call still hits that runner's fatal override by design — the raw helper stays unreachable there, and the fatal message names the wrapper so the stop is not misread as a module contract violation. The reachability answer is therefore "yes on the complete suite, through the accounted wrapper; no on the focused tier", and a host taking such an arm runs the complete suite to have the skip folded.

Each mapping declares its source path and a positive `minimum_assertions` floor. Selection and whole-registry validation finish before the module body is sourced; unknown or invalid selections fail closed before any module body runs, and an executed module falling below its assertion floor fails the run afterward; and every successfully selected invocation writes a unique persistent log (by default under `.prflow/tmp/test-module-logs/`; overridable with `--log-dir`; logs accumulate without automatic pruning — clear the directory or point `--log-dir` elsewhere during heavy iteration) with a terminal summary and a failure recap whenever an assertion or module boundary fails. Focused runs are the iteration default; they do not replace the whole-suite completion gate, which each tier discharges by its own signal (issue #1607). The complete suite executes the same sourceable module through `lib/test/module-harness.sh`, enforcing the same floor. Its missing, crashing, malformed-tally, and assertion-shortfall failures use a separate boundary tally that is added to the terminal failure count. Each module writes assertions to a private tally which is validated before being appended to the shared suite tally, so an over-broad module write cannot erase an earlier verdict. A CI-side diff-time gate protects the floors themselves against silent erosion: `lib/test/assertion-floor-retention-check.py` (wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `lint` job, which already checks out full history, mirroring the coverage-map retention check above) fails RED when a registered module's `minimum_assertions` floor in `scripts/workflow-flight-recorder-registry.json` is **lowered** relative to the merge base, unless the decrease is declared with a reason in `lib/test/assertion-floor-retention-allow.json`. It covers **every** registered module — not only the `exact`-policy subset — closing a gap where lowering the floor for a module carrying no `assertion_floor_policy` was caught by nothing. (That population is identified by its defining property rather than enumerated or counted — it changes as modules are added and re-policed.) The merge-base plumbing it shares with `lib/test/coverage-map-retention-check.py` lives in the common `lib/test/retention_check_common.py`, so each gate applies *unknown is not zero* to its base comparand identically (a base ref it cannot soundly establish is reported as degraded, never as a clean pass).

**Suite routing is asserted against the tree, not against its own lists (issue #867).** `lib/test/test_module_runner.py` classifies every `lib/test/test_*.py` suite into exactly one of `POOLED_SUITES`, `SERIAL_BY_EXCLUSION_SUITES`, or `MODULE_DRIVEN_SUITES`. Total-and-disjoint membership was previously the whole guarantee, so a suite could sit in `MODULE_DRIVEN_SUITES` while `lib/test/run.sh` also drove it serially and the run stayed green while executing it twice — the defect issue #865 fixed, latent for nearly a year. The two membership tuples are now executable claims about where the tree actually drives each suite: a `MODULE_DRIVEN_SUITES` member must occur zero times in `lib/test/run.sh` and in exactly one file across `lib/test/modules/*.sh` plus `lib/test/module-harness.sh`, and a `SERIAL_BY_EXCLUSION_SUITES` member must occur at least once in `lib/test/run.sh` — so deleting a driver block without reclassifying is caught too. The matcher is the `$LIB`-anchored quoted invocation shape `"$LIB/test/<name>"`, not a bare basename: a module file can mention a suite's path in comments without driving it — `lib/test/modules/create-issue-contract.sh` does — so a basename scan would be red against a correct tree, and it would let a comment satisfy the presence direction. Whole-line comments are stripped before matching either way, so a commented-out driver cannot satisfy a presence claim. The module-side claim counts **distinct files**, never occurrences, because one owning module legitimately carries several occurrences for a suite it drives; that also avoids introducing any suite-to-owning-module mapping, which does not exist. `POOLED_SUITES` is deliberately excluded — an existing pin already asserts set equality against the parsed `devflow_pool_open` triples in `module-harness.sh`'s `devflow_python_suite_pool_open` (the pool's single membership definition, which `run.sh` and `run-python-pool.sh` share), a stronger guarantee than a name scan. The scanning helper takes the `run.sh` path and the module directory as parameters (defaulting to the real tree, a single-level directory listing via `iterdir()`, no repository-root-anchored walk), so each direction is proven RED against a planted violation in a scratch copy, with a planted bare-basename comment as the positive control. Retiring the per-file `#810` guard this generalizes lowers `harness-python-guards`' coupled assertion-count triple from 39 to 38.

**The suite tallies three outcomes — passed, failed, and *skipped* (issue #456).** A check can **self-skip**: it neither passed nor failed because the environment could not run it or express its condition. The terminal summary makes skips visible so `0 failed` is never mistaken for "everything ran": with nothing skipped it prints `N passed, M failed` (byte-identical to the historical format); with `K` skips it prints `N passed, M failed, K skipped`, followed by one line per skipped check naming the check, its **kind**, and the reason. The `[ "$FAIL" -eq 0 ]` pass/fail predicate is unchanged — a skip never fails the suite (a run whose pass/fail/skip tallies cannot even be *derived* aborts fail-closed before that predicate, by design — unknown is never reported as zero). Each skip carries a **kind** — `blocking-gate` (a real gate that *should* have run here but could not — e.g. the `#434` stale-prose self-scan when `origin/main` is unresolvable or the working tree is dirty) and `host-capability` (the host cannot express the condition — e.g. the `#423 T6b` strict-ASCII-stdout probe on a build that won't downgrade stdout to ASCII). Every self-skip routes through a single `skip <name> <kind> <reason>` helper (a comment-aware meta-assertion turns the suite RED if a `printf '  NOTE ` emit — the contiguous emit shape a maintainer would actually write; an echo-based or variable-assembled emit is a disclosed non-goal — appears in `lib/test/run.sh` outside that helper), and the renderer lives in `lib/test/summary.sh` (added to the CI shellcheck scope explicitly, since `lib/test/` is otherwise excluded). **Every job that runs the suite checks out full history (`fetch-depth: 0`), because a bounded checkout depth silently converts a history-reading gate into a `blocking-gate` skip (issue #1219).** Two gates read history that the last N commits need not contain: the `#434` stale-prose self-scan needs `origin/main` to resolve, and the `#719` baseline-corpus control reads blobs at a *fixed* past commit via `git show <ref>:<path>` — a commit that only moves further behind the branch tip, so no bounded depth keeps resolving it. `ci.yml` has always checked out full history; since issue #1219 so do the two agent-running cloud jobs that verify in-env, `devflow-implement.yml`'s `claude` job and `devflow.yml`'s `command` job (each checkout's own comment states the requirement and why a targeted fetch of the needed commits was rejected). **A skipped check is never a clean pass:** `/prflow:review-and-fix` records any skips in `verification_evidence.skipped_checks` (distinct from `result: skipped`, which means the whole suite run was skipped), and a run with a non-empty `skipped_checks` is not reported by the loop as a clean pass.

**CI shell-lint scope for `lib/test/` (issue #745).** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `lint` job lints the repo's shell with `git ls-files '*.sh' | grep -v '^lib/test/' | xargs -r shellcheck --severity=warning -e SC1091` — the `lib/test/` exclusion exists because that tree carries deliberately-malformed `gh`-stub and adversarial fixtures. The excluded-but-shipped files are re-added by a hand-maintained explicit file list in the same job, and `lib/test/run.sh` gets a **dedicated step** because it is the only file that needs `--extended-analysis=false`: ShellCheck's dataflow pass allocates on the order of 15 GB on a file this size and evicts the 16 GB runner mid-analysis, while the flag brings it to roughly 1.15 GB and ten seconds. The flag alone is not sufficient — `--extended-analysis` landed in ShellCheck **0.10.0**, and on the 0.9.x that `ubuntu-latest` ships the flag errors while the equivalent `# shellcheck extended-analysis=false` directive has no effect — it is reported as an unknown directive (SC1107) and ignored — so the lint would OOM exactly as if the flag were absent. The job therefore installs a **pinned** ShellCheck (≥ 0.10.0) onto `PATH` ahead of every ShellCheck step rather than trusting the runner image's version, which drifts with the image; `lib/test/run.sh` asserts that floor, the flag, and the pin. The measured memory/time figures live in the `ci.yml` pin step's own comment as a dated past-time snapshot to re-measure after a ShellCheck upgrade — not an invariant. The two sides of the carve-out are reconciled mechanically by `lib/test/lint-carveout-guard.py` (driven from `lib/test/run.sh`), which derives the set of `lib/test/**/*.sh` files CI actually lints from `ci.yml` and fails RED, naming the path, when a tracked `lib/test` script is neither in that set nor under the single declared exempt prefix `lib/test/fixtures/`. It is a best-effort reader of a human-maintained YAML file and fails **closed** on every shape it cannot interpret (unreadable or non-YAML `ci.yml`, no locatable `shellcheck` invocation, a changed `grep -v '^lib/test/'` exclusion, or a `git ls-files` enumeration returning zero rows — an unestablished population, never an empty one), because a red guard forces human attention rather than reporting coverage it never established; its two known limitations — a pipeline restructured so the glob and the `shellcheck` literal are no longer co-located, and a step gated inactive by an `if:` condition it does not evaluate — are enumerated in its module docstring.

**Suite-side ruff Python-lint gate (issue #1621).** CI's `lint` job runs `ruff` but is *not* the required check, so a Python lint regression was invisible to both an implement run's own completion gate and the merge gate — `lib + python tests` stayed green while a violation rode through. The remedy is a gate inside the suite: a `monolith`-shard-resident block in `lib/test/run.sh` runs `ruff check` over the `git ls-files '*.py'` set (index-reading, no recursive walk) through the suite's `assert_eq`, failing closed on *any* nonzero rc (findings rc 1 or ruff's own error rc 2) and surfacing ruff's output so the run can self-correct. The scan passes `--no-force-exclude`, so a future ruff config pairing `force-exclude` with an `exclude` covering tracked `*.py` cannot turn the gate into a vacuous rc-0 green over an empty checked set. Presence is **execution-verified** (`ruff --version`, then `python3 -m ruff --version`), never `command -v`, so a present-but-unrunnable ruff routes to the skip rather than a spurious FAIL. **Selection is family-preferring, not first-runnable (issue #2050):** `devflow_ruff_select_cmd` chooses the candidate — PATH `ruff` preferred, `python3 -m ruff` as fallback — whose reported major.minor family matches the pin in `.prflow/lint-manifest.json` (reduced by `devflow_ruff_family`), so a stale off-family `ruff` at the front of PATH no longer judges the tree under the wrong rule set when an in-family interpreter module is reachable. When **neither** candidate matches the manifest family the gate self-skips (kind **`blocking-gate`**) naming the resolved version and the pinned family, rather than linting under the wrong rules; when the manifest pin is a sentinel reading (`unreadable`, `parse-failed`, `absent`) selection falls back to today's first-runnable order. Because ruff is **not** preflight-guaranteed (`lib/preflight.sh` guarantees only `git`/`gh`/`jq`/`python3`), a host without it does not silently pass: the gate self-skips through the existing `skip` helper with kind **`blocking-gate`**, so a skipped lint reads as *not a clean pass* under the `#456` accounting. A non-vacuity assertion proves the gate actually ran by requiring ruff's specific signal — exit 1 plus the `F401` (unused-import) code — on a planted violation under the gitignored `.prflow/tmp`, so an inert or errored ruff cannot fake the proof. `F401` is used rather than `E731` because `E731` is not in ruff 0.16's default select set. For the gate to *arm* on the required check rather than self-skip, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s shard job installs a pinned `ruff==0.16.*` on every shard uniformly; that pin is one member of a **reconciliation set** the suite holds to the manifest family. The set is reconciled **mechanically**, not by comment: `run.sh` extracts the pinned spec from each job's uncommented lines and asserts that the shard job declares one at all (its removal would leave the gate self-skipping while `lib + python tests` stayed green, since a `#456` skip never fails the suite), that the `lint` job still declares one — the positive control that stops two absent reads satisfying the comparison vacuously — that the two `ci.yml` specs are equal, and (**issue #2050**) that the `ruff==` install spec in `.github/workflows/devflow-implement.yml`'s `claude` job — the step that arms the gate for the cloud implement tier's in-env verification — shares the manifest ruff family too, so a family bump cannot silently leave the implement workflow behind. That last assertion (reusing `devflow_ruff_pin`) protects this repository's own workflow copy; a consumer's installed copy converges on their next `install.sh` run, and a sentinel reading of the spec fails the assertion rather than passing vacuously. **Scope is ruff only:** `shellcheck` and `actionlint` share CI's non-required `lint` job and are deliberately out of scope for this gate. **The repository's ruff settings live in the root `ruff.toml` (issue #742), which governs this gate and CI's `lint` job alike because both run from the repository root; it must never gain an `exclude`, `extend-exclude` or `force-exclude` key, which would narrow the checked set out from under the gate.**

**Brand-`DevFlow` bucket reconciliation (issue #1745).** The consumer-facing `devflow`→`prflow` rename left a residue of brand-cased `DevFlow` prose with no recorded statement of which occurrences are finished, load-bearing, or still owed. `lib/test/lint-brand-devflow-sweep.py` (a member of the `lib/test/lint-*.py` family) closes the class: it enumerates the tracked tree via index-reading `git ls-files` (never a root-anchored walk, issue #711) and classifies every case-sensitive `b"DevFlow"` occurrence into a recorded bucket in `lib/test/brand-devflow-buckets.json`, failing closed **both directions** — a new occurrence in an unclassified file reds, and a stale assignment (a swept/deleted pending file, or a frozen-provenance entry matching nothing) reds. The buckets, in first-match order: `transient` (a changeset `version-consolidate` deletes on merge — excluded so its deletion cannot fire a false stale RED), `frozen-record` (append-only `.prflow/learnings/`+`.prflow/logs/` byte-contents), `frozen-historical` (`CHANGELOG.md` history), `frozen-tooling` (this feature's own lint/data files, whose literals are the matching machinery), `frozen-provenance` (a quoted `"DevFlow"` VALUE a superseded selector matches, counted independently of the first-match short-circuit so the reverse guard cannot fail open), and `pending` (ordinary renameable prose, recorded per file in `pending_sweep_baseline`).

A sixth, **occurrence-level** freeze (`frozen.occurrences`, issue #2003) handles a file that mixes renameable and must-keep `DevFlow`: each entry names a `file` and a `context` substring, every `DevFlow` on a line of that file containing the `context` is frozen and **subtracted from that file's renameable remainder**, so a two-spelling explainer, a superseded-spelling reference, or a pinned user-facing string on a specific line stays frozen while the file's ordinary occurrences are still swept — no whole-file bucket needed. On the provenance branch the quoted-value brand is excluded from the occurrence count so a context matching the value line is not subtracted twice; a stale entry (its `context` matches no brand-bearing line) fails the run closed, like the provenance reverse check. Renameable-bucket reconciliation is per-file **PRESENCE, not count**: an exact count of a high-churn file (`lib/test/run.sh`) drifts on a clean-textual concurrent merge, so a file already in `pending_sweep_baseline` is known sweep-debt that does not re-red while only a currently-clean file introducing brand prose does. Frozen buckets are not count-bounded, and an unreadable tracked file fails the run closed. **The mass renameable-prose sweep to `PRFlow` that drains `pending_sweep_baseline` proceeds in batches (issue #1985, continued by #2003); each batch renames ordinary prose and reclassifies clearly-frozen occurrences into the correct bucket until the baseline is empty.**
